# Phase 7K — Failure Recovery, Capacity, Backpressure & Regional Topology — AI Voice Agent Platform

Architecture specification. It contains no application code, no SQL migration, no Alembic revision, no deployment manifest and no runtime configuration.

## 1. Document Authority, Baseline and Scope

### 1.2 Provenance tags

Every value and rule in this document carries one of these tags.

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| [FROZEN] | Stated by an approved Phase 1–7J artifact or an executed migration. Cited, never changed here. |
| [OD] | Fixed by an owner decision. Earlier decisions are cited by their ID; the five new ones are OD-7K-01 … OD-7K-05 (§27). |
| [7K] | A design rule of this document, inside the space the frozen sources leave open. |
| [CAND] | A candidate engineering value. It is unmeasured, it binds nobody, and it becomes a value only through the benchmark named beside it. |
| [MEAS] | A value that has no defensible default and must come from a measurement. It is carried as a symbol. |
| [LATER] | Owned by a later phase or by a named owner outside 7K. |

**No measured value exists in this document.** No benchmark, load test, chaos test or restore drill was executed for it. Every number is [FROZEN], [OD] or [CAND].

### 1.4 Authority

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| AUT-7K-01 | 7K is bound by 7A AUTH-01: it does not contradict 7A–7J. Where 7K needs something a frozen document does not contain, it records a controlled reconciliation for 7L (§31.1) and does not edit the source. |
| AUT-7K-02 | Authority is per concern (7A §5; 7B §5.1). Executed migrations govern physical schema. The owning Phase-6 document governs domain semantics. 7D–7J govern their own mechanisms. 7K governs only: recovery scheduling and sequencing, capacity and sizing method, backpressure and fairness, worker lifecycle values, regional topology and disaster restoration. |
| AUT-7K-03 | 7K sets values only where a frozen document hands the value to 7K by name (§3). It does not reopen OD-7B-01/02, OD-7C-01…07, OD-7D-01…03, OD-7E-01…03, OD-7G-01…04, OD-7H-01…18, OD-7I-01…31 or OD-7J-01…15. |
| AUT-7K-04 | A statement in this document that a component, function, table, role or check "exists" is made only where it was read in the repository. Everything else is described as required, designed or future. |

### 1.5 Scope

In scope: the end-to-end recovery model; recovery precedence; Voice, Billing and mandatory-work recovery scheduling; event-transport Redis capacity, failover values and generation migration; backpressure and tenant fairness; the capacity model and parameter registry; worker lifecycle, scheduling and shutdown; regional topology and disaster restoration; operational capacity of retention, erasure and privacy controls; validation of the 7J measurement parameters; the test and benchmark plan; the five owner decisions.

Out of scope, and not done here: any SQL, migration, Alembic revision, runtime worker, production configuration or deployment file; any change to a route, error code, event, envelope, stream key, consumer group, retry budget, retention period or operator authority; any vendor choice; Phase 7L.

## 2. Assumptions and Design-versus-Runtime Status

### 2.1 What exists today, and what does not

| Area | State in the repository | Consequence for 7K |
|---|---|---|
| Phase 5 schema | 112 executed migrations | Cited as physical fact |
| D0 backend foundation | `backend/voice_agent/platform/…`, API skeleton, tests | No relay, consumer, gateway, dispatcher, reconciler or reaper exists |
| Relay (7D), transport adapter (7E), handler-contract record (7F), recovery ledger (7G), delivery schema extensions (7H), gateway, accounting, completion evidence, erasure journal, subject index, sealing, access guard (7I), observation accounting and telemetry stack (7J) | Designed only. Each needs the governed migrations and implementation its own document lists | Every 7K rule that relies on one of them is gated by that document's activation blocker (§29) |
| Voice accumulator, finalization guard, persisted service-end time | Not in the schema (7B §26.1; verified in `012_5C.sql`: no accumulator for speech seconds, synthesized characters or agent minutes, no guard, `completed_at` nullable) | The Voice recovery finalizer (§12) is designed against the obligations IO-7B-15 … IO-7B-17 and cannot be activated before them |
| Persisted heartbeat of a call or conversation | None in PostgreSQL: no migration `001`–`112` contains a heartbeat column. The frozen stale-session reaper reads a Redis `session:*` heartbeat with a TTL (3B §17, §18.2) | 7K does not invent a heartbeat column. Durable staleness evidence is limited to existing columns (§12.2) |
| Production infrastructure | None described as deployed | Every topology statement is a requirement |

### 2.2 Assumptions

| ID | Assumption | Basis | If false |
|---|---|---|---|
| ASM-7K-01 | A region has at least three availability zones | 3F §10.1 ("1/3 of cluster capacity") | A region with fewer zones cannot place the event-transport cluster under TOP-7K-06; such a region is not conformant and is not activated |
| ASM-7K-02 | The managed PostgreSQL service and the object store are not characterized: failover mode, replication synchrony, incarnation behaviour and object-store semantics are unknown | 7I JRN-19 makes the same statement | Each property is a validation item (§29.3); the fail-closed branch applies until it is validated |
| ASM-7K-03 | The Celery broker is the shared hot-tier Redis (7D §33.1; 3F §16), which is a different cluster from the event transport (7E DEP-01, DEP-02) | Frozen | — |
| ASM-7K-04 | Load is unknown. No request, call, event or tenant count is assumed | 7A §32.2 | The capacity model (§15) is symbolic |

### 2.3 Wording reconciliation: Socket.IO

The 7K assignment text describes Socket.IO as "dashboard/supervisor-only". 7A WS-01 is the frozen position: raw WebSocket is the standard for every realtime channel, no Socket.IO server is built, and the Socket.IO role is satisfied only to the extent 6A froze it. 7K follows 7A. Nothing in this document depends on Socket.IO, and no event-bus operation is placed on any WebSocket path.

## 3. Incoming Handoff Reconciliation

### 3.1 Status vocabulary

Each obligation has one **design status** and zero or more **open gates**.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| DR | DESIGN RESOLVED: this document states the architecture decision, its rules, its failure behaviour and its validation method. |
| OD | OWNER DECISION REQUIRED at the time of discovery. Four were decided on 2026-10-12 and the fifth, OD-7K-05, on 2026-10-13 (§27); the row shows the decision that resolved it. None is open. |
| BLK | BLOCKED: the design cannot be completed in 7K. |
| +ID | IMPLEMENTATION DEFERRED: nothing is built; the named obligation carries it. |
| +MR | MEASUREMENT REQUIRED: the source's exit condition asks for a measured value, a drill or a validation that no design document can supply. **The exit condition is not met.** |
| +CR | CONTROLLED RECONCILIATION REQUIRED (added in the remediation): the 7K design is complete and fails closed, but the behaviour cannot be activated until the named conflict of §31.1 is consolidated in 7L and implemented, because a frozen contract does not yet express something the design needs. **The exit condition is not met.** |

A row marked DR+MR or DR+CR closes the design and leaves the source's exit condition open. It is not a closed handoff.

### 3.2 Register

"Owner" is the implementation owner. "Activation" names the gate of §29.2 that must hold before the behaviour is relied on.

**Reading of the totals.** No handoff is blocked at design level. Most are not finished: seventy-six rows have an exit condition ("measured", "values fixed", "drill performed", "validated") that only an executed measurement can satisfy. They are listed as gates in §29 and handed on in §30.

**Change in the remediation.** Design resolved: 92, unchanged. Blocked: 0, unchanged. Implementation deferred: 47, unchanged. Measurement required: 76, unchanged. New: eight rows also need a controlled reconciliation of a frozen contract before the behaviour they describe can be activated. None of the eight is marked BLK, because for each the 7K design is complete and its fail-closed branch is the behaviour in force until then: no key is deleted (GEN-7K-11, SUP-7K-09), no recovered content is stored (MRA-7K-06), no journal is copied across regions (REG-7K-16), and the Billing check stays off (SEC-7K-19). They are not closed handoffs either.

**Change in the third remediation.** No count changed: 92 design resolved, 0 blocked, 47 implementation deferred, 76 measurement required, 8 controlled reconciliations, 3 rows naming OD-7K-05. Four rows changed in content, not in status: row 34 now cites RED-7K-34 and -35; rows 35, 53 and 71 now cite MRA-7K-14 and -15. Row 34 does not gain +CR: the admission of a replacement node is enforced by frozen 7E alone, and the wording CNF-7K-11 asks of 7E blocks no activation (§31.1).

**Change in the fourth remediation.** No count changed: 92 design resolved, 0 blocked, 47 implementation deferred, 76 measurement required, 8 controlled reconciliations. Row 34 now cites RED-7K-36 … -38 and does not gain +CR: the enforced part of the admission uses the frozen flag as it stands, and the part that no frozen mechanism enforces is limited by a validation gate (AG-7K-02), not by a consolidation (CNF-7K-11). Rows 27 and 71 now cite REG-7K-26.

**Change in the fifth remediation.** No count changed, recounted from the tables: 92 design resolved, 0 blocked, 47 implementation deferred, 76 measurement required, 8 controlled reconciliations, 3 rows citing OD-7K-05. Row 34 now cites RED-7K-39; rows 35, 53 and 71 now cite MRA-7K-16. ## 4. Non-Negotiable Invariants

7K adds no invariant that weakens one below, and it lists them so that every later section can be checked against them. Each is frozen; the right-hand column names where 7K relies on it.

| ID | Invariant | Frozen source | Relied on in |
|---|---|---|---|
| INV-7K-01 | The platform is a modular monolith. A runtime role is an execution role, never a microservice or a bounded context. 7K introduces no new service, broker, database product or infrastructure dependency | 7A §6.6; 7D TOP-01; 7I JRN-01 | §5, §14 |
| INV-7K-02 | PostgreSQL is authoritative. Redis, Celery and WebSocket state never are. Recovery reads PostgreSQL | 7A PR-01, RS-02, SOT-01 … SOT-04 | §6, §7, §21 |
| INV-7K-03 | A committed outbox row is never lost; Redis availability never gates a commit; a transport failure never rolls back a valid business transaction | 7A DUR-03, DUR-05; 7D PTX-04 | §8, §13 |
| INV-7K-04 | Durable delivery is at-least-once. No component claims exactly-once | 7A DEL-04 | §6 |
| INV-7K-05 | Durable acceptance is `XADD` followed, on the same connection, by `WAITAOF 1 1` with a positive bounded timeout, with every capability condition true. `WAIT` is never a substitute. An uncertain write is never `CONFIRMED` | 7E ACC-01, ACC-02, WAO-01 … WAO-08, CAP-01 … CAP-09 | §9 |
| INV-7K-06 | Dedicated event-transport Redis Cluster per active region; Redis Open Source 7.2 or later; at least three primaries; exactly one replica per primary; `noeviction` on every node; AOF with `appendfsync everysec`; `min-replicas-to-write` ≥ 1; the sole replica automatic-failover eligible; FMD-09 membership | OD-7E-01, OD-7E-02; 7E TOPO-01 … TOPO-12 | §9 |
| INV-7K-07 | Partitioning is by `aggregate_id`, never by organization. `P_f = 1` per durable family in `g1`. `P_f` never changes in place | OD-7E-03; 7E PRT-03, LCY-05 | §9.7 |
| INV-7K-08 | No acknowledgement without durable evidence: owner commit, then evidence check, then accounting commit, then `XACK` | 7F XAK-05; 7G PAT-01; 7I GWY-08, GWY-10 | §10 |
| INV-7K-09 | Idempotency and business-effect guards are domain-owned. The recovery ledger never decides whether an effect happened | 7F §13, CPM-02; 7G RDM-02 | §10, §11 |
| INV-7K-10 | Retry budgets are bounded and are not spent by infrastructure failures. A valid obligation is never discarded by event operations | OD-7G-01; 7D DSP-03; 7G DAU-02 | §11, §13 |
| INV-7K-11 | Mandatory post-commit work is recovered from durable state, never from a one-shot enqueue. Mandatory audit commits with its business transaction | 7A CEL-08; OD-7D-02 | §12 |
| INV-7K-12 | EV-079 is written only by the shared finalization operation under its guard; its occurrence time is the persisted service-end time; recovery never writes `usage_events` and never synthesizes usage | OD-7B-02; 7B OCC-01 … OCC-07 | §12 |
| INV-7K-13 | No audio or media travels through any bus. Nothing on the conversational hot path waits on publication, a consumer, a monitoring export, a recovery workflow or an infrastructure probe | 7A VOX-02, VOX-03; 7E VHP-01; 7J ISO-02 | §5.6, §22 |
| INV-7K-14 | Tenant context is transaction-local and comes from trusted state. One transaction serves one organization | 7A TEN-02; 7I TCX-05, TCX-06 | §13, §14 |
| INV-7K-15 | Region binding is authoritative and is never inferred from a client-controlled field. An `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization's data stays in its contracted region. There is no residency downgrade and no silent cross-region routing. The profile is set only by governed provisioning | 7A RES-02, RES-03; OD-7I-22, OD-7I-25; 7I CRF-01 … CRF-15 | §20, §21 |
| INV-7K-16 | No cross-region replication of event streams. No global event bus | 7A RES-03; 7E TOPO-08, RSD-02 | §20 |
| INV-7K-17 | Every irreversible erasure is journaled before it commits. A database recovered to an earlier point is quarantined until the journal is reconciled. Erased data is never served, sent or shown again | OD-7I-13, OD-7I-16; 7I JRN-07, JRN-12, JRN-16, INV-18, INV-23 | §21 |
| INV-7K-18 | Legal hold, preservation, sealing and the content access guard are never overridden by a cleanup schedule or by capacity pressure | OD-7I-24 … OD-7I-31 | §19 |
| INV-7K-19 | Each retention is separate. A Redis trim is never legal or business retention | 7A §32.3; 7I §14.1 | §19 |
| INV-7K-20 | Monitoring, audit logs and telemetry are never authoritative business state and never a control | 7J PRN-01, SECM-01; 7A AUD-05 | §23 |
| INV-7K-21 | No numeric event-pipeline objective is binding until measured and approved by the owner. An unmeasured hour prevents a verdict | OD-7J-01, OD-7J-11 | §24 |
| INV-7K-22 | Prices, usage, quotas, financial reconciliation and security policy are server-authoritative | 7A BIL-01, BIL-02; 7H COM-02 | §12, §21.8 |

## 5. Deployment and Workload Topology

### 5.1 The regional stack

A **regional stack** is the complete set of stores and workloads of one active region. Nothing in it is shared with another region.

```text
 REGIONAL STACK (one per active region)
 +------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | PostgreSQL primary + in-region standby    PgBouncer (transaction mode, 3F §15.1)         |
 |   owner tables | outbox | recovery ledger | delivery rows | accounting | subject index   |
 |                                                                                          |
 | EVENT-TRANSPORT Redis Cluster (ET)        HOT-TIER Redis Cluster (HT, 3F §16)            |
 |   3 primaries + 1 replica each            sessions, caches, locks, presence,             |
 |   12 stream keys only (7E NS-08)          Celery broker; no stream key                   |
 |                                                                                          |
 | Object storage (in region): recordings, documents, exports, daily and weekly database    |
 |   snapshots, erasure-journal prefix (own credentials and key), telemetry evidence prefix |
 | Key service and secret manager (regional root keys, 7I KEY-05)                           |
 | Telemetry stack (7J RSD-7J-02)            Controlled egress path (7H §14.4)              |
 +------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
   Stack class E: no copy of anything leaves the region (OD-7K-01).
   Stack class S: an authorized disaster-recovery copy of PostgreSQL, objects, journal and keys
                  may exist in one other region (OD-7K-02). Streams are never copied.
```

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| TOP-7K-01 [7K] | **Two Redis clusters, two failure analyses.** ET carries durable and SIGNAL streams and nothing else (7E DEP-02). HT carries the Celery broker and session state. An ET outage delays event publication and consumption; it does not affect a Celery enqueue. An HT outage loses post-commit enqueues and session heartbeats; it does not affect event durability. Every failure row in this document names which cluster it concerns. |
| TOP-7K-02 [OD-7K-01, OD-7K-02] | **Stack class.** A stack is class **E** if it hosts, or is provisioned to host, any `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization; otherwise it is class **S**. The class is a property of the deployment-conformance record that 7I CRF-06 reads, is fixed when the stack is provisioned, and is never changed for a stack that has hosted an `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization. A `STANDARD` organization hosted in a class E stack receives class E treatment. |
| TOP-7K-03 [FROZEN] | Every workload carries the region tag of its database and of its transport and refuses to start if they differ (7D RES-7D-02; 7I RSY-02). |
| TOP-7K-04 [7K] | No workload of one stack holds a credential, an endpoint or a network path to a store of another stack, with one exception: the replication channels of TOP-7K-05. |
| TOP-7K-05 [OD-7K-02] | For a class S stack whose residency policy and contracts authorize it, the only cross-region channels are provider-level replication of PostgreSQL, of object storage and of key material to one designated recovery region (§20.4). No application workload uses them. **The erasure-journal prefix and its key are replicated only under the conditions of §20.5, which are not met today** (REG-7K-16). |

### 5.2 Fault domains

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| TOP-7K-06 [FROZEN + 7K] | **ET placement.** The six ET nodes are spread over three availability zones, two nodes per zone, such that the primary and the replica of one shard are never in the same zone or on the same host (7E TOPO-06). With zones Z1, Z2, Z3 the initial placement is: shard A primary Z1, replica Z2; shard B primary Z2, replica Z3; shard C primary Z3, replica Z1. Roles move at failover; the zone of a node does not. |
| TOP-7K-07 [7K] | **Consequence, stated.** The loss of one zone removes the primary of one shard and the replica of another. Two of three shards are then unable to confirm (7E TF-11, TF-22). Because the relay's gate is per loop and the probe covers every target shard (7E HPR-02), **all durable publication in the region pauses** until both shards are capable again, and group reads and acknowledgements pause on the shards whose primary has no replica inside the lag bound (RED-7K-29). Nothing is lost: the outbox holds every row (INV-7K-03) and every entry and pending entry stays in its stream. This is the availability cost of OD-7E-02 = A; §9.3 bounds its duration and §16.1 sizes the outbox for it. |
| TOP-7K-08 [7K] | ET persistent volumes are zonal block storage, one per node, never shared, never network-replicated across zones as a substitute for the Redis replica. |
| TOP-7K-09 [7K] | The stream gateway (7I GWY-15) runs at least two instances in two zones. The relay runs at least two replicas in two zones. Every other event-plane role tolerates the loss of one zone with at least one running instance, except the crash-loop isolation activity and the restore reconciler, which are deliberately serial. |
| TOP-7K-10 [7K] | The in-region PostgreSQL standby is in a different zone from the primary. The object-store prefixes that hold database snapshots and the erasure journal are in a failure domain distinct from the database's own storage (7I JRN-19). |

### 5.3 Workload roles

All are execution roles of the one deployable image (INV-7K-01). §14 gives each one's operational contract.

| Role | Purpose | Defined by |
|---|---|---|
| `api` | HTTP requests; producing transactions; callback ingress | 6A; 7D §10; 7H §11 |
| `voice_gateway` | Raw-WebSocket media and the turn loop; SIGNAL publisher | 3B; 7D §32 |
| `relay` | Outbox to ET | 7D §13 |
| `stream_gateway` | Every group command on a stream key | 7I §9.5 |
| `consumer.<context>` (nine) | The 7F pipeline for the groups of one context, including its sweep | 7F; 7G §12; 7I CTX-01 |
| `isolation` | One crash-loop suspect at a time | 7G §17 |
| `accounting_reconciler` | Unaccounted-obligation detection | 7I §9.6 |
| `event_ops_tooling` | Replay executors, repair publisher, governed operations, material view | 7G §30 – §42; 7I §13 |
| `trim` | Safe-trim watermark | 7E §34 |
| `dispatcher`, `delivery_sweeper` | Webhook attempts and stale claims | 7H §8 |
| `callback_processor` | Stored callback inputs | 7H §11 |
| `scheduler` | Timer ticks (APScheduler) | 7A CEL-07; §14.3 |
| `worker.<context>` | Celery commands and owner reconcilers | 7D §33 – §35 |
| `voice_recovery` | Stale-session reaper, recovery finalizer, dispatch and stale-command reconcilers | 3B §18.2; 7B; 7D §35.4, §35.5; §12 |
| `maintenance` | Retention, sealing, erasure, purge, outbox cleanup tooling | 7I §14 |
| `journal_writer`, `restore_reconciler` | Erasure journal; quarantine exit | 7I §14.8 |
| `observer` | Telemetry reads and accounting folds | 7J §5, §6.4 |
| `provisioning` | Groups, registrations, handler-contract records | 7E §30; 7F IO-7F-31; 7I ACC-15 |

### 5.4 Database principals

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| TOP-7K-11 [FROZEN] | Each role connects under the principal its owning document fixes: `app_api`; `app_worker` for the relay (7D TOP-07) and for background work that is not a durable consumer (7I CTX-05); one principal per consuming context (7I CTX-01); `app_voice_reconciler` and `app_billing_reconciler` (executed migrations); the gateway, observer, maintenance, tooling, journal-writer and restore-reconciler principals that 7I and 7J require and that do not exist yet. 7K creates no role and widens no grant. |
| TOP-7K-12 [7K] | Each principal has its own PgBouncer pool with its own server-connection limit (§17.1). A pool is never shared between two roles, so backlog, replay or cleanup in one role cannot consume another role's connections. |

### 5.5 Dependencies of each plane

| Plane | Needs | Does not need |
|---|---|---|
| Producing transaction | PostgreSQL | ET, HT, gateway, key service, telemetry |
| Publication | PostgreSQL, ET | Gateway, consumers, HT, telemetry |
| Consumption | PostgreSQL, ET, gateway, recovery ledger, handler-contract record; key service only when parking | Relay, HT, telemetry |
| Webhook delivery | PostgreSQL, secret manager, egress path | ET, gateway |
| Callback ingress | PostgreSQL, key service | ET, HT |
| Live call media | Provider chain, in-process pipeline, HT for session state | PostgreSQL per frame, ET, gateway, telemetry |
| Erasure | PostgreSQL, journal store, key service | ET |

### 5.6 Isolation of the voice path

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| TOP-7K-13 [FROZEN + 7K] | No relay, gateway, consumer, reconciler, reaper, replay executor, maintenance pass, fold or probe runs in a `voice_gateway` process or on its nodes' reserved capacity (7D TOP-06; 7E TRM-11). |
| TOP-7K-14 [7K] | `voice_gateway` and `api` have reserved database server connections (§17.1) and reserved node capacity that no event-plane autoscaling may take (§18). |
| TOP-7K-15 [7K] | A recovery or catch-up mode is entered and left without any call to, lock on, or configuration push to a `voice_gateway` process. |

---

## 6. Failure Classification and Recovery Principles

### 6.1 Classes

| Class | Definition | Outcome that is permitted | Outcome that is never permitted |
|---|---|---|---|
| FC-A Availability | A dependency cannot serve. Durable state is intact | Delay; accumulation in PostgreSQL or in a PEL | Loss; a spent retry budget; a terminal state |
| FC-U Uncertain | An operation was sent and its result is not known | Repetition with the same identity; duplicate | Treating it as confirmed; treating it as not done where an external effect may exist |
| FC-C Crash | A process ended with work in flight | Recovery by lease, claim expiry or idle reclaim | Dependence on the dead process's memory |
| FC-P Poison | One item fails every time | Bounded attempts, then parking with exact material | Blocking other items; silent drop |
| FC-I Integrity | An invariant of a store was violated (missing entry, forged row, contradicting evidence) | Fail closed; a visible case; owner reconciliation | Inventing the missing fact |
| FC-D Disaster | Durable state itself was lost or rewound | Restoration under §21; explicit, declared loss | Declaring a recovery without authoritative evidence |
| FC-S Security | A control refused or a credential is suspect | Containment under 7I | Degrading a control to restore throughput |

### 6.2 Principles

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| FCL-7K-01 | **PostgreSQL decides.** Every recovery decision reads PostgreSQL. A Redis position, a PEL, a Celery task, a metric or a log line is never the reason an obligation is created, completed or dropped (INV-7K-02, INV-7K-20). |
| FCL-7K-02 | **Evidence before acknowledgement, at every boundary.** The order of INV-7K-08 is never shortened to recover faster. |
| FCL-7K-03 | **Doubt about transport resolves toward repetition.** Doubt about an external effect that cannot be repeated safely (a provider call, a payment, a refund, an HTTP delivery already sent) resolves toward **hold and evidence**, never toward a blind repeat and never toward "it did not happen" (7D VDK-07, VSC-02; OD-7K-03 condition 4). |
| FCL-7K-04 | **Unknown stays unknown.** Where evidence is missing, the frozen vocabulary is used and nothing is rounded up: `UNKNOWN` (7D), `HELD` and its classes (7F, 7G), `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` (7G), `UNCERTAIN` send state (7H), `uncertain` eligibility and `UNMEASURED` hours (7J), quarantine (7I). |
| FCL-7K-05 | **Recovery is found by state, not by memory.** A recovery worker finds its work by a predicate over durable state. It keeps no cursor whose loss could hide work. Every action is a compare-and-set from an expected state, so a repeat, a concurrent twin and a restart converge. |
| FCL-7K-06 | **Infrastructure failures spend nothing.** No recovery path added by 7K calls `fn_mark_outbox_failed` toward `FAILED`, increments `handler_failure_count`, spends a delivery attempt or consumes a replay item because a dependency was down. The frozen rules that already guarantee this (7D DSP-03; 7G GTE-05; 7H XRT-09) are not narrowed. |
| FCL-7K-07 | **Recovery never runs on the media path** (INV-7K-13). |
| FCL-7K-08 | **A wait is bounded by a deadline or a recorded decision.** Where a rule below says a worker waits, the row names the bound. Where no bound exists, the document says so and names the residual (§31.2). An alert or an owner's name is never a bound. |

### 6.3 Recovery precedence

Two recovery actors can reach the same object. The rules below make their outcome single-valued.

**Layers.** A lower layer never acts on an object while a higher layer holds it, and leaves that object's state untouched.

| Layer | Holder | Established by | Effect on lower layers |
|---|---|---|---|
| L0 Quarantine | The database incarnation is not activated | 7I JRN-12 | Every guarded function of the event pipeline refuses. Nothing below runs |
| L1 Preservation and fences | Legal hold; sealing; content deadline; organization not `ACTIVE`; publication fence; a group `CLOSED` for a cutover or onboarding; the money gate of §21.8 | 7I HLD-11, CAG-01; 7H ADM; 7F HCG-21, CUT-4; OD-7K-03 | The named operation is refused or held exactly as its frozen rule states |
| L2 Governed operation | An approved replay, redrive, retirement, cutover, generation migration or restore step that has bound the object | 7G T-10, GR-2; 7F TKO-08; §9.7; §21 | Automatic actors skip the bound object |
| L3 Dependency gate | Transport gate (7D §25); dependency gate (7G §16); pre-claim gate (7H XRT-09); pacing zone (BPR-7K-03) | In-memory, per worker | No claim, read or sweep is issued; durable state does not change |
| L4 Automatic recovery | Lease expiry; stale-entry sweep; delivery sweeper; reconcilers; the reaper | This document and its sources | Acts only by compare-and-set |
| L5 Normal processing | — | — | — |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RPR-7K-01 | **One arbiter per object.** For each object the arbiter is the row lock and compare-and-set named in §7. No actor decides an outcome from anything else. |
| RPR-7K-02 | **Terminal evidence wins.** If a committed owner effect, completion record, terminal delivery status or terminal call status exists, every recovery actor converges on it. No actor undoes a committed effect to make its own attempt consistent. |
| RPR-7K-03 | **The restrictive state wins over the permissive one.** Where two transitions are both enabled, the one that stops automatic execution takes precedence: a case in `PARKED`, `REPLAYING` or `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` blocks the handler (7G RTY-02); a dispatch key in `SUBMITTING` or `AMBIGUOUS` blocks redispatch (7D VDK-07); a `HELD` delivery is not claimed (7H). |
| RPR-7K-04 | **Automatic actors are peers.** Between two L4 or L5 actors there is no priority. The arbiter of RPR-7K-01 picks one; the other observes a compare-and-set miss and stops without a side effect. |
| RPR-7K-05 | **A detector never writes an outcome.** The accounting reconciler, the delivery sweeper's stale check, the reaper's scan and every observer may open work (a case, a release, a candidate). None writes a handler result, a completion, an acknowledgement or a resolution (7I ACC-05). |
| RPR-7K-06 | **Order between dependent objects is fixed.** Call before conversation (§12.3). Outbox drain before stream boundary (7F HCG-20). Journal reconciliation before activation, activation before any claim (7I JRN-13). Money gate before any automated financial run (§21.8). Old-generation fence before old-generation drain proof (§9.7). |
| RPR-7K-07 | **Clocks are not mixed.** A lease, a deadline, a retention limit and a staleness threshold over PostgreSQL rows are judged on the database clock. A PEL idle time is Redis's own. A local deadline is a monotonic local timer started before the action it bounds. No rule compares a value of one clock with a value of another, except where a frozen rule proves an order between them (7J AOA-27). |

### 6.4 The five recovery modes

They are separate procedures with separate entry and exit conditions.

| Mode | Entered when | What runs | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| Startup recovery | A workload instance starts | Region-tag check; incarnation check; capability gate (relay); handler-contract admission (consumers); registry and manifest checks. No scan specific to "the previous instance" exists, because no instance owns durable state | The instance is admitted and begins normal work. Work the dead instance held returns through continuous reconciliation |
| Continuous reconciliation | Always | Lease expiry and reclaim; the stale-entry sweep; the delivery sweeper; the callback sweep; the accounting reconciler; owner reconcilers | Per object: its terminal state, or a case |
| Scheduled sweeping | On a timer tick (§14.3) | Retention, sealing, erasure batches; trim; cleanup; completeness folds; consumer-name maintenance | Per pass: the predicate returns no row, or the pass is bounded and resumes at the next tick |
| Manual incident intervention | An operator opens an approved operation | Replay modes R2 – R5; governed repair; replica replacement; restore | The operation is terminal and every item has a definite result (7G PLN-06) |
| Recovery completion | An incident is closed | The completion criteria of §25.3 | All of them hold, each proved from authoritative state |

---

## 7. Authoritative Recovery-State Matrix

For each boundary: the record that is the truth, what proves completion, who recovers, what fences a stale actor, and what an uncertain outcome becomes.

| # | Boundary | Authoritative record | Proof of completion | Recovery actor | Fence against a stale actor | Uncertain outcome | Escalation |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Business transaction | Owner tables | `COMMIT` returned | None: an uncommitted transaction left nothing | — | Client retries under the HTTP idempotency contract (6A §16) | — |
| 2 | Transactional outbox | `audit.domain_event_outbox` row | The row exists (same commit) | Relay | `trg_outbox_tenant_check`; producer idempotency (7D §11) | Not applicable: atomic with 1 | — |
| 3 | Relay claim and lease | Row `status`, `claimed_by`, `claimed_at` | `CLAIMED` under this identity | Any relay loop after lease expiry | Compare-and-set on `claimed_by`; local deadline (7D LSE-03) | Claim transaction failed: assume nothing claimed (7D CLM-07) | Expired-claim reclaim count |
| 4 | Redis availability and durable acknowledgement | The outbox row; never the stream | `WAITAOF` returned `[≥1, ≥1]` on the `XADD` connection with capability established | Relay, by republication | Capability gate; transport gate | `UNKNOWN`: deferral, republication, same `event_id` | Gate-open duration; oldest eligible age |
| 5 | Mark published | Row `status = PUBLISHED`; position record (7I ACC-13) | Compare-and-set returned true | Lease expiry and republication | Compare-and-set | Mark reply lost: retry is a no-op | Compare-and-set miss count |
| 6 | Consumer-group dispatch | PEL (transport state only) | Entry delivered to a member | Stale-entry sweep through the gateway | Gateway binding registry; handler-contract admission | Read reply lost: entry pending, reclaimed (7F RDL-10) | Oldest pending age |
| 7 | Stream gateway | Gateway holds no durable state | — | Any gateway instance | Delivery token; member ownership of the PEL entry (7I GWY-11) | Gateway crash: entry pending | Gateway availability |
| 8 | Business-effect commit | Owner guard and effect rows; completion record | Owner `COMMIT` returned | Redelivery; the guard decides | Unique constraint, state compare-and-set or row lock (7F §19) | Commit outcome unknown: `HANDLER_FAILED`, dependency-wide, not counted (7G FC-10) | Unknown-commit count |
| 9 | Completion accounting | Accounting row (7I ACC-01) | Insert committed | Gateway on redelivery; accounting reconciler | Primary key (group, `event_id`) | Insert outcome unknown: no `XACK`; redelivery repeats the no-op insert | Unaccounted pairs; `T_obl` |
| 10 | Acknowledgement | PEL | `XACK` returned ≥ 1 | Redelivery takes the duplicate path | — | Reply lost: re-issue; else leave pending (7F XAK-09) | `ACK_UNCONFIRMED` |
| 11 | Reclaim and retry | Recovery case `handler_failure_count` | Failure record committed | Sweep | Case row lock (7G RTY-07) | Record outcome unknown: not repeated in that attempt (7G RTY-06) | Open-retry age |
| 12 | Parking and recovery ledger | Recovery case and material | Parking transaction committed, then `XACK` | Redelivery finds the case (7G PAT-04) | Case identity | Parking commit unknown: no `XACK` | Parked-case count and age |
| 13 | Authorized replay | Operation and item records | Item result recorded; case transition | The operation's own recovery (7G TGT-09) | Compare-and-set `PARKED → REPLAYING` | Executor died after the effect: re-run proves `ALREADY_COMMITTED` | `REPLAYING` age |
| 14 | Webhook delivery | `webhooks.webhook_deliveries` row; claim, admission and closure records | Closure record committed | Delivery sweeper after `T_claim_lease` | `claim_seq` fence (7H DSM-01) | Sent, outcome unrecorded: `UNCERTAIN`; one attempt spent; receiver deduplicates | Stale-claim age |
| 15 | Provider callback | Receipt and processing input | Receipt terminal | Callback sweep | Processing compare-and-set; owner guards | Acknowledged and unprocessed: owed, swept | Stale receipt age |
| 16 | Provider command (telephony) | `voice.call_sessions`; `voice.call_dispatch_keys` | Terminal transition on provider evidence | Voice reconcilers | Dispatch state machine; state compare-and-set | `SUBMITTING` / `AMBIGUOUS`: held for evidence, never redialled | Held-for-evidence age |
| 17 | Conversation accounting | Finalization guard; EV-079 outbox row | Finalization transaction committed | Recovery finalizer | The guard | Corrupt or missing state: reconciliation, nothing billed | Unfinalized age |
| 18 | Privacy erasure | Erasure-step state; the journal | `VERIFIED` | Step resumes from its journal state | `INTENT_DURABLE` precondition (7I JRN-06) | Journal unreachable: wait at `INTENT_REQUESTED` | Deadline approaching; deadline missed |
| 19 | Audit | `audit.audit_events` | Same commit as the mutation | None needed (OD-7D-02) | — | — | Audit write failure rate |
| 20 | Database restore | The journal; the activation record | Activation record issued | Restore reconciler | Incarnation fence (7I JRN-12) | Any contradiction: quarantine persists | Quarantine state |

## 8. Outbox, Relay and PostgreSQL Recovery

### 8.1 Relay values (closes 7D HO-7K-05; DD-09 tuning)

Symbols are defined in the parameter registry (§15.5). The frozen values are unchanged.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBX-7K-01 [FROZEN + 7K] | Claim batch `B_claim` = 50 and lease `L_claim` = 300 s stay the V1 values (7D POL-01, POL-02). 7K proposes no change. A change is admissible only with the evidence of BM-7K-01 and under 7D LSE-05. |
| OBX-7K-02 [7K] | **Lease safety inequality.** `T_claim_tx + T_publish + T_mark + T_pause ≤ L_claim − σ`, where `T_claim_tx` and `T_mark` are the statement timeouts of the claim and progress transactions, `T_publish` is the publish timeout the relay hands the adapter, `T_pause` is the longest process pause the deployment tolerates before the relay is restarted, and `σ` is the safety margin. Configuration that violates it is rejected at relay start. With the candidates of §15.5 the left side is 5 + 10 + 5 + 30 = 50 s against 300 − 30 = 270 s. |
| OBX-7K-03 [7K] | **What `σ` protects.** A pause between the relay's deadline check and its Redis write can let a write happen after the lease expired. The result is a duplicate, never a loss (7D LSE-08). `σ` therefore trades duplicate volume against nothing else, and its value is a duplicate-rate tuning (BM-7K-02). |
| OBX-7K-04 [7K] | **Idle and probe cadence.** Idle delay: starts at `d_idle_0`, doubles per empty claim to `d_idle_max`, resets on a non-empty claim, with random jitter of ±`j` (7D POL-06). Probe delay while the gate is open: starts at `d_probe_0`, doubles to `d_probe_max`, with jitter. **The probe backoff level is not reset when the gate closes; it is reset only after a batch completes with no connection-level outcome.** A gate that reopens within `T_flap` of closing therefore probes at its previous delay, which bounds the duplicate entries a flapping transport can cause to one claim batch per loop per `d_probe_max` (7D GATE-06). This is a backoff policy inside 7D GATE-04 and changes no gate transition. |

### 8.2 PostgreSQL unavailable, Redis available

| Component | Behaviour | Authoritative state proving it | Bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| Producers | Cannot commit. No fact exists, so no event is owed | Absence of the row | Service availability of PostgreSQL |
| Relay | Claim fails; database backoff; the transport gate is not involved (7D CLM-07, F7D-01). A batch already published and not marked stays `CLAIMED` | Row `CLAIMED` | Republished after `L_claim`: at most `B_claim` duplicates per loop |
| Consumers | Handler fails with a connection or shutdown class; `TRANSIENT_DEPENDENCY`; the dependency gate opens; no failure is counted (7G FC-10, GTE-05) | No case change | Resumes on a healthy probe |
| Gateway | Evidence and accounting functions unreachable: acknowledgement refused; entries stay pending | PEL | Same |
| Dispatcher | Cannot claim. An attempt in flight cannot record its outcome; the sweeper later closes the claim as `UNCERTAIN` and one attempt is spent (7H OD-7H-05) | Delivery row | A duplicate HTTP request is possible; the receiver deduplicates on `event_id` |
| Callback ingress | Receipt cannot commit; the route answers a server error; nothing was acknowledged; the provider retries (7H CBK-04) | Absence of a receipt | Provider's own retry policy |
| Live calls | Continue in process. A turn checkpoint that cannot commit is not counted: only committed turns are usage (7B §31.6). New call admission fails | `voice.call_sessions`; conversation totals | Under-billing only, never over-billing |
| Erasure | Steps wait | Step state | Deadlines remain obligations (7I JRN-29) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBX-7K-05 [7K] | A restart of the same database instance does not change its incarnation and does not quarantine. A promotion, a restore or a point-in-time recovery does (7I JRN-12) and enters §21. The relay, the consumers and the dispatcher never decide which occurred; the incarnation fence does. |

### 8.3 Redis (ET) unavailable, PostgreSQL available (7A FM-01)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBX-7K-06 [FROZEN] | Business transactions commit. The first batch that meets a connection-level outcome opens the gate; no claim is made while it is open; rows stay `PENDING`; `attempt_count` does not grow; no row can reach `FAILED` (7D GATE-01 … GATE-08, DSP-03; 7E CLS-01). |
| OBX-7K-07 [7K] | **Accumulation.** While the gate is open the outbox grows by the commit rate of durable events. The storage it needs is `S_out(T) = λ_e × T × (s̄_row + s_idx)` (§15.3). The stack is sized so that `S_out(T_ET_out)` fits in the reserved database headroom for the design outage `T_ET_out` (§17.2). |
| OBX-7K-08 [7K] | **A row whose `attempt_count` reached `max_attempts` is not released by a deferral** (7D DSP-03); it waits for lease expiry. After repeated outages such rows publish one lease later than others. They are counted (7J OBP), never failed, and need no action. |
| OBX-7K-09 [FROZEN] | SIGNAL entries published during the outage are dropped and counted (7D CLD-06). Consumers idle; entries already in streams are intact when ET returns with storage intact (7E TF-14). |

### 8.4 Both unavailable

Nothing commits and nothing is published. Recovery order: PostgreSQL first, because it is the authority; then the incarnation check (OBX-7K-05); then ET capability (7E CAP-08); then the gates close on their own probes. HT loss in the same event is handled by §12.6: a Celery task that was never accepted is recovered by its owner's state scan, not by the broker.

### 8.5 Relay crash points

| ID | Case | Result |
|---|---|---|
| OBX-7K-10 [FROZEN] | Crash before publication (7D CRS-01 … CRS-03; 7A FM-02) | Row `PENDING` or `CLAIMED`; reclaimed after `L_claim`; no duplicate |
| OBX-7K-11 [FROZEN] | Crash after a confirmed publication, before the mark (7D CRS-06; 7E TF-21; 7A FM-03) | Row `CLAIMED`; republished with the same `event_id`; the first entry has no position record, the second has one, and accounting is by `event_id` (7I ACC-18 a) |
| OBX-7K-12 [FROZEN] | `WAITAOF` shortfall, timeout or lost reply (7E CL-14 … CL-17) | `UNKNOWN`; deferral by the database's 30 s; gate opens; never `PUBLISHED` on that attempt |

**Bound on duplicates from relay recovery.** Per gate opening or crash, per loop: at most `B_claim` entries. With `n_loop` loops: `B_claim × n_loop` per event. Flapping is bounded by OBX-7K-04.

### 8.6 Catch-up after an extended outage (closes 7D HO-7K-02)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBX-7K-13 [7K] | **Catch-up is ordinary publication.** No special mode changes a correctness rule. Rows are claimed in `available_at, id` order (7D GATE-08). |
| OBX-7K-14 [7K] | **Publication rate model.** One loop completes a cycle in `T_cycle = T_claim_tx + W + T_mark`, where `W` is the confirmation wait. Under `appendfsync everysec`, `W` is bounded below by the wait for the next background fsync on the primary and on the replica; 7E states "up to about a second" and gives no number. Loop throughput is `B_claim / T_cycle`; fleet throughput is `μ_pub = n_loop × B_claim / T_cycle` until the database claim path or a Redis primary saturates. `W` is [MEAS] (BM-7K-01). No throughput figure is asserted here. |
| OBX-7K-15 [7K] | **Catch-up time.** `T_cu = N_backlog / (μ_pub − λ_e)`, defined only for `μ_pub > λ_e`. The relay fleet is sized so that `μ_pub ≥ ρ_cu × λ_e,peak` with catch-up factor `ρ_cu` > 1 ([CAND] 2). |
| OBX-7K-16 [7K] | **Catch-up is paced by the transport's memory, not by the outbox's size** (BPR-7K-03). Publishing faster than consumers drain moves the backlog from PostgreSQL, where it is cheap and unbounded by `maxmemory`, into Redis, where it ends in `OOM` refusals. |
| OBX-7K-17 [7K] | **Exit.** Catch-up is over when the oldest eligible age (7D OBS-7D-02) has stayed below the steady-state threshold for `T_cu_exit`, and every durable group's oldest pending age is below its lag bound (BPR-7K-08). Until then the catch-up thresholds of 7J STM-04 apply. |

### 8.7 Outbox cleanup under recovery

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBX-7K-18 [FROZEN] | Cleanup deletes only by the two frozen predicates, narrowed by 7G ILK-01 … ILK-07 and 7I ACC-06. It is never run while the stack is quarantined, and never as a response to disk pressure. |
| OBX-7K-19 [7K] | Disk pressure on the database during an outage is answered by adding storage and by restoring the dependency. No row that is `PENDING`, `CLAIMED`, undispositioned `FAILED`, or `PUBLISHED` with an unaccounted group is ever deleted to free space (INV-7K-03). |

---

## 9. Redis Durability and Failover

### 9.1 Topology, restated as the decisions 7K makes

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-01 [FROZEN] | The event transport is the cluster of INV-7K-06. 7K changes no property of it. |
| RED-7K-02 [7K] | **Three primaries in V1.** The minimum of 7E DEP-03 is the V1 size. A fourth shard is added only for a new topology generation (§9.7), by moving empty slots (7E LCY-06). |
| RED-7K-03 [7K] | **No off-node backup of ET.** The ET cluster keeps AOF on its persistent volumes and nothing else: no scheduled RDB snapshot is copied to object storage, and periodic `BGSAVE` is disabled. Reasons: an ET backup is never a recovery source (7E ROLE-05); a copy would prolong the residue of erased content (7I RR-05); a periodic fork adds copy-on-write memory that §16 would have to reserve. This narrows 3F §11.2 for the ET cluster only and is recorded as CNF-7K-02. The HT cluster is unchanged. |
| RED-7K-04 [7K] | **Persistent volume.** One volume per node, sized by §16, with the node's AOF directory and nothing else. A node whose volume is replaced starts empty and joins only as a replica through RED-7K-17. |

### 9.2 Slot placement (closes 7E HE-7K-01, first part)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-05 [7K] | **Placement is decided before `g1` is provisioned and is then fixed.** After the first durable write, a durable key's slot is never moved (7E LCY-06). The remedy for a wrong placement is a new generation. |
| RED-7K-06 [CAND] | **Candidate placement.** The default even split puts `voice`, `campaign` and `compliance` on one shard and `crm`, `identity`, `organization`, `knowledge`, `integrations` and the SIGNAL key on another (7E §15.4, illustrative). 7E names Voice, CRM and Campaign as the high-volume families (7E §50.3). The candidate separates them: |

| Shard | Durable keys (slot) | Other |
|---|---|---|
| A | `voice` (1361), `compliance` (1024), `identity` (11812), `agent` (8402) | — |
| B | `campaign` (2317), `billing` (9920), `workflow` (10460), `knowledge` (14106) | — |
| C | `crm` (16093), `organization` (16343), `integrations` (13875) | SIGNAL `voice` (14922) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-07 [7K] | The candidate rests on one qualitative source statement and no measurement. It is confirmed or replaced by BM-7K-03 before provisioning. The remaining empty slots are distributed evenly. |
| RED-7K-08 [7K] | `P_f` stays 1 for every family in `g1` (INV-7K-07). The criteria for a later generation are GEN-7K-01. |

### 9.3 Shard states and what each one does

"Capable" is 7E `shard_capable`. The states below are an operational reading of it; they add no condition to it.

| State | Entered when | Durable publication | Consumption | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAPABLE` | 7E T-01 | Confirms | Normal | — |
| `NO_REPLICA` | Replica lost (TF-11), or failover completed and the old primary has not rejoined (TF-22) | Refused: `NOREPLICAS` or capability failure; non-terminal | **Paused on that shard** (RED-7K-29): group reads and acknowledgements are write commands and are refused with `NOREPLICAS`. Entries and pending entries are retained | A node that returns after a process start, or a governed replacement: flagged, and capable only through the admission of RED-7K-34. A node that returns in the same process: RED-7K-38 |
| `REPLICA_LAGGING` | Lag above `min-replicas-max-lag`, or `WAITAOF` shortfall | `UNKNOWN` or refused; non-terminal | Paused while the lag is above `min-replicas-max-lag` (RED-7K-29); continues on a `WAITAOF` shortfall alone | Lag returns inside the bound |
| `AOF_FAULT` | AOF last write status not OK on either node | Refused by capability (HP-06) | Reads continue while the node serves | Primary: storage repaired; AOF healthy; then the frozen check. Replica: RED-7K-24 |
| `MEMORY_LIMIT` | A primary at `maxmemory` | `OOM`: `TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE` | Continues; frees memory through acknowledgement and trim | Below the limit (HP-07) |
| `FAILING_OVER` | Primary marked failed; election in progress | Refused | Interrupted for about `cluster-node-timeout` plus election | Promotion; then `NO_REPLICA` |
| `PARTITIONED` | Primary cannot reach its replica or the majority | Refused after `min-replicas-max-lag`; nothing confirmed (TF-15) | Paused on the isolated primary after `min-replicas-max-lag` (RED-7K-29); on the majority side after failover the promoted node is `NO_REPLICA` | Partition heals; the old primary rejoins as replica (RED-7K-37, path 4b) |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | Both nodes down, storage intact (TF-12, TF-16) | Refused | None | Either node restarts and reloads its AOF. Both start flagged, and the replica is admitted again (RED-7K-37, path 5) |
| `LOST` | Every durable copy destroyed (TF-17, TF-19) | — | — | Outside the model: §11.5 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-09 [FROZEN] | In every state other than `CAPABLE`, no entry on that shard is `CONFIRMED`, and by 7E HPR-02 every relay loop's gate is open, so **no durable publication happens in the region**. |
| RED-7K-29 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Consumption is not independent of the replica** (corrected in the remediation; the first submission said consumption "continues from the primary"). `XREADGROUP`, `XACK`, `XAUTOCLAIM` and `XCLAIM` modify group state and are write commands (7E MF-10), and a primary configured with `min-replicas-to-write 1` refuses write commands with `NOREPLICAS` while no replica is connected inside the lag bound (7E MF-05, TOPO-04). On such a shard the gateway's reads and acknowledgements are therefore refused; consumers of its keys idle on the gateway's retryable refusal; no handler result is acknowledged and none is lost, because the owner commit, the completion record and the pending entry all remain (INV-7K-08). When the replica is back inside the bound, entries idle longer than `reclaim_min_idle` are reclaimed and take the duplicate path (CSM-7K-03). A refused acknowledgement is a dependency-wide condition and is never counted as a handler failure (7G FC-10). Stated from the Redis 7.2 documentation, not from an executed test; the exact reply of each consumer command in this state is recorded by T-7K-10. |
| RED-7K-10 [7K] | **Design target.** The region's publication pause after a single node or zone failure lasts `T_recap = T_detect + max over affected shards of T_restore_capability`. `T_recap` is [MEAS] (T-7K-10). The stack is sized for `T_recap,design` ([CAND] 30 minutes) of accumulation at peak (§16.1) and alerts when a pause exceeds `T_recap,alert` ([CAND] 10 minutes). |
| RED-7K-11 [7K] | **Not adopted, and recorded.** Two changes would shorten or remove the pause: a second replica per shard with `WAITAOF 1 <all replicas>`, which needs the 7E FMD-09 revalidation and an amendment of OD-7E-02; and a shard-aware claim, which needs a change to the executed claim function. Neither is made here. The pause is residual risk RR-7K-01. |

### 9.4 Failover values (closes 7E HE-7K-05)

| Parameter | Value | Tag | Constraint it must satisfy |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cluster-replica-validity-factor` | 10 | [FROZEN] 7E TOPO-12 | Non-zero. Not changed by 7K |
| `cluster-node-timeout` (`N_to`) | 15 000 ms | [CAND]; the Redis default | RED-7K-12 |
| `repl-ping-replica-period` (`P_ping`) | 10 s | [CAND]; the Redis default | RED-7K-12 |
| `min-replicas-to-write` | 1 | [FROZEN] | — |
| `min-replicas-max-lag` (`LAG_BOUND`) | 10 s | [CAND]; the Redis default | RED-7K-12, RED-7K-13 |
| `WAITAOF` timeout (`t_wao`) | 3 000 ms | [CAND] | RED-7K-14 |
| Publish timeout (`T_publish`) | 10 s | [CAND] | OBX-7K-02; RED-7K-14 |
| Capability evidence age (`T_cap`) | ≤ 5 s | [CAND] | RED-7K-15 |
| Probe delay | 1 s doubling to 30 s, jitter ±20% | [CAND] | OBX-7K-04 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-12 [7K] | **Validity window.** The automatic-failover validity window is `V = N_to × 10 + P_ping` (7E MF-23): 160 s with the candidates. The normal single-failure proof of 7E TOPO-12 needs the replica's data age at the primary's failure to be inside `V`. A capable shard's replica was connected within `LAG_BOUND` no longer than `T_cap` before the last confirmation, and is marked failed-over about `N_to` after the primary stops. Required: `LAG_BOUND + T_cap + N_to + t_elect < V`. With the candidates: 10 + 5 + 15 + `t_elect` < 160. Configuration that violates the inequality is rejected by the deployment-verification step (7E CAP-07). |
| RED-7K-13 [7K] | `LAG_BOUND` is long enough that a healthy replica under peak write load is not declared lagging (which would refuse writes), and short enough that an isolated primary stops accepting writes quickly (7E MF-05). Its value is confirmed by BM-7K-04 under peak append rate. |
| RED-7K-14 [7K] | `t_wao` is positive (7E WAO-03), is at least twice the fsync period so that a normal fsync cycle on both nodes fits, and satisfies `t_wao + margin ≤ T_publish`. A value below the first bound would turn normal operation into systematic `UNKNOWN` outcomes and gate flapping; a value above the second is refused by the adapter (`NOT_ATTEMPTED`). Final value: BM-7K-01. |
| RED-7K-15 [7K] | **Capability evidence age.** A publish call relies on capability evidence no older than `T_cap`. Evidence older than that is refreshed before the call or the call returns `TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE` (7E CL-21). A confirmation is still true whatever the evidence age, because it is a `WAITAOF` result; `T_cap` bounds only how long an ungoverned membership or policy change (7E TF-20) could go unseen. |
| RED-7K-16 [7K] | **TF-23.** A replica disconnected for longer than `V` is alerted at `V / 2`. From `V` on, the shard is treated as needing replacement (RED-7K-17) even if the primary is healthy, because a primary failure would then leave the shard without automatic failover. No entry is lost in this case (7E TF-23). Promotion of a stale replica by an operator is never part of the procedure. |

### 9.5 Replica restoration

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-17 [7K] | **One admission, entered by a fact Redis itself produces** (rewritten in the fourth remediation; FG-7K-R4-01). The earlier text said that in the rejoin path and in the replacement path alike "the shard is treated as not capable … until the durability admission … has succeeded", and TR-1 required a shard that is not capable. Nothing made that true for a returning node without the `nofailover` flag: the frozen check could report the shard capable first, so the precondition of TR-1 did not hold on every permitted path. The model is now this. (1) **Every event-transport node starts not failover-eligible** (RED-7K-36). A replica whose process has started is shown with `nofailover`; its shard is not capable by 7E CAP-04 (8) and HP-05 until the admission of RED-7K-34 has cleared the flag. That is the governed admission, and it is the only way a started node becomes failover-eligible. (2) A replica that returns **in the same process** carries no flag, and no frozen mechanism can withhold capability from it. 7K claims none. Which of those returns need no new admission, which are safe only on validated behaviour, and what the operation then does, is RED-7K-37 and RED-7K-38. (3) **Replacement** is used when a rejoin is not expected within `T_rejoin_wait` ([CAND] 10 minutes), when the node's volume is lost, when its AOF faulted (RED-7K-24), or when an admission keeps failing (RED-7K-31). It is a governed topology operation (7E FMD-09 b) with a recorded approval, run from runbook RB-7K-03 (RED-7K-18). The admission needs no publication, so it can always be reached from a shard that is not capable (third remediation; FG-7K-R3-01). 7E RC-02 states the conditions without which a rejoined shard is never capable; 7K removes none (CNF-7K-11). |
| RED-7K-18 [7K] | **Replacement order.** (1) Precondition, read from Redis: the shard's replica is absent, failed or flagged, so the frozen check reports the shard not capable (7E T-02, T-12) and nothing is being confirmed on it. (2) **Fence the old node:** the deployment platform confirms its process is terminated and its volume cannot be attached to a new node. (3) Remove it from the cluster on every remaining node (`CLUSTER FORGET`). Step 2 precedes step 3 because a forgotten node that is still reachable is re-learned when the forget period ends. (4) Start a new, empty node in a zone other than the primary's. **Like every node it starts with `cluster-replica-no-failover yes`** (RED-7K-36). Before it joins: its configuration is attested for every other item of 7E CAP-07, and its ACL set matches the cluster's digest (7I RDS-15). (5) Join it and make it the replica of the shard's primary. It carries the `nofailover` flag: the shard cannot be capable (7E CAP-04 (8)) and the node cannot be promoted automatically while its admission is incomplete (7E MF-22). (6) **Historical durability is verified** under RED-7K-19: the observations A1 … A7 hold and PO-1 is discharged. The operation records `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`. This step issues no command on a stream key and needs no publication and no `WAITAOF` reply. (7) Only after step 6, and only while its evidence is still bound (A7 is read again first): set `cluster-replica-no-failover no` in the **running** configuration of the primary, if it still carries its start value (RED-7K-36 (4)), and then of the replica; confirm that `CLUSTER NODES`, read on the primary, no longer shows `nofailover` on the replica; read A7 once more; then issue the CAP-07 attestation for the two running nodes and record the baseline of RED-7K-38. **The value `no` is never written to a start configuration,** so a node that restarts at any point of this step, or at any later time, starts flagged again. If a reading of A7 differs, the replica's running value is set back to `yes` before anything else and the admission starts again (RED-7K-33). (8) The frozen capability check runs, read-only (7E CAP-09, HP-01 … HP-08). When it holds, the operation records `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`; the probe turns healthy; gates close on their own. **No step waits for a confirmed publication:** the first confirmation after step 8 is observed afterwards and gates nothing (RED-7K-35). Changing a replica's failover eligibility is itself a governed topology operation (7E FMD-09 b). **Identity.** Steps 3 – 7 use the infrastructure identity that performs governed topology operations and the deployment verification of 7E CAP-07. Its use is logged and alerted (7I RDS-11). It issues no command on a stream key, and the procedure is a topology operation, not a 7G recovery operation. **The same steps 6 – 8 admit a returning node** that started flagged (RED-7K-37, paths 2b, 3b, 4a, 5, 7a); steps 2 – 5 are then not taken. |
| RED-7K-19 [7K] | **Durability admission of a restored replica** (rewritten in the remediation; FG-7K-05). **Invariant I-DUR** (scope corrected in the fifth remediation; FG-7K-R5-02): at every instant at which a shard is capable, and for every durable key of a live generation on it, the AOF on stable storage of *each* of its two nodes reproduces a dataset that contains every **required entry** of that key, as RED-7K-39 defines the required retained set. An entry removed by a lawful trim, as RED-7K-39 defines one, is outside the invariant, and its absence is not a loss. An entry that every *present* group acknowledged while a registered group is missing is inside it (sixth remediation; FG-7K-R6-01). The earlier text said "every entry confirmed on that shard", which no node can satisfy once the trim worker has run. 7E proves the invariant for a pair that was together when each entry was confirmed (7E ACC-01, TF-16). After a rejoin or a replacement it has to be re-established for the returning node, and the tables below say what establishes it. **Evidence is bound** to the primary's run identifier, the replica's run identifier and the replica's node identifier, all read first and last; if any of them changes, every observation and every proof below is void and the admission starts again. **The replica count stays one and `WAIT` is never used:** nothing here amends OD-7E-02, 7E FMD-09 or 7E WAO-07. **Recorded states** (third remediation, FG-7K-R3-01; fourth, FG-7K-R4-01): `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`, `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` and `POST_ADMISSION_CONFIRMATION_VERIFIED`, and, for a replica that returned without the flag, `UNFLAGGED_UNVERIFIED`. They are conceptual names for what the operation record of IO-7K-12 shows; they introduce no database object, no event and no stream write, **and a record withholds nothing: capability and promotion are withheld only by the `nofailover` flag** (RED-7K-34, RED-7K-36). The tables A and B below establish the first; RED-7K-34 gives the transitions; RED-7K-35 gives the third, which is an observation and never a precondition; RED-7K-38 gives the fourth. |

**A. Observations.** Each is necessary. **None, alone or together, is proof of durability:** they report what a node says about itself at one instant.

| # | Observation on the replica (unless stated) | Source | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Link to the primary up; no synchronization in progress; not loading | `INFO replication` (`master_link_status`, `master_sync_in_progress`); `INFO persistence` (`loading`) | Full synchronization finished; dataset load finished |
| A2 | Its replication offset has reached the primary's `master_repl_offset` read at the start of the check | `INFO replication` on both nodes | Replica replication offset |
| A3 | AOF is enabled, and `appendonly yes` with `appendfsync everysec` is attested for the node | `INFO persistence` (`aof_enabled`); CAP-07 attestation | AOF enablement |
| A4 | After a full synchronization: a rewrite is seen in progress after A1 and is then seen complete, or the rewrite counter rose after A1; a rewrite whose start after A1 was not established does not count. In every case: no rewrite in progress or scheduled, and the last one succeeded | `aof_rewrite_in_progress`, `aof_rewrite_scheduled`, `aof_last_bgrewrite_status`, `aof_rewrites` | AOF rewrite and incremental files |
| A5 | The last AOF write succeeded; no background fsync is pending; the delayed-fsync counter did not rise between two reads at least one fsync period apart | `aof_last_write_status`, `aof_pending_bio_fsync`, `aof_delayed_fsync` (a cumulative counter, not a state) | fsync completion; delayed or failed fsync |
| A6 | Which synchronization took place: the primary's full and partial synchronization counters read before the node attached and after A1 | `INFO stats` on the primary (`sync_full`, `sync_partial_ok`, `sync_partial_err`). For a node that returned by itself the "before" reading is the last baseline reading of RED-7K-38 taken under the primary's current run identifier; where none exists, the synchronization is treated as full | Selects PO-1 (a) or PO-1 (b) |
| A7 | The run identifiers are those read at the start | `INFO server` (`run_id`) on both nodes | Restart of either node during or after synchronization |

The field names are stated from the Redis 7.2 documentation, not from an executed test. A field that is absent, unreadable or has an unexpected form on the deployed build makes the admission **fail**; it is never skipped.

**B. Proof obligation of historical durability.** `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` is reached only when PO-1 is discharged, in form (a) or form (b). PO-1 concerns the required entries (RED-7K-39) that were confirmed **before** the node attached, and nothing else. It is a claim about the dataset a node's files reproduce, never about the whole history of confirmations. PO-2 is kept in the table under its old name so that earlier references resolve; since the third remediation it is **not** a proof obligation of the admission and **not** a precondition of capability.

| # | Obligation | What discharges it | What it rests on |
|---|---|---|---|
| PO-1 (a) | *Rejoin with a partial resynchronization, same volume.* The file this node reloaded reproduces a dataset that contains every required entry confirmed before the failure | The node is the same member with the same attested volume, and A6 shows no full synchronization for it. Every entry confirmed while it was a member was fsynced on this node before it was confirmed (7E ACC-01, WAO-02: it was the primary or the counted replica of that confirmation). Its file is a prefix of the command stream of the shard's primary of that time, its own where it was the primary: it lacks an entry only where it also holds the `XTRIM` that removed it. Where that trim was lawful (RED-7K-39 (e)) the entry is not required; where it was not, the removal is found by 7I ACC-16 and is not excused by this proof. A trim the primary applied after this node left is not yet in its file, which then holds more than is required and never less; the resynchronization applies it. Nothing rewrote its AOF since. **Not available to a node whose AOF faulted** (RED-7K-24) | Frozen 7E (ACC-01, WAO-02, TRM-01 … TRM-03, TRM-05, TRM-06); V-3 only if a rewrite ran since the node returned |
| PO-1 (b) | *Full synchronization* (every replacement; a rejoin that could not resynchronize partially). The replica's stable storage reproduces the primary's dataset as transferred, which contains every required entry confirmed before the failure | The primary holds every required entry: it fsynced each before it was confirmed, and an entry leaves it only by a lawful trim (7E ACC-01, TF-10, TRM-01 … TRM-03; RED-7K-39). Confirmation needs the replica's fsync, so no entry was confirmed between the loss of the old replica and this node's attachment. The snapshot the primary transferred is its dataset at that moment: it contains every required entry; it does not contain an entry that was trimmed earlier, and need not. **The proof never compares the snapshot with the history of confirmations or with the position records of PostgreSQL, and it reads no group, no registry and no watermark:** a registered group that is missing changes nothing in what is copied, and every entry retained for it is in the snapshot. After a full synchronization the replica's AOF holds that dataset only through the AOF rewrite it performs after loading. The discharge is: A1, then a rewrite that **started after** A1 has **completed successfully** (A4), with A5 and A7 holding afterwards | **V-1.** Without V-1 validated for the running build and storage class, PO-1 (b) cannot be discharged |
| PO-2 (withdrawn as an admission obligation) | *Durability of entries published after the admission.* An offset reported by `INFO` says what the replica received, not what it stored; PO-1 never relies on one (RED-7K-30) | **Nothing in the admission discharges it, and nothing waits for it.** The earlier text made the first `CONFIRMED` outcome after capability a condition of the admission, while RED-7K-18 made the admission a condition of capability: a cycle. The relation between a write and the replica's stable storage is stated, for each future entry, by that entry's own `WAITAOF 1 1` returning `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]` on the issuing connection (7E MF-04, ACC-01, ACC-05). That is the frozen acceptance mechanism doing its ordinary work after capability; it is not evidence about the past and is not consulted before publication is enabled. The first such confirmation is recorded as `POST_ADMISSION_CONFIRMATION_VERIFIED` (RED-7K-35) | Frozen 7E; **V-2** for the interval in which a rewrite is still running. For a flagged node that interval ends before capability (PO-1 (b)); for an unflagged node it is what V-2 covers (RED-7K-38) |

**C. Version behaviour that this document does not assert.** Each is validated on the deployed Redis build, configuration revision and volume class by the fault-injection cases of T-7K-11 (§26.1), and the result is recorded in the deployment-verification evidence next to the 7E CAP-07 attestation, bound to the `redis_version` of **both** nodes of a pair (V-2 concerns what a replica reports and what a primary counts), the configuration revision and the volume class. A record that names another build of either node, another revision or another volume class is no record (RED-7K-31).

| ID | Behaviour | Needed by |
|---|---|---|
| V-1 | When a replica reports, after a full synchronization, that its AOF rewrite completed successfully, the AOF files on its volume reproduce the synchronized dataset after a process kill and a host power loss | PO-1 (b) |
| V-2 | A replica does not report an AOF-fsynced offset to its primary, and so is not counted by `WAITAOF`, while its AOF does not yet reproduce its dataset (the interval between a full synchronization and the completion of the rewrite that follows it) | The soundness of frozen `WAITAOF` confirmation on a shard whose replica was fully synchronized: every confirmation after admission, and the interval of RED-7K-38. Not a transition proof |
| V-3 | A replica with an intact volume that is fully resynchronized, in the same process or after a restart, keeps a loadable AOF containing every entry it had fsynced, until the rewrite that follows the synchronization completes | The interval of RED-7K-38 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-39 [FROZEN + 7K] | **The required retained set, and what authorizes a removal** (fifth remediation, FG-7K-R5-02; corrected in the sixth, FG-7K-R6-01, and in the seventh, FG-7K-R7-01 and FG-7K-R7-02). The fifth remediation defined the set by the watermark alone. That watermark is computed over the groups *present* on a key (7E TRM-02), so an entry every present group had acknowledged fell out of the set while a *registered* group was missing, although 7E forbids trimming in exactly that state (TRM-03) and recreates the missing group at `0` so that it reads every retained entry again (BST-03). The sixth remediation still let the test oracle judge a past removal by the watermark of the moment of the test, and let it demand every entry that was merely present in Redis; both are corrected here. **Nine things are kept apart.** (a) *The registered owed-group set* of a position, with the accounting records of its pairs: every group whose registration record covers it, open, or closed at a boundary at or above it (7I ACC-02, ACC-15), and for each such group the accounting row, the recovery case or the retirement disposition of the pair. It is PostgreSQL state and does not depend on which groups Redis shows. (b) *The current safe-trim watermark* `W(K)`: the value of 7E TRM-02, **defined only while every group registered for `K` is present on `K` (TRM-03) and trimming is not paused for `K` (TRM-07)**. In any other state there is no watermark, and nothing may be trimmed. **It is the admission bound for a trim that is about to be issued, and nothing else.** It is recomputed from the present positions of the groups, so after a group was recreated at `0` (7E BST-03) it can be lower than (c); a governed repair cannot put a cursor below the stream's first entry (7G BKL-09), and a cursor found there by any other means changes nothing in what follows. It is not a record of what earlier trims removed and proves nothing about them. (c) *The historically applied trim boundary*: what lawful trims have already removed. No frozen schema records it and 7K adds no record; Redis shows it only as the first retained ID, which is an observation. It can lie above (b). (d) *Actual stream retention*: the IDs currently present on a node. (e) *A lawful trim*: an `XTRIM K MINID m` issued by the trim worker (TRM-01) at a moment when (b) was defined, with `m` no greater than the value (b) had at that moment (TRM-02, TRM-06). **Lawfulness is a fact of the moment of execution:** no later change of a group, of the registry or of the watermark makes a lawful trim unlawful or an unlawful one lawful. (f) *The evidence of a trim's authorization at its execution*: which groups were registered and which were present, each present group's last-delivered-id and smallest pending ID, whether trimming was paused, the command with its arguments and its issuing identity, and its order among the commands on the key. **No frozen schema persists it, and 7K adds no record of it.** In production it exists at most in the trim worker's operational log and in its anomaly record (TRM-03), whose retention no frozen rule fixes; in a test it is recorded synthetically (TE-1 … TE-7 below). (g) *A legitimately trimmed entry*: a confirmed entry that a lawful trim removed. It has left `Q(K)` for good. (h) *An unlawfully removed entry*: a confirmed entry removed by anything else. (i) *A removal whose authorization cannot presently be proved*: a confirmed entry that is absent, with the records of (a) complete and (f) not available. It is reported as exactly that. (1) **Definition.** For a durable key `K` of a live generation, the *required retained set* `Q(K)` is every entry confirmed on `K` (7E ACC-01) that no lawful trim has removed. A *required entry* is a member of `Q(K)`. The definition does not use the watermark: an entry leaves `Q(K)` only by a lawful trim, and while a registered group is missing or trimming is paused no trim is lawful, so every retained entry stays required, including one that every present group acknowledged. *Confirmed* is the outcome `CONFIRMED` of 7E ACC-01 for that entry and nothing weaker (9). (2) **What I-DUR promises.** Under every fault inside the 7E model (7E FMD-06) a required entry is on the stable storage of both nodes of a capable shard. That is the frozen guarantee, unchanged. (3) **What it does not promise.** That an entry a lawful trim removed still exists, or reappears because a group was later recreated or moved back; nor that an entry which was never confirmed survives anything (9). (4) **Evidence about a removal: three kinds, none of which stands in for another.** (i) *In PostgreSQL* (a): **every group of the registered owed-group set, present on the key or not,** has for the event an accounting row, a recovery case that holds its material or is resolved, or the recorded disposition of a governed retirement (7I ACC-15). A lawful trim implies it: every registered group was then present and had acknowledged the entry, and an acknowledgement is issued only after the accounting commit or the parking commit (INV-7K-08; 7G PAT-01, RTY-10). It is necessary and not sufficient: the same rows exist when an accounted entry is removed by an ungoverned command. (ii) *The state at the execution of the trim* (f). It alone shows that a removal was a lawful trim, and it is available only where it was recorded. (iii) *What Redis shows now*: the first retained ID and, where one is defined, the current watermark (b). They are observations of the present. **The current watermark is never evidence about a past removal, and an absent ID at or above it is not thereby an unauthorized removal.** Example: both groups acknowledge through `1000`; the trim worker issues `XTRIM K MINID 1001`, which removes `800`; later, under a governed pause, a group is recreated at `0`, or its cursor is found at `500`, and the watermark is `501` or lower. `800` was trimmed lawfully, stays outside `Q(K)` and is not required to reappear, and its position record remains (5). **A watermark computed from the present groups alone proves nothing about an absent registered group. The age of an entry is never evidence, and neither is the age of its position record.** (5) **Position records outlive entries** (7I ACC-11: until the outbox row is deleted and 30 days beyond). A position whose entry is no longer in Redis is the ordinary state of an acknowledged event. (6) **Three outcomes for a confirmed entry that is absent.** *Unlawfully removed* (h): a registered owed group lacks the evidence of (4)(i), or (4)(ii) shows a removal that was not a lawful trim, a trim while a registered group was missing or while trimming was paused included. It is a loss or a governance violation (7E TF-20), whatever its cause. The admission does not detect it. 7I ACC-16 does (`GONE` with no accounting row; a group that does not exist also yields `GONE`, so the unaccounted pairs of a missing group become cases at once), and against a transport that reports false state 7I ACC-17 does; the obligation is then carried by PostgreSQL (7I ACC-05). *Legitimately trimmed* (g): (4)(i) is complete and (4)(ii) shows a lawful trim. *Authorization not presently provable* (i): (4)(i) is complete and (4)(ii) is not available, which is the ordinary position in production, where no record of (f) is durable. **Neither a lawful trim nor a loss is then asserted.** The frozen response decides, unchanged: a pair with neither an accounting row nor a case goes to 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 and from there to ACC-05, whatever removed its entry, and nothing is inferred to be complete; where every pair is accounted, no obligation is outstanding, no case is opened and nothing is published again, and the removal is reported as not proved lawful (ES-14). Evidence that later shows an unlawful removal makes it (h). **A later operation on a group creates no claim to an entry that was already trimmed:** a recreation at `0` and a governed repair give the group the retained entries from its new position on (7E BST-03; 7G BKL-09); anything below the first retained entry can be obtained only by an approved R3 backfill from the sources of 7G BKL-05, and is `SOURCE_MISSING` where none of them holds it. (7) **Access.** The admission of RED-7K-34 computes no watermark, reads no registry, lists no entry and issues no command on a stream key: PO-1 argues about whole datasets, so it holds unchanged while a registered group is missing. `W(K)` and the presence check are the trim worker's (7E TRM-02, TRM-03); the classification of a position is the accounting reconciler's, with `XINFO GROUPS` and `XPENDING`, both payload-free (7I ACC-16). No identity gains a command, no payload is read, and this rule makes the outbox no replay source. (8) **Not established by a frozen source, and not claimed.** A durable record of an applied watermark (c). A durable record of the state a trim met at its execution (f). That a node's files reproduce a prefix-consistent dataset across a rewrite, which is V-1 and V-3 and is not validated (AG-7K-02). (9) **Present is not confirmed** (seventh remediation; FG-7K-R7-02).
