An entry is in `Q(K)` only if it was confirmed: `XADD` returned its ID on the primary that owns `K`, and `WAITAOF 1 1` then returned local ≥ 1 and replicas ≥ 1 on that same connection to that same primary (7E ACC-01, WAO-01, WAO-02). An entry that is visible in Redis without that reply is not confirmed (7E MF-01, ACC-02; 7D PRB-05). A shortfall such as `[1, 0]`, a timeout, a lost reply or a reset connection leaves its outcome `UNKNOWN` (7E WAO-03, WAO-05; TF-04, TF-05, TF-08, TF-09), and an entry accepted by a primary cut off from its replica was never confirmed (7E TF-15). Such an entry may survive a fault or vanish in it. Neither breaks I-DUR, and the frozen consequence is unchanged: no position is recorded and the row is not marked `PUBLISHED` (7D PRB-02; 7I ACC-18 (d)); the row is released to `PENDING` or left `CLAIMED` for lease expiry and is never `FAILED` on that account (7D DSP-03, LOOP-04); it is published again with the same `event_id` (7D AMB-01); a copy that survived is a duplicate, absorbed by consumer idempotency (7D AMB-05; 7F). **Evidence that an entry was confirmed** is one of two things and nothing else. *CE-1*, in PostgreSQL: a committed position record for (`K`, ID), which is written only for an entry confirmed before its transaction began (7I ACC-13; the invariant of ACC-18). *CE-2*, in a test only: the test publisher's own record of the `XADD` reply carrying the ID, followed on the same connection to the same primary by the `WAITAOF 1 1` reply `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]` that gave that entry its outcome (for a pipelined batch as 7E ACC-07 states it). **Not evidence:** that the entry is present on one node or on both; a replication offset, which shows receipt and not storage; an `INFO` field; a reply obtained on another connection or for another shard (7E WAO-04, ACC-08); an outbox row whose publication was attempted. An entry whose outcome was `UNKNOWN` stays without evidence even where a later reply on the same connection would have covered it: the oracle reads outcomes and derives none. **This adds no way of confirming a publication and changes no outcome.** CE-1 and CE-2 are readings of what the frozen mechanism already decided; only the test oracle uses them. The classes are PF-1 … PF-9 below. |

| # | Entry | In `Q(K)` | Redis observation | PostgreSQL evidence | If it is absent from a node |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ES-1 | Confirmed, every registered group present, not yet delivered to some group | **Yes** | ID above that group's last-delivered-id | Position record; that pair has no accounting row and no case | A loss. Inside the model it cannot happen (I-DUR). Outside it: 7I ACC-17, then ACC-05 |
| ES-2 | Confirmed, delivered, still pending in one or more groups | **Yes** | In the group's PEL | Position record; no accounting row; possibly a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`, which holds no material, or a Billing case awaiting T-14 | A loss. 7I ACC-16 `GONE`, then ACC-05; for a Billing case 7G BIL-06 |
| ES-3 | Confirmed, acknowledged by every registered group, all of them present, not yet trimmed | **Yes, until a lawful trim removes it** | ID below `W(K)`; entry present | Every pair of the registered owed-group set has an accounting row, or a case with material or resolved | No obligation depends on it, but a correct node still holds it (7E FMD-06). Its absence without a lawful trim is ES-5 |
| ES-4 | Removed by a lawful trim: an authorized prior trim (g) | No, and never again: no later movement of a group or of the watermark brings it back into `Q(K)` | Entry absent. That every registered group was present and trimming was not paused **when it was trimmed** is a fact of that moment: Redis does not show it afterwards, and the current watermark, which may since have become lower (ES-6, ES-11), says nothing about it | As ES-3, for every group of the registered owed-group set. The position record remains (7I ACC-11). In a test, the evidence TE-1 … TE-5 of the trim that removed it | Not a loss. It is not required to reappear, wherever the current watermark stands |
| ES-5 | Removed without authorization (h): an ungoverned `XDEL`; a trim past the watermark of its moment; **a trim issued while a registered group was missing or while trimming was paused**; a lost or truncated file | **Yes**, at the time of the removal | Entry absent. What makes the removal unlawful is a fact of its execution: the command was not the trim worker's `XTRIM`, or it ran while no watermark was defined, or its `MINID` exceeded the watermark of that moment; the trim worker's record of the missing-group anomaly. **An absent ID at or above the current watermark is not such a fact** | A group of the registered owed-group set, present or not, with neither an accounting row nor a case; or that evidence complete and the trim nevertheless unlawful | **A loss or a governance violation** (7E TF-20). Found by 7I ACC-16 or ACC-17; recovered from PostgreSQL by ACC-05. Never excused by age, and never by the acknowledgements of the present groups alone |
| ES-6 | Entry in the range of a governed `XGROUP SETID` or of another governed backward move of a group | Yes while it is present and the moved group has not acknowledged it again. An entry trimmed lawfully before the operation stays outside, also where the group's new position is below it | Trimming is paused for the key while the operation runs (7E TRM-07); afterwards `W(K)` is recomputed and can be lower. It bounds the next trim and judges no earlier one. A governed repair does not put a cursor below the stream's first entry (7G BKL-09); a recreation at `0` does (7E BST-03) | The operation's record (7G); the accounting rows of the first delivery remain | A present entry the moved group has not re-read is required again. An entry trimmed before the operation is not a loss: what the group can no longer re-read is the operation's concern (7G R3), not a durability fault |
| ES-7 | Entry on a key of a superseded or a retired generation; position on a recovery or replay generation | Superseded or retired key: no, I-DUR concerns live generations. Recovery or replay generation: yes, under its own registrations | Old key fenced, then destroyed by its governed operation. A position on another generation names its own generation and key | Retirement: lag 0, an empty PEL and the PostgreSQL proof (GEN-7K-09). Supersession: a recorded class for every pending entry (SUP-7K-05). **The owed-group set of a position is read from the registrations of its own key and generation (7I ACC-19), never from those of the original** | Not a loss under I-DUR for a destroyed key; the obligations are carried by PostgreSQL (DRS-7K-13; MRA-7K-14, -16). On a live recovery generation: as ES-1 … ES-5 |
| ES-8 | Entry no longer retained while PostgreSQL still holds an obligation for it | It was | `GONE`, or the key does not exist | A pair with neither an accounting row nor a case; or a case without material | The transport no longer carries the obligation; PostgreSQL does: 7I ACC-05, or classes RM-3 … RM-5 after a restore. No completion is inferred |
| ES-9 | Position record that outlives an authorized trim | No | Entry absent | Position record present until 7I ACC-11 purges it; every pair of its registered owed-group set accounted | Not a loss. The record is history, not a claim that the entry exists |
| ES-10 | **Acknowledged by every present group while one or more registered groups are missing** | **Yes.** No watermark is defined and no trim is lawful (7E TRM-03) | Every present group has acknowledged it; a registered group is absent from `XINFO GROUPS`; the trim worker records the anomaly and trims nothing | The missing group's registration is open and covers the position; its pair is accounted or not | A loss. Where the missing group's pair is unaccounted: 7I ACC-16 `GONE` at once, then ACC-05. Where it is accounted: no obligation is lost, and the removal is still ES-5 |
| ES-11 | Retained entry after the missing group was recreated at `0` (7E BST-03) | **Yes**, until the recreated group has acknowledged it and a lawful trim removes it | The group is present again with its cursor at `0`; the watermark is defined again and stays at the oldest retained ID until the group advances | The registration is unchanged (start `0`); the accounting rows of earlier deliveries remain, so a redelivery is a duplicate (7F) | A loss of the redelivery 7E BST-03 promises. An entry trimmed lawfully before the group went missing is not demanded |
| ES-12 | Position below the explicit start of a later governed registration (7E BST-04) | Not on account of the new group: its registration does not cover the position (7I ACC-15: `start < R`). It is in `Q(K)` only while no lawful trim removed it | The new group's cursor is at its stated start; trimming was paused while it was created (7E TRM-07) | The registration record with its start position and operation reference | Not a loss for the new group. Backfill below its start is a 7G replay decision, not a durability claim |
| ES-13 | Position at or below the boundary of a governed group retirement (7E RET-03; 7G §38) | Yes until a lawful trim removes it. Once the group is destroyed and removed from the registry it no longer enters the watermark | The group is absent **and no longer registered**, which is not the missing-group state of ES-10 | The registration closed at its boundary with the operation reference; each pair at or below it has an accounting row or the retirement's recorded disposition (7I ACC-15) | Not a loss once trimmed under the new watermark, given that evidence. Without it the pair is 7I ACC-16 `GONE`, then ACC-05 |
| ES-14 | Confirmed entry that is absent, with every pair accounted and no evidence of the state at its removal ((i); the ordinary position in production) | Not decidable | Entry absent; the first retained ID is above it. The current watermark is not used | Every pair of its registered owed-group set has an accounting row, a case with material or resolved, or a retirement disposition. No record of (f) exists | **Neither a loss nor a lawful trim is asserted.** No obligation is outstanding, so no case is opened and nothing is published again. It is reported as not proved lawful; evidence of an unlawful removal makes it ES-5. With a pair unaccounted it is not this class: 7I ACC-16 or ACC-17, then ACC-05 |

**Classes of an entry before a fault** (seventh remediation; FG-7K-R7-02). ES-1 … ES-14 class a *confirmed* entry by what is owed for it and by what removed it. PF-1 … PF-9 class every entry a test can find on a key before a fault, the unconfirmed ones included, and say what the oracle may demand of each. A *loss* is a breach of the frozen guarantee for a confirmed entry (7E FMD-06) under a fault inside the model; under a fault outside it the same absence is the disaster path of 7E FMD-02. *Inconclusive* means that the case neither passes nor fails on that entry: the result names the evidence that is missing, and an inconclusive case has not passed and opens no gate.

| # | Entry before the fault | Does I-DUR require it to survive | Evidence required before the fault | Absence permitted after the fault | PostgreSQL consequence of an absence | Is an absence a transport loss | Verdict of the oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF-1 | Confirmed and still required: owed to a registered group, present or missing (ES-1, ES-2, ES-10, ES-11; ES-6 while present) | **Yes** | CE-1 or CE-2; the ID present on the primary at `q`; the registrations that cover it (O-1) | None | A pair without an accounting row or a case: 7I ACC-16 `GONE`, or ACC-17 at `T_obl`, then ACC-05. An accounted pair: nothing | **Yes** | **Fails** if the ID is missing from the node under test (O-4) |
| PF-2 | Confirmed and lawfully trimmed before `q` (ES-4, ES-9) | No | CE-1; the evidence of RED-7K-39 (4)(i) for every registered owed group; in a test, TE-1 … TE-5 for the trim that removed it | Always: it is absent before the fault and is not required to reappear | None. The position record stays until 7I ACC-11 purges it; no case is opened and the row is not published again | No | **Passes.** Never fails for the absence. A reappearance fails only under O-5 with its preconditions; otherwise it is recorded and fails nothing: every group had acknowledged the entry, and a copy that returns can at most be a duplicate (7E FMD-03) |
| PF-3 | Confirmed, owed to no group any more, still retained (ES-3; ES-13 after the retirement) | **Yes, until a lawful trim removes it:** 7E FMD-06 does not end with the last acknowledgement | As PF-1, and every pair of its registered owed-group set accounted | None between `q` and the reading: the trim worker is stopped | None: no pair is unaccounted, no case is opened, nothing is published again | Yes, as a breach of survival. No delivery obligation is lost | **Fails** if the ID is missing (O-4). The result states that no obligation was affected |
| PF-4 | Outcome `UNKNOWN`, entry present: `XADD` returned an ID, or may have run, and the acknowledgement was a shortfall, timed out, was lost or was ambiguous (7E ACC-02, WAO-03, WAO-05) | No | In a test: the publisher's record of the `XADD` reply and of the reply that was not `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]`, or of its absence (TE-7). In PostgreSQL: no position record for the ID, and the row not `PUBLISHED` on that attempt | Yes, under any fault. It may equally survive | None caused by the absence. The row is `PENDING` or `CLAIMED` and is published again with the same `event_id` (7D DSP-03, AMB-01); the relay reads no stream to decide (7D TPT-10, AMB-04). A copy that survived is a duplicate with one effect (7D AMB-05; 7F) | No | Neither the absence nor the survival fails O-4. **Fails** if a position record or a `PUBLISHED` mark exists for that attempt, if the row is `FAILED` or dropped, or if the oracle demands the ID |
| PF-5 | Not confirmed, or stored on one node only: no reply yet when the fault strikes; fsynced on one node (`[1, 0]`, `[0, 1]`); accepted by a primary cut off from its replica (7E TF-15) | No | In a test: the reply counts as recorded (TE-7), and the ID set of each node read on that node (TE-5). A replication offset is not used | Yes. It may be on one node and not on the other, and a returning primary discards what the promoted node does not hold (7E MF-08) | As PF-4 | No | Not demanded. A difference between the two nodes in this class fails nothing; O-5 compares a node only with its own reading |
| PF-6 | Entry whose ID has no committed position record: a confirmed entry whose relay stopped before the mark (7E TF-21; 7I ACC-18 (a)); an entry of PF-4 or PF-5; an entry no publisher of the platform wrote | Only if it was confirmed, which PostgreSQL cannot show for it | CE-2, where the test recorded it. Without CE-2 there is no evidence of confirmation | With CE-2: none. Without it: not decidable | None: no position exists, so 7I ACC-16 never judges this ID. The row is `CLAIMED`, its lease expires, and it is published again and recorded (7D AMB-02, CRS-06). An unrecorded entry can add a delivery and cannot hide a loss (7I ACC-18 (a)) | With CE-2: yes. Without it: not shown | With CE-2: as PF-1 or PF-3. Without it: **inconclusive** on that ID. It is not demanded, and its absence is not reported as a loss |
| PF-7 | Entry whose pair already has a durable recovery case (7I ACC-05; 7G) | Yes, where it is confirmed and not lawfully trimmed: a case removes nothing from `Q(K)` | As PF-1; the case, and whether it holds material (identifiers only) | None | A case that holds its material or is resolved carries the obligation whatever happens to the entry. A case without material (`OPEN_RETRY`, `HOLD`, a Billing case awaiting T-14): as ES-2 | Yes for the entry. The obligation is at risk only where the case holds no material | **Fails** if the ID is missing (O-4). A case never excuses the absence and never stands in for CE-1 or CE-2 |
| PF-8 | Entry on a key of a live replay or recovery generation: an R4 or R5 publication of the repair publisher (ES-7) | Yes, where it is confirmed, under the registrations of its own key and generation (7I ACC-19) | CE-1 with publisher kind `REPAIR` (7I ACC-13), or CE-2 from the repair publisher's connection | None for a confirmed entry. For an unconfirmed one: as PF-4 | Confirmed: as PF-1. Unconfirmed: no `REPAIR` position is recorded, so the item stays in the remainder of its plan and is publishable from its stored capture (MRA-7K-17 (2)); no deadline restarts (MRA-7K-17 (3)) | Confirmed: yes. Unconfirmed: no | As PF-1, PF-3 or PF-4, each ID judged on its own key. Nothing is inferred from the key of the original generation |
| PF-9 | Entry on a key of a lawfully superseded or retired generation (ES-7) | No: I-DUR concerns live generations | The record of the governed operation: for a retirement lag 0, an empty PEL and the PostgreSQL proof (GEN-7K-09); for a supersession a recorded class for every pending entry (SUP-7K-05) | Yes, once the key is fenced and destroyed by its governed operation | None: the obligations are carried by PostgreSQL (DRS-7K-13; MRA-7K-14, -16) | No | Not demanded, and no entry of a superseded stream is read (MRA-7K-07). **Fails** if a key of a live generation is treated as this class without that record |

**Evidence a test records** (seventh remediation; synthetic, in a test only). The oracle needs two facts that no production record holds: what state a trim met when it ran, and which entries were confirmed. A test records them itself, in the test cluster, for synthetic entries. **None of it is a production record: no frozen schema holds it, 7K adds no table, column or log for it, no rule of §5 – §25 reads it, and nothing may assume that it exists outside a test run.**

| # | Evidence | How the test obtains it |
|---|---|---|
| TE-1 | Group state before each removing command | With the consumers stopped: `XINFO GROUPS` and the `XPENDING` summary of each group on the key, both payload-free, read before the trim worker's pass or before the injected command |
| TE-2 | The removing command and its place in the order | The command, its arguments, its issuing identity and its position among the commands on the key, from the test cluster's command capture, limited to the commands that remove entries or create, move or destroy a group |
| TE-3 | Registered-group state at that moment | The registration records (7I ACC-15) set against the groups TE-1 shows, and whether a trim pause was in force for the key |
| TE-4 | Governed pauses and group movements | The 7G operation records, and their order relative to TE-2 |
| TE-5 | Retained IDs before the fault | The IDs on the key before and after each removing command and at `q`, read on each node (IDs only) |
| TE-6 | Retained IDs after the fault | The IDs on the node under test, started alone |
| TE-7 | Confirmation | For each entry the test publishes: the connection, the primary, the `XADD` reply and the `WAITAOF 1 1` reply or its absence, in order. Where the reply is `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]` this is CE-2 |

From TE-1 … TE-4 the test decides, for each removing command, whether it was a lawful trim **at its execution**: issued by the trim worker's identity, every registered group present, no pause in force, and a `MINID` no greater than the watermark computed from TE-1. The IDs it removed are the difference of the two readings of TE-5 around it. The watermark of any later moment takes no part in that decision.

**The retained-set oracle** (used by T-7K-10, T-7K-11 and T-7K-16; specified, not executed; corrected in the seventh remediation, FG-7K-R7-01 and FG-7K-R7-02). Readings are taken at a quiesce point `q` before the fault, with publishers, consumers and the trim worker stopped and the replica caught up, which is read as equal ID sets on the two nodes and says nothing about what either has stored (a case that places an entry on one node only says so, and that entry is in `U_q`); they are evaluated on the node under test started alone. Entries are checked by ID; no body is copied into a log. **No verdict rests on a Redis reading alone, none assumes that every `PUBLISHED` row still has its entry in Redis, none treats the acknowledgements of the present groups as proof for an absent registered group, none judges a past removal by the watermark of `q`, and none demands an entry for which there is no evidence of confirmation.** Three sets are fixed at `q` for each key. `P_q`: the IDs present on the primary. `C_q`, the *confirmed required IDs*: the members of `P_q` that have CE-1 or CE-2. `U_q = P_q \ C_q`: the present IDs without evidence of confirmation (PF-4, PF-5, and PF-6 without CE-2). Nothing is trimmed between `q` and the reading, so `C_q` is exactly what a correct node must still hold after a fault inside the model. A case whose `C_q` is empty on the key it tests is inconclusive.

| Step | Source | What is read | Pass and fail |
|---|---|---|---|
| O-1 | PostgreSQL | The position records of the shard's live keys, which are also CE-1; **the registration records of every group for each key, open and closed, with start and boundary (7I ACC-15)**; for each position its registered owed-group set with accounting rows, cases and retirement dispositions (identifiers only); the status of the outbox rows the test published | — |
| O-2 | Redis, each node, at `q`; the test's record | Per key: `P_q`, and the same reading on the replica; the groups present, each with its last-delivered-id and pending IDs; **whether every registered group is present and trimming is not paused**; `W(K)` computed as 7E TRM-02 does only if so, and otherwise recorded as undefined, **in either case only as the bound a trim issued at `q` would have to respect**; TE-1 … TE-5 and TE-7; from these `C_q` and `U_q` | — |
| O-3 | PostgreSQL and the test's record, against Redis, at `q` | For every position of O-1 that is absent from `P_q`: (i) the PostgreSQL evidence of RED-7K-39 (4)(i) for **every group of its registered owed-group set, present or absent**; (ii) the removing command to which TE-5 attributes the absence, and whether TE-1 … TE-4 show it to have been a lawful trim at its execution | **Fails** (ES-5) if a registered owed group has no evidence; if the removing command was not a lawful trim at its execution (another identity or another command, a registered group missing at that moment, trimming paused at that moment, a `MINID` above the watermark of that moment); or if TE-1 … TE-5 were recorded throughout and no recorded command accounts for the absence. **Passes for the position** (ES-4) where (i) is complete and (ii) shows a lawful trim. **Inconclusive for the position** (ES-14) where (i) is complete and TE-1 … TE-5 were not recorded for the removal: neither a loss nor a lawful trim is reported. **Does not fail** because a position is absent, and **the watermark of `q` is not consulted:** an absent ID at or above it fails nothing (T-7K-11 (ak)) |
| O-4 | Redis, the node under test, after the fault | Per key: the IDs present; group state | **Fails if an ID of `C_q` is missing** (PF-1, PF-3, PF-7, PF-8; ES-1, ES-2, ES-3, ES-10, ES-11), or if such an ID is shown as acknowledged by a group that had not acknowledged it at `q`: it must be pending in that group or above its last-delivered-id (7E FMD-06). The watermark is not consulted: nothing is trimmed between `q` and the reading, and while a registered group is missing every confirmed retained entry is required. **An ID of `U_q` is not demanded:** whether it is present or absent is recorded and fails nothing (PF-4 … PF-6). An ID trimmed before `q` is not demanded (PF-2) |
| O-5 | The same node, where a case says "reconstructs the dataset" | The node's ID set and group state after the fault, compared with **that node's own reading before the kill** | **An oracle for what a completed rewrite leaves on a volume (V-1), never for I-DUR:** equality is what V-1 states, while 7E guarantees the survival of confirmed entries only. It is applied only where the case has established all three: (i) nothing changed the key from `q` to the kill: publishers, consumers and the trim worker stayed stopped, and the primary's reading just before the kill equals its reading at `q`; (ii) the reference is the node's own ID set and group state, read on that node just before the kill, never inferred from the other node or from an offset; (iii) an AOF rewrite on that node started after `q` and reported successful completion before the kill. Then it **fails** unless the two readings are equal: V-1 is not validated for the build and AG-7K-02 stays closed. A missing ID of `C_q` is in addition a failure of O-4; a missing ID of `U_q`, or a surplus ID, is a failure of V-1 only and is not reported as the loss of a confirmed entry. **Where (i) … (iii) are not all established O-5 is not applied, and O-4 alone decides.** After a kill during a rewrite (V-3) only O-4 applies: the node may hold more than `C_q`, never less |
| O-6 | PostgreSQL, after the fault | The unaccounted pairs; the outbox rows and the position records of the entries of `U_q` | Each unaccounted pair has its entry on the node (O-4), or had already failed O-3. **Fails** if an entry of `U_q` has a position record, if its row is `PUBLISHED` on that attempt, or if a row is `FAILED` or dropped because its entry vanished |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-34 [7K] | **The admission state machine** (third remediation, FG-7K-R3-01; corrected in the fourth, FG-7K-R4-01). The recorded states are conceptual: they are what the operation record of IO-7K-12 shows, and they add no condition to 7E `shard_capable`, no database object, no event and no stream write. **A record enforces nothing.** What withholds capability and promotion is the `nofailover` flag, which Redis sets from the node's own configuration and which the frozen check reads (7E MF-22, CAP-04 (8), HP-05). Every precondition below is therefore a fact that can be read from Redis, never "the shard is not capable" as an assertion of the operation. The tables give, for each state, what defines it and what is permitted in it, and for each transition, which proofs must hold and which are **not** required. |

| State | Defined by | Replica automatic-failover eligible | Durable publication on the shard | Consumption on the shard |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *Not admitted* | A Redis fact: `CLUSTER NODES`, read on the shard's primary, shows `nofailover` on the replica. Entered by a process start (RED-7K-36), by step E-1 (RED-7K-38) or by TR-F | **No** (7E MF-22) | **None.** The shard is not capable by 7E CAP-04 (8); every outcome is non-terminal (RED-7K-09) | Governed by Redis, not by the admission: group commands are accepted again once the primary's `min-replicas-to-write` condition is met (RED-7K-29). They are not publications and confirm nothing; a regression of group state gives duplicates only (7E MF-10) |
| `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` | A record, made while the flag is still present. The node's stable storage contains every required entry confirmed on the shard before the node attached (RED-7K-39) | Not yet: it becomes eligible in step 7, which is permitted only from this state | **None** | As above |
| `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` | A record, made when the flag is absent, the frozen capability check holds and the baseline of RED-7K-38 is stored | Yes | **Enabled.** Each entry is confirmed by its own `WAITAOF 1 1` (7E ACC-01) | Normal |
| `POST_ADMISSION_CONFIRMATION_VERIFIED` | A record: an ordinary publication was confirmed on the shard after admission | Yes | As the previous state | Normal |
| `UNFLAGGED_UNVERIFIED` | Redis facts: the replica shows no flag, and the baseline of its admission no longer holds (RED-7K-38) | **Yes**, by Redis. 7K cannot withhold it before E-1 takes effect | **Whatever the frozen check permits; it can report the shard capable.** An entry is confirmed only by its own `WAITAOF 1 1`; what that reply is worth in this state is V-1 and V-2 | Governed by Redis |

| # | Transition | What must hold | Explicitly not required | Acts |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TR-0 | Any state → *not admitted* | Nothing: it is not an act of the operation. A node's process starts with the start value `yes` (RED-7K-36) | A record; a detection; an operator | Redis, from the node's start configuration |
| TR-1 | *Not admitted* → `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` | (i) `CLUSTER NODES`, read on the primary at the first and at the last reading, shows `nofailover` on the replica. Frozen 7E CAP-04 (8) then makes the shard not capable; the operation relies on the flag it read, not on a state it recorded. (ii) Observations A1 … A7. (iii) PO-1 discharged in form (a) or (b). (iv) The validation record of V-1 … V-3 exists for the running builds of both nodes, the configuration revision and the volume class (RED-7K-31). (v) The run identifiers of both nodes and the replica's node identifier are equal at the first and the last reading | A publication; a `WAITAOF` reply; a write to any stream key; a canary; PO-2; an elapsed interval or an offset as proof (RED-7K-30) | The topology identity of RED-7K-18, with read-only commands on the two nodes. The rewrite that PO-1 (b) waits for is performed by the replica itself after it loads the synchronized dataset; no client starts it |
| TR-2 | `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` → `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` | (i) The evidence of TR-1 is still bound: A7 read again and unchanged. (ii) RED-7K-18 step 7 is complete: the running value is `no` on both nodes, the flag is absent on the replica in `CLUSTER NODES` read on the primary, the CAP-07 attestation is issued for the two running nodes, the baseline is recorded. (iii) The frozen `capability_established()` of 7E §23.5.2 is true in a read-only check (HP-01 … HP-08) | A `CONFIRMED` outcome; PO-2; any 7K condition beyond TR-1; a start configuration that says `no`, which never exists | Step 7: the topology identity, as a governed topology operation (7E FMD-09 b). Step 8: the frozen probe, which never writes (7E §26.1) |
| TR-3 | `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` → `POST_ADMISSION_CONFIRMATION_VERIFIED` | The observation of RED-7K-35 | It gates nothing, so nothing is required of it | The operation record, read from PostgreSQL |
| TR-F | Any state before `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` → *not admitted* | Any failure of RED-7K-31; any fault of RED-7K-33 | — | The operation: a flag already cleared in the replica's running configuration is set back to `yes` first. A restart needs no act of the operation: TR-0 |
| TR-U | `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` or later → `UNFLAGGED_UNVERIFIED` | A detection, not a proof: a reading of the drift check differs from the baseline while the replica shows no flag (RED-7K-38) | **A shard that is not capable.** The frozen check may report it capable throughout, and this transition does not pretend otherwise. An approval | The drift check of the topology identity, read-only |
| TR-E | `UNFLAGGED_UNVERIFIED` → *not admitted* | Step E-1 has taken effect: `CLUSTER NODES`, read on the primary, shows `nofailover` on the replica | Any durability proof: this transition removes eligibility and establishes nothing | The topology identity: one `CONFIG SET` on the replica's running configuration, a governed change of failover eligibility (7E FMD-09 b). Where it cannot be issued, the deployment platform terminates the replica's process, which then starts flagged or stays down |
| TR-R (withdrawn) | — | The third remediation returned an admitted replica to *not admitted* by a record alone after a full synchronization or an AOF repair, while Redis still showed the node eligible and the frozen check could report the shard capable. That record changed nothing in Redis. Replaced by TR-0, TR-U and TR-E | — | — |

**What changes no state:** a `WAITAOF` shortfall; lag; a link interruption after which the baseline still holds (RED-7K-37, path 2a).

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-36 [7K] | **Every node starts not failover-eligible** (fourth remediation; FG-7K-R4-01). (1) The start configuration of every event-transport node, primary or replica, new or old, carries `cluster-replica-no-failover yes`. The value `no` exists only in a **running** configuration, set by the topology identity in step 7 of RED-7K-18. (2) A node cannot change its own start configuration: `CONFIG REWRITE` is granted to no identity and the configuration is mounted read-only. Both are items of the deployment verification (IO-7K-03). (3) **What follows from frozen rules alone.** A replica whose process has started attempts no automatic failover (7E MF-22), from its first instant and by its own configuration, whatever another node believes about it. Once the primary's `CLUSTER NODES` shows the flag, 7E CAP-04 (8) and HP-05 are false, the probe is UNHEALTHY and every relay gate is open (7E HPR-02). For every node whose process started, the admission therefore precedes failover eligibility by construction, and precedes capability from the moment the flag is visible on the primary. (4) **A primary.** A node that starts and is the shard's primary carries the start value too. The flag has no effect on a primary, and the frozen check reads it on the replica only. Its running value is set to `no` in step 7, and only when the shard's other member names it as its master with the link up (`ROLE` on the replica) and `CLUSTER NODES` on both nodes shows the same primary for the shard's slots, so that a returning former primary that still believes itself primary is never cleared. Until then a restarted primary's running value differs from what its last attestation says; the frozen check does not read it (7E CAP-07: the relay holds no `CONFIG` privilege), and the drift check treats the primary's changed run identifier as a difference from the baseline (RED-7K-38). (5) **The visibility interval, stated.** Between a replica's start and the first cluster-bus packet from it that the primary processes, the primary's `CLUSTER NODES` can still show the node without the flag. Whether the frozen check can report the shard capable inside that interval is asserted in neither direction; it is observed by T-7K-11 (q). The replica is not promotable in it (3), and a confirmation inside it stands on the same basis as the interval of RED-7K-38 (5). (6) **Relation to 7E.** TOPO-11 and CAP-07 require `cluster-replica-no-failover no` on the replica of a capable shard and on every node the attestation covers. They are met at TR-2 for the running nodes, which is what `CONFIG GET` reads (7E CAP-07). They do not describe a start value, or a replica under admission; that wording is CNF-7K-11. No 7E rule is weakened: a flagged replica only ever makes the frozen check stricter. (7) Stated from the Redis 7.2 documentation, not from an executed test: that the flag is taken from the start configuration at process start, and that the value can be changed at run time and is then reflected in `CLUSTER NODES` (T-7K-11 (j), (q)). On a build where either fails, (3) does not hold or TR-2 is unreachable; that build is not admitted (AG-7K-02). |
| RED-7K-37 [7K] | **Every way a replica returns** (fourth remediation; FG-7K-R4-01). The two tables below cover the same paths. In the first, "enforced" means that a frozen rule, acting on a fact Redis itself produces, withholds capability until TR-2. "Not enforceable" means that no such mechanism exists, and 7K claims none. No path lets an elapsed interval or a replication offset stand for proof (RED-7K-30), none changes `WAITAOF 1 1` or the replica count, and none contains a manual promotion. |

| Path | What Redis and the frozen check do | Failover-eligible | Historical-durability evidence required | Who can enforce exclusion from eligibility | Admission before capability | Capability before any verification record | If verification fails |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Governed replacement of a lost replica | A new, empty node; always a full synchronization. It starts flagged: the shard is not capable (CAP-04 (8)) | No, until TR-2 | PO-1 (b): A1 … A7, and a rewrite that started after A1 and completed | Redis, from the start configuration (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No | RED-7K-31: the node stays flagged; repeat, or use another node |
| 2a. Partial resynchronization on the same volume, the same process of both nodes (a link interruption) | Not capable while the link is down or the lag is above the bound; capable again when HP-05 holds | Yes, throughout | None new: the node's AOF was neither replaced nor rebuilt. The drift check confirms the baseline | Nobody, and nothing needs to | **None is required** | Yes: the pair never left `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` | If the baseline cannot be confirmed, the path is 3a |
| 2b. Partial resynchronization after the replica's process restarted | It starts flagged: not capable | No, until TR-2 | PO-1 (a): A6 shows no full synchronization; the same member and attested volume | Redis (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No, apart from the visibility interval of RED-7K-36 (5) | RED-7K-31 |
| 3a. Full resynchronization in the same replica process (the backlog was exceeded; or the primary's process restarted and offers no partial resynchronization) | The frozen check can report the shard capable as soon as the link is online and within the lag bound and the AOF status is healthy. It has no input that says whether the replica's AOF reproduces its dataset | **Yes**, by Redis | PO-1 (b), which can be taken only after the flag is set | Before the fact: **no frozen mechanism.** After detection: the topology identity, by E-1 | **Not enforceable; not claimed** | **Yes** | TR-U, E-1, TR-E, then the admission (RED-7K-38). If E-1 cannot be issued, the platform terminates the replica's process |
| 3b. Full resynchronization after the replica's process restarted | It starts flagged: not capable | No, until TR-2 | PO-1 (b) | Redis (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No, apart from the visibility interval | RED-7K-31 |
| 4a. Return after a failover; the former primary's process restarted | It starts flagged and joins as the replica of the promoted node: not capable (7E RC-01 until it joins, CAP-04 (8) afterwards) | No, until TR-2 | PO-1 (a) or (b), as A6 decides; with no baseline under the new primary's run identifier, the synchronization is treated as full | Redis (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No, apart from the visibility interval | RED-7K-31; replacement after `T_rejoin_wait` |
| 4b. Return after a failover; the former primary was not restarted (a partition that healed) | The former primary demotes itself in the same process, with the running value `no` it had as an admitted node. Frozen RC-02 can report the shard capable | **Yes**, by Redis | As 3a: the baseline names another primary, so the synchronization is treated as full | As 3a | **Not enforceable; not claimed** | **Yes** | As 3a |
| 5. Both nodes restart | Both start flagged. Each reloads its own AOF (7E TF-16). The replica is flagged: not capable | No, until TR-2 | PO-1 (a) or (b), as A6 decides | Redis (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No, apart from the visibility interval | RED-7K-31. No required entry is at risk meanwhile: both files hold them (TF-16) |
| 6a. AOF write failure on the replica that clears in the same process | Not capable while the status is not OK (7E HP-06); capable again when it is OK | **Yes**, by Redis | None can be taken in place. The node is re-admitted only as a replacement (RED-7K-24) | While the fault lasts: the frozen check, by HP-06. After it clears: nobody, before E-1 | **Not enforceable once the status is OK again; not claimed** | **Yes**, if the status clears before E-1 takes effect | E-1 on the first reading that shows the fault; then replacement |
| 6b. AOF failure repaired with a restart, a repaired file or a new volume | It starts flagged: not capable | No, until TR-2 | Replacement on an emptied volume: PO-1 (b). PO-1 (a) is not available to a node whose AOF faulted | Redis (RED-7K-36) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No | RED-7K-31 |
| 7a. Redis software change (always a process start) | It starts flagged: not capable | No, until TR-2 | As 2b or 3b; and TR-1 (iv) needs the validation record for the new pair of builds | Redis (RED-7K-36); and TR-1 (iv) | **Mandatory; enforced** | No, apart from the visibility interval | RED-7K-31: not capable until the validation has been run for that pair of builds, outside production |
| 7b. Configuration change without a restart | A governed change: E-1 first, then the change, then a new configuration revision | No, from E-1 | PO-1 as A6 decides; the validation record for the new revision | The topology identity, by the order of the procedure. An ungoverned change is 7E TF-20 and is found by the drift check | **Mandatory; enforced for the governed procedure only** | Only after an ungoverned change, until the drift check finds it | E-1; RED-7K-31 |

| Path | Supplied by frozen 7E | Depends on validated V-1 / V-2 / V-3 | Needs a controlled amendment |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MF-22, CAP-04 (8), HP-05: no promotion and no capability while flagged. ACC-01, TF-11: nothing was confirmed while the shard had no replica, so every required entry is in the transferred snapshot | V-1, to discharge PO-1 (b) | None for safety. CNF-7K-11 (a) words the start value and the admission in 7E |
| 2a | ACC-01, WAO-02: every required entry was fsynced on this node before it was confirmed. FMD-06: replica lag and connection loss are inside the model | None | None |
| 2b | As path 1 for the exclusion. ACC-01, WAO-02 and TF-10 for the file the node reloaded | None for PO-1 (a) | None |
| 3a | ACC-01 for every later entry: only its own `WAITAOF 1 1` confirms it. TF-10 for the primary's file. TF-11: nothing was confirmed while the replica was away | **V-2** (the replica is not counted before its AOF reproduces its dataset), **V-3** (its earlier AOF stays loadable until the rewrite completes) and **V-1** (a completed rewrite is on stable storage) | **Yes, to enforce the order:** CNF-7K-11 (b). Without it the path is safe only on V-1 … V-3 and is otherwise unavailable (AG-7K-02) |
| 3b | As path 1 | V-1 | None |
| 4a | As path 1; TF-22 and RC-01 for the interval without a replica | V-1 where the synchronization is full | None |
| 4b | As 3a | V-1, V-2, V-3 | As 3a |
| 5 | TF-16 for the entries; as path 1 for the exclusion | V-1 where the replica resynchronizes fully | None |
| 6a | HP-06 while the fault lasts; ACC-01 for later entries | **Not covered by V-1 … V-3.** That a write failure which clears leaves every previously fsynced entry in the file is asserted nowhere; it is observed by T-7K-11 (r) and T-7K-16, and until it is shown for the build AG-7K-02 is closed | As 3a |
| 6b | As path 1 | V-1 | None |
| 7a | As path 1 | The record is void for a new build: all three are validated again | None |
| 7b | CAP-07 attests one configuration revision; TF-20 covers an ungoverned change | All three, for the new revision | None |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-38 [7K] | **A replica that returns without the flag: baseline, detection, and what the interval rests on** (fourth remediation; FG-7K-R4-01). (1) **Baseline.** TR-2 stores, for the admitted pair: the node identifier and the run identifier of each node, the primary's `sync_full` counter, the two builds and the configuration revision. Total replica membership is one (7E CAP-04), so the primary's `sync_full` counts the full synchronizations of this replica; one requested by any other client is counted as well and is treated as a difference, which is the safe direction. A missing or unreadable baseline is a difference; the current value never stands in for a past one. (2) **Drift check.** The topology identity reads these fields with read-only commands every `T_drift` ([CAND] at most `T_cap`), as part of the periodic drift check RED-7K-26 already requires. It is not a new component, and it issues no command on a stream key. A field that is absent or unreadable is a difference. (3) **The baseline holds:** nothing changes (path 2a). (4) **It does not hold, and the replica shows no flag:** TR-U is recorded and step **E-1** follows at once: `CONFIG SET cluster-replica-no-failover yes` on the replica, confirmed in `CLUSTER NODES` read on the primary. From that reading on, the shard is not capable by the frozen check, the node is *not admitted* (TR-E), and the admission of RED-7K-34 runs with its ordinary preconditions. PO-1 (b) needs a rewrite that is seen to start after A1 (A4). Where the replica's own rewrite ran before the operation could observe it, the operation restarts the replica's process through the deployment platform: the node starts flagged, synchronizes again, and is observed from its attachment. No client issues a rewrite command. (5) **What the interval before E-1 rests on.** In it the replica is failover-eligible, the frozen check can report the shard capable, and rows can be confirmed. No 7K record, timer or check protects it, and no frozen mechanism bounds its length: the drift check can be late, or down. Its safety does not depend on its length. It depends on: (a) frozen 7E: an entry is confirmed only by its own `WAITAOF 1 1` on the issuing connection (ACC-01, WAO-02); the primary's file holds every required entry (TF-10); nothing was confirmed while the replica was disconnected (TF-11); (b) **V-2**: while the replica's AOF does not reproduce its dataset it reports no AOF-fsynced offset and is not counted, so nothing is confirmed against a file that lacks it; (c) **V-3**: until its rewrite completes, the replica's earlier AOF stays loadable and holds every entry it had fsynced, which by (a) includes every required entry (a trim it had not yet received leaves it with more, never less); (d) **V-1**: once the rewrite reports success, the new files reproduce the synchronized dataset after a process kill and a power loss. Under (a) – (d), I-DUR holds at every instant of the interval, also when the replica is promoted inside it and the promoted node is later restarted from its own files. (6) **If V-1 … V-3 are not validated for the running pair** the interval is not safe, so paths 3a and 4b must not be reachable. They are not: AG-7K-02 admits no cluster on such a build, and a changed build or revision enters only through a process start or a governed change, both of which are flagged first (paths 7a, 7b). A cluster that runs without the record is outside every statement of this section. That is a statement about a gate, not about Redis. (7) **No claim beyond this.** The record `UNFLAGGED_UNVERIFIED` withholds nothing. E-1 does, from the moment Redis shows the flag. Making the frozen check itself refuse in paths 3a, 4b and 6a needs an input 7E does not have: CNF-7K-11 (b). |

**Progress: why a healthy shard always reaches capability.** Every action a transition needs is permitted in the state it starts from, and no transition has a precondition that only a later state can supply. TR-0 needs nothing. TR-1 needs the flag, which a process start or E-1 supplies without any admission; reads of `INFO`, `CLUSTER NODES` and `ROLE`; and the replica's own rewrite. None is a publication, and none is refused on a shard that is not capable. TR-2 needs configuration changes by the topology identity, which RED-7K-18 permits exactly from `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`, one attestation and one read-only probe. TR-3 is not needed for anything. TR-U needs reads only and holds whatever the frozen check reports. TR-E needs one `CONFIG SET`, or the termination of a process. **TR-1 is the only transition that requires a shard to be incapable, and it starts from a state that is defined by the flag which makes the shard incapable.** No path of RED-7K-37 reaches TR-1 while the frozen check can report the shard capable. The cycle of FG-7K-R3-01 is not recreated: capability is required by no transition before TR-2, and a confirmation by none at all. A healthy replacement with no publisher running reaches `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` (T-7K-11 (i)). Whether `cluster-replica-no-failover` can be changed at run time and is then reflected in `CLUSTER NODES` is stated from the Redis 7.2 documentation and is validated by T-7K-11 (j); on a build where it cannot, TR-2 is unreachable, the shard stays not capable, and that build is not admitted (AG-7K-02).

**Safety.** At an instant at which the frozen check reports a shard capable, its replica shows no flag on the primary. Such a replica is in exactly one of two situations. (S-1) It was admitted in its current process and its baseline holds: PO-1 held at TR-2, every later entry was fsynced on both nodes by its own confirmation (7E ACC-01), and the node's file was not rebuilt since. I-DUR holds on frozen 7E, and on V-1 where the admission used PO-1 (b). (S-2) Its baseline does not hold, or it started and its flag is not yet visible on the primary: the interval of RED-7K-38 (5) or of RED-7K-36 (5). I-DUR holds on frozen 7E with V-1, V-2 and V-3, and for path 6a on the observation named there. A baseline either holds or does not, so there is no third situation. Neither situation relies on a record having been written first, on an elapsed interval or on an offset. Where the validation S-2 needs is missing, AG-7K-02 is closed.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-35 [7K] | **Post-admission confirmation: an observation without effect.** After `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` the operation record notes the first publication position (7I ACC-13) recorded after that state on a durable key of the shard. A position is recorded only in the transaction that marks a row `PUBLISHED`, and a row is marked only after `WAITAOF 1 1` returned `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]` (7I ACC-18; 7E WAO-02), so the record shows that the frozen acceptance mechanism has worked once on the admitted pair. **It checks nothing else and changes nothing:** capability does not wait for it and is not withdrawn for its absence; failover eligibility does not depend on it; the replacement operation is terminal at `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`, and the observation is appended to its record when it occurs. On a shard whose keys carry no traffic it may not occur for a long time, and that is not a fault. **No canary exists and none is added:** the probe never writes a key or an entry (7E §26.1; 7D GATE-04), no synthetic event type is defined, and a test write is not an acceptance. **A shortfall after admission** is the ordinary case of 7E WAO-05: the outcome is `UNKNOWN`, the gate opens, no row is marked `PUBLISHED`, the shard is `REPLICA_LAGGING` (§9.3) and capability returns through the frozen probe. It does not void `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`, which concerns entries confirmed earlier, and it does not set the flag again. Shortfalls that persist while the probe reports the shard capable are alerted under the thresholds of §23.2 and diagnosed under RB-7K-02; if the cause is the replica's AOF, RED-7K-24 applies. |
| RED-7K-30 [7K] | **What is never accepted as proof.** An elapsed interval of any length, including the "twice the fsync period" of the first submission. A replication offset. A `WAIT` reply. A healthy AOF status. A dataset comparison made in memory. Each can be true while the replica's volume lacks a required entry. A `WAITAOF` reply for a later entry is not proof of historical durability either: it speaks about the writes of its own connection (7E MF-04), and it is never requested in order to enable publication. **A recorded state is not proof either, and it is not a control:** it shows that a check was made, and it withholds neither capability nor promotion (RED-7K-34). |
| RED-7K-31 [7K] | **Failed admission.** If an observation fails, a field is unreadable, a run identifier changes, PO-1 cannot be discharged, the validation record for V-1 … V-3 is missing or names another build of either node, another configuration revision or another volume class, the flag cannot be cleared or its absence cannot be confirmed in step 7, the attestation cannot be re-issued, or `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` is not reached within `T_adm,max` ([CAND] 30 minutes): the shard stays not capable, by the flag and not by this rule: the node keeps `nofailover`, has it set again where step 7 had begun, and starts with it again if it restarts (RED-7K-36); **no durable publication happens in the region** (RED-7K-09); the outbox absorbs (INV-7K-03). The remedy is governed and is one of: repeat RED-7K-18 with another node; repair the storage and repeat; or, where the validation record is what is missing, run the validation on the deployed build. There is no timed fallback, no manual promotion and no "accept as healthy" override. The pause is RR-7K-01 and is unbounded while admission keeps failing; that is stated, not hidden. |
| RED-7K-32 [7K] | **Rejoin, admissibility** (rewritten in the fourth remediation; FG-7K-R4-01). The third remediation disabled "automatic rejoin after a failover" by deployment configuration until V-3 was validated, and named nothing that made a returning node wait. The rule is now this. A node that returns **after a process start** is flagged and is admitted under RED-7K-34, by PO-1 (a) or (b) as A6 decides. It is never failover-eligible while it synchronizes or rewrites, so the exposure the earlier text closed with V-3 does not arise for it. A node that returns **in the same process** is RED-7K-38. No rejoin mode exists outside these two. Where the admission of a returning node fails, or `T_rejoin_wait` passes, the node is fenced and replaced (RED-7K-18). |
| RED-7K-33 [7K] | **Faults during an admission.** *Replica restarts during synchronization, or after it and before `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`:* its run identifier changes; all evidence is void; it starts flagged (RED-7K-36), resynchronizes, and the admission starts again from *not admitted*. *Replica restarts after `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` and before or during step 7 (a proof without an admission):* the record is bound to a run identifier that no longer exists and is void; the node starts flagged; the state is *not admitted*. No start configuration says `no`, so the interval the third remediation left open, a restart after the revision had been written, does not exist. *Replica restarts after `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`:* TR-0; it is flagged, and a new admission is needed. *Primary process restarts at any point before `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`:* it reloads its own AOF, which holds every required entry (7E TF-10); its run identifier changes, so the state returns to *not admitted*; a flag already cleared in the replica's running configuration is set back first; the primary carries its start value until step 7 (RED-7K-36 (4)). *Primary fails during the admission and stays away beyond the node timeout:* before step 7 the replica is flagged and attempts no failover (7E MF-22); the shard is `UNAVAILABLE`, not lost, until the primary returns. During step 7, after the replica's running flag was cleared, the replica is eligible and its historical durability was verified: its promotion is the normal failover of 7E; the operation ends without recording `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`, the shard is `NO_REPLICA` (7E RC-01), and the former primary returns under path 4a. *Primary's storage lost before admission:* the shard's only durable copy is gone. That is the correlated loss of 7E TF-17 / TF-19, outside the model, and is recovered from PostgreSQL under §11.4. The interval during which a shard has one durable copy is therefore the whole of `NO_REPLICA` plus the admission, and it is part of RR-7K-01. *Both nodes restart after `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`:* both start flagged; each reloads an AOF that holds every required entry, by I-DUR and 7E TF-16, which is what T-7K-11 (h) tests by starting the replica alone; the replica is then admitted again (path 5). *A `WAITAOF` shortfall after admission:* RED-7K-35; no state changes. *E-1 cannot be issued:* the replica's process is terminated through the deployment platform. If that fails as well, the interval of RED-7K-38 (5) continues under a critical alert; it is not declared safe on any other ground, and nothing is recorded as admitted. |
| RED-7K-20 [7K] | **Why no required entry is lost across a replacement.** Before the failure every required entry was fsynced on both nodes. The node that survives holds all of them in its AOF and is the primary during the replacement. The new node cannot be promoted before `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` (RED-7K-18 steps 4 and 7), and nothing is confirmed on the shard before step 7 clears the flag (RED-7K-34, RED-7K-36). From `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` on, both nodes satisfy I-DUR for every required entry confirmed so far, under V-1; every entry confirmed later is fsynced on both by its own confirmation (7E ACC-01). At no moment is the automatically promotable set a node whose stable storage lacks a required entry. |
| RED-7K-21 [7K] | **The old node never returns by itself.** A node removed under RED-7K-18 is wiped before any reuse. If it reappears as a cluster member, total replica membership is two, `shard_capable` is false (7E T-04 … T-06) and publication stops until it is removed: the frozen check is the backstop. |
| RED-7K-22 [7K] | Replacement of a **primary's** volume is not a separate procedure: the replica is promoted by automatic failover first, and the former primary is then a replica to rejoin or replace. An operator never promotes manually to shorten the wait (7E TOPO-11). |

### 9.6 Memory, disk and persistence faults

| ID | Fault | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| RED-7K-23 [FROZEN + 7K] | `OOM` on a primary | `XADD` refused; `TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE`; gate opens; outbox absorbs (7E MEM-02). Relief is acknowledgement plus trim, or added capacity (7E MEM-03). 7K adds the pacing of BPR-7K-03 so that the limit is not normally reached. Whether each consumer command is accepted at the limit is a property of the deployed Redis version and is validated by T-7K-12, not assumed |
| RED-7K-24 [7K] | Disk pressure or AOF write failure | The node's AOF status turns unhealthy; capability is withdrawn (HP-06); outcomes are non-terminal (7E CL-16, CL-20). **On a replica:** at the first reading that shows the fault the operation takes step E-1 (RED-7K-38), so that the node cannot return to capability by itself when the status clears. The node is then fenced and re-enters as a replacement on an emptied volume (RED-7K-18): no observation can show in place that a faulted file still holds every required entry, and PO-1 (a) is not available to it. If the status clears before E-1 takes effect, the frozen check can report the shard capable again; that is path 6a of RED-7K-37, it is not enforced against, and what it rests on is stated there. **On a primary:** free or extend the volume; AOF healthy; then capability. If the primary's AOF is damaged beyond repair the node is treated as lost: its replica is promoted and it is replaced. A node is never restarted with a truncated or repaired AOF and then allowed to remain primary without the replica having been admitted under RED-7K-19 |
| RED-7K-25 [7K] | Node clock moving backwards across a reload (7F HE-7K-7F-06; HCG-17) | ET nodes run a disciplined time source with step corrections disabled after start (slew only). A node whose clock offset exceeds `Δ_clock,max` ([CAND] 500 ms) at its start check does not join. A handler-contract cutover is not started while any ET node reports a clock alarm. Entry-ID monotonicity inside one stream does not depend on the clock (Redis never issues an ID at or below the stream's last one), so this protects only the cross-node comparison HCG-17 makes |

### 9.7 ACLs and non-event users (closes 7I HE-7K-7I-03)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RED-7K-26 [7K] | **ACL parity on failover.** ACL files are provisioned to every node from one source with a recorded digest. A node whose digest differs is not capable: parity is part of the pre-join check (RED-7K-18 step 4) and of the periodic drift check. After a failover the promoted node's digest is verified before the shard is declared capable. |
| RED-7K-27 [7K] | The ET cluster has no non-event user: 7E DEP-02 leaves it nothing else to serve. Key prefixes for non-event Redis users therefore concern the HT cluster only and stay under 3F §16. |
| RED-7K-28 [7K] | Provisioning overhead is bounded by the registry: 12 keys, 19 group placements in `g1` (7E §29.3). It is constant in tenants. |

### 9.8 Topology generations (closes 7E HE-7K-01 and 7F HE-7K-7F-08)

#### 9.8.1 When a new generation is opened

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| GEN-7K-01 [7K] | A new generation is proposed when at least one criterion below has held for the stated period at steady state (not during an outage or catch-up), and is opened only by a governed change with the measurement attached. |

| # | Criterion | Measured by | Threshold |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-1 | One key's append rate approaches the measured ceiling of a single key on one primary | `λ_family,p95` against the BM-7K-03 single-key ceiling | > `ρ_key` ([CAND] 60%) for `D_gen` ([CAND] 7 days) |
| G-2 | One primary's CPU is dominated by one family | Shard CPU share by key | > [CAND] 60% for `D_gen` |
| G-3 | A healthy group's lag grows although its workers are below their scaling ceiling and the gateway is not saturated | Group lag trend against consumer capacity (§15.4) | Sustained growth for [CAND] 24 hours |
| G-4 | Steady-state memory of one shard leaves less than the reserved headroom, attributable to one family with every group healthy | §16 | Below `H_min` for `D_gen` |
| G-5 | Placement correction after BM-7K-03 or production evidence | — | — |

A stale group, an outage backlog or a trim pause is never a reason for a new generation: more partitions do not shrink a backlog that a missing consumer causes.

#### 9.8.2 The combined procedure

It merges 7E LCY-07, 7F TK-1 … TK-9, the 7I registration records (ACC-15) and gateway registry (GWY-03), and the 7J expected registry (EVD-08). It edits none of them (CNF-7K-03).

| Step | Action | Owner rule | Reversible |
|---|---|---|---|
| GM-1 | Governed change approved: new `PARTITION_COUNT`, slot placement, key list. The relay build, consumer build and gateway registry that know both generations are deployed and proven running everywhere (deployment gate) | 7E LCY-05; 7F CUT-2, CUT-3 pattern | Yes |
| GM-2 | Headroom check: both generations fit in memory at peak with the reserve of §16 | RMS-7K-05 | Yes |
| GM-3 | Provision new keys and every registered group at `0`; no relay writes them and no consumer reads them | 7E BST-01; 7F TK-1 | Yes: delete the empty keys |
| GM-4 | Write group registration records for the new keys | 7I ACC-15 | Yes |
| GM-5 | Per subscribed group: take the change marker, close and quiesce, write the per-key intervals, open, prove admission | 7F TK-2 … TK-6 | Yes: a further governed record generation removes the key |
| GM-6 | Extend the gateway binding registry, the probe target set and the telemetry expected registry to the new keys | 7I GWY-03; 7E §26.3; 7J EVD-08 | Yes |
| GM-7 | Enable relays for the new generation, one loop first, then the fleet. Both generations now receive rows; consumers read both | 7E LCY-07 step 2; 7F TK-7, TK-8 | Yes: GM-7R |
| GM-8 | **Fence the old generation's publishers**, in three layers, each verified on every node, replicas included, because a replica can become the primary. (a) The deployment platform confirms that no relay or repair-publisher process configured for the old generation is running. (b) A governed ACL revision gives the relay and repair-publisher users key patterns that name the surviving generation only. Redis ACLs have no negative pattern (7I RDS-09), so the frozen pattern `stream:global:d:*` is replaced by positive per-generation patterns, which narrows it. The revision's digest is verified on every node (7I RDS-15). (c) Every connection of those two users opened before (b) is closed, unless T-7K-22 has shown on the deployed build that an ACL change binds open connections | 7E LCY-07 step 3; RED-7K-26 | Yes, until GM-11 |
| GM-9 | **Final boundary.** After GM-8, read each old key's last generated ID on its primary, twice, at least `T_publish` plus a margin apart. The two readings must be equal, and the shard's replica must have reached that ID. The value is `B(K)`. A reading that changed means a publisher is not fenced: return to GM-8. The reading is made by the operation under the recovery-operator user; the reply that carries the last generated ID also carries entry fields (7I RDS-17), so only the ID is extracted and recorded and the rest is discarded in memory (SEC-7K-09) | 7F HCG-17 pattern | — |
| GM-10 | **Drain proof:** every condition R-1 … R-10 of GEN-7K-09, per old key and per group, read after GM-9 and again immediately before GM-11 | 7E LCY-07 step 4; 7F TKO-07; 7G TPG-05 | — |
| GM-11 | **Retire the old generation**, inside one approved operation (R-9): re-validate the approval; `XGROUP DESTROY` for every group on every old key; delete the keys through the lifecycle authority of GEN-7K-11; close the registrations at `B(K)` with the operation reference (7I ACC-15); remove the keys from the registries. Each key moves `PROVEN → GROUPS_DESTROYED → DELETED`, recorded after each step, so a restart resumes from the recorded state | 7E LCY-07 step 5 | **No** |
| GM-12 | Later, separate governed change: retire the old keys' intervals | 7F TK-9 | — |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| GEN-7K-02 [7K] | **Why GM-8 has three layers.** A paused relay process can outlive its lease and write late (7D LSE-08). Confirming termination removes the process; the ACL revision removes the permission from any process the platform failed to see; closing the connections removes a session that authenticated before the revision. After GM-8 no `XADD` to an old key can succeed, so the boundary of GM-9 is final, and GM-9 tests exactly that by reading it twice. An in-flight `XADD` either executed before the revision, and is at or below the boundary, or was refused, and its row is republished to the new generation (7E CL-03 is non-terminal). The ACL revision is applied by the governed ACL source of 7I RDS-15; whether applying it in this procedure needs wording in 7I is part of CNF-7K-08. |
| GEN-7K-03 [7K] | **Abort before GM-7** removes the new generation: no entry was ever written to it. |
| GEN-7K-04 [7K] | **Abort after GM-7 (GM-7R)** treats the *new* generation as the one to retire: stop its relays, apply GM-8 … GM-11 to it. The old generation never stopped. Either generation can be the survivor until one is fenced. |
| GEN-7K-05 [7K] | **The irreversible step is GM-11**, and it is taken only on the proof of GM-10. There is no time-based retirement, no retirement on a forecast, and no retirement because both generations are consuming memory. |
| GEN-7K-06 [7K] | During GM-7 … GM-11 an event is delivered from one key only: a row is published to the generation its relay runs. A republication may land on the other generation; the guard is keyed on `event_id` (7F RDL-14) and accounting on (group, `event_id`) (7I ACC-19). |
| GEN-7K-07 [7K] | A generation migration and a handler-contract cutover of the same group never overlap (7F TKO-08). A migration is not started during an outage, a catch-up, a quarantine or an open R4 / R5 operation. |
| GEN-7K-08 [7K] | `P_f` never changes in place, slots of non-empty durable keys never move, and no key is renamed (INV-7K-07; 7E LCY-04 … LCY-06). |
| GEN-7K-09 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Conditions of ordinary retirement** (rewritten in the remediation; FG-7K-03). The first submission let GM-10 pass with a non-empty PEL where each pending entry had a disposition or a case with material. That contradicted 7E LCY-07 step 4 and 7F TKO-07, which require lag 0 **and** an empty PEL. Every condition below is required; none substitutes for another. |

| # | Condition | Proved from | Frozen source |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | Relay and repair-publisher publication to the old generation is fenced | GM-8: platform record, ACL digest on every node, connection lists | 7E LCY-07 step 3 |
| R-2 | No late publisher can append after the final boundary | GM-9: two equal readings of `B(K)` after the fence | 7F HCG-17, HCG-23 |
| R-3 | Every group registered for the family exists on the key, the key is onboarded for it, and every running member was admitted under the handler-contract record in force | 7E registry; handler-contract record | 7E TRM-03; 7F TKO-02, HCG-02 |
| R-4 | Every group has observed the final boundary: its last delivered ID equals `B(K)`. This is "lag 0". A lag value that Redis reports as undetermined is not zero | `XINFO GROUPS` on the primary | 7E LCY-07 step 4 |
| R-5 | **The PEL of every group on the key is empty** | `XPENDING` (summary form) on the primary, read after R-4 | 7E LCY-07 step 4; 7F TKO-07 |
| R-6 | Every entry that was ever pending has its required disposition and preservation evidence: for every position recorded on the key at or below `B(K)`, every owed group has an accounting row or a recovery case; every such case in `PARKED`, `REPLAYING` or `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` holds its material or is the privacy exception of 7G RPM-01; no case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` has an observation on the key | PostgreSQL: 7I ACC-03 listing empty for the key; ledger | 7G TPG-05, RPM-01; 7I ACC-06, ACC-19 |
| R-7 | No non-terminal replay or recovery operation names the key as a source or a target | Ledger operation records | 7G TPG-05 |
| R-8 | **R-5 and R-6 are independent.** A case with material does not show that the Redis entry was acknowledged, and an empty PEL does not show that an accounting row exists. Each is read from its own store | — | 7I ACC-09 |
| R-9 | The retirement is one approved, audited operation under hard dual control, whose plan fingerprint names the keys, the groups, each `B(K)` and the proofs read | Operation and approval records; audit row in the same transaction as each ledger change | 7G GRT-01, AUT-02, AUT-05; 7I OPA-07, OPA-08 |
| R-10 | Each Redis command that destroys state is issued by an identity the frozen permission model gives it to | GEN-7K-11 | 7E SCY-05; 7I RDS-10, RDS-11 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| GEN-7K-10 [7K] | **How a PEL becomes empty.** Only by a genuine acknowledgement through the gateway, on the evidence of INV-7K-08. The migration never issues `XACK`, `XCLAIM`, `XGROUP SETID` or `XGROUP DELCONSUMER` to empty a PEL, and never parks an entry in order to release it. An entry held under a frozen rule keeps the old generation alive for as long as that rule holds it: a Billing entry held for reconciliation until Billing's terminal decision (7G BIL-04; SEC-7K-20); a compatibility hold for at most 24 hours (7G IMP-03). While it waits, both generations exist and the memory condition of GM-2 keeps applying. That wait has no deadline. The frozen exits of RTN-7K-09 are the only ways it ends, and it is part of RR-7K-02. |
| GEN-7K-11 [7K] | **Authority of the destructive commands.** `XGROUP DESTROY` is held by the recovery-operator user, retrievable only while an approved group operation is `RUNNING` (7I matrix 6, OPA-11). **Deleting a stream key is held by nobody:** 7I RDS-10 denies `DEL` and `UNLINK` on stream keys to every runtime user, matrix 6 denies `DEL` to the recovery operator explicitly, and 7I RDS-11 forbids the infrastructure break-glass identity as the path of a recovery operation. 7E LCY-07 step 5 and 7E SCY-05 name key deletion as an operator-only step and leave the permission model to 7I. The frozen model therefore has no identity that may execute the last step of GM-11. 7K does not widen the recovery operator and does not route the step through break-glass. It states the minimum authority that would be needed, and records the reconciliation as CNF-7K-08: a dedicated **stream-lifecycle** ACL user holding `UNLINK` on the durable key pattern and nothing else (no read of an entry, no `XADD`, no group command, no SIGNAL key); its credential retrievable by the tooling workload only while an approved retirement or supersession operation is `RUNNING` **and** has recorded, for the key named, the proofs its procedure requires; hard dual control; an audit row per key. **Until CNF-7K-08 is consolidated and implemented, GM-11 cannot delete a key, and no topology-generation change is started** (AG-7K-13): GM-1 requires a procedure that can end. No authorized implementation is claimed to exist. |
| GEN-7K-12 [7K] | **Ordinary retirement and disaster supersession are different operations** with different preconditions and different proofs. Neither may be run under the other's approval. |

| | Ordinary retirement (§9.8) | Supersession after a lossy database event (§21.6) |
|---|---|---|
| System state | Healthy; database authoritative and current | Database rewound; transport may be ahead of it |
| How pending entries end | Consumed and acknowledged; PEL empty (R-5) | Never consumed: the entries may describe facts the database no longer holds |
| Safety proof | R-1 … R-10: nothing is left in the old keys that anyone owes | SUP-7K-07: every authoritative obligation is rebuilt from PostgreSQL, independently of the old keys |
| Material capture | None | None from a stream, ever. A restored PostgreSQL row is captured only under §21.6.2 |
| Fence | Publishers of the old generation | Every reader and every publisher of every pre-event generation |
| Authorization | Governed change; dual control | Incident reference; the restore's authorization; dual control |

**Counterexamples** (regression cases T-7K-22 (a) … (e), §26.1).

| Case | First submission | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A worker dies holding an entry on an old key | Retirement could pass: the entry "had a case" or was assumed dispositioned | R-5 fails while the entry is pending. The sweep reclaims it; it is processed and acknowledged; only then can R-5 hold |
| An `XACK` reply is lost | The entry could be counted as done | The entry is still pending or it is not: R-5 reads Redis. If pending, redelivery takes the duplicate path and acknowledges again (7G PAT-06) |
| Billing holds an entry for reconciliation | Passed, because a case with material existed | R-5 fails until Billing's terminal decision and the acknowledgement that follows it (GEN-7K-10) |
| A case exists for a pending entry | Passed | R-5 fails; R-8 says a case is not an acknowledgement |
| A relay paused before the fence appends afterwards | Prevented by two layers | Prevented by three (GM-8); detected by GM-9 if it nevertheless happened; R-4 would then fail for every group |

---

## 10. Consumer, Gateway and Acknowledgement Recovery

### 10.1 Crash and duplicate cases

| ID | Case | Result | Recovery latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| CSM-7K-01 [FROZEN] | Duplicate entry (republication; two generations; R4) | Each delivery runs the 7F pipeline; the owner guard admits one effect (7F §19) | — |
| CSM-7K-02 [FROZEN] | Worker dies before the owner commit (7F CRP-02 … CRP-06) | Entry pending; the sweep reclaims it after `reclaim_min_idle`; processed normally | `reclaim_min_idle` (300 s) plus one sweep period. This is the floor for any entry a dead worker held, whatever objective applies to its group |
| CSM-7K-03 [FROZEN] | Worker dies after the commit, before `XACK` (7F CRP-08) | Redelivery; the guard proves completion; the completion record exists; the gateway inserts the accounting row and acknowledges | Same |
| — | Two members hold one entry after a reclaim (7F CRP-11) | The database arbitrates; both may request acknowledgement; the gateway honours the member that owns the PEL entry (7I GWY-11) | — |
| — | Group state regresses after an ET failover (7E MF-10) | Entries delivered again; duplicate path | — |

### 10.2 The gateway as a capacity and availability element (closes 7I HE-7K-7I-08)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CSM-7K-04 [7K] | **Sizing model.** A gateway instance carries, per durable (group, key) placement, one blocked read per active reader plus acknowledgement traffic. Its capacity is the lesser of its Redis connection budget, its database connection budget for the evidence and accounting functions, and its CPU for token computation. Required instances: `n_gw = max(2, ⌈ (r_read + r_ack) / c_gw ⌉ + 1)`, with `c_gw` the measured per-instance capacity (BM-7K-05). The `+ 1` keeps capacity with one instance lost. |
| CSM-7K-05 [7K] | **Acknowledgement before read.** When an instance is saturated it serves acknowledgement requests before read requests. An acknowledgement completes an obligation and frees transport memory; a read creates pending work. |
| CSM-7K-06 [7K] | **Per-identity admission.** Each consumer identity has a bounded number of in-flight gateway requests. A request above the bound is refused with a retryable response before any Redis command is built. A refusal never drops an entry: nothing was read. |
| CSM-7K-07 [7K] | **An entry read but not handed over** (the caller disconnected) stays pending in Redis and returns through the sweep. The gateway never buffers entries to disk and never retries a hand-over on its own. |
| CSM-7K-08 [FROZEN + 7K] | **All gateway instances down.** Consumers idle; entries stay pending and pin trimming; publication continues (7I GWY-15). The transport then fills at the publication rate, so BPR-7K-03 paces the relay, and the outbox absorbs. No obligation is lost and none is acknowledged. |
| CSM-7K-09 [7K] | **Added latency.** Each read batch and each acknowledgement batch costs one more in-region round trip, one evidence read and one accounting insert per (group, event) (7I GWY-17). These are inside the handler path's budget of §17.3 and are measured by BM-7K-05. No figure is asserted. |

### 10.3 Deployment and compatibility holds

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CSM-7K-10 [FROZEN + 7K] | An entry a running build cannot classify is held, unacknowledged, for at most `compat_hold_max_age` (24 hours, 7G IMP-03) and is then parked with exact material. While held it pins its key's trim watermark. The deployment procedure therefore deploys consumers able to read a new type or version before any producer emits it (7C rollout order), and the hold count per group is a deployment gate: a rollout that raises it is stopped. |

### 10.4 Cutover windows (closes 7F HE-7K-7F-05, HE-7K-7F-07)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CSM-7K-11 [7K] | **A closed group accumulates.** During CUT-4 … CUT-8 the group reads nothing. Its keys grow by `λ_key × T_cut × s̄_entry`. A cutover is admitted only if that growth at peak, for the budgeted duration `T_cut,max` ([CAND] 15 minutes), fits within the shard's free memory above the AMBER threshold of BPR-7K-03. |
| CSM-7K-12 [7K] | A cutover is scheduled in a low-traffic window, never during an outage, catch-up, quarantine, generation migration or open R4 / R5 operation, and one group at a time per family. |
| CSM-7K-13 [7K] | **A publication fence pauses producers.** While it is held, every producing transaction of an affected type waits on the fence barrier. The wait is bounded by a lock timeout `T_fence_wait` shorter than the request's own budget; on timeout the producing transaction fails as a whole (state and outbox row together, 7A PR-02) with the existing retryable server-error class of its API contract. 7K adds no error code. |
| CSM-7K-14 [7K] | **Fence budget.** A cutover that has held its fence for `T_fence,max` without reaching CUT-7 is abandoned (7F HCG-25 permits abandonment until CUT-7). [CAND] 60 s; for a type produced by call-lifecycle transactions of the Voice family, [CAND] 10 s. |
| CSM-7K-15 [7K] | **Precondition for raising a fence.** The outbox has no `PENDING` or `CLAIMED` row of the affected type older than one relay cycle, the transport gate of every relay loop is closed, and every ET shard is `CAPABLE`. This makes the drain proof of CUT-5c a matter of seconds or the cutover is not started. |
