| CSM-7K-16 [7K] | A call-lifecycle transaction that fails on a fence timeout is recovered exactly like any other lost transition: by provider-event processing and the Voice reconcilers of §12. The fence is never on the media path; it can delay a control-plane commit, not audio. |

---

## 11. Poison, Parking, Retry and Replay Recovery

### 11.1 Poison and reclaim values

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| PRK-7K-01 [FROZEN] | The budget of five recorded failures, the classification table, the parking order and the terminal-disposition authority are those of 7G §10 – §24. 7K changes none. |
| PRK-7K-02 [FROZEN + 7K] | The V1 reclaim values of 7G IDL-01 … IDL-06 are kept as the starting values: `T_start` 60 s, `T_attempt_max` 120 s, `T_margin` 120 s, `reclaim_min_idle` 300 s, sweep every 30 s, batch 100. The inequality `reclaim_min_idle ≥ T_start + T_attempt_max + T_margin` is never relaxed. 7K adds one constraint: the completion-token expiry `T_token` satisfies `T_token ≥ T_start + T_attempt_max` so that a handler inside its time budget never commits under an expired token (7I CEV-11). Tuning is BM-7K-05. |
| PRK-7K-03 [7K] | **Expected time to park.** A persistently failing event parks after five recorded failures spaced by the reclaim delay: about 20 to 25 minutes with the V1 values (7G RTD-01). This is a consequence, not an objective. |
| PRK-7K-04 [7K] | **Isolation capacity** (7G HE-7K-7G-09). The isolation activity handles one suspect at a time, each bounded by `T_attempt_max`. One instance clears at least `3600 / T_attempt_max` suspects per hour (30 with the V1 value). More instances may run, each still serial. A queue of suspects older than `T_iso,alert` is alerted; suspects stay pending and are never skipped. |
| PRK-7K-05 [7K] | **Poison never becomes a capacity leak.** A parked case releases its stream entry (7G PAT-10). What remains is the ledger row and its material, bounded by §17.4. A held entry (compatibility, key not onboarded, Billing held for reconciliation) still pins trimming and is governed by RTN-7K-08 … -11. |

### 11.2 Replay

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| PRK-7K-06 [FROZEN] | Replay after partial processing applies remaining effects once: every replay mode enters the unchanged 7F pipeline, and the guard skips what is committed (7A FM-10; 7G TGT-03). |
| PRK-7K-07 [FROZEN] | The ceilings of 7G THR-02 stand: 10 items per second per operation; 2 per second for CON-06 and CON-10; one running R2 or R3 per target group; one running R4 or R5 per region; 10 000 candidates per plan. No benchmark exists, so none is raised. |
| PRK-7K-08 [7K] | **Dual control and throughput** (7I HE-7K-7I-07). The wait for a second approver is outside every throughput figure and inside every recovery-time estimate: `T_replay = T_plan + T_approve + N / r`. Runbooks state `T_approve` as a human interval with its roster dependency (7I OPA-18); it is never assumed to be zero. |

### 11.3 The backlog-pressure signal (closes 7G HE-7K-7G-03; THR-04)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| BPR-7K-09 [7K] | **Definition.** For a target group `g`, `pressure(g)` is raised when any of these holds: its oldest pending age exceeds `A_hi(g)`; its lag in entries exceeds `Q_hi(g)`; any ET shard that carries one of its keys is in the AMBER or RED zone (BPR-7K-03); the gateway is refusing requests for saturation; the database pool of its context is above `u_db,hi`; the region is in catch-up for a family it reads (OBX-7K-17). It is cleared when all of them have been below their low thresholds for `T_clear`. Thresholds are [MEAS] with the symbols of §15.5. |
| BPR-7K-10 [7K] | While `pressure(g)` is raised, every R2 and R3 operation targeting `g` is paused (7G THR-04). While any ET shard is AMBER or RED, or the relay's oldest eligible age is above its catch-up threshold, R5 is paused and R4 is paused **unless that R4 is the approved recovery of the incident that caused the condition** (PRK-7K-10). A paused operation spends nothing and fails nothing (7G THR-05). |
| BPR-7K-11 [7K] | Replay executors use their own limiter, their own database pool and no reader's admission bound (7G THR-03). They are the first workload reduced under pressure and the last restored (§13.4). |

### 11.4 Loss of transport state with PostgreSQL intact (7E TF-17, TF-19, TF-20; closes 7G HE-7K-7G-08 in part)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| PRK-7K-09 [FROZEN + 7K] | **The loss set is computed from PostgreSQL.** After a shard is rebuilt empty (keys and groups recreated at `0`, 7E BST-03), the obligations that were in it are exactly the unaccounted (outbox row, group) pairs of 7I ACC-03 whose positions lie on the lost keys. Their outbox rows still exist: 7I ACC-06 forbids deleting a `PUBLISHED` row while an owed group is unaccounted. No obligation depends on the lost Redis state. |
| PRK-7K-10 [7K] | **Two frozen ways to recover it.** (a) *Per pair:* the accounting reconciler classifies each pair `GONE`, or reaches the deadline `T_obl`, captures the row and opens a class `V` case; R2 then processes it for that group only (7I ACC-05, ACC-16, ACC-17). (b) *Per row:* one bounded R4 over the affected families and `published_at` range republishes each row once; every subscribed group receives it and already-accounted groups take the duplicate path (7G §32). |
| PRK-7K-11 [7K] | **Choice rule.** With `N_row` affected rows and `N_pair(g)` unaccounted pairs per group: `T_R4 = N_row / r_R4` and `T_R2 = max over g of N_pair(g) / r_R2(g)`, each plus planning and approval time. The runbook (RB-7K-06) computes both from the ACC-03 listing and chooses the smaller, with R4 requiring its own capability and incident reference (7G DSR-09). Both may be used: R4 first, then R2 for what remains. Neither is started before every affected shard is `CAPABLE` and its keys are onboarded. |
| PRK-7K-12 [7K] | **Before accounting exists** (7I AB-7I-10 not yet met) no durable consumer is active, so this case cannot arise in production. 7K defines no interim procedure that would rely on Redis state. |

### 11.5 Missing stream entry (closes 7G HE-7K-7G-10)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| PRK-7K-13 [7K] | **Incident procedure** (RB-7K-07), for a pending ID whose entry is gone (7G §39). (1) Stop trimming on the key; issue no claim on that (key, group). (2) Confirm the `TRANSPORT_INTEGRITY` cases exist. (3) Recover each identity from an authoritative source, in this order: the transport position record that maps (key, entry ID) to an `event_id` (7I ACC-13), then the outbox row; a transport observation in the ledger; a bounded R4 over the interval; the owner's rebuild. (4) For an identity that is recovered, the obligation continues as a consumer case of its own class. (5) For one that is not, the case stays `PARKED` and visible and is handed to 7L's record with the incident reference; it never ages out (7G PRT-02). (6) The pending IDs are released only by the governed repair after the cases are durable (7G DGL-06). Independently, the deadline of 7I ACC-17 opens a case for every unaccounted pair whatever Redis reports. A payload is never rebuilt from logs or metrics. |

---

## 12. Voice, Billing and Mandatory-Work Recovery

### 12.1 What is frozen, and what 7K owns

Frozen (OD-7B-01, OD-7B-02; 7B §26.1, §31): one finalization operation, FIN-1 … FIN-4; two invokers, the normal post-call worker and the recovery worker; one EV-079 per finalization generation; occurrence time = the persisted service-end time; recovery never writes usage and never invents a time. 7K owns the recovery worker's detection, schedule, concurrency and alerting (IO-7B-05), and the schedules of every other mandatory-work reconciler (7D HO-7K-04).

Nothing in §12 exists. The finalization operation, the accumulator, the guard and the persisted service-end time are obligations IO-7B-15 … IO-7B-17. 7K places requirements on them and invents no column, state or function name.

### 12.2 Durable state available for detection

| Fact | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Call status, `ended_at`, `updated_at` (trigger-maintained), `termination_reason`, link to the conversation | `voice.call_sessions` (`011_5C.sql`) | Exists |
| Conversation status (`ACTIVE`, `COMPLETED`, `SUMMARIZED`), `started_at`, `completed_at` (null until completion), `updated_at`, token and turn counters | `voice.conversations` (`012_5C.sql`) | Exists |
| Committed turns with `completed_at` and `created_at` | `voice.turns` (`012_5C.sql`) | Exists |
| Accumulated speech seconds, synthesized characters, agent minutes | — | Does not exist (IO-7B-15) |
| Finalization guard | — | Does not exist (IO-7B-16) |
| Persisted service-end time, distinct from `completed_at` | — | Not defined (IO-7B-17) |
| Heartbeat | Redis `session:*` key with a TTL, hot tier (3B §17) | Exists by design; **not durable and not authoritative** |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| VOX-7K-01 [7K] | **No persisted heartbeat is introduced.** The only heartbeat a frozen contract supports is the hot-tier TTL key. It is evidence for the stale-session reaper's liveness test and for nothing else. Durable progress evidence is the existing `updated_at` of the conversation and call rows and the committed turn rows. |
| VOX-7K-02 [7K] | A Redis key, its absence, a TTL expiry, a WebSocket state or a metric is never the reason a conversation is finalized (FCL-7K-01). |

### 12.3 Two workers, in a fixed order

| Worker | Frozen origin | Question it answers | Durable effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 Stale-session reaper | 3B §18.2; 7D PCI-38 | "Has this call's media path died?" | The frozen forced transition of the call to `FAILED` and EV-006 through the outbox |
| W2 Recovery finalizer (the "stale-conversation reaper" of 7B) | 7B PRD-27; IO-7B-05 | "Has this conversation's accounting been left unfinalized after its call ended?" | Invokes the shared finalization operation; nothing else |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| VOX-7K-03 [7K] | **Call before conversation** (RPR-7K-06). W2 acts on a conversation only when its call session is in a terminal status in PostgreSQL. W2 never ends a call, never decides that a call has died and never reads the hot tier. A conversation whose call is not terminal is not a W2 candidate, however old it is; it is W1's and the provider-event path's to end first. |
| VOX-7K-04 [7K] | **Why this prevents a false positive.** Finalizing while the call is live would claim the guard and leave later turns unbilled and unrepresented. A terminal call status is an authoritative, committed statement that metered service has stopped. Elapsed time is not. |

### 12.4 W1: liveness evidence and false-positive guards

W1 is a frozen component. 7K specifies its schedule and the conditions under which its time-based evidence may be acted on.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| VOX-7K-05 [FROZEN] | Usage accumulated per turn is committed in the per-turn checkpoint transaction and survives a process death, a Redis loss and a restart (7B ACC-01, ACC-03). Nothing W1 or W2 does can add to it. |
| VOX-7K-06 [7K] | Turns that were never checkpointed are not usage (7B §31.6). A process death therefore under-bills by at most the turns in flight and never over-bills. 7K accepts no mechanism that would estimate them. |
| VOX-7K-07 [7K] | **Heartbeat expiry counts only while the hot tier's memory is continuous.** W1 keeps a random epoch token in the hot tier. If the token is missing or changed (the hot tier restarted, failed over with loss, or was flushed), W1 forces no transition for one full heartbeat-expiry period plus margin, during which live gateway processes rewrite their heartbeats. Absence of a heartbeat after a hot-tier discontinuity is not evidence of death. |
| VOX-7K-08 [7K] | **W1 unreachable hot tier.** If W1 cannot read the hot tier it forces nothing. |
| VOX-7K-09 [7K] | **Durable backstop.** A call session in a non-terminal status whose `updated_at` is older than `T_call_stale` and for which the hot tier (continuous, per VOX-7K-07) holds no live heartbeat is a W1 candidate even if its `session:*` key no longer exists. This covers the session whose hot-tier entry was lost: the frozen reaper reads "abandoned `session:*` entries" and would otherwise never see it. `T_call_stale` exceeds the longest legitimate interval between two updates of a live call's row, which is a 6D runtime value (compare 7D VSC-04); 7K sets none. |
| VOX-7K-10 [7K] | W1's forced transition is the frozen one, guarded on the stale status, so a provider event or a normal end that commits first makes W1 a no-op (7D VSC-01 pattern). W1 does not release capacity outside the frozen terminal transition and the frozen capacity reconciler (6K §54.5). |
| VOX-7K-11 [7K] | W1 never applies to a session in a command-pending state that the stale-command reconciler owns (`WRAP_UP`, `TRANSFERRING`, and the look-back set of 7D §35.5.2): those end only on provider evidence (7D VSC-03). The split is by status, so the two workers have disjoint candidate sets. |

### 12.5 W2: the recovery finalizer

**Candidate predicate** (evaluated on the database clock):

```text
conversation is not finalized            -- the guard of IO-7B-16 is unclaimed
AND conversation is not marked RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED (VOX-7K-16)
AND its call session is in a terminal status
AND now() − (call session's terminal-transition time) ≥ T_fin_grace
```

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| VOX-7K-12 [7K] | **`T_fin_grace`** gives the normal finalizer time to win. It affects only how often two invokers race, never the result (7D PCW-08): the guard admits one. [CAND] 5 minutes. |
| VOX-7K-13 [7K] | **Scan ownership.** W2 runs in the `voice_recovery` role under the Voice reconciler principal. Voice tables carry forced row-level security, so a cross-tenant listing needs a guarded function that returns identifiers only: organization and conversation. That function does not exist and is a logical requirement on the IO-7B-16 migration (IO-7K-04). W2 never bypasses row-level security and never lists with a tenant context it chose from a payload. |
| VOX-7K-14 [7K] | **Per candidate, one transaction**, with the tenant context set transaction-locally from the listed row's organization (7D PCW-07; 7I TCX-05): call the shared finalization operation. W2 passes identifiers only. It passes no totals, no time and no flag that changes what the operation computes. |
| VOX-7K-15 [7K] | **Outcomes of one invocation**, exactly one of: `FINALIZED` (guard claimed, usage finalized, one EV-079 outbox row, committed); `ALREADY_FINALIZED` (no write); `CORRUPT_STATE` (VOX-7K-16); `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (the call is not terminal after all: no write); `DEFERRED` (lock not obtained or dependency failure: no write, retried next pass). |
| VOX-7K-16 [7K] | **Corrupt or missing state.** If the durable totals or the persisted service-end time are missing or fail validation, the operation claims no guard, writes no usage and no outbox row, and sets a durable marker `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` with a coded reason on the conversation's finalization record, in its own transaction. The marker is what makes the condition explicit, removes the conversation from W2's candidate set, and gives the owner's reconciliation (7L, IO-7B-18) its work list. Its physical form belongs to the IO-7B-16 migration (IO-7K-05). **Until that marker exists, W2 is not activatable**: without it a corrupt conversation would be re-evaluated on every pass with no durable record that it was found. |
| VOX-7K-17 [7K] | **Validation is total.** Every durable state maps to valid or corrupt; there is no third branch in which W2 "fixes" a value. The predicates are the 6D amendment's (IO-7B-17). 7K requires at least: a service-end time is present; it is not earlier than the conversation's start and not later than the database clock; every total is non-negative; the turn counter agrees with the committed turns. |
| VOX-7K-18 [7K] | **Leaving `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`** is an owner action with a recorded reference: the Voice owner repairs or confirms the durable state through its own governed path, clears the marker, and W2 or the owner then invokes the same operation. No path finalizes with values supplied by an operator, and Billing receives nothing until then (7B OCC-05). |
| VOX-7K-19 [OD-7B-02 + 7K] | **Occurrence time.** EV-079's `occurred_at` is written by the finalization operation from the persisted service-end time and from nothing else. W2 has no parameter through which a time could enter. Excluded as sources, by construction: W2's clock, the scheduler tick time, the relay's publish time, the delivery time, and **any timestamp that a recovery actor wrote from its own clock**. |
| VOX-7K-20 [7K] | **Consequence for W1.** If W1's forced transition writes an end time from its own clock, that value is a recovery actor's time and is not service-end evidence. The 6D amendment therefore has to say which persisted value is the service-end time of a conversation whose call was ended by W1. 7K's requirement on IO-7B-17 (IO-7K-06): the amendment names that value for every way a call can end; where it names none, the finalization operation returns `CORRUPT_STATE`. 7K's non-binding recommendation is the time of the last committed per-turn checkpoint, because the totals stop there by definition (7B §26.1 defines agent minutes "up to the last durably checkpointed turn"). The choice is a Voice and Billing matter and is not made here. |
| VOX-7K-21 [7K] | **Concurrency.** Any number of W2 instances, and the normal finalizer, may invoke the operation for one conversation at once. The guard claim (FIN-1) is the only arbiter; exactly one transaction commits an EV-079 row. W2 takes no claim of its own and no leader lock. |
| VOX-7K-22 [7K] | **Corrupt rows cannot starve healthy ones.** A pass pages through the whole candidate set by key order in bounded batches; it does not restart from the oldest row. Marked conversations are outside the predicate. |
| VOX-7K-23 [7K] | **Schedule and restart.** W2 runs every `p_W2` ([CAND] 60 s). A missed tick loses nothing: the predicate finds every unfinalized conversation at the next pass. A W2 instance that dies mid-pass leaves either a committed finalization or nothing. |
| VOX-7K-24 [7K] | **Alerting and escalation** (signals are 7J's: M-146 … M-148, AL-46). Unfinalized age above `T_fin_alert` ([CAND] 15 minutes): alert. Any `CORRUPT_STATE`: alert at once, and a count by reason. **Billing's evidence horizon is 90 days on the usage row's `occurred_at` (7F RET-7F-09).** An EV-079 whose service-end time is older than that horizon is held by the Billing consumer for Billing-owned reconciliation and is never applied by the normal handler. A conversation unfinalized or marked for more than `T_fin_critical` ([CAND] 7 days) is therefore escalated to the Voice and Billing owners, well inside the horizon. An alert is not a bound (FCL-7K-08): no deadline forces a corrupt conversation to resolve, and that is residual risk RR-7K-04. |

**State transitions of a conversation's accounting** (logical; names are descriptive, not schema).

| From | Event | Actor | To | Writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Accumulating | Turn checkpoint commits | Live runtime | Accumulating | Totals (7B ACC-01) |
| Accumulating | Call reaches a terminal status; service-end time persisted | Call lifecycle / runtime, per IO-7B-17 | Ended, unfinalized | The service-end time |
| Ended, unfinalized | Finalization invoked, state valid | Normal finalizer or W2 | Finalized | Guard, finalized usage, one EV-079 row |
| Ended, unfinalized | Finalization invoked, state invalid | Normal finalizer or W2 | Reconciliation required | The marker only |
| Reconciliation required | Owner repairs state and clears the marker | Voice owner | Ended, unfinalized | Owner's governed write |
| Finalized | Finalization invoked again | Anyone | Finalized | Nothing |

No transition leads from Accumulating to Finalized, and none is driven by a clock alone.

### 12.6 Other mandatory-work recovery (closes 7D HO-7K-04; 7A FM-12, FM-13)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MWR-7K-01 [FROZEN + 7K] | Every reconciler finds its work by a predicate over existing columns (7D PCW-04) and acts by re-dispatching owner work or by a frozen owner transition (7D PCW-05). 7K assigns each a period `p` and a staleness threshold `θ`. Both change only detection delay and duplicate work, never correctness (7D PCW-08). |
| MWR-7K-02 [7K] | **Threshold rule.** `θ ≥ T_normal,p99 + T_enqueue_gap`, where `T_normal,p99` is the measured time the normal path needs and `T_enqueue_gap` covers a broker outage. Until measured, each `θ` is the candidate in the table. A `θ` shorter than the normal path only wastes work; a longer one only delays recovery. |
| MWR-7K-03 [7K] | **Per-pass bound.** A reconciler pass handles at most `b` candidates per organization and `b_total` in all, oldest first within each organization, so one organization's backlog cannot consume a pass (FAR-7K-08). |
| MWR-7K-04 [7K] | **Hot-tier outage.** While the Celery broker is unavailable, every post-commit enqueue fails (7D §33.1). Committed operations are not failed (7D PCW-09). On recovery the reconcilers' first passes carry the whole gap; MWR-7K-03 paces them. |

**Ownership matrix.** "Guard" is what makes a duplicate dispatch harmless; all are frozen.

| Flow (7D §34) | Owner / role | Detection predicate | `p` [CAND] | `θ` [CAND] | Action | Guard | Escalation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PCI-07 API call placement | Voice / `voice_recovery` | Dispatch key `RESERVED`, or `CLAIMED` with an expired claim | 15 s | Claim expiry (frozen column) | Dispatch under the frozen claim → begin → place → record protocol; past the maximum dispatch age, the frozen `INITIATED → FAILED` with capacity release (7D VDK-06) | Dispatch state machine | Age of `RESERVED` |
| PCI-07, PCI-33 `SUBMITTING` / `AMBIGUOUS` | Voice reconciler principal | Those states | 30 s | — | **Never dialled again.** Resolved by provider evidence or an operator (7D VDK-07). Capacity stays held | Reconciliation functions | Held-for-evidence age; alert at [CAND] 5 minutes |
| PCI-08 … PCI-11 in-call commands | Voice / `voice_recovery` | Command-pending status older than the 6D reconciliation grace | 15 s | 6D runtime value | Only under the adapter's declared, tested capability (7D VCC-01 … VCC-03); evidence unobtainable: hold, retry with bounded backoff (7D VSC-06) | State compare-and-set | Sessions held pending evidence |
| PCI-13, PCI-25 callback receipts | Owning context / `callback_processor` | Receipt `RECEIVED` or `PROCESSING` older than `θ` | 30 s | 2 minutes | Re-dispatch from the stored processing input (7H CBK-10) | Processing compare-and-set; owner guards | Stale receipt age |
| PCI-28 payment receipts | Billing | `RECEIVED` older than `θ`; `RETRY_PENDING` with `next_retry_at` due | 30 s | 2 minutes | Frozen Billing processing | Guarded settlement functions | — |
| PCI-29 refunds | Billing reconciler principal | `PENDING` | 60 s | Per 6M §19 | Provider poll by the reservation key; guarded settlement | Guarded functions | Pending-refund age |
| PCI-15 reindex | Knowledge | Job `BUILDING` older than `θ` | 60 s | 15 minutes | Re-dispatch | `uq_chunk_position`; completion functions | — |
| PCI-16, -17 ingestion | Knowledge | Document `PROCESSING` with no active job | 60 s | 5 minutes | Re-dispatch | Stage compare-and-set | — |
| PCI-18 reprocess | Knowledge | Job `PENDING` older than `θ` | 60 s | 5 minutes | Re-dispatch | Same | — |
| PCI-20, PCI-32 campaign preparation | Campaign | Campaign `PREPARING` older than `θ` | 60 s | 10 minutes | Re-enqueue | Idempotent enqueue function; state compare-and-set | — |
| PCI-21 import | Campaign | Frozen 3C stale-job reaper | 60 s | 3C value | Mark `FAILED` (frozen) | — | — |
| PCI-22 integration activation | Integrations | Non-OAuth `CONNECTING` older than `θ` | 60 s | 5 minutes | Re-dispatch | Guarded activation functions | — |
| PCI-30 accounting finalization | Voice | §12.5 | 60 s | `T_fin_grace` | W2 | The guard | §12.5 |
| PCI-34 capacity release | Billing / Voice | Frozen capacity reconciler | 6K value | 6K value | Frozen | — | — |
| PCI-36 periodic billing runs | Billing | Due by state (period ended, not generated) | Per 6K | — | Frozen | Guarded functions | Subject to §21.8 after a restore |
| PCI-38 stale sessions | Voice | §12.4 | 15 s | Heartbeat expiry | W1 | State compare-and-set | — |
| Flows recovered by an outbox event (PCI-04, -05, -06, -12, -35, -37) | — | — | — | — | None: the relay and the consumer contract recover them | — | — |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MWR-7K-05 [7K] | Every candidate value above is unmeasured. None is assumed for any telephony provider: where the adapter has no declared capability the command is not enabled (7D VCC-03), so the reconciler has no candidate. Exotel is not assumed to support a status query or a repeatable command. |
| MWR-7K-06 [7K] | **Analytics projection recovery** (7F HE-7K-7F-04). The projection loop is a scan of unprojected, due ledger entries (5J §8, §9); its period is [CAND] 5 s and it is bounded per pass by MWR-7K-03. A missed pass delays a dashboard and loses nothing. Analytics is the first consumer class slowed under pressure (§13.4). |
| MWR-7K-07 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Provider unavailable** (7A FM-09). Work is retried under the owner's contract with no transaction held open; the circuit breaker is 6A §21's. 7K adds that a reconciler whose provider breaker is open issues no provider call and re-evaluates at the breaker's half-open time, so reconcilers do not amplify an outage (BPR-7K-15). |
| MWR-7K-08 [7K] | **Stale-job reconciliation.** A job row that has not progressed is detected by its own status and timestamp, never by the absence of a Celery task. A reconciler never reads broker state. |
| MWR-7K-09 [OD-7D-02] | **Mandatory audit** needs no recovery worker: it commits with its business transaction. 7K verifies only the restore case: a restored database holds mutation and audit row together or neither (DRS-7K-14). |
| MWR-7K-10 [7K] | **Payment and billing work** is reconciled only by Billing's frozen functions under Billing's principals. 7K schedules it and defines no financial rule. After a restore it is gated by §21.8. |

---

## 13. Backpressure and Tenant Fairness

### 13.1 Principles

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| BPR-7K-01 [7K] | **Backpressure moves work toward PostgreSQL, never out of the system.** Every pressure response is one of: *slow* (lower rate), *queue* (leave durable work where it is), *pause* (issue no claim or read) or *fail retryably* (refuse a request that the caller repeats). None deletes an unacknowledged durable event, releases a retention pin, converts mandatory work into best-effort work, or acknowledges without evidence. |
| BPR-7K-02 [FROZEN] | A transport failure never rolls back or blocks a valid business transaction, and no producer checks transport health before committing (7D PTX-04; INV-7K-03). Backpressure is therefore never applied to a producing transaction by the event plane. The only producer-facing waits are the publication fence (CSM-7K-13) and the admission controls of the owning APIs, which predate 7K. |

### 13.2 Layers

| Layer | Queue | Admission control | Signal | Response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Outbox | PostgreSQL rows | None on producers | Eligible backlog; oldest eligible age | Scale relay loops (ASC-7K-02); storage alert |
| Relay | — | Transport gate; pacing zone | ET memory zone | BPR-7K-03 |
| ET streams | Stream entries | `maxmemory`, `noeviction` | Used memory per primary | Refusal is non-terminal |
| Gateway | — | Per-identity in-flight bound | Saturation refusals | CSM-7K-05, -06 |
| Consumer groups | PEL | Read admission bound (7F RDL-12) | Lag; oldest pending age | Scale workers; `pressure(g)` |
| Recovery and replay | Ledger cases; operation items | Operation limiter | `pressure(g)` | Pause (BPR-7K-10) |
| Webhook dispatcher | Delivery rows | Per-organization share; per-endpoint cap | Due backlog by organization | FAR-7K-03 … -06 |
| Callback ingress | — | Pre-identity guards | Ingress concurrency | BPR-7K-12 |
| CRM imports, campaign preparation, RAG ingestion | Job rows | Per-organization concurrency | Job age | BPR-7K-16 |
| Analytics projections | Projection ledger | Per-pass bound | Projection lag | First to slow |
| Monitoring exporters | Bounded in-memory queues | Drop newest (7J ISO-04) | Dropped-telemetry counters | Never back-propagates |

### 13.3 Relay pacing by transport memory (closes 7E HE-7K-03; 7D HO-7K-02)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| BPR-7K-03 [7K] | **Zones.** Let `u` be the highest ratio of used memory to `maxmemory` among the primaries of the relay's target set, read with the read-only memory information the probe already uses. GREEN: `u < θ_1`. AMBER: `θ_1 ≤ u < θ_2`. RED: `u ≥ θ_2`. [CAND] `θ_1` = 0.60, `θ_2` = 0.75. Entry and exit use different thresholds (`θ − h`, [CAND] `h` = 0.05) so a zone does not flap. |
| BPR-7K-04 [7K] | **Response.** GREEN: no pacing. AMBER: each loop inserts a delay between claims so that the fleet's publication rate does not exceed the measured drain rate of the slowest healthy group on the affected shard. RED: the loop issues no claim; it re-reads `u` at the probe cadence. In RED no row is claimed, so no `attempt_count` grows and nothing is deferred: this is not a transport failure and does not open the transport gate. |
| BPR-7K-05 [7K] | **Why pacing is safe.** Publishing later is always permitted: the outbox is the obligation and the relay's cadence is a 7K value (7D POL-06). Pacing weakens no acceptance condition, and a paced row is published with the same identity. The cost is publication latency, which is alerted (oldest eligible age). |
| BPR-7K-06 [7K] | **Pacing never waits for a specific consumer.** It reads memory, not group state. A group that is offline for good is a governance problem (7E RET-02): pacing then holds the region's publication in AMBER or RED and the response is the escalation of RTN-7K-09, not the deletion of the group. Until that is resolved, the region is degraded: the outbox grows and events of every family are late. This is stated, not hidden (RR-7K-02). |

### 13.4 Overload tiers

Each tier names what slows, queues, pauses or fails, and the state that proves it. A tier is entered by its trigger and left when the trigger has been clear for `T_clear`.

| Tier | Trigger | Slows | Queues | Pauses | Fails retryably | Authoritative proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Normal | — | — | — | — | — | — |
| 1 Elevated | Any `pressure(g)`; AMBER on a shard; a context pool above `u_db,hi` | Relay (AMBER pacing); analytics projection loop; ingestion and import concurrency | — | R2 / R3 for the pressed groups; R5; non-incident R4; trim-pausing maintenance; cutovers; generation migrations | — | Outbox rows `PENDING`; operation state `PAUSED`; job rows |
| 2 Saturated | RED on a shard; gateway refusing; outbox oldest age above its catch-up threshold | As tier 1 | New campaign preparation and import jobs stay in their queued status | Relay claims (RED); every replay; retention *row purges* that are not deadline-bound; completeness folds are deferred to their minimum cadence | Gateway read requests above the per-identity bound | As above; campaign `PREPARING`; import job `PENDING` |
| 3 Dependency down | PostgreSQL, ET, HT, key service, journal, secret manager or egress path unavailable | — | Whatever the missing dependency serves | The matching gate (L3) | Callback receipts (server error; the provider retries); API mutations that need the dependency | The absence of the committed row; pending entries; `INTENT_REQUESTED` steps |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| BPR-7K-07 [7K] | **Never slowed, paused or shed by any tier:** the voice media path and call control; producing transactions; mandatory audit; the relay's publication of already-claimed rows; acknowledgement of completed work (CSM-7K-05); erasure and sealing steps that are at or past a deadline; the content access guard; hold placement; the security controls of 7I. A deadline-bound privacy step has priority over throughput work on the same pool. |
| BPR-7K-08 [7K] | **Group-lag bound and `T_obl`** (7I HE-7K-7I-06, -08, -10). Each durable group has a lag bound `A_lag(g)`: the oldest-pending age it is expected to stay under outside incidents. It is [MEAS] per group (BM-7K-05); until measured the candidate is 15 minutes for every group. The obligation deadline satisfies `T_obl ≥ A_lag,max + T_recap,design + T_margin` so that a healthy group, or a region paused by a single failure, opens no cases; and `T_obl < A_hold,alert`, the age at which a `PUBLISHED` row held by the accounting interlock is alerted (7I ACC-17). [CAND] `T_obl` = 2 hours; `A_hold,alert` = 6 hours. **The deadline rule is never suspended by 7K**, by an outage declaration or by any operator switch: 7I ACC-17 is what detects a transport that reports false state, and a switch that silences it would be a way to hide a lost obligation. Only quarantine stops it, because the function itself refuses there (L0). The consequence is stated: an outage of consumption longer than `T_obl` opens one class `V` case per unaccounted pair. That is correct, lossless and expensive; §17.4 sizes it, and `T_obl` is set above the design outage for that reason. |

### 13.5 Amplification control

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| BPR-7K-12 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Callback ingress** keeps its frozen contract: verify, deduplicate, commit receipt and input, acknowledge (7H §11.2). It is not subject to tenant API rate limits (7A CB-07; 7H CBK-12). Its protection is the layered pre-identity guards of 6J §24.6 and a bounded ingress concurrency per provider route. Above the bound the route answers a server error **before** any state is written, which the provider treats as a retry. Tenant REST limits are never applied to it, and a verified callback is never dropped after it was acknowledged. |
| BPR-7K-13 [7K] | **Telemetry storms.** Recovery and replay raise span, log and counter volume. Telemetry queues are bounded and drop newest (7J ISO-04); a full queue never slows the work it describes. Obligation indicators do not depend on those queues: they are read from PostgreSQL (7J AOA-13). |
| BPR-7K-14 [FROZEN] | **Endpoint auto-suspension** (7H HE-7K-7H-04) is not adopted. DEP-6J-07 stays FUTURE. A dead endpoint costs at most its `max_attempts` per event and is contained by the per-endpoint cap (FAR-7K-05). Adopting suspension would change tenant-visible behaviour and is a later governed change with its own owner decision. |
| BPR-7K-15 [7K] | **Retry amplification is bounded at every layer, by a frozen budget:** relay deferral 30 s and one batch per gate opening; consumer five recorded failures at a constant reclaim delay; delivery `max_attempts` on the frozen schedule with jitter that only shortens; callback processing a bounded attempt count; replay one execution per item. 7K adds no retry loop. A reconciler or sweeper whose target dependency has an open breaker or gate issues no attempt until the breaker's own re-test (MWR-7K-07). |
| BPR-7K-16 [7K] | **Bulk producers.** A contact import, a campaign start and a knowledge ingestion each produce events in proportion to their size (7H CNF-7H-03). Their job workers run under a per-organization concurrency bound and a regional bound, so the event rate one organization can create is the product of its bound and the per-job rate. The bounds are operational values ([MEAS], BM-7K-02, -03); they change when a job finishes, never whether it finishes, and no entitlement is implied (7H §20.1: none exists). |
| BPR-7K-17 [7K] | **Recovery exit.** Overload is over when: no shard is above GREEN; every group's oldest pending age is below `A_lag(g)`; the outbox's oldest eligible age is below its steady-state threshold; no gate is open; every paused operation has been resumed or explicitly cancelled; and these have held for `T_clear`. Until then the incident stays open (§25.3). |

### 13.6 Fairness (closes 7E HE-7K-04; 7H HE-7K-7H-01)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| FAR-7K-01 [7K] | **Where fairness can and cannot be enforced.** Streams are shared and are read in append order (7E OD-7E-03). A group cannot skip one organization's entries: it must acknowledge every entry (7E GRP-08), and skipping would pin trimming. Fairness inside a stream is therefore obtained by **bounding what one entry and one organization can occupy**, not by reordering. |
| FAR-7K-02 [7K] | **Consumer side.** (a) One attempt is bounded by `T_attempt_max` (7G IDL-02), so a slow tenant's entry holds a worker for a bounded time. (b) A worker processes at most `c_org` entries of one organization concurrently ([CAND] half its in-flight bound); a further entry of that organization that it has already read waits in memory, inside the worker's `T_start`, while entries of other organizations proceed. An entry it cannot start within `T_start` is left pending (7G IDL-01), not dropped. (c) A failing entry never blocks others (7G RTY-12). |
| FAR-7K-03 [7K] | **Webhook dispatch: per-organization share.** A claim batch contains at most `⌈φ × B_d⌉` deliveries of one organization while deliveries of other organizations are due, where `B_d` is the batch size and `φ` the share ([CAND] 0.25). When no other organization has due work, the share does not apply. The claim that implements this is the governed migration requirement MR-7H-02; the frozen claim orders globally by due time and gives no such bound (7H COM-04). |
| FAR-7K-04 [7K] | **Within one organization**, deliveries are claimed in due-time order. |
| FAR-7K-05 [7K] | **Per-endpoint in-flight cap** `c_ep` ([CAND] 2). A slow receiver holds at most `c_ep` dispatcher slots for at most its `timeout_ms` each (≤ 30 s). A delivery not claimed because its endpoint is at the cap stays `PENDING` with its `next_attempt_at` unchanged. |
| FAR-7K-06 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Release after a long suspension** (7H TEN-7H-10). The backlog that becomes due at reactivation is drained under FAR-7K-03 and FAR-7K-05 like any other. Fairness and caps never reorder for correctness, never drop a delivery and never spend an attempt (7H COM-06). |
| FAR-7K-07 [7K] | **Lock-then-recompute consumers** (CON-03, CON-09; 7F HE-7K-7F-03). A burst on one organization or knowledge base serializes on that one row. Other organizations are unaffected because the lock is per row. The burst's own completion time is linear in its size; BM-7K-06 measures the per-event hold time. No batching across events is introduced: one transaction per entry stands (7F CPM-04). |
| FAR-7K-08 [7K] | **Reconcilers and maintenance passes** take at most `b` items per organization per pass (MWR-7K-03). Time-based erasure selects by deadline across organizations and takes each organization's hold gate in share mode only for its own rows; an organization whose gate is held exclusively is skipped for that pass and retried, so others do not wait behind it (7I HE-7K-7I-13). |
| FAR-7K-09 [7K] | **Poison isolation.** One poison entry costs five attempts of at most `T_attempt_max` each and then leaves the stream. One crash-looping entry is moved to the isolation activity after the frozen threshold. Neither can hold a group. |
| FAR-7K-10 [7K] | **Starvation-freedom criterion** (tested by T-7K-04, T-7K-05, T-7K-25, T-7K-26). For any organization X that submits a bounded workload `w` while another organization Y submits an arbitrarily large one: the completion time of X's work is at most `(1 + ε) ×` its completion time when Y is absent **plus** a term bounded by the configured caps and independent of Y's volume. For shared streams the bounded term is the work already appended ahead of X's entries at the moment they were appended; fairness cannot remove that, and the criterion is stated with it. `ε` is [MEAS]. |

---

## 14. Worker Scheduling, Concurrency and Shutdown

### 14.1 Common contract

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| WKR-7K-01 [7K] | **No instance owns durable work.** Every worker is stateless across restarts. Work belongs to a row, and ownership is a lease or claim in that row. |
| WKR-7K-02 [7K] | **Identity.** Each instance generates a fresh identity at start, of the frozen forms (7D RID-04; 7E GRP-06), never reused. |
| WKR-7K-03 [7K] | **Startup order.** Region-tag check; credentials; incarnation check at pool checkout (7I JRN-12 b); role-specific admission (capability gate; handler-contract admission; registry equality). An instance that fails any check does not start work and reports not-ready. Liveness never depends on a dependency's health, so an outage does not restart workers in a loop (7D SHD-08). |
| WKR-7K-04 [7K] | **Cancellation.** A worker checks for shutdown between items and between batches, never inside a transaction. Cancelling never commits a partial effect and never issues a release that the frozen functions do not provide (7D SHD-05). |
| WKR-7K-05 [7K] | **Graceful drain.** On the stop signal: stop taking new work; let in-flight items reach a safe boundary within the role's drain period; record known outcomes; leave the rest to the lease. The termination grace period of the workload is `T_grace(role) ≥ T_drain(role) + T_record`. |
| WKR-7K-06 [7K] | **Drain periods.** Relay: one publish exchange plus the progress transaction, `T_publish + T_mark` ([CAND] 15 s; 7D SHD-07). Consumer: `T_attempt_max` plus acknowledgement ([CAND] 130 s; 7F SHD-02). Dispatcher: one attempt, at most the endpoint timeout plus recording ([CAND] 40 s). Gateway: the longest blocked read (`BLOCK`) plus one acknowledgement batch. An instance killed before its drain ends loses nothing; it causes redelivery or one spent delivery attempt. |
| WKR-7K-07 [7K] | **Restart.** No start-up step "releases the previous instance's claims". They expire. |
| WKR-7K-08 [7K] | **Scale-in** is a shutdown. A consumer name with pending entries is never deleted on scale-in (7F SHD-05); idle names are removed only by the governed maintenance of 7G RCL-12, on its three conditions. |
| WKR-7K-09 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Deploy during backlog** (7A FM-11). Old and new builds coexist. A build is admitted to a group only if it can execute every required obligation (7F HCG-02). A rolling deploy replaces at most `1 / n` of a role at a time and proceeds only while the role's lag signal is not worsening. |

### 14.2 Per-role contracts

"Scale signal" is the input of §18. "Max in flight" is per instance.

| Role | Logical owner | Principal | Trigger | Durable work | Claim / fence | Lease | Max in flight | Scale signal | Backpressure role | Activation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Relay | Platform | `app_worker` | Continuous loop | Outbox row | Claim function; compare-and-set on `claimed_by` | 300 s; no renewal | One batch per loop | Eligible backlog; oldest age | Paced (BPR-7K-03) | AG-7K-01 |
| Stream gateway | Platform | Gateway principal (future) | Requests | None | Binding registry; delivery token | — | Per-identity bound | Request rate; refusals | CSM-7K-05, -06 | AG-7K-03 |
| Normal consumer | Consuming context | Context principal | Blocked reads | PEL entry | Owner guard; handler-contract admission | Redis idle time | Admission bound `K_in` | Lag; oldest pending age | Raises `pressure(g)` | AG-7K-03 |
| Reclaim sweep | Same group | Same | Timer inside the member, every 30 s | PEL entry | Same | `reclaim_min_idle` | Sweep batch | — | Suspended by the dependency gate | AG-7K-04 |
| Isolation | Event operations | Context principal of the target | Suspect present | PEL entry; case in `HOLD` | Pre-recorded attempt | — | 1 | Suspect queue age | — | AG-7K-04 |
| Recovery-case and disposition handlers | Event operations; owning domain for class `V` | Tooling principal (future) | Operator | Case | Case row lock; class authority (7G §24.4) | — | — | — | — | AG-7K-04 |
| Replay executor | Event operations | Tooling principal | Approved operation | Operation item | `PARKED → REPLAYING` | Operation's own recovery | Limiter | — | First paused | AG-7K-04 |
| Trim | Platform | Trim ACL user (no database) | Timer | None | Watermark | — | One key at a time | — | Not run during a trim pause | AG-7K-02 |
| Accounting reconciler | Platform | Reconciler principal (future) | Timer | Unaccounted pair | Case identity | — | Per-pass bound | Watermark age | Not run in L0 / regional outage | AG-7K-03 |
| Voice W1, W2, dispatch, stale-command | Voice | `app_voice_reconciler` and Voice worker | Timer | Session, dispatch key, conversation | State machines; the guard | Dispatch claim expiry | Per-pass bound | Candidate count | Provider breaker | AG-7K-06 |
| Mandatory-work reconcilers | Each owner | Owner's worker or reconciler principal | Timer | Owner state row | Owner compare-and-set | — | Per-pass, per-organization bound | Candidate age | MWR-7K-03 | AG-7K-06 |
| Analytics projection | Analytics | Analytics context principal | Timer | Projection ledger entry | Ledger claim | — | Per-pass bound | Projection lag | First slowed | AG-7K-03 |
| Dispatcher | Integrations | Integrations worker | Continuous poll | Delivery row | `claim_seq` | `T_claim_lease` | `c_d`; `c_ep` per endpoint | Due backlog | FAR-7K-03 … -05; pre-claim gate | AG-7K-07 |
| Delivery sweeper | Integrations | Same | Timer | Stale claim | `claim_seq` | — | Per-pass bound | — | — | AG-7K-07 |
| Callback processor and sweep | Owning context | Context worker | Hint; timer | Receipt | Processing compare-and-set | — | Per-pass bound | Stale receipt age | Key-service gate | AG-7K-07 |
| Retention, sealing, purge, erasure | Platform; privacy owner | Maintenance principal (future) | Timer | Erasure step or batch | Journal state; hold gate; row lock | — | Batch size | Overdue count | Deadline-bound work is never paused | AG-7K-09 |
| Journal writer | Platform | Journal-writer principal (future) | Step in `INTENT_REQUESTED` | Step | Nonce; sequence | Single writer with fencing where required (7I JRN-05) | 1 writer | Open-step age | — | AG-7K-09 |
| Observation folds | Platform | Observer principal (future) | Timer | Obligation record | Stream lock without wait | — | Batch per call | Fold age | Completeness fold deferred in tier 2, never below once per day | AG-7K-12 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| WKR-7K-10 [7K] | **Relay fleet.** `n_loop ≥ 2`, on at least two replicas in two zones. Every loop uses the same lease (7D LSE-05); during a lease change each uses the smaller value. |
| WKR-7K-11 [7K] | **Relay loops per replica** is a configuration value; each loop holds one database connection while claiming or marking and one dedicated Redis connection per primary during an exchange (7E AFF-02). |
| WKR-7K-12 [7K] | **Relay scaling ceiling.** `n_loop,max` is the lesser of the relay pool's database connections and the count at which BM-7K-01 shows no further throughput. Scaling beyond it is refused by configuration. |
| WKR-7K-13 [7K] | The relay never runs in an `api` or `voice_gateway` process (7D TOP-05, TOP-06). |
| WKR-7K-14 [7K] | A relay that cannot establish capability claims nothing and stays not-ready (7E CAP-08); it is not restarted for that. |
| WKR-7K-15 [CAND] | **Read loop.** `COUNT` 10; `BLOCK` 5 000 ms; admission bound `K_in` 20 entries per instance; one read activity per physical key (7F RDL-07). Correctness holds for any value (7F HE-7K-7F-01). |
| WKR-7K-16 [7K] | `BLOCK` is finite and shorter than the drain period, so shutdown and handler-contract revalidation are observed (7F RDL-08, HCG-04). |
| WKR-7K-17 [7K] | `K_in` is small enough that every admitted entry starts within `T_start`: `K_in ≤ c_w × T_start / t̄_h`, with `c_w` the instance's concurrency and `t̄_h` the measured mean handler time. |
| WKR-7K-18 [7K] | **Worker count per group.** `n_w(g) = ⌈ λ_g,peak × t̄_h(g) / (c_w × ρ_w) ⌉ + 1`, with target utilization `ρ_w` ([CAND] 0.6). Minimum two per group in two zones. |
| WKR-7K-19 [CAND] | **Dispatcher.** `T_claim_lease` 300 s and `T_attempt_max` 60 s (7H DSM-02, recommended), keeping `30 s < T_attempt_max < T_claim_lease`; claim batch `B_d` 20; poll every 1 s with jitter; jitter fraction `J` 0.10 (the frozen upper bound); sweeper every 30 s. `T_admit` = 6 s is fixed by owner decision and is not a 7K value. `T_handoff` 1 s (7H, recommended); admission lock timeout 2 s, below `T_admit`. |
| WKR-7K-20 [CAND] | **Trim** every 60 s per key. Cadence changes memory, never correctness (7E TRM-11). Trim is the only writer of `XTRIM` and never runs while a governed operation holds a trim pause on the key. |
| WKR-7K-21 [7K] | **Gateway instances:** CSM-7K-04. Instances are stateless and interchangeable; a consumer reconnects to any. |

### 14.3 Scheduling

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SCH-7K-01 [FROZEN] | APScheduler is a timer trigger that calls in-process services. It never calls the platform's own REST interface and is never a source of truth (7A CEL-07). |
| SCH-7K-02 [7K] | **A tick is not work intent.** A tick only asks "is there due work?". The answer comes from a predicate over durable state. A tick that is lost, late, duplicated or fired on two replicas changes nothing but the time of detection. |
| SCH-7K-03 [7K] | **The scheduler role** runs at least two replicas. Each replica fires its own ticks. Duplicate unsafe effects are impossible because every scheduled action is a claim, a compare-and-set or an idempotent guarded function (§7). A non-blocking PostgreSQL advisory try-lock per job name MAY be used to avoid wasted duplicate scans; it is an efficiency measure and is never required for correctness (compare 7F CDP-04). No Redis lock is used for this. |
| SCH-7K-04 [7K] | **Missed schedules.** No job keeps "last run" as its definition of what is due. A job that should run daily selects everything due as of now; after an outage one pass covers the gap, in bounded batches. A job with a legal or approved deadline carries the deadline on the row (7I JRN-29), so lateness is measurable per item. |
| SCH-7K-05 [7K] | **Operational time is not business time.** A tick time, a pass time, a fold time or a recovery time is never written into an event's `occurred_at`, a usage row's `occurred_at`, a delivery's due time basis, or an incident's occurrence time (7B OCC-04; 7I SEAL-04). Each of those is read from the row that owns it. |
| SCH-7K-06 [7K] | **Cadence register.** Candidates, all [CAND]: reconcilers as §12.6; analytics projection 5 s; sweep 30 s; delivery sweeper 30 s; trim 60 s; accounting reconciler 60 s; routine fold at most once per scrape interval (15 s); completeness fold daily; retention, sealing and erasure pass every 15 minutes; outbox cleanup daily by the operator procedure (7D CLN-07); ledger cleanup daily; consumer-name maintenance weekly; ACL and configuration drift check every 5 minutes; restore drill monthly (3F §11.4). |
| SCH-7K-07 [7K] | Jitter of ±10% is applied to every period so replicas and jobs do not align. |
| SCH-7K-08 [7K] | **Maintenance windows** (7G HE-7K-7G-04). An operation that pauses trimming (`XGROUP SETID` repair, future-group creation, group retirement, R3 over a stream range) is admitted only if `λ_key,peak × T_op,max × s̄_entry` fits above AMBER on the shard (as CSM-7K-11), carries a maximum duration after which it is stopped and trimming resumes, and is not run in tier 1 or above. |
| SCH-7K-09 [7K] | Celery carries commands whose loss is recovered by a state scan. No Celery beat schedule is the only trigger of mandatory work, and no alternative broker is introduced (INV-7K-01). |

---

## 15. Resource Capacity Model

### 15.1 Method

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CPM-7K-01 [7K] | The model is symbolic. It gives formulas over named inputs. An input is [FROZEN], sourced, [CAND] or [MEAS]; an output inherits the weakest tag of its inputs. No output of this section is a measurement and none is a hardware recommendation. |
| CPM-7K-02 [7K] | The model is recomputed from measured inputs before each activation gate of §29.2. Replacing a candidate by a measurement changes numbers, not architecture: every rule that uses a value refers to it by symbol. |
| CPM-7K-03 [7K] | Sizing uses the peak of each workload shape, not an average, and adds the reserve of the scenario it must survive (§15.6). |

### 15.2 Workload shapes and the events they produce

The event counts per action come from the frozen catalog (7B §11 – §14; 7E §18). Rates are inputs.

| Shape | Driving input | Durable events per unit (families) | SIGNAL per unit | Consumer groups touched |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Concurrent inbound calls | Call start rate `r_call`; mean duration `d_call`; turns per call `n_turn` | Per call: call lifecycle events and one EV-079 (`voice`); qualification, summary where produced | `n_turn` turn signals plus tool-execution signals | 6 groups on `voice` |
| Outbound campaign launch | Contacts per campaign `N_c`; dial pacing `r_dial` | Per contact: campaign contact events (`campaign`), then a call as above | As above | 3 on `campaign`, 6 on `voice` |
| Conversation-completion burst | Calls ending per second `r_end` | `call.ended`, EV-079 and follow-ups (`voice`) | — | 6 on `voice`; Billing ingests up to five usage rows per EV-079 |
| Contact import | Rows per import `N_imp`; rows per second `r_imp` | One contact event per row (`crm`) | — | 2 on `crm`; webhook fan-out: one delivery per subscribed endpoint per row (7H CNF-7H-03) |
| Knowledge ingestion | Documents per second `r_doc` | Document events (`knowledge`) | — | 2 on `knowledge` |
| CRM and workflow fan-out | Mutation rate `r_crm`, `r_wf` | One event per mutation (`crm`, `workflow`) | — | 2 on `crm`; 1 on `workflow` |
| Webhook deliveries | Event rate of eligible types `λ_wh`; subscribed endpoints per organization `E` | — | — | Deliveries `λ_wh × E`; requests ≤ `max_attempts` each |
| Provider callback burst | Callbacks per second `r_cb` | Those the owning context derives | — | — |
| Outage accumulation | Outage length `T_out` | `λ_e × T_out` rows or entries | — | — |
| Catch-up | Backlog `N_b` | — | — | All |
| Prolonged group outage | `T_grp` | `λ_key × T_grp` retained entries per key | — | The offline group |
| Replay during recovery | `N_replay`; `r_replay` | R4, R5: one entry per item; R2, R3: none | — | Target group, or all (R4) |
| Retention and cleanup backlog | Rows past a deadline `N_due` | — | — | — |

### 15.3 Core formulas

Let `F` be the set of durable families, `λ_f` the append rate of family `f`, `s̄_f` its mean entry size in bytes including the envelope, and `g_f` the number of groups on its key.

| # | Quantity | Formula |
|---|---|---|
| C-01 | Durable event rate | `λ_e = Σ_f λ_f` |
| C-02 | Entry size bound | `s_max = 1 048 576 + s_env` bytes (7E SIZ-01, SIZ-02) [FROZEN]. Mean `s̄_f` is [MEAS] |
| C-03 | Retained entries of a key | `N_ret(f) = λ_f × A_slow(f)`, with `A_slow(f)` the age of the oldest entry its slowest group has not acknowledged (the trim watermark lag) |
| C-04 | Stream memory of a key | `M_str(f) = N_ret(f) × (s̄_f + o_e)`, with `o_e` the per-entry overhead of the stream encoding [MEAS] |
| C-05 | PEL and group overhead | `M_pel(f) = Σ_g N_pend(g, f) × o_p + g_f × o_g`, with `o_p` per pending entry and `o_g` per group [MEAS] |
| C-06 | SIGNAL memory | `M_sig = N_signal × (s̄_sig + o_e)` (7E SGR-02), bounded by construction |
| C-07 | Dataset of a shard | `D_s = Σ over its keys of (M_str + M_pel) (+ M_sig)` |
| C-08 | Outbox rows | `N_out = λ_e × (T_unpub + R_pub) + N_failed + N_held`, with `T_unpub` the publication delay, `R_pub` = 7 days [FROZEN], and `N_held` the rows the accounting interlock holds past 7 days |
| C-09 | Outbox storage | `S_out = N_out × (s̄_row + s_idx)`, five indexes [FROZEN] |
| C-10 | Outbox growth in an outage | `ΔS_out = λ_e × T_out × (s̄_row + s_idx)` |
| C-11 | Relay throughput | `μ_pub = n_loop × B_claim / (T_claim_tx + W + T_mark)` (OBX-7K-14) |
| C-12 | Consumer service capacity of a group | `μ_g = n_w × c_w / t̄_h(g)`; stable only if `λ_g < μ_g` |
| C-13 | Catch-up time | `T_cu = N_b / (μ − λ)` for the publishing or consuming stage that is the bottleneck |
| C-14 | Ledger rows | `N_case = N_open + r_res × 90 d`, with `r_res` the resolution rate [FROZEN 90 days, OD-7G-03] |
| C-15 | Replay material | `S_mat = N_mat × (s̄_f + o_enc)`, each item at most 90 days from parking or 7 days from resolution [OD-7I-09, -10] |
| C-16 | Delivery rows | `N_del = λ_wh × E × (1 + r_replay) × R_del`, with `R_del` 30 or 90 days by terminal status [FROZEN]; history records `≈ N_del × ā × 3` for claim, admission and closure with `ā` mean attempts |
| C-17 | Dispatcher capacity | `μ_d = n_d × c_d / t̄_att`; per endpoint at most `c_ep / t̄_att` |
| C-18 | Accounting and completion rows | `N_acc = Σ_f λ_f × g_f × (R_pub + 30 d)` and the same order for completion records [7I ACC-11, RET-27] |
| C-19 | Database connections | `C_db = Σ over roles of n_inst(role) × p(role)`, constrained by §17.1 |
| C-20 | Telemetry series | Upper bound 46 229 by the arithmetic of 7J §9.4 with its candidate instance counts; recomputed as `Σ S × B × I` when `I` changes |
| C-21 | Tenant concentration | With organization shares `q_i` of a family's rate, the largest share `q_max` sets the burst a single tenant can cause: `λ_f,burst = λ_f × q_max × k_burst`. Shares are [MEAS] |
| C-22 | Storage growth in an outage | Sum of C-10 (PostgreSQL), stream growth on shards that still accept writes, and delivery rows that stay `PENDING` |

### 15.4 Stability conditions

| ID | Condition |
|---|---|
| CPM-7K-04 | **Publication:** `μ_pub ≥ ρ_cu × λ_e,peak` (OBX-7K-15). |
| CPM-7K-05 | **Consumption, per group:** `μ_g ≥ ρ_cu × λ_g,peak`. A group for which this fails pins its key's memory without bound (7E §34.2). |
| CPM-7K-06 | **Delivery:** `μ_d ≥ ρ_cu × λ_wh,peak × E × ā`. |
| CPM-7K-07 | **Burst absorption:** for a burst of `N` events at rate above `μ`, the queue peaks at `N × (1 − μ / r_burst)` and drains in `N / μ × (1 − μ / r_burst) / (1 − λ / μ)`. The queue is in PostgreSQL (outbox, job rows, delivery rows) or in a stream; each has the reserve of §16 and §17. |
| CPM-7K-08 | **Memory, per shard:** `D_s,peak ≤ θ_1 × maxmemory` in the baseline and peak scenarios, so that pacing is not entered in normal operation. |
| CPM-7K-09 | **Catch-up after a dependency outage of length `T_out`:** the backlog `λ × T_out` drains in `T_cu = λ × T_out / (μ − λ)`. With `μ = ρ_cu × λ` this is `T_out / (ρ_cu − 1)`: with the candidate `ρ_cu = 2`, catch-up takes as long as the outage lasted, at peak rate. |

### 15.6 Scenarios

Each scenario fixes which inputs are at peak and which reserve is consumed. Results are formulas until BM-7K-* supplies inputs.

| Scenario | Inputs | What must hold | Reserve consumed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Baseline | Mean rates; every group healthy | CPM-7K-04 … -06, -08 with margin | None |
| Peak | Peak call rate, a campaign launch and an import together; `q_max` tenant burst | Same; queues bounded by CPM-7K-07 | Burst reserve |
| Degraded | One ET node or one zone lost | Publication paused for `T_recap`; outbox grows by C-10; consumers continue on shards whose primary has its replica and pause on the others (RED-7K-29) | Outbox outage reserve |
| Prolonged outage | ET, or one group, or the gateway, down for `T_out` | No loss; memory within `maxmemory` by pacing; outbox within storage; cases opened after `T_obl` | Outbox and ledger reserve |
| Recovery | Catch-up at `ρ_cu`; replay within its ceilings; privacy passes continue | CPM-7K-09; deadline-bound work not starved (BPR-7K-07) | Catch-up capacity |

---

## 16. Redis Memory, Disk, AOF and Network Sizing

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RMS-7K-01 [7K] | **Per-shard memory requirement.** `maxmemory ≥ D_s,peak / θ_1`, where `D_s,peak` is C-07 in the peak scenario plus the retained backlog of the largest tolerated consumer outage on that shard: `λ_key,peak × T_grp,design × (s̄ + o_e)` summed over its keys. |
| RMS-7K-02 [7K] | **Node memory.** `maxmemory ≤ f_mem × M_node`. The remainder covers replication output buffers, client buffers, the copy-on-write pages of an AOF rewrite, and allocator fragmentation. `f_mem` is [CAND] 0.60 until BM-7K-03 measures rewrite copy-on-write under peak append rate. A node that is killed for memory during a rewrite is a node failure (§9.3), not a data loss. |
| RMS-7K-03 [7K] | Primary and replica of a shard have identical `maxmemory` and identical policy (7E FMD-10, CAP-05). |
| RMS-7K-04 [7K] | **Operating headroom.** Between `θ_2 × maxmemory` and `maxmemory` nothing is published. That band exists so that consumer commands and trims still have memory while the backlog drains (7E MEM-03). |
| RMS-7K-05 [7K] | **Two generations.** During a migration both generations' keys exist. A migration is admitted only if the sum fits under `θ_1` at peak (GM-2). |
| RMS-7K-06 [CAND] | `T_groupless` = 24 hours for the two group-less keys (7E GLR-01). It is transport retention only. |
| RMS-7K-07 [7K] | **Stale or offline group** (7E HE-7K-03). Memory grows at `λ_key × (s̄ + o_e)` until the group returns. Time to AMBER from a healthy state: `(θ_1 × maxmemory − D_s) / (λ_key,peak × (s̄ + o_e))`. That time is the budget for the group to recover or for the governed decision of RTN-7K-09; it is computed per shard and exported as a forecast. |
| RMS-7K-08 [7K] | **Trim pause.** Growth during a pause is the same formula with every group's entries retained; the admission rule is SCH-7K-08. |
| RMS-7K-09 [7K] | **SIGNAL cannot starve durable publication** (7I HE-7K-7I-03). SIGNAL memory is bounded by `N_signal` (C-06). `N_signal` is set so that `M_sig ≤ ψ × maxmemory` of its shard, [CAND] `ψ` = 0.05. A SIGNAL publisher that meets any error drops and counts (7E SPB-02); it never retries into pressure. |
| RMS-7K-10 [7K] | **AOF rewrite cadence** (7I HE-7K-7I-06). Trimmed entries remain in AOF files until a rewrite. A rewrite is triggered by size growth and, at the latest, every `T_aof_rw,max` ([CAND] 24 hours) by the operations role, one node at a time and never on a primary and its replica together. After a rewrite a trimmed entry exists in no file of that node. The residue interval of an erased subject's event in transport is therefore at most the trim-pin age plus `T_aof_rw,max` (RR-7K-05). |
| RMS-7K-11 [7K] | **Disk.** Volume size `≥ k_aof × maxmemory`, with `k_aof` covering the current base file, the incremental file and a full rewrite in progress. [CAND] `k_aof` = 3. Disk usage above [CAND] 70% is alerted; a full disk is RED-7K-24. |
| RMS-7K-12 [7K] | **Network and connections.** Per primary: one dedicated connection per relay loop per exchange; gateway connections for reads and acknowledgements; the trim worker; the observer's one connection per shard (7J PRF-11). Consumer workers hold none (7I GWY-13). The largest entry is about 1 MiB (C-02); client and replication buffer limits are never set below it (7E TOPO-09). |

### 16.1 Reserve for a publication pause

For the design pause `T_recap,design`, the outbox reserve is `λ_e,peak × T_recap,design × (s̄_row + s_idx)` and no Redis memory is needed, because nothing is published. For a prolonged ET outage the same formula holds with `T_ET_out`.

---

## 17. Database, Worker, Delivery and Storage Sizing

### 17.1 Connections

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DBS-7K-01 [FROZEN + 7K] | Application processes connect through the pooler in transaction mode (3F §15.1). Session state is therefore never relied on: tenant context is transaction-local (7I TCX-05), and advisory locks used by any rule of 7F, 7I or 7K are transaction-scoped. |
| DBS-7K-02 [7K] | **Budget.** `Σ over pools of C_srv(pool) ≤ C_max − C_reserved`, with `C_max` the server's connection limit and `C_reserved` held for replication, administration and the restore reconciler. Each pool of TOP-7K-12 has its own `C_srv`. |
| DBS-7K-03 [7K] | **Reserved pools.** `api` and `voice_gateway` pools are sized first, from their own peak, and are not reduced to make room for the event plane. Then, in order: maintenance (deadline-bound privacy work), relay, gateway, consumers, dispatcher, reconcilers, tooling, observer. A role that would exceed the remaining budget is scaled down, not the reserved ones. |
| DBS-7K-04 [7K] | **Relay.** One connection per loop while claiming or marking: `C_srv(relay) ≥ n_loop`. |
| DBS-7K-05 [7K] | **Recovery-case read** (7G HE-7K-7G-02). One point read on the case identity per entry on the normal path. It is on the handler path's budget (§17.3) and uses the ledger's unique index. If the ledger is unavailable the dependency gate opens (7G GTE-08); the read is never skipped or cached negatively. |

### 17.2 Outbox and PostgreSQL storage

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DBS-7K-06 [7K] | **Ledger growth** (7G HE-7K-7G-07). Rows: C-14. Material: C-15. Provenance and grants: one record per (group, event, type) touched by a cutover, capture or backfill. Unresolved cases have no upper bound in time (7G PRT-02), so capacity is planned for `N_open,design`, the cases one design outage produces: `Σ_g λ_g,peak × max(0, T_out,design − T_obl)`. Cleanup of resolved cases runs daily in bounded batches (7G PCL-04). |
| DBS-7K-07 [7K] | **Delivery tables** (7H HE-7K-7H-05). `webhook_deliveries` is partitioned (7H); partitions are created ahead of time for at least [CAND] three periods and are dropped only when wholly past retention and wholly free of content under 7I (a partition that still holds a body is an erasure and is journaled, 7I JRN-21). Volumes: C-16. Purge runs in batches of `b_purge` under the journal protocol. |
| — | **Outbox.** No partitioning and no new index are introduced (7D PHY-11, SGL-04). The claim scan uses `idx_outbox_claim`; its cost under a backlog of `N_b` pending rows is measured by BM-7K-02. Dead-tuple growth from the `PENDING → CLAIMED → PUBLISHED` updates is an operational vacuum concern and is part of the same benchmark. |

### 17.3 Handler-path budget

Per entry, on the normal path, the database work is: recovery-case read; tenant-context set; owner transaction (guard, effect, completion record); evidence read; accounting insert. Per delivery attempt: claim; admission write; re-check read; closure write (7H HE-7K-7H-07).

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DBS-7K-08 [7K] | The per-attempt transactions of 7H are short and independent; none is held across the HTTP request (7A PR-06). Their cost is measured by BM-7K-08 together with the admission lock wait. |
| DBS-7K-09 [7K] | The tenant-context sequence and each guarded-function call add a constant number of statements per entry (7I HE-7K-7I-01). They are measured, not estimated (BM-7K-06). |
| DBS-7K-10 [7K] | **Costs of 7I OD-7I-18 and OD-7I-19** (HE-7K-7I-10): nine pools (TOP-7K-12); one completion insert per (group, event); one accounting insert; one position insert per publication; one token computation per delivery and one verification per acknowledgement; one keyed-digest verification and one provenance insert per guarded transition. Storage: C-18. All are inputs to BM-7K-05 and BM-7K-06. |
| DBS-7K-11 [7K] | **Primary-only content reads** (7I HE-7K-7I-16). Content-returning functions raise on a standby (7I CAG-03), so delivery list, detail and replay reads are served by the primary. The read capacity this removes from replicas is added to the `api` pool on the primary. |
| DBS-7K-12 [7K] | **Observer.** A small fixed pool of its own, never a workload's (7J PRF-11); each observer function under a statement timeout and index-supported (7J PRF-08). |

### 17.4 Case volume from a prolonged consumer outage

With accounting active, a group offline longer than `T_obl` produces one class `V` case per unaccounted pair (7I ACC-17). Volume: `λ_g × (T_out − T_obl)`. Recovery throughput through R2 is at most the frozen ceiling (PRK-7K-07): the time to clear is `N / r_R2(g)` plus approvals. With `T_obl` = 2 hours [CAND], a four-hour outage of a group receiving `λ_g` events per second leaves `7 200 × λ_g` cases; at 10 items per second that is `720 × λ_g` seconds of replay. This arithmetic is why `T_obl` is set above the design outage and why a consumer outage is an incident before it reaches `T_obl`.

### 17.5 Object and telemetry storage

Erasure journal: one object per batch or step (7I JRN-28); volume proportional to deliveries and cases, retained for the backup horizon plus 30 days (7I RET-24): with the frozen one-year weekly snapshot, about 13 months. Sealed-content vault: proportional to rows past their deadline while held or undecided; unbounded in time by design (7I RET-30). Telemetry: OBS-7K-10.

---

## 18. Autoscaling and Saturation Response

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| ASC-7K-01 [7K] | **Scale on backlog and age, not on CPU alone.** An event-plane role scales on the queue it drains: eligible backlog and oldest eligible age (relay); lag and oldest pending age (consumers); due backlog (dispatcher). CPU and memory remain the guard against overcommit (3F §9.1). |
| ASC-7K-02 [7K] | **Relay.** Scale out when oldest eligible age exceeds its steady-state threshold with every gate closed and the zone GREEN. Do not scale out when a gate is open or the zone is AMBER or RED: more loops would not publish more. Ceiling: WKR-7K-12. |
| ASC-7K-03 [7K] | **Consumers.** Scale out per group on sustained lag growth while the gateway is not saturated and the context's pool is below `u_db,hi`. Ceiling: the context's pool and the gateway's capacity. |
| ASC-7K-04 [7K] | **Scale-in is slow.** A stabilization window of at least `reclaim_min_idle` for consumers and `L_claim` for relays, so that scale-in does not cause reclaim churn. Scale-in follows WKR-7K-05 … -08. |
| ASC-7K-05 [7K] | **ET is not autoscaled.** Its node count, memory and slot placement change only by governed operations (§9.5, §9.8). No automation adds a replica (7E TOPO-10, FMD-09). |
| ASC-7K-06 [7K] | **Saturation of a ceiling** is an alert and a tier (§13.4), not a reason to exceed it. The response is the tier's, plus capacity added by a governed change. |
| ASC-7K-07 [7K] | **Reserved capacity.** Node capacity reserved for `voice_gateway` and `api` is not available to event-plane autoscaling, by scheduling priority and quota (TOP-7K-14). |
| ASC-7K-08 [7K] | Autoscaling inputs are operational telemetry. Loss of telemetry freezes the current replica counts; it never scales a role to zero. |

---

## 19. Retention, Erasure and Privacy Operational Capacity

7K sets cadence, batch shape and capacity for the 7I controls. It changes no retention period, no hold rule, no access rule and no erasure semantics. Where a schedule and a 7I rule could disagree, the 7I rule wins and the schedule waits.

### 19.1 Separate lifetimes

| Domain | Lifetime | Authority | What 7K sets |
|---|---|---|---|
| Business data | Owner's retention profile | 4I §9.4 | Nothing |
| Audit | 1 year hot, 7 years cold | 5J §20 | Nothing |
| Outbox | `PUBLISHED` 7 days and until accounted; `FAILED` ≥ 30 days and dispositioned | `077_5J1`; 7G ILK; 7I ACC-06 | Cleanup cadence; `A_hold,alert` |
| Redis streams | Until safely trimmed | 7E §34 | Trim cadence; pin escalation; rewrite cadence |
| Recovery-ledger metadata | 90 days after resolution; unresolved never | OD-7G-03 | Cleanup cadence |
| Replay material | ≤ 90 days from parking; ≤ 7 days from resolution | OD-7I-09, -10 | Pass cadence |
| Delivery metadata and bodies | 30 / 90 days; lineage and cancellation limits | 6J; OD-7I-01, -02 | Purge cadence and batch |
| Callback inputs | Terminal transaction; 7 days if `FAILED` | 7H CBK-18; OD-7I-03 | Pass cadence |
| Accounting, position, completion records | Row life plus 30 days; guard horizon | 7I RET-25 … -27 | Value for state-guarded consumers (RTN-7K-03) |
| Telemetry | 30 days; 13 months; 14 days | OD-7J-03 | Storage sizing |
| Erasure journal | Completion plus longest backup retention plus 30 days | 7I RET-24 | The backup retention value (REG-7K-07) |
| Backups | 7 days continuous; 30 days daily; 1 year weekly | 3F §11.1 | Kept as frozen (REG-7K-07) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RTN-7K-01 [7K] | **One retention pass**, every 15 minutes [CAND], performs in order: seal or erase rows at or past their content deadline; time-based erasure batches; purge of content-free rows; cleanup of expired metadata. The first two are deadline-bound and are never paused by a tier (BPR-7K-07). The pass is bounded per run and resumes; lateness is visible per row because each row's deadline is on the row (7I CAG-02). |
| RTN-7K-02 [7K] | **Batch shape** (7I HE-7K-7I-11). A time-based batch holds at most `b_er` rows and at most one organization's rows per hold-gate acquisition (RTN-7K-04). One journal object per batch (7I JRN-28). `b_er` is [MEAS]: large enough that journal append latency does not dominate, small enough that the erasure transaction's lock time stays under the handler path's tolerance. |
| RTN-7K-03 [7K] | **Purge and cleanup cadence.** Content-free row purges and ledger cleanup run daily in batches of `b_purge`, lowest priority on the maintenance pool. Completion records of state-guarded consumers, whose guard horizon is unbounded, are kept [CAND] 90 days beyond their outbox row: long enough to cover every replay source of 7G (the longest is the 90-day material limit) and bounded, because early purge fails safe (7I CEV-15). |
| RTN-7K-04 [7K] | **Hold gate** (7I HE-7K-7I-13). An erasure or purge transaction takes the organization's hold gate in share mode and holds it only for that organization's rows in the batch. Hold placement takes it exclusive and is short. A pass that finds a gate held exclusive skips that organization and continues (FAR-7K-08). |
| RTN-7K-05 [7K] | **Evidence-store retention barrier** (7F HE-7K-7F-06). An exclusive barrier pauses every handler of that evidence store. Retirement therefore runs in a low-traffic window, in chunks whose exclusive hold is bounded by `T_barrier,max` ([CAND] 5 s) with a lock timeout; a chunk that cannot get the barrier in time is abandoned and retried. Handlers that wait longer than their statement timeout fail as dependency-wide and are not counted (7G FC-10). |
| RTN-7K-06 [7K] | **Lock order.** Maintenance transactions take locks in the order 7H and 7I fix (organization or gate, then endpoint, then delivery or case row) and use a lock timeout. A deadlock abort of an erasure transaction is retried; its journal intent stays durable (7I JRN-08). |
| RTN-7K-07 [7K] | **Deadlines under a journal outage** (7I JRN-29). Each pending step carries its deadline. An *approaching* deadline is raised `T_dl,warn` ([CAND] 72 hours) before the limit; a *missed* deadline is a privacy incident. Neither suspends nor extends the obligation, and there is no unjournaled path. |

### 19.2 Trim pins (closes 7G HE-7K-7G-05; 7I HE-7K-7I-06)

A pending entry pins every later entry of its key. For a family that carries personal data this prolongs transport residue (7I RR-05).

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RTN-7K-08 [7K] | **Sources of a pin and their frozen bounds.** Compatibility hold: 24 hours, then parked (7G IMP-03). Recorded handler failures: five attempts, then parked. Crash-loop: threshold and isolation limit, then parked. Key not onboarded: until onboarding completes, a governed operation in progress. Billing entry held for reconciliation, beyond its horizon or origin-unverifiable: until Billing's terminal decision (7G §35; SEC-7K-20). An offline or closed group: until it returns. A trim pause: SCH-7K-08. |
| RTN-7K-09 [7K] | **Monitoring and escalation.** The pin age of a key is the age of its oldest unacknowledged entry in any group. Above `A_pin,warn` ([CAND] 6 hours) the owning group's owner is alerted. Above `A_pin,max` ([CAND] 24 hours) the condition is an incident with a named cause from RTN-7K-08 and one of the frozen exits is pursued: restore the group; complete the onboarding; obtain Billing's decision; or, for an obligation that no longer exists, the governed retirement of 7G §38 with its owner approval. |
| RTN-7K-10 [7K] | **What 7K never does.** No entry is deleted, trimmed past the watermark, acknowledged without evidence or parked without a frozen reason in order to release a pin. No group is destroyed because it pins (7E RET-02). `A_pin,max` is an escalation age, not a deletion age. Where none of the frozen exits is available the pin stays, the transport stays in its zone, and the residue is RR-7K-02 and RR-7K-05. |
| RTN-7K-11 [7K] | **Interaction with accounting and observation.** A pinned entry is unaccounted; after `T_obl` it has a case (7I ACC-17) and its obligation no longer depends on the stream. The 7J obligation record for it stays open with no time limit (7J AOA-19). Neither changes because trimming was delayed, and neither may be closed to make trimming possible. |

### 19.3 Reviewers and dual control

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RTN-7K-12 [7K] | **People are a capacity.** Dual control needs two available holders of the capability; the frozen roster minimum is three (7I SEAL-12, OPA-18). Recovery-time estimates include approval time (PRK-7K-08). When the authorization issuer or the tooling is down, no approval can be recorded, dependent operations wait, and nothing proceeds without it (7I HE-7K-7I-12). The issuer is therefore a dependency of: organization lifecycle actions, erasure approvals, replay approvals and inspections; its availability objective is set with the tooling's, and its outage fails those actions closed. |

### 19.4 Subject index, holds, sealing and the access guard

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| RTN-7K-13 [7K] | **Write amplification** (7I HE-7K-7I-14). Every write of a contact-linked resource adds one index write in the same transaction. For bulk imports and campaign materialization the amplification is one per row; it is part of BM-7K-02's import workload and of the job concurrency bound (BPR-7K-16). |
| RTN-7K-14 [7K] | **Backfill** runs as a maintenance job in bounded batches per organization, lowest priority, and is not a precondition of ordinary operation; it is a precondition of that organization's certification (7I HLD-24). The index-first rule stands: in production the index is installed before tenant data exists (OD-7I-31). |
| RTN-7K-15 [7K] | **Completeness check and observability** read maintained counts, never scan content tables per scrape (7J PRF-15). |
| RTN-7K-16 [7K] | **Merge contention** (7I HE-7K-7I-15). A contact merge takes the organization's gate exclusive. Bulk merges are therefore serialized per organization and run as a job with a per-organization concurrency of one; other organizations are unaffected. |
| RTN-7K-17 [7K] | **Values.** The recommended values of 7I are adopted as candidates without change: exception length 30 days; privacy-owner bound 90 days; cumulative cap 180 days; escalation at 7, 30 and 90 days; roster of three. They are operational candidates for the privacy owner to confirm at activation. **None bounds RR-10**: unauthorized overdue retention has no maximum duration, and no 7K schedule, alert or staffing level changes that (OD-7I-28). |
| RTN-7K-18 [7K] | **Sealing and vault capacity.** Vault volume grows with rows past their deadline that are held or undecided, and has no time bound. It is sized from the count of such rows, reported, and never relieved by erasing a sealed row without its disposition. |
| RTN-7K-19 [7K] | **Access-guard cost** (7I HE-7K-7I-16). One deadline evaluation per content read and per row of a retention predicate. The delivery list route calls the tenant read function per row; its page size is the frozen one and its cost is in BM-7K-08. |
| RTN-7K-20 [7K] | **Handoff margin.** Each path that hands content out of the database checks the guard, then hands off within its own timeout (7I CAG-10). The margin `T_ho,margin` must exceed the longest clock-read-to-handoff latency of that path; it is [MEAS] per path (T-7K-45). The residual race is 7I RR-12 and is not reduced by 7K. |
