| RTN-7K-21 [7K] | **A stopped pass is not an exposure.** If the retention pass is down, content past its deadline stays stored and **denied** by the guard (7I CAG-12). The pass cadence therefore bounds how long a row is denied-but-unsealed, not how long it is readable. |
| RTN-7K-22 [7K] | **Capacity pressure never opens a privacy path.** No tier, backlog or outage response skips a hold check, widens an exception, releases a seal, relaxes the guard or runs an erasure without its journal intent (INV-7K-17, INV-7K-18). |

### 19.5 Key service and rotation

Key-service behaviour under outage is §22.1. Rotation creates a new version for new records and keeps older versions decrypt-only (7I KEY-09); no bulk re-encryption is scheduled, so rotation adds no batch load. Verification-key rotation (7I STP-10) is part of every restore (7I JRN-13 step 7) and of the normal rotation calendar.

---

## 20. Regional Topology and Residency

### 20.1 Profiles and stack classes

| Profile (7A RES-01) | Where it runs | Cross-region copy |
|---|---|---|
| `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` | A class E stack in its contracted region, created only by governed provisioning (7I CRF-05) | None, of anything (OD-7K-01) |
| `STANDARD` | A class E or class S stack, as deployed | Class E: none. Class S: only as OD-7K-02 permits |
| `REGIONAL` | Not creatable in V1: 5B §26 marks it future and 7I CRF-06 can create only the `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` pair | Not designed here; a future governed decision |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-01 [FROZEN] | Region binding is read from platform state fixed at creation (7I CRF-01 … CRF-04). No request field, header, payload, stream key or configuration value supplied by a tenant selects a region or a stack. |
| REG-7K-02 [FROZEN] | No workflow in this document changes an organization's profile or region, moves an organization between stacks, or routes one organization's data through another stack. No recovery procedure contains such a step. Recovery kind F re-homes a whole class S stack; it writes no organization's region binding and moves no organization (REG-7K-24). |
| REG-7K-03 [OD-7K-01] | **Class E, every store in region:** PostgreSQL primary and every standby and replica; continuous archive, daily and weekly snapshots and their encryption keys; ET and HT Redis with their persistence volumes; object storage with every version; key service and secret manager; telemetry stack, logs and cold archives; the erasure journal and its key; the egress path and its proxy; the event-operations tooling (7A RES-02; 7I RSY-01 … RSY-14). |
| REG-7K-04 [OD-7K-01] | **Class E, what is disabled.** The cross-region elements of 3F are not provisioned for a class E stack: no second-region database replica, no cross-region object replication, no weekly snapshot copy to another region, no standby cluster elsewhere (3F §11.1, §11.3, §12.1). The weekly snapshot is replicated to a second failure domain **inside** the region (7I RSY-07). 7E RSD-04 is satisfied by construction: no ET copy exists anywhere else. This narrows 3F for class E and is CNF-7K-01. |
| REG-7K-05 [OD-7K-02] | **Class S, what may exist.** Where the stack's residency policy and the contracts of every organization it hosts authorize it, and only then: a provider-level replica of PostgreSQL, replication of object storage, and replicated key material, in one designated recovery region. The authorization is recorded in the stack's conformance record with the policy and contract references (REG-7K-17). Without that record the stack has no cross-region copy. The erasure-journal prefix and the journal's key belong to that copy only under §20.5. |
| REG-7K-06 [OD-7K-02; INV-7K-16] | **Never copied, in any class:** ET streams, PELs and persistence files; HT state; telemetry stores (7J RSD-7J-03). There is no global event bus and no Redis replica in another region. |

### 20.2 Backups and the journal

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-07 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Backup retention value** (7I RET-23 hands it to 7K). The frozen 3F schedule is kept: 7 days of continuous archive, 30 days of daily snapshots, 1 year of weekly snapshots. 7K does not lengthen it, because a longer horizon keeps erased data in backup media longer (7I RR-05) and lengthens the journal's retention. Shortening it is a governed change with a privacy and a recovery assessment. The journal horizon follows: completion plus one year plus 30 days. |
| REG-7K-08 [7K] | **Journal placement.** One journal per stack, in the stack's object storage, under its own prefix, credentials and key (7I JRN-01, RSY-13). **Class E: no replica of the prefix outside the region, under any decision of this document.** Class S: a promoted database in a recovery region cannot leave quarantine without the journal (DRS-7K-09), so a copy of the prefix there is what OD-7K-02 implies; it exists only under §20.5, as an asynchronous copy that is never authoritative (OD-7K-05). A journal that cannot be read or proved complete keeps the database quarantined in every class (7I JRN-16). |

### 20.3 Egress, provisioning, telemetry, operators

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-09 [7K] | **Egress path** (7H HE-7K-7H-06; 7I HE-7K-7I-04). All webhook and plugin traffic leaves through the stack's controlled egress path, in region whatever the destination (7I RSY-12). The component that opens the TCP connection to the destination is the one that resolves the name, validates every returned address, pins the validated address and applies the organization's egress policy for that request (7H §14.4; 7I EGR-05). If a forward proxy is used, that component is the proxy; the adapter then passes the hostname, not an address, and the proxy is inside the trust boundary of the adapter. A design in which validation happens before a hop that resolves again is not conformant. Connections to destinations are not pooled across organizations. |
| REG-7K-10 [7K] | **Enterprise provisioning conformance** (7I HE-7K-7I-12, -13). The conformance record of a class E stack lists, per item of REG-7K-03, the evidence that the store is in region and that REG-7K-04 holds, with the verification date and verifier. It is produced by an infrastructure verification run, re-verified on every topology change and at least quarterly, and is what the provisioning function reads (7I CRF-06). A failed or stale record blocks the creation of any `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization; it does not affect existing ones, and a drift found for a stack that already hosts one is a residency incident. Lead time and retry of a failed provisioning change are procedural (RB-7K-12): the change is all-or-nothing (7I CRF-14) and is simply run again. |
| REG-7K-11 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Telemetry** is one complete stack per region with no cross-region path (7J RSD-7J-02, -03). The dead-man signal of a stack is received by a receiver outside that stack's failure domain; for class E it is a content-free heartbeat (7J NTF tier), so that the loss of a region is detected from outside without any data leaving. |
| REG-7K-12 [FROZEN] | **Operator access.** Tooling runs in region. What reaches an operator outside the region is the redacted view; whether unmasked viewing from outside is a transfer is a contract item for 7L (7I RSY-09). 7K adds no operator authority and no break-glass path. |

### 20.4 Regional outage detection and control-plane behaviour

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-13 [7K] | **Detection** uses the external dead-man receiver and external synthetic checks. A stack never decides about itself that it is lost, and no other stack acts on such a belief. |
| REG-7K-14 [7K] | **No automatic regional action.** There is no automatic traffic switch, promotion or re-routing between regions in any class. Class E: the response to a regional outage is to wait for the region or restore inside it. Class S: DRS-7K-05. |
| REG-7K-15 [7K] | **Unavailable is not recovered.** A stack is *unavailable* while it cannot serve. It is *recovered* only when the completion criteria of §21.9 are proved. A stack that answers requests after a restore but before those criteria is quarantined, not recovered, and serves nothing (L0). |

### 20.5 The erasure journal across regions (class S only; FG-7K-04)

The first submission said the journal prefix and its key are replicated to the recovery region and did not reconcile that with 7I. The source-level position is this.

| Source | What it says | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| 7I JRN-01 | The journal lives "in the existing approved in-region object storage", with its own credentials, key and lifecycle | Every stack |
| 7I JRN-04 | The reference set is encrypted under "the per-region platform key"; the region is associated data of every record, so a record cannot be "moved between regions" | Every stack |
| 7I JRN-05 | "One monotonic, gap-free sequence per region" | Every stack |
| 7I JRN-18 | Only the journal writer creates objects; only the restore reconciler reads; no backup job holds a credential for the prefix | Every stack |
| 7I JRN-19 | Validation before activation includes "in-region placement (RSY-13)" | Every stack |
| 7I RSY-13 | "In region; one journal per region; no cross-region replica of the prefix; the restore reconciler runs in region" | Matrix 10, which is headed "For an `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization" |
| 7I KEY-05 | Root keys are per region and per environment | Every stack; its in-region-only sentence names `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` |
| OD-7K-02 (later, owner) | A copy is kept where authorized; condition 6 requires the frozen 7I quarantine and journal reconciliation before activation | Class S |
| OD-7K-05 (later, owner; option B) | The journal stays authoritative in its origin region under the frozen write-ahead protocol. The recovery copy is separately authorized and asynchronous. Recovery is permitted only on proof that the copy is complete against the origin journal; otherwise the recovery database stays quarantined, with no override | Class S |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-16 [7K; OD-7K-05] | **The conflict, the decision, and what each document can do.** For class E the prohibition is absolute and nothing below touches it. For class S, RSY-13's own scope is `INDIA_ENTERPRISE`, but JRN-01, JRN-04, JRN-05, JRN-18 and JRN-19 are unconditional and describe no second copy, no reader in another region, no journal key usable in another region and no sequence continued elsewhere. OD-7K-02 is a later owner decision that authorizes the copy as policy, and **OD-7K-05 (option B, approved) fixes what that copy is: an asynchronous recovery copy of a journal that stays authoritative in its origin region** (REG-7K-26). Neither decision rewrites frozen 7I, and 7K cannot (AUT-7K-01). **Resolved at the level of design policy:** OD-7K-05. **What 7K specifies:** REG-7K-17 … REG-7K-26, a design that satisfies every condition of both decisions and fails closed. **What needs a controlled 7L reconciliation before anything is activated:** CNF-7K-10 (a) … (f) (§31.1), with the wording it requires; frozen 7I stays as it is until then. **Until CNF-7K-10 is consolidated, implemented and validated: no journal prefix and no journal key is replicated to another region, a database promoted in another region cannot leave quarantine, and recovery kind F is not activatable.** The owner's approval of the policy is not an approval to activate it in production (AG-7K-08), and the path is described nowhere in this document as production-ready. |
| REG-7K-17 [OD-7K-02, condition 1] | **Authorization record.** In the stack's conformance record, before any copy exists: the residency policy reference; for every organization the stack hosts, the contract reference that permits a copy in the recovery region; the identities of REG-7K-18; the approver and date; and the statement that the stack is class S and has never hosted an `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization (TOP-7K-02). Admitting an organization whose contract does not permit the copy is refused while the copy exists. A missing or stale record means no copy. |
| REG-7K-18 [7K] | **Identities.** The origin region `R_o`, the single recovery region `R_r` and the stack identifier are fixed in the conformance record at provisioning. They are never chosen at incident time, and never read from a replicated object, a request or a tenant-supplied value (INV-7K-15). |
| REG-7K-19 [7K] | **Copied records stay records of the origin region.** The copy consists of byte-identical objects under the same names. A record is never re-encrypted, re-authenticated, re-sequenced or renamed for the recovery region. The reconciler in `R_r` authenticates each record exactly as 7I JRN-04 defines, with `R_o` as the region in the associated data, taking `R_o` from the conformance record. A record that authenticates only under another region value, or under no value, fails authentication and keeps the stack quarantined (7I JRN-16). |
| REG-7K-20 [7K; OD-7K-05] | **Completeness of the copy, proved against the authoritative origin journal.** In addition to 7I JRN-13 step 1, all of the conditions C-1 … C-5 below. `H_j` is the authenticated head sequence number of the recovery copy. `H_o` is the head of the authoritative origin journal, established after the origin writer is fenced. Cross-region recovery is permitted only if all five hold; OD-7K-05 makes that a condition of the owner and not only a design rule. |

| # | Condition | Detects |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | The chain is contiguous from the promoted database's `journal_applied_through` to `H_j`, and every record authenticates | A gap; an altered or foreign record |
| C-2 | No fork: among all versions of all objects there is one record per sequence number, and the chain hash at every `CHECKPOINT` matches the chain | A forked or rewritten history; a rolled-back object |
| C-3 | **Database high-water mark:** `H_j ≥ S_db`, where `S_db` is the highest journal sequence number recorded anywhere in the promoted database (step and batch confirmations of 7I JRN-06 step 3, hold and key records, `journal_applied_through`) | PostgreSQL replication ahead of journal replication |
| C-4 | **Origin head:** `H_j = H_o`, where `H_o` is read from the origin store itself as an authenticated latest record with a consistent listing, at or after the moment the origin journal writer was fenced (DRS-7K-05 (3)), so that no append can follow the reading. Nothing else establishes `H_o` (REG-7K-21) | A missing replication tail; a copy that stopped being replicated |
| C-5 | The journal's key and every key version the records name are available in `R_r` for verification and decryption (REG-7K-22) | A key that was never replicated, or was rotated after the last replication |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-21 [OD-7K-05] | **C-4 cannot be waived, replaced or overridden.** A record missing from the tail is an erasure, a hold or a key destruction that completed in `R_o` and that `R_r` does not know. Activating without it either serves erased data or lets a later pass erase held data. Neither direction can be decided mechanically, so there is no default. **Not a substitute for the reading of `H_o`:** a replication-lag estimate, or a lag figure reported by the provider, however small; a `CHECKPOINT` that was itself copied; the head of the copy or of any secondary store; a monitoring status; an assertion by an administrator. Each describes what arrived, and none shows that nothing later was written at the origin. **If the origin store is unavailable and `H_o` cannot be authoritatively established, completeness is unknown.** The outcomes U-1 … U-8 below then apply, all of them, for as long as it stays unknown. **No operator, application service or incident-response process may override this** (DRS-7K-09; 7I JRN-16). The incident authorization of DRS-7K-05 (2) is not such an override: it permits a promotion to be attempted and decides nothing about its completeness. **Accepted limitation (OD-7K-05).** With the frozen origin-region write-ahead protocol and an asynchronous copy, the permanent loss of `R_o` *including its authoritative journal* leaves kind F unable to complete. For V1 that is not a supported recoverable disaster: no recovery-point objective, no recovery-time objective and no regional-disaster guarantee is claimed for it (DRS-7K-01a; RR-7K-09). Once the path is activated the limitation stays, unless the owner approves a later change of policy. It limits recoverability and is never a reason to weaken erasure, tenant isolation, audit or journal completeness. |

| # | While completeness is unknown | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| U-1 | The recovery stack stays quarantined | 7I JRN-12 (the four layers), JRN-16; DRS-7K-08 |
| U-2 | Ordinary reads and writes are denied | 7I JRN-12 (a), (b): every guarded function refuses, and the pool begins no transaction |
| U-3 | Event consumption and replay are not resumed | RS-2 … RS-11: the event-plane workloads stay unadmitted; no supersession and no R4 starts (MRA-7K-14, class RM-0) |
| U-4 | Financial operations are not resumed | DRS-7K-17: no financial clearance exists for the incarnation |
| U-5 | Protected content is not read | 7I JRN-12; REG-7K-22: the key ring is usable by the restore reconciler only, for verification and decryption of journal records |
| U-6 | No alternative journal head is created | REG-7K-23: the sequence under `R_r` starts only from a proven `H_j`. With C-4 unproven no genesis record is written and no head is promoted, automatically or by hand |
| U-7 | Incident evidence is kept and the outcome is recorded as unresolved | The incident record of RB-7K-10: the authorization, the fencing evidence, the readings of `H_j` and `S_db`, and the failed or impossible reading of `H_o`. The stack is recorded as *quarantined, completeness unknown* |
| U-8 | No override | DRS-7K-09; 7I JRN-16. An incomplete or unverifiable copy is never classified as recovered (REG-7K-15) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| REG-7K-22 [7K] | **Keys.** Root keys stay per region (7I KEY-05). What reaches `R_r` is the origin's journal key and the key versions restored rows name, held in `R_r`'s key service in a ring that is separate from `R_r`'s own keys, usable for verification and decryption only, by the restore reconciler's identity in `R_r` only, and only while the incident authorization of DRS-7K-05 (2) is recorded. It is never usable to generate or encrypt. If the origin key service is unavailable and the ring lacks a key version a record needs, that record cannot be authenticated: C-5 fails and the stack stays quarantined. A restored row whose key version is absent is unreadable content and is reported, not repaired (DRS-7K-16). A version destroyed in `R_o` whose destruction record is in the copy is destroyed in the ring by 7I JRN-13 step 3. |
| REG-7K-23 [7K] | **Journaling after promotion.** The copy of the origin journal becomes read-only: no identity in `R_r` can create an object under it. The promoted stack writes a **new journal sequence under `R_r`'s identity**, so no origin sequence number is reused. Its first record is an authenticated record that names its predecessor: the origin region, `H_j`, and the chain hash at `H_j`. A later reconciliation of this stack authenticates the predecessor chain to `H_j` under `R_o`, then the new chain under `R_r`, and rejects any continuation that does not start from exactly that predecessor. The record type for this genesis link is part of CNF-7K-10; 7K names no type. |
| REG-7K-24 [7K] | **Readiness of the recovery region and tenant routing.** Before a promotion is authorized, `R_r` has, verified in its own conformance record: a key service and secret manager; object storage with the replicated prefixes; a **new, empty** event-transport cluster satisfying INV-7K-06 (DRS-7K-05 (6)) and a hot tier; the telemetry stack; the controlled egress path; and workloads carrying `R_r`'s region tag for database and transport (TOP-7K-03). Routing is switched for the stack as a whole by the recorded decision, in the control plane, never per organization and never from a client-supplied value. No organization's `data_residency_profile` or `region_ref` is written (7I RSY-14; REG-7K-02). The stack is re-homed; no organization is moved between stacks. That 7I's regional rules do not describe a re-homed class S stack is part of CNF-7K-10. |
| REG-7K-25 [7K] | **Return to the origin, and a further failover.** Never automatic. The fenced origin stack is never reactivated as it was: its database is an abandoned incarnation, and its journal writer and workloads stay without credentials. Returning to `R_o` is a second kind F in the opposite direction, under its own authorization: a new database incarnation in `R_o` built from `R_r`'s data, quarantined, reconciled against the lineage of REG-7K-23 (origin chain to `H_j`, then the `R_r` chain), with a new empty transport and the money gate closed. If the origin journal is found to hold records after `H_j`, C-4 was false when the promotion was activated: that is a privacy incident, the affected rows are treated by the journal's records before any return, and the incident is reported, not absorbed. A second failover from `R_r` to a third region is not designed: one recovery region is designated (REG-7K-05). |
| REG-7K-26 [OD-7K-05] | **The origin protocol is unchanged.** For an eligible class S stack the erasure journal is authoritative in `R_o`, and a record is durable exactly when frozen 7I says so: JRN-06, JRN-07, JRN-09, JRN-20 and JRN-29 apply as written. The recovery copy is replicated asynchronously after the origin write and takes no part in that protocol. Consequently: an outage of `R_r` or of the replication channel does not by itself delay an otherwise valid erasure, sealing, legal-hold or key-destruction operation in `R_o`; replication lag relaxes no erasure deadline and enters no deadline computation (7I JRN-29); and the copy is never read to decide anything in `R_o`. **Not introduced, by this document or by the decision:** a dual-region synchronous journal acknowledgement; durability in the recovery region as a prerequisite of completing an erasure; a third-region witness; a consensus service; an event broker; an additional or global journal; automatic promotion of a journal head; a recovery bypass for an operator; automatic rollback of an erasure. **Class E is a separate limitation and is not affected:** a class E stack has no cross-region copy of anything (OD-7K-01; REG-7K-03, -04, -08), it never falls back to class S, no residency profile is downgraded and no contractual region is reinterpreted (REG-7K-01, -02; TOP-7K-02). Class E is limited because nothing leaves the region; class S is limited because a copy that left asynchronously cannot prove itself complete. |

**Counterexamples** (regression cases T-7K-40 (c) … (g), §26.1).

| Case | First submission | Now |
|---|---|---|
| PostgreSQL replication is ahead of journal replication: the promoted database holds a step confirmed at sequence *n*; the copy ends at *n* − 3 | The chain is contiguous to its own head and agrees with its own latest checkpoint; reconciliation could pass | C-3 fails (`H_j < S_db`). Quarantine persists |
| The copy lacks the last records of the origin; the database copy does not reference them either | Not detectable from the copy; reconciliation could pass and erased data could be served | C-4 fails when `H_o` is read, or cannot be evaluated when the origin store is unreachable. In both cases quarantine persists |
| The copy holds a different record at a sequence number than the origin wrote | Possibly detected by authentication only | C-2 fails, and the chain hash breaks at that record (7I JRN-13 step 1). Quarantine persists |
| The origin region and its journal store are permanently lost; the copy looks intact and the provider reports zero lag | Not addressed | Completeness is unknown (REG-7K-21). U-1 … U-8 apply; the stack stays quarantined and is not promoted. Not a supported recoverable disaster (OD-7K-05) |
| The recovery region is unreachable while erasures are due in the origin | Not addressed | The origin completes them under the frozen protocol and waits for nothing (REG-7K-26) |

---

## 21. Disaster Restoration and Reconciliation

### 21.1 What is claimed, and what is not

| Failure | Frozen target (3F §10.1, §12.3) | Mechanism it rests on | 7K statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pod or node | Immediate; under 2 minutes | Stateless workloads; rescheduling | Unchanged |
| Availability zone | Under 5 minutes; no data loss | Zone spread | Holds for stateless roles. **For durable event publication it does not hold as stated:** a zone loss pauses publication for `T_recap`, and consumption on the affected shards (TOP-7K-07, RED-7K-29). No committed data is lost |
| Database corruption | Under 4 hours; under 15 minutes of data | Point-in-time recovery | A target. The recovery time now also contains journal reconciliation, the money gate and, for a lossy restore, transport supersession (DRS-7K-02) |
| Region, class S, partial failure: the origin journal readable and provably complete | Under 30 minutes; under 5 minutes of data | Cross-region replica, manual switch | A target, **unvalidated**, reachable only where REG-7K-05 is authorized, **and not activatable today**: the journal copy it needs is not expressible in frozen 7I (REG-7K-16, CNF-7K-10). It completes only on the proof of REG-7K-20. Promotion is by decision (OD-7K-02) |
| Region, class S, origin region lost together with its authoritative journal | The 3F figures do not apply | None | **Not a supported recoverable disaster in V1. No recovery-point objective, no recovery-time objective and no guarantee is claimed** (OD-7K-05; DRS-7K-01a) |
| Region, class E | — | None exists | **No recovery-point or recovery-time objective is claimed** (OD-7K-01) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-01 [OD-7K-01] | **Class E, stated plainly.** Every copy of a class E stack's data is inside its contracted region. If the region is unavailable, the service is unavailable until the region returns or is restored from in-region backups. **If the region is permanently destroyed, including the failure domain that holds its backups, the data is unrecoverable.** The architecture offers no mechanism against this and makes no claim about it. Changing this requires a separate owner-approved residency decision and contractual amendment. It is residual risk RR-7K-03. |
| DRS-7K-01a [OD-7K-05] | **Class S, stated plainly.** The cross-region copy of a class S stack can assist recovery from a **partial** regional failure, in which the authoritative origin journal stays readable and its head can be established after fencing, so that the copy is proved complete (REG-7K-20). **Complete loss of the origin region, including permanent loss of its authoritative journal, is not a supported recoverable disaster in V1.** The recovery-region database then stays quarantined and is not promoted into service (REG-7K-21). No recovery-point objective, no recovery-time objective and no regional-disaster guarantee is claimed for that scenario, by this document or through any figure taken from 3F. An incomplete or unverifiable copy is never classified as recovered. This is residual risk RR-7K-09, accepted by the owner. It is separate from the class E limitation of DRS-7K-01, which has a different cause. |
| DRS-7K-02 [7K] | **Recovery time is a sum, not a number.** `RTO = T_detect + T_decide + T_fence + T_restore + T_journal + T_money + T_transport + T_verify + T_reopen`. `T_journal` grows with the erasures since the recovery point (7I JRN-28) and is bounded by the checkpoint interval (DRS-7K-24). `T_money` is zero for a proven no-loss promotion and otherwise depends on the provider reconciliation (§21.8); it does not delay non-financial service. No value is stated until a drill measures each term (T-7K-35, T-7K-40). The 3F targets are neither confirmed nor withdrawn by 7K: they are unvalidated. |
| DRS-7K-03 [7K] | **Data-loss statement.** Restoring PostgreSQL to a point `P` loses every transaction committed after `P`. The frozen targets bound `P`'s distance from the failure only if the underlying replication or archive actually achieved it at the time; the achieved point is read and recorded at each restore (DRS-7K-06), never assumed. |

### 21.2 Kinds of recovery

They are different procedures and are never substituted for one another.

| Kind | Trigger | Automatic | Data loss | Procedure |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. In-region failover of an ET shard | Primary failure | Yes | None of confirmed entries (7E FMD-06) | §9 |
| B. Same-instance restart of PostgreSQL | Crash, restart | Yes | None | OBX-7K-05 |
| C. In-region database promotion | Primary lost; standby in another zone | By the managed service | **Unknown until proved** (DRS-7K-04) | §21.3 – §21.9 |
| D. In-region restore from backup | Corruption; no usable standby | No | After the recovery point | §21.3 – §21.9 |
| E. Disaster replay of events | Transport state lost, PostgreSQL intact | No | None of obligations | §11.4 |
| F. Cross-region business recovery | Partial regional failure, class S, authorized; the origin journal readable and proved complete (OD-7K-05) | **No** | After the replica's last applied position | §21.3 – §21.9, with DRS-7K-05 |
| — | Origin region lost with its authoritative journal, class S | — | Not recoverable from the copy: the recovery stack stays quarantined | DRS-7K-01a; REG-7K-21 |
| — | Region lost, class E | — | Possibly everything | DRS-7K-01 |

### 21.3 Classifying a database event

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-04 [7K] | **Lossless or lossy.** Every promotion, restore and point-in-time recovery produces a new incarnation and quarantine (7I JRN-12). It is then classified. **Lossless** only if a recorded proof exists that no transaction acknowledged as committed by the old primary is absent from the new one: the service's synchronous-commit guarantee, validated for this deployment (ASM-7K-02), together with the positions read at the event. **Lossy** in every other case: asynchronous standby, any restore from backup, any point-in-time recovery, any cross-region promotion, and any promotion whose proof cannot be produced because the old primary is unreadable. The default is lossy. |
| DRS-7K-05 [OD-7K-02] | **Cross-region promotion, conditions.** All are required, in order, and each is recorded: (0) CNF-7K-10 is consolidated and implemented and the recovery region is ready (REG-7K-16, REG-7K-24); (1) the stack is class S with REG-7K-05 authorized by the record of REG-7K-17; (2) an explicit incident authorization by the platform owner for this incident; no standing or automatic authorization exists; (3) the old primary, the old region's workloads and its journal writer are fenced: their service endpoints are withdrawn and their credentials are revoked, so that a region that returns cannot serve, write or append to its journal; (4) the replica's replication lag and last applied position are read and recorded as the recovery point; (5) the promoted database stays quarantined until the journal reconciliation of 7I JRN-13 completes against the copied journal, including the completeness conditions C-1 … C-5 of REG-7K-20; where completeness cannot be established the promotion is not completed, and neither the authorization of (2) nor any later decision substitutes for the proof (REG-7K-21; OD-7K-05); (6) the event transport in the recovery region is a new, empty cluster; (7) the event is classified lossy. Replicated data is never served because it is present; it is served only after §21.9. |

### 21.4 Restore-point selection

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-06 [7K] | The recovery point `P` is the latest point that is consistent and precedes the damage. For corruption it precedes the first corrupting transaction. `P`, the failure time `T_f`, the source (standby, continuous archive, snapshot) and the resulting lost interval `(P, T_f]` are recorded before restoration starts and are inputs to every later step. |
| DRS-7K-07 [FROZEN] | A backup whose recovery point is older than the start of the journal's guaranteed coverage is not eligible to be restored into service (7I JRN-13 step 2, JRN-17). |

### 21.5 Sequence

Event-plane workloads are scaled to zero, or held unadmitted, from RS-2 to RS-11. The order is fixed (RPR-7K-06).

| Step | Action | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| RS-1 | Declare the incident; choose the kind; obtain the authorization the kind needs | DRS-7K-05 |
| RS-2 | **Fence.** Stop the event-plane workloads. Terminate every session on the instance being recovered and keep it unreachable under the service endpoint (7I JRN-12 c). Fence the old primary | DRS-7K-08 |
| RS-3 | Record `P`, `T_f` and the classification | DRS-7K-04, -06 |
| RS-4 | Restore or promote. The new incarnation is quarantined by default | 7I JRN-12 |
| RS-5 | **Before activation, fence the transport** (lossy only): every reader and every publisher of every generation that existed before the event, on every ET node, in the three layers of SUP-7K-03. RS-7 is not started while the fence is incomplete on any node | DRS-7K-10; SUP-7K-03 |
| RS-6 | **Before activation, invalidate derived hot-tier state** (lossy only): every HT key space that caches an outcome or a decision derived from PostgreSQL | DRS-7K-15 |
| RS-7 | Journal reconciliation, 7I JRN-13 steps 1 – 7, including the time-based passes, verification, key rotation and the activation record. Once CNF-7K-09 is consolidated, the reconciler also writes here, in quarantine, the recovery privacy clearances of MRA-7K-03. The money gate is closed at activation by construction | 7I JRN-13; DRS-7K-17; MRA-7K-03 |
| RS-8 | **Transport supersession** (lossy only): the operation of §21.6.1, with material handled only under §21.6.2 | DRS-7K-10 … -13; SUP-7K-01 … -10; MRA-7K-01 … -17 |
| RS-9 | Consistency verification | §21.7 |
| RS-10 | Billing reconciliation for the lost interval; gate clearance (independent of RS-11 for non-financial service) | §21.8 |
| RS-11 | Reopen workloads in order: provisioning checks; trim; gateway; consumers; relay; dispatcher and sweepers; reconcilers and the Voice recovery workers; scheduler jobs. Nothing is reopened before MRA-7K-13 holds; in particular outbox cleanup and the accounting reconciler are not admitted before the restore's R4 holds its captured sources (MRA-7K-15 (3)) | WKR-7K-03; MRA-7K-13 |
| RS-12 | Prove completion | §21.9 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-08 [FROZEN + 7K] | **The quarantine does not depend on anyone remembering it.** It is the four layers of 7I JRN-12. 7K sets the cache bound of the per-checkout incarnation check, `T_inc_cache` ≤ 5 s [CAND], and requires that the application's pool refuses to begin a transaction on a mismatch. RS-2 is an additional layer, not the control. |
| DRS-7K-09 [7K] | **Dependencies of leaving quarantine:** the journal store readable and complete; the key service holding the journal's key and every key version the restored rows name; the authorization issuer for the reconciler's activation. If any is missing the stack stays quarantined. There is no override in V1 (7I JRN-16); for the cross-region journal copy OD-7K-05 makes that binding on every operator, application service and incident-response process (REG-7K-21). |

### 21.6 Transport state after a lossy database event

After a lossy event the transport can be **ahead** of the database: it can hold an entry whose producing transaction is in the lost interval. That entry describes a fact that no longer exists in the authoritative store. If a consumer applied it, a usage row, a CRM activity or a webhook delivery would be created for something the platform has no record of.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-10 [7K] | **Pre-event generations are superseded.** After a lossy event no entry of any pre-event generation is ever read by a consumer again. The fence of RS-5 makes that true before the database can be activated: the gateway cannot read those keys and no publisher can write them. |
| DRS-7K-11 [7K] | **Obligations are rebuilt from the restored database, which is the authority** (INV-7K-02), and pre-event content is re-emitted or materialized only from a restored PostgreSQL row and only where §21.6.2 admits it. **One matrix decides every (event, group) pair: MRA-7K-14** (third remediation; FG-7K-R3-02). The branches below summarize it and add nothing to it. (a) Rows `PENDING` or `CLAIMED`: published to the new generation by the relay as usual, except those that 7I JRN-15 (b) tombstoned in quarantine, which are not published (class RM-2). (b) Rows `PUBLISHED` whose verdict is `CLEAR`, with an owed group that has no accounting row, **whether or not that group already has a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`**: one bounded R4 republishes the row's captured source to the new generation (7G §32; MRA-7K-08). This route creates no case and changes none, so it has no precondition that a case be absent; an existing case continues under its own state when the new entry is delivered (7G RTY-02) (class RM-3). A plan that stops leaves the row's source and the pair's class as they are; what follows when no publication comes is decided by the deadlines of MRA-7K-16 and the table of MRA-7K-17, never by a plan's state. (c) Recovery cases with material: continue unchanged, after the treatments 7I JRN-25 applied to them in quarantine (class RM-1). (d) Recovery cases in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`, which hold no material: **the entry on the superseded key is never read** (MRA-7K-07), and **no second case is ever opened for the identity** (7G PID-01). Where the event's verdict is `CLEAR` and its row is captured, the case is served by (b). Where no admissible source exists, it stays the one case of its identity and goes to its owning domain (class RM-4). (e) Deliveries, receipts and steps: by 7I JRN-24 … JRN-30. **No branch treats the existence of a case, the presence of bytes in Redis or the presence of a restored row as proof that the content may lawfully be stored again.** |
| DRS-7K-12 [7K] | **New generation.** A new topology generation is provisioned on empty keys with the same partition counts: keys, groups at `0`, registration records, per-key intervals, gateway and probe registries (GM-3 … GM-6). Relays are enabled for it only, and only after the supersession operation is complete (SUP-7K-06, S9). The superseded keys are destroyed by that operation and by nothing else. |
| DRS-7K-13 [7K] | **Why nothing owed depends on the destroyed transport.** Take any entry `e` on a superseded key. (i) The restored database has its outbox row: the obligation is rebuilt under DRS-7K-11 (a) or (b), or, where the row is barred from re-emission, it is preserved as a named owner reconciliation under MRA-7K-10. (ii) It has no row but has a case for the group: DRS-7K-11 (c) or (d), that is class RM-1 or RM-4 of MRA-7K-14. (iii) It has neither. Then either the row had been cleaned up before `P`, which 7I ACC-06 permits only when every owed group had an accounting row or a case in the database as of `P`, both of which were restored; or the row was created after `P`, and the fact it describes is not in the authoritative store. In no branch does an authoritative obligation depend on the destroyed entry. In branches (i) and (ii) an obligation whose content may not be stored again is not completed and not dropped: it stays a visible, unresolved case or reconciliation item for its owning domain (7G DAU-02, PRT-02). The loss in branch (iii, second case) is the loss of the lost interval itself (DRS-7K-03); it is not caused by the supersession. |
| — | **Relation to frozen text.** 7E LCY-07 step 4 retires a generation after a drain, and 7G TPG-05 requires a disposition for every pending entry of a retired key. Both describe the governed migration of a healthy system, which §9.8 now satisfies in full (GEN-7K-09). A lossy database event is a fault outside the 7E model (7E FMD-02 hands such faults to 7G and 7K), and draining would mean consuming entries with no authoritative origin. The supersession is therefore a separate disaster operation with its own proof (GEN-7K-12); its relation to those two rules is CNF-7K-04, and the authority it needs is CNF-7K-08. |

#### 21.6.1 The supersession operation (FG-7K-06)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SUP-7K-01 [7K] | **Identity and record.** One operation per lossy database event, of kind *transport supersession*, with: a unique operation identifier; the incident reference; the restore classification record (DRS-7K-04) with `P` and `T_f`; the database incarnation that was lost and the incarnation now running; every pre-event topology generation with its keys; the recovery generation; the plan fingerprint (7I OPA-08). The fence of S1 happens while the database is quarantined, when no ledger function runs (7I JRN-12 a); its evidence is recorded with the incident outside the database and is copied into the operation record at S0. From S0 on, every step writes the operation record and an audit row in the transaction of the ledger change it describes (7G AUT-05). |
| SUP-7K-02 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Authorization.** The operation destroys consumer groups and is the precondition of an R4, and it touches the high-risk groups (CON-06, CON-10). Each of those is under hard dual control in 7I OPA-07, so the approver is a different human from the requester, on the plan fingerprint, with the re-validation of 7I OPA-10 at request, approval, start and **before each destructive step**. It needs `recovery.group.operate` and, for the R4 it enables, `recovery.disaster-replay` with the incident reference (7G DSR-09). For recovery kind F it additionally needs the incident authorization of DRS-7K-05 (2). 7G §42 and the 7I capability matrix define no operation kind for it: that is part of CNF-7K-08, and until it is consolidated the operation cannot be approved. |
| SUP-7K-03 [7K] | **Fence (S1), before activation.** Complete only when all three layers are verified on **every** node, replicas included. (a) *Workloads:* the deployment platform attests zero running instances of the relay, the repair publisher, the gateway, the trim worker, the obligation reconciler and the tooling's Redis-using components. (b) *Credentials:* for the relay-publisher, repair-publisher, gateway, trim, reconciler and health-probe ACL users, the passwords are removed and their connections closed, which is the containment action 7I RDS-13 already defines. (c) *ACL revision:* a governed revision, applied from the governed ACL source with its digest verified on every node (7I RDS-15), in which the key patterns of those users name the recovery generation only. New credentials are issued under that revision and not before S9. **Proof that no old publisher can append:** (a), (b) and (c), then, for each old key, the group positions (`XINFO GROUPS`: last delivered ID, entries read, lag) read twice, at least `T_publish` plus a margin apart, and equal both times; an append between the readings raises the lag of every group. Where Redis reports the lag as undetermined, or the key has no group, the proof rests on (a), (b) and (c) and the record says so. No command that returns an entry's fields is used for this (SUP-7K-08). **A fence on part of the cluster is not a fence:** a node with the old digest takes no traffic (7I RDS-15), RS-7 is not started, and nothing reopens. |
| SUP-7K-04 [7K] | **Inventories (S2, S3).** *Transport, read-only, identifiers only:* per old key, whether it exists; per group on it, the last delivered ID, entries read, lag, and the complete list of pending IDs with consumer, idle time and delivery count (`XINFO GROUPS`, `XPENDING`). *Authoritative, from PostgreSQL, per group:* outbox rows not `PUBLISHED`; unaccounted (row, group) pairs (7I ACC-03); recovery cases by state, with or without material; registration records. The transport inventory decides nothing by itself (FCL-7K-01). It tells the operation which pending entries need a disposition proof. |
| SUP-7K-05 [7K] | **Disposition proof for every pending entry (S4).** Each pending (key, group, entry ID) is mapped to an event through the restored position records (7I ACC-13) or a case's transport observation, and is recorded in exactly one class of the table below. The classes are tested in the order D-6, D-2, D-4, D-1, D-5, so an entry whose group has a case is classed by that case and never by the row alone. The route recorded with a class is the class of MRA-7K-14 for that (event, group) pair. A key is not destroyed while one of its pending entries has no recorded class. |

| Class | Condition | Preservation evidence | Entry bytes read |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Maps to a restored outbox row, and the group has no case for the event | The row, and its route: class RM-2, RM-3 or RM-5 of MRA-7K-14 | No |
| D-2 | Maps to a case that holds material | The case and its material (7G RPM-01); class RM-1 | No |
| D-3 | *Withdrawn in the second remediation.* It was the class in which an entry was read and captured. No class reads an entry | — | — |
| D-4 | Maps to a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material | The durable non-capture record of MRA-7K-10, naming the case, the event's verdict and the class by which its obligation continues: RM-2 or RM-3 where an admissible source exists, RM-4 where none does. The record opens no case | **Never** |
| D-5 | Maps to nothing in the restored database | The record that no authoritative origin exists (DRS-7K-13 (iii)); identifiers only | Never |
| D-6 | The pending ID's entry is missing | The `TRANSPORT_INTEGRITY` case of 7G DGL-02 | Not possible |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SUP-7K-06 [7K] | **Steps and restart.** Each step is entered only from the recorded completion of the one before, and each is safe to repeat. |

| Step | Action | Durable state after it | A restart finds, and does |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 | Open and approve the operation; import the S1 evidence | Operation `APPROVED`, then `RUNNING` | An unapproved plan: nothing has happened |
| S1 | Fence (SUP-7K-03); done before activation | Evidence with the incident | Verifies every node again; a partial fence is completed or the restore does not proceed |
| S2 | Transport inventory | Inventory in the operation record | Reads again; the keys are fenced, so the result is the same or the key is gone (S8) |
| S3 | Authoritative inventory | Inventory in the operation record | Recomputes from PostgreSQL |
| S4 | Disposition class per pending entry | One record per entry | Entries without a record are classified; recorded ones are kept |
| S5 | *Withdrawn in the second remediation.* No entry is captured (MRA-7K-07) | — | Nothing to do |
| S6 | Reconcile cases and consumer state: every obligation of S3 has its recorded route, which is its class of MRA-7K-14; for every pair of class RM-3 the route is recorded only when the restore's R4 operation holds the row's captured source, or the capture was refused and the pair is re-classed (MRA-7K-15); registrations of old keys closed with the operation reference so that every position recorded on the key stays covered (7I ACC-15; CNF-7K-08); gateway and probe registries without the old keys | Operation record | Re-verifies |
| S7 | Create the recovery generation (GM-3 … GM-6) on empty keys | Keys, groups at `0`, registrations, intervals | Group creation is idempotent (7E BST-06); both generations present, old ones fenced, new one unwritten |
| S8 | Destroy, per old key: re-validate the approval; check that every pending entry has its class and that no non-terminal operation names the key; `XGROUP DESTROY` each group; delete the key (SUP-7K-08) | Per key: `PROVEN → GROUPS_DESTROYED → DELETED`, recorded after each command | Resumes from the recorded state of each key; a key whose groups are gone and which still exists is deleted; a key already absent is recorded `DELETED` |
| S9 | Completion record (SUP-7K-10); credentials issued under the new revision; relays enabled for the recovery generation only | Operation `COMPLETED` | A second completion returns the stored record |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SUP-7K-07 [7K] | **Independence.** Every obligation in the S3 inventory is rebuilt, held or handed over from PostgreSQL state alone (DRS-7K-13). The operation proves it by re-running S3 after S8: the two inventories are equal in pairs and in case identities, and no identity has gained a second case. A case state differs between them only where a recorded entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) moved it. Nothing is marked completed, resolved, accounted or acknowledged by the supersession. |
| SUP-7K-08 [7K] | **Authority of each Redis command.** |

| Command | Used at | Issued by | Frozen basis | Available today |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Removing passwords, closing connections, applying an ACL revision | S1 | The governed ACL source | 7I RDS-13, RDS-15 | The mechanism is 7I IR-17, not built. Whether its use inside a recovery procedure needs wording against 7I RDS-11 is CNF-7K-08 |
| `XINFO GROUPS`, `XPENDING` | S1 proof, S2 | Recovery-operator user, retrievable only while the approved operation is `RUNNING` | 7I matrix 6; OPA-11; RDS-17 (both are payload-free) | By design; not built |
| `XRANGE`, `XINFO STREAM`, `XREADGROUP`, `XAUTOCLAIM`, `XCLAIM` without `JUSTID`: every command whose reply carries an entry's fields | — | **Never issued on a superseded key, by any identity** | MRA-7K-07; 7I RDS-17 | Prohibited |
| `XGROUP CREATE` | S7 | Provisioning user | 7I matrix 6 | By design; not built |
| `XGROUP DESTROY` | S8 | Recovery-operator user | 7I matrix 6; 7G AUT-06 | By design; not built |
| Key deletion | S8 | **Nobody** | 7I RDS-10 and matrix 6 deny it; RDS-11 forbids break-glass for it | **No.** GEN-7K-11; CNF-7K-08 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SUP-7K-09 [7K] | **Consequence of the missing authority.** Until CNF-7K-08 is consolidated and implemented, S8 cannot delete a key. The operation then stops in `RUNNING` with every key at `GROUPS_DESTROYED` or earlier. The keys stay fenced and the event plane stays closed, because credentials are issued only at S9: no consumer reads the old keys, no publisher writes them, and DRS-7K-10 holds. The restore is not complete (§21.9, criterion 2), and the residue of §19.2 persists on those keys. 7K does not give the recovery operator a wider privilege and does not use break-glass to finish. A stack is not admitted to production without the authority (AG-7K-08). |
| SUP-7K-10 [7K] | **No replay from a destroyed key; completion.** No R3 plan may name a superseded key; any operation that names one is terminal or cancelled before S8 (7G TPG-05). A generation number is never reused, so a superseded key name never exists again. The completion record is unique per operation and holds: per key, the group positions read at S2 and the counts of each class D-1 … D-6; every non-capture record; every case left for owner reconciliation; the approvals and re-validations; the S3 inventory before and after. It holds no payload. |

#### 21.6.2 Admission of recovered material (FG-7K-01)

The first submission captured the bytes of a superseded entry into a recovery case once the journal had been reconciled. It did not show that those bytes were still lawful to store. They need not be: 7I leaves a published entry in its stream when its subject is erased (7I ERA-S2) and takes no action on a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`, because no material exists yet (7I ERA-S3). After a restore such a case is unchanged by the journal, and the entry still holds the erased content.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-01 [7K] | **Candidates.** A *recovery candidate* is any pre-event content that a recovery step could store or publish again. (K) The bytes of an entry on a superseded key, for a case without material. **These are never read, so they are never stored** (MRA-7K-07); the candidate exists only to give the case its verdict and its route. (R) A restored `PUBLISHED` outbox row, which the two frozen readers of such a row could capture: the governed R4 capture and the capture for an unaccounted obligation (7I RET-01, ACC-05; 7G DSR-12). Restored `PENDING` and `CLAIMED` rows are not 7K candidates: 7I JRN-15 (b) decides them in quarantine and 7K adds nothing. |
| MRA-7K-02 [7K] | **Who can know.** Whether a candidate's content was erased, sealed or made undecidable after the recovery point is recorded in the journal. Only the restore reconciler can read the journal, and only in quarantine (7I JRN-04, JRN-18, JRN-27). 7K gives no other actor a journal credential, a decryption right or an override. Eligibility therefore cannot be decided by the supersession operation; it can only be **received** from the reconciler. |
| MRA-7K-03 [7K] | **Recovery privacy clearance.** A durable record, written in quarantine by a restore-only function of the reconciler: one for **every** restored `PUBLISHED` outbox row and one for every recovery case restored in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material. It holds the candidate's identity, its attribution reference (7I HLD-17), the incarnation being activated, the journal head proven in 7I JRN-13 step 1, and a verdict: `CLEAR`, or `BARRED` with one reason of MRA-7K-04. Identifiers only. **One verdict per event:** the verdict is a property of the event's content and attribution, so the reconciler computes it once and writes the same verdict to the row's record and to the record of every such case of that event; where both exist they are equal, and the assertion before activation includes that. Before activation the reconciler asserts that the number of row clearances equals the number of restored `PUBLISHED` rows; a difference keeps the stack quarantined. A `PUBLISHED` row that has no clearance record at all was therefore published after activation and is outside this section. **Frozen 7I defines no such record.** It is required wording of CNF-7K-09. Until it exists no candidate has a clearance, and MRA-7K-06 applies to all of them. |
| MRA-7K-04 [7K] | **Conditions of `CLEAR`.** The reconciler evaluates the restored database for the time up to `P` and the journal for the time after it. `CLEAR` requires that no row of the table below applies. Each row keeps the treatment frozen 7I gives the situation; 7K invents none. |

| # | Situation | Evidence | Verdict | Frozen treatment that is preserved |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Subject erasure, completed or pending, of a subject the attribution reference reaches by a current or a historical path of the subject index; the type is PII-bearing (7I CLS-03) or of unknown content (CLS-04) | Erasure steps up to `P`; `INTENT` and `COMPLETED` records after `P`; subject index | `BARRED`, `SUBJECT_ERASURE` | 7I JRN-25, first principle; ERA-08; JRN-15 (b). An identifier-only type is not barred by this row (7I ERA-S5) |
| 2 | Organization erasure of the candidate's organization | Journal records; key-destruction records | `BARRED`, `ORGANIZATION_ERASURE` | 7I ERA-17 |
| 3 | A journaled time-based erasure names the case or the row (material cap, post-resolution erasure, `FAILED` payload tombstone) | `TIMED_INTENT`, `TIMED_COMPLETED` | `BARRED`, `TIMED_ERASURE` | 7I JRN-24; JRN-25 rows 6, 7, 10 |
| 4 | The row or case is sealed | Seal intent or completion in the journal; seal record | `BARRED`, `SEALED` | 7I SEAL-02, SEAL-07: the seal is re-applied; an ordinary store holds no content |
| 5 | A legal hold reaches the candidate | Hold records, applied in sequence order (7I JRN-10) | Alone: `CLEAR`. Together with any of rows 1 – 4, 6 or 7: `BARRED`, `HELD_RESTRICTED`, with the other reason recorded | The hold preserves and does not stop ordinary use before the deadline (7I CAG-04, SEAL-14). A hold never turns into a deletion (7I HLD-03) |
| 6 | Attribution cannot be resolved: no reference, an unknown aggregate type, an unverified relation, an unusable key version, or no index entry for the reference | Subject index; 7I HLD-11 (c) | `BARRED`, `ATTRIBUTION_UNPROVEN` | 7I HLD-18: such content is never classified as unrelated by default |
| 7 | The candidate's content deadline has passed, or cannot be computed | The deadline function of 7I CAG-02 | `BARRED`, `DEADLINE_PASSED` | 7I CAG-01, CAG-09, CAG-13: denied from the first statement; unknown is not eligible. A `PUBLISHED` row and a case without material have no deadline of their own (7I CAG-02), so for 7K candidates this row arises only as an unknown deadline, which is also an integrity alarm (7I CAG-15) |
| 8 | The restored row is in an earlier lifecycle state than its journaled evidence | 7I JRN-24 (iv) | Decided after the 7I JRN-25 treatment | 7I JRN-25 |
| 9 | Journal records are missing or contradict the restored row | 7I JRN-13 step 1; JRN-24 (v) | **None is written** | 7I JRN-16: the stack stays quarantined and the supersession never starts |
| 10 | The journal copy cannot be proved complete (recovery kind F) | REG-7K-20 | None is written | 7I JRN-16 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-05 [7K] | **Bytes that outlive their lawful content.** No superseded entry is read by any recovery step, whatever its verdict. It leaves Redis when its key is destroyed (S8), and no copy of it is made first. Until then the key is fenced and the residue is that of §19.2. |
| MRA-7K-05a [7K] | **Platform-scoped candidates.** A candidate with no organization has no hold gate and no subject. It is `CLEAR` only if its type is identifier-only; its capture takes the row lock and makes the current-timeline checks of MRA-7K-08 that do not concern an organization. |
| MRA-7K-06 [7K] | **Default deny.** A restored row whose clearance is `BARRED`, names another incarnation, is consumed or revoked, or does not match the row's identity and payload digest, is not captured and not republished. A consumed clearance means the row was captured once under MRA-7K-08: its stored source, and never the row again, is what the restore's R4 publishes (MRA-7K-15). No case receives bytes from a stream. While CNF-7K-09 is not in force no clearance exists and the stack is not reopened (MRA-7K-10). |
| MRA-7K-07 [7K] | **Reading a superseded entry is prohibited** (second remediation; FG-7K-R2-01). The first remediation checked the clearance, then read the entry with `XRANGE`, then re-checked eligibility in a PostgreSQL transaction. The re-check could stop the bytes from being stored. It could not undo the read. **Read-time eligibility of a Redis entry cannot be established within the frozen contracts,** for four independent reasons. (1) *No lock can span the read.* Eligibility can change through an erasure, a seal, an organization erasure or a passed deadline, each of which serializes on the hold gate or a row lock in PostgreSQL. To make a Redis read atomic with those, the gate or a transaction would have to be held across the Redis call. 7I HLD-10 holds the gate "only for the length of one short transaction" and never "across a wait for a person or another process", and 7E ROLE-06 allows no PostgreSQL transaction to be open during Redis I/O. A timeout on the call bounds the wait and does not remove it. (2) *No common clock.* Eligibility is decided on the PostgreSQL primary's clock, read inside the deciding function (7I CAG-03). The instant at which Redis executes a read is on Redis's clock, and no frozen rule orders the two (RPR-7K-07). A check that ends "before" the read is before it only by an assumption about network delay. (3) *No guarded reader exists.* Every function that returns or copies content is in the governed inventory of 7I CAG-05, and the copy it makes is written in the transaction that evaluated the guard, under the row lock (7I CAG-10 (i)). A stream entry is outside the guard (7I CAG-17 (b)), and the inventory contains no read of a stream entry by a recovery executor. Adding one would be a new content path that 7I does not authorize. (4) *A handoff margin does not help.* 7I CAG-10 (iii) bounds a transfer of content that was eligible when it was fetched under the guard. Here nothing was fetched under the guard. **Therefore:** the supersession issues no command whose reply carries an entry's fields (SUP-7K-08); class D-3 and step S5 are withdrawn; a case without material is never given bytes from a stream. This creates no privilege and bypasses no access rule: it removes a read. What it costs is stated in MRA-7K-10. |
| MRA-7K-08 [7K] | **The one content read that remains, and why it is eligible at the moment it is made.** A restored `PUBLISHED` row with a `CLEAR` clearance is captured by the frozen capture function that 7I CAG-05 (7) and RET-01 already list, and by no other reader. Everything happens in **one PostgreSQL transaction, `READ COMMITTED`, with a statement timeout and a lock timeout**; the data key is obtained, generate-only, before the transaction begins, so the transaction contains no call to another process. *Locks, in the order of 7I HLD-15:* the organization's hold gate in **exclusive** mode; then the outbox row under the lock of 7G DSR-12 (a); the clearance is changed last, by one conditional update, and no other transaction locks a clearance before a gate or a content row, so no cycle is possible. *Checks,* each a statement issued after the gate was obtained, so each sees every transaction that released the gate earlier: the clearance is `CLEAR`, unconsumed, for the running incarnation, and its recorded identity and payload digest equal the row's; no erasure step or time-based batch **in any journal state from `INTENT_REQUESTED` on** (7I JRN-06, JRN-21), no seal intent or seal record, and no organization-erasure operation reaches the row's attribution reference or organization; the database is activated for the running incarnation (7I JRN-12 a). *Outcome:* all hold: the source is stored encrypted with its digest and provenance, the clearance is set `CONSUMED`, the audit row is written, and the transaction commits. Any check fails, raises, times out or cannot be evaluated: nothing is stored, the clearance is set `REVOKED` in that same transaction where it can commit, and otherwise nothing commits and the next attempt re-evaluates. **The read and the eligibility decision are the same transaction, on one clock, under the lock that every erasure, purge and sealing function must take** (7I HLD-03, HLD-10, SEAL-03). The captured source is then a guarded row with its own clocks: its later use by R4 or R2 is the frozen material fetch of 7I CAG-05 (4) with the handoff margin of 7I CAG-10 (iii), and the residual of that handoff is 7I RR-12, which 7K neither widens nor claims away. Taking the gate in exclusive mode adds a participant to the list of 7I HLD-10; that is required wording of CNF-7K-09. |
| MRA-7K-09 [7K] | **Each boundary of MRA-7K-08, stated.** |

| Condition | Result |
|---|---|
| The content deadline passes between a pre-check and the read | There is no pre-check to rely on: the checks and the read are one transaction. A `PUBLISHED` row has no deadline (7I CAG-02); the captured source gets its own from the instant it is stored |
| Redis is slow, times out or is unreachable | No effect on the capture: it makes no Redis call. A later publication from the captured source is the frozen handoff of 7I CAG-10 (iii) |
| PostgreSQL and Redis clocks differ | Eligibility uses the PostgreSQL clock only. The executor's monotonic clock is used only for the frozen handoff margin. No Redis time enters any decision (RPR-7K-07) |
| The clearance is revoked or consumed concurrently | The conditional update finds no `CLEAR` row; nothing is stored |
| An erasure intent is in progress (`INTENT_REQUESTED`, `INTENT_DURABLE`) | The capture is refused: an intent that exists is treated as an erasure. If the intent is prepared only after the checks, its erasure transaction must take the gate, waits for the capture to commit, then finds the stored source and erases it (7I ERA-12, ERA-S6) |
| A sealing operation is in progress | The same: the sealing function takes the gate in share mode (7I SEAL-03) and so runs wholly before or wholly after the capture |
| A subject or organization erasure commits concurrently | Before the gate is obtained: seen, capture refused. After the capture commits: it erases the stored source |
| A hold is placed or released concurrently | Placement and release take the gate exclusive (7I HLD-10) and serialize with the capture. A hold alone does not bar; a hold placed afterwards reaches the stored source through its case and attribution (7I HLD-17). **During quarantine no hold can change outside the reconciler:** every guarded function refuses and no transaction begins (7I JRN-12 a, b), and the reconciler applies hold records in sequence order before it writes any clearance (7I JRN-10; RS-7). No capture runs in quarantine |
| The process crashes before the transaction | Nothing was read |
| It crashes inside the transaction | Rollback: nothing stored, the clearance unchanged. The plaintext existed only in the memory of the dead process and of the rolled-back session |
| It crashes after the commit | The source is stored once, the clearance is `CONSUMED`; a repeat finds no `CLEAR` clearance and stores nothing |
| Plaintext in memory after eligibility ends | The plaintext exists only between the row read and the encrypted write, inside the transaction that holds the gate; no erasure or seal can commit in that interval. It is discarded at commit or rollback and is carried by no cache, queue, task argument or log (7I CAG-10 (iv)) |
| The current-timeline eligibility source fails (the index is unverified, a function raises, the ledger is unreadable, a lock times out) | No capture. Fail closed, as 7I CAG-09 requires of a deadline that cannot be computed |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-10 [7K] | **What happens to every candidate that is not `CLEAR`** (second remediation; FG-7K-R2-02). The table below gives the outcome for each reason of MRA-7K-04, for a case without material (K) and for a restored `PUBLISHED` row (R). Four things hold in every row. (1) **7K deletes nothing and alters no row.** A `PUBLISHED` outbox row "is transport", is "not altered", is "not extended by a hold" and "has no ordinary content reader" (7I RET-01, CAG-02): its payload is reachable only through the two capture functions, and it ages out by its frozen lifecycle. 7K therefore asks for no tombstone of such a row and adds no deletion rule that a legal hold could conflict with. (2) **The barrier is the refusal of the capture functions**, not the absence of the bytes: with it in force, a `BARRED` row cannot reach Redis, a replay, a webhook delivery, the Billing consumer or any read path. That refusal is required wording of CNF-7K-09, because frozen 7I ACC-05 captures an unaccounted row at `T_obl` unconditionally. (3) **No case is made terminal, and no second case is opened.** The obligation of each affected group is carried by the one case of its identity (7G PID-01, PID-03). Where the group already has a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`, that same case is the one that moves to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`; where it has none, the first and only case of the identity is opened in that state. In both, the case is for its owning domain, with no material; it is not `RESOLVED`, not rejected, and never aged out (7G DAU-02, PRT-02; 7I ERA-03, INV-09). Frozen 7G has no entry into that state without material (T-08 and RPM-01 require it; T-13 starts from `PARKED` or `REPLAYING`; 7I ERA-13); 7K invents none and performs none, and the entry is required wording of CNF-7K-09 (c). Until it exists an existing case stays in its restored state, blocked and visible, and a pair without a case stays listed by 7I ACC-03. This table concerns candidates that are **not** `CLEAR`; a case without material whose event is `CLEAR` is not in it and follows class RM-3 of MRA-7K-14. (4) **Where safety cannot be proved, quarantine stays closed.** |

| # | Reason | Frozen 7I treatment; is it sufficient | Evidence that it completed | Content left in an ordinary store | Sealed vault | Tombstone | Obligation; owner reconciliation | May the stack leave quarantine | 7L amendment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Subject erasure, completed or pending | Owner rows, material, bodies and inputs are erased again; a pending intent is run through the erasure function in quarantine and ends erased or withdrawn (7I JRN-13 step 3, JRN-25). Sufficient for stored content. **Not sufficient against re-emission:** the `PUBLISHED` row and the case without material are untouched (7I ERA-S1, ERA-S3) | 7I JRN-26; no step for the subject left before `ERASED` or `WITHDRAWN` | R: the payload, in the outbox, with no ordinary reader. K: none | No | Not lawful under 7I RET-01 ("not altered"), and not needed | Owed. The owning domain reconciles (7G R6). Reason family *erased* | Only with the capture refusal in force | CNF-7K-09 (a), (b), (c) |
| 2 | Organization erasure | The organization's event-pipeline copies and keys are erased (7I ERA-17). Sufficient for stored content; not against re-emission | 7I JRN-26; the key-destruction records applied | As row 1. Its key is destroyed, so nothing could be encrypted for it either | No | As row 1 | Owed only where an obligation to another party survives; one owed to the erased organization is closed by the owner's reconciliation as superseded (7I ERA-17), never by 7K | As row 1 | CNF-7K-09 (a), (b), (c) |
| 3 | Journaled time-based erasure | Rows with content: 7I JRN-24, JRN-25 rows 6, 7 and 10. A case without material: no treatment exists. A row restored as `PUBLISHED` that the evidence says was a tombstoned `FAILED` row: unreachable state, so row 9 | 7I JRN-26 | K: none. R: not applicable (a `PUBLISHED` row has no time-based erasure, 7I JRN-30 a) | No | Applied by 7I to the rows its evidence names; none added | Owed; owner reconciles. Reason family *erased* | Yes for the rows 7I treats; for the case, only with the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) | CNF-7K-09 (c) |
| 4 | Sealed | The seal is re-applied to every row its journal record names, whatever the restored decision says; the content leaves its store for the vault (7I SEAL-07). Sufficient. A case without material has nothing to seal | Seal record and vault entry present; the store empty | None | **Yes**, for what the seal names | Not lawful: sealing is not erasure (7I SEAL-02, CAG-08) | Owed; the case is not moved by the erasure transition, because nothing was erased. Owner reconciles. Reason family *withheld* | Yes for sealed rows; for the case, with CNF-7K-09 (c) | CNF-7K-09 (c) |
| 5 | Legal hold together with another restriction | Held rows are skipped by every erasure and recorded `SKIPPED_HELD` (7I HLD-03, ERA-05); at their deadline they are sealed with basis `HELD` (7I SEAL-14). A `PUBLISHED` row is not extended by a hold (7I RET-01). Sufficient for preservation | Step or batch shows `SKIPPED_HELD`; hold records applied in order | Held content stays where it is until its own deadline. R: the payload, unreachable | At the deadline, by the frozen pass | **Never.** 7K applies no deletion to content a hold may reach | Owed; owner reconciles. Reason family *withheld* | Only with the capture refusal in force | CNF-7K-09 (a), (b), (c) |
| 6 | Unresolved attribution; incomplete subject index | Such content is never classified as unrelated by default. Inside its retention it is untouched; where a hold can make it uncertain a decision record is opened before the deadline; at the deadline it is sealed (7I HLD-11 c, HLD-18, HLD-22). Sufficient for stored content | Decision record where one is due; otherwise none is required inside retention | Yes: the row stays as it is, **inside its retention deadline**. R: the payload, unreachable | Only at its deadline | Never | Owed; owner reconciles. Reason family *withheld* | Only with the capture refusal in force. Without it, the row would be captured at `T_obl`: closed | CNF-7K-09 (a), (b), (c) |
| 7 | Content deadline passed, or unknown | Denied by the guard from the first statement; the time-based passes run to completion in quarantine and erase or seal (7I CAG-01, CAG-09, CAG-13; JRN-13 step 4). Sufficient. For a 7K candidate this is an unknown deadline and an integrity alarm | Access code of the guard; the pass's record | None readable | Where the pass seals | Only as the pass's own erasure | Owed; owner reconciles. Reason family *withheld* | Yes once the passes have run; an unknown deadline that persists is row 9 | CNF-7K-09 (c) for the case |
| 8 | Restored row in an earlier lifecycle state than its evidence | 7I JRN-24 (iv) and the store treatment of JRN-25, in quarantine. Sufficient. The verdict is computed after it | The reconciliation report of 7I JRN-27 | As the treatment leaves it | As the treatment leaves it | As JRN-25 says for that store | As JRN-25 says: a terminal status only where the outcome is a fact the restore did not undo | Yes after the treatment; a treatment that cannot complete is row 9 | None |
| 9 | Missing or contradictory journal evidence; an unreachable state; a clearance count that does not match | 7I JRN-16. Sufficient | — | Whatever was restored, unreachable: nothing runs | — | None | Unchanged; nothing is decided | **No.** No override in V1 | None |
| 10 | Cross-region journal copy not provably complete | 7I JRN-16, through REG-7K-20 | — | As row 9 | — | None | Unchanged | **No** | CNF-7K-10; OD-7K-05 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-11 [7K] | **Two reason families for the case, and no third.** *Erased* (rows 1 – 3): the frozen reason `PRIVACY_MATERIAL_ERASED` with `material_state = ERASED_UNDER_PRIVACY_RULE` and the journal reference, as 7I JRN-25 rows 6 and 7 use for a case that had material. *Withheld* (rows 4 – 7): nothing was erased, so that reason would be false (7I CAG-08). A distinct reason is needed, meaning "the source may not be read after a restore", with the verdict recorded; its name is for CNF-7K-09 and none is chosen here. In both families the case is unresolved and belongs to its owning domain. |
| MRA-7K-12 [7K] | **Cost of MRA-7K-07, stated.** An obligation in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` whose event still has its restored row and a `CLEAR` verdict loses nothing: the row is the source, through the R4 of class RM-3. A case reaches `PARKED` within about 25 minutes of its first failure, and a compatibility hold within 24 hours (PRK-7K-03; 7G IMP-03), while the row lives at least 7 days after publication, so this is the ordinary situation. An obligation whose row is already gone, which can only be an entry held by a key that was not onboarded for more than 7 days, has no source that may lawfully be read. It goes to its owner's reconciliation, unresolved, as the same case (class RM-4; RR-7K-10). The first submission's own finding F-7K-04 wanted that entry captured; the capture is what could not be made safe. |
| MRA-7K-13 [7K] | **Reopening** (rewritten in the third remediation; FG-7K-R3-02). The earlier text asked for a `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` case for *every* case without material. That contradicted DRS-7K-11 (d) for an event whose verdict is `CLEAR`, and it read as a second case for an identity that already had one. The stack is not reopened (RS-11) unless the reopening precondition of every class of MRA-7K-14 holds, that is: (1) every candidate has a verdict and the verdicts of one event agree, so class RM-0 is empty; (2) the capture functions refuse a row whose verdict is not `CLEAR`; (3) every pair of class RM-3 has its row's captured source held by the restore's R4 operation; (4) every case of class RM-4 has been moved, as the same case, to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` by the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c); (5) every pair of class RM-5 has its first and only case, opened by that entry; (6) the accounting reconciler is admitted together with the consumers, with 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 unmodified (MRA-7K-16 (7)). No condition asks for a case where one exists, and none asks for a transition of a case of class RM-1, RM-2 or RM-3. While CNF-7K-09 is not in force no verdict exists, so (1) fails, every pair is in class RM-0, and the restore does not complete. |
| MRA-7K-14 [7K] | **The authoritative matrix** (third remediation; FG-7K-R3-02). It decides every (event `e`, group `g`) pair of the owed set (7I ACC-02) for which the restored database holds no accounting row. A pair with an accounting row is complete: its effect and its evidence were restored together, and nothing is owed. DRS-7K-11, MRA-7K-10, MRA-7K-13, SUP-7K-05 and §21.9 refer to this table and state no rule that differs from it. **Inputs, read from PostgreSQL only:** the status of the restored outbox row of `e`, or its absence; the case of (`g`, `e`), its state and whether it holds material; the event's verdict (MRA-7K-03). *Not `CLEAR`* means `BARRED` for any reason of MRA-7K-04, or a clearance that is `REVOKED`, names another incarnation or does not match its row (MRA-7K-06). *Admissible source* means a restored row with an unconsumed `CLEAR` clearance, or the captured source stored from it under MRA-7K-08, for as long as that source exists. **The classes are tested in the order RM-0 … RM-6, and a pair is in exactly one.** A case that is `RESOLVED` is history (7G RTY-02): the obligation completed before the recovery point and was restored with its effect, and no recovery step touches it. |

| Class | Restored outbox row | Case of (`g`, `e`) and its material | Verdict | Authoritative owner | Source of permissible material | 7G transition that applies | Replay or republication | Does the case stay pending | Owner reconciliation mandatory | Acknowledgement | Reopening precondition | 7L amendment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RM-0 | Any | Any | **None written, or not verifiable:** rows 9 and 10 of MRA-7K-04; a count or an equality assertion of MRA-7K-03 fails | The restore reconciler | None | None | None | Every restored state is unchanged; nothing runs | Not decided | None is issued; no consumer runs | **Not reopened.** Quarantine persists (7I JRN-16) | None for row 9. CNF-7K-10 and OD-7K-05 for row 10 |
