Never the row, never a stream | Unchanged: T-10 … T-13 by R2; T-14 by the owner; T-15 where the class permits it. None by a recovery step | R2 for `PARKED` (7G §31). A restored `REPLAYING` item is recovered by its operation (7G TGT-09). None for `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | Yes, until those transitions | `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`: yes. `PARKED`: no | The superseded entry is destroyed unread and unacknowledged. A later entry of the event on the recovery generation takes the parked-duplicate path: no handler, the observation, then `XACK` (7G PAT-07). A Billing case in `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`: **no `XACK` before T-14** (7G BIL-04; SEC-7K-20); the entry lost with its key is the case of 7G BIL-06 | None beyond 7I JRN-26 | None |
| RM-2 | `PENDING` or `CLAIMED` | None; or `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` | Not a 7K candidate: 7I JRN-15 (b) decided the row in quarantine | The relay for the publication; the consuming group's worker for the case | The row itself, by ordinary publication to the recovery generation after S9. A row that 7I JRN-15 (b) tombstoned is not published: its publisher case goes to the producing owner, and a consumer case of that event is class RM-4 | None by a recovery step. On delivery the 7G gate lets the pipeline continue for no case and for `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` (RTY-02); then T-01 … T-09 as the worker's outcome decides | Ordinary publication; not a replay | Yes, until the worker's outcome | No | Ordinary: after the owner commit, the evidence and the accounting row (INV-7K-08); with an open case, only after its T-09 commit (7G RTY-10) | None specific | None |
| RM-3 | `PUBLISHED` | None; or `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material | `CLEAR` | Event operations for the R4; the consuming group's worker for the case | The restored row, captured once under MRA-7K-08 into the captured source of the restore's R4 (7G DSR-07, DSR-12), then published to the recovery generation by the repair publisher. A case receives material only if its worker later parks it, from the new entry as read (7G RPM-06) | **None by a recovery step, and no case is created.** On delivery: RTY-02 continues the pipeline; T-01 where no case exists; T-02, T-03, T-06, T-07, T-08 or T-09 for an existing case, exactly as frozen. Where no publication has come by the operational replay deadline: the park of MRA-7K-17 (4), which is wording of CNF-7K-09 (c) | **R4, and it is mandatory** (MRA-7K-15). A stopped plan changes neither | No | As RM-2. With CNF-7K-09 (c): **the same case** moves to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, class `V`, no material, with the reason family of MRA-7K-11. It ends only by T-14, which only the owning domain can record | Nothing exists to acknowledge, and nothing is issued. No handler runs; for Billing no usage is inserted | **Not reopened** until the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) has moved the case. A pair that enters this class after reopening is moved by the same entry, through the restore's operation | CNF-7K-09 (a), (c), (d) |
| RM-5 | `PUBLISHED` and not `CLEAR`; or `CLEAR` where the capture was refused, the captured source was erased, or the R4 item failed closed; or `CLEAR` and captured, where the frozen response of 7I ACC-16 or ACC-17 is due and no stored capture can lawfully be used any more (MRA-7K-16 (3)) | None | Not `CLEAR`, or `CLEAR` with no admissible source | The owning (consuming) domain | **None.** Both capture functions refuse the row (MRA-7K-10) | Without CNF-7K-09: none, and frozen 7I ACC-05 would capture the row at `T_obl`, which is why the stack stays closed. With CNF-7K-09 (c): (none) → `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, the first and only case of the identity, class `V`, no material | None | Yes: listed by 7I ACC-03 until its case exists, then `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | **Yes** | Nothing exists to acknowledge | **Not reopened** until the refusal is in force and the case exists | CNF-7K-09 (a), (b), (c) |
| RM-6 | Absent | None | None exists for a row or a case that is absent | Nobody: nothing is owed | None | None | None | No case exists | No | None | None | None |

Notes to the matrix. *RM-3 and the deadline:* a pair of class RM-3 that has no case stays subject to 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 as frozen; once 7I ACC-05 has opened its case it is class RM-1 (MRA-7K-16). *RM-6:* a pair whose row is absent and whose accounting row is still retained (7I ACC-11) is complete and in no class. For a pair with no row, no case and no accounting row, either every owed group was accounted or had a case before the row was cleaned up (7I ACC-06) and that evidence has since reached its own retention, or the row was created after the recovery point (DRS-7K-13 (iii)). In both, the restored database holds no obligation. *A case in `HOLD` that stores crash-loop material* (7G CLP-03) is not a case without material: with an admissible source it follows RM-2 or RM-3 and its material stays with it; without one it stays `HOLD` with its material, blocked and visible, and the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) for it is the frozen parking with that stored material, as 7I JRN-25 row 7 already applies where an erasure reaches such a case. *Recovery kind F* and every case in which the transport is lost as well use the same classes: no class reads a stream.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-15 [7K] | **Rules that hold in every class.** (1) **One identity, one case** (7G PID-01, PID-03). No recovery step inserts a case for an identity that has one. The capture-and-case path of 7I ACC-05 acts only on a pair that 7I ACC-03 lists, which is a pair with no case. The entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) is a transition of the existing case in class RM-4 and an insert only in class RM-5. Every step is keyed by the identity and is a compare-and-swap under the case's row lock (7G RSM-02), so two workers converge on one result (7I ACC-07, ACC-20). (2) **No transition by 7K.** The restore procedure, the supersession and the R4 move no case. In particular they never use T-07, which is the governed park of an entry that is still pending and readable, never T-08 or T-13, and never a terminal resolution. The only entry they may apply is that of CNF-7K-09 (c), once it exists. The park of MRA-7K-17 (4) is the second form of that entry; it is applied by the reconciler's pass at a deadline fixed at the capture, never by an R4 plan and never because a plan stopped. (3) **One capture per restored row, by the restore's R4.** A clearance is consumed once (MRA-7K-08). The restore's R4 operation, in one or more bounded plans (7G DSR-08, THR-02), therefore names every restored `PUBLISHED` row whose verdict is `CLEAR` and that has at least one pair of class RM-3, and captures it during S6: after activation, before any superseded key is destroyed, and before outbox cleanup is admitted at RS-11. Its publication starts only after S9, on keys that are onboarded and on shards that are capable (7G TPG-03; PRK-7K-11); between the capture and the publication the captured source in the ledger carries the obligation. The order matters for a pair with a case: a case satisfies the cleanup interlock (7I ACC-06), so without the capture its row could age out and leave the obligation with no source. Republication from the captured source reaches every group of the family (7G DSR-04), so one capture serves every pair of the row; a group whose case is `PARKED`, `REPLAYING` or `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` takes the parked-duplicate path. The accounting reconciler's capture of such a row finds the clearance consumed and is refused: the row is never read a second time. **No wait is added to the frozen deadline** (corrected in the fifth remediation; FG-7K-R5-01). The fourth remediation restarted the age of such a pair at the R4's position record and let a replay that was not terminal hold the pair open. Both are withdrawn: 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 apply to the pair exactly as frozen, and their response is MRA-7K-16. (4) **A refused or lost source re-classes; it never completes.** A capture that is refused (the clearance becomes `REVOKED`), a captured source erased before publication (7I ERA-S6), and an R4 item that ends `SOURCE_MISSING` or `PROVENANCE_MISSING` (7G DSR-06, DSR-11) move each pair of the row to RM-4 or RM-5 in the operation record. **An item that a stopped or cancelled operation left unpublished moves no pair:** its source is still there, and the class of a pair follows its source, not the state of a plan (MRA-7K-17), and the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) follows. (5) **The only sources a handler may run from.** For event `e` the handler of group `g` runs only from an entry on the recovery generation that the relay published from a `PENDING` or `CLAIMED` row or that the repair publisher published from the captured source of a `CLEAR` row; or from an R2 recovery delivery of the case's own stored material. Never from a superseded entry, a row that is not `CLEAR`, a log, an audit row or mutable domain state (7G RPM-08). (6) **Acknowledgement.** No recovery step issues `XACK`. An entry on the recovery generation is acknowledged only in the frozen order: after the accounting row (INV-7K-08), after the T-09 commit of an open case (7G RTY-10), after the parking commit (7G PAT-01), and for Billing not before T-14 (7G BIL-04). (7) **Crash and outage.** A crash before a transition or a capture commits leaves the prior state and the pair is classed again from PostgreSQL; a crash after it finds the new state. Classing, capture and the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) make no Redis call, so Redis being unavailable delays only the R4's publication, whose outcomes are non-terminal (7E CLS-01); nothing is completed, dropped or acknowledged meanwhile. |
| MRA-7K-16 [FROZEN + 7K] | **The deadline of a restored pair** (fifth remediation; FG-7K-R5-01). (1) **One clock, the frozen one.** The age of an unaccounted pair is that of 7I ACC-17 and nothing else: database `now()` minus the earliest `recorded_at` of the row's positions, or the row's `published_at` where it has none. Positions recorded before the restore were restored with the row and count as they stand, on whatever generation and key they name; a position on a superseded key is not discarded. **No restore-specific clock or interval exists.** The activation of the incarnation, the capture by the R4 and the R4's own position record are facts about the pair; they enter neither its age nor its deadline. The time the stack spent in quarantine is part of the age. (2) **No state of an operation is a condition.** An R4 that is `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, slow, stuck, unpublished or unavailable suspends nothing. 7I ACC-16 classes the pair from its positions as frozen: a position on a key that no longer exists is `GONE` (7I ACC-19), so a pair whose only positions are on superseded keys goes to its response at once, and a pair with an R4 position on the recovery generation waits only while that position is `UNDELIVERED` or `PENDING` **and** the frozen age is at most `T_obl`. (3) **The response is the frozen one: a case, decided from PostgreSQL alone.** 7I ACC-05 opens the case of the pair: class `V`, `PARKED`, reason `OBLIGATION_UNACCOUNTED`, recovered by the unchanged R2. After a restore only the origin of its material differs, because the row may be read once (MRA-7K-08). Where the row's clearance is consumed, ACC-05 does not read the row: the case's material is a byte-exact copy of the capture already stored under 7G DSR-12, with its digest and provenance, made inside the ledger in the transaction that inserts the case, under the hold gate and the current-timeline checks of MRA-7K-08 applied to the stored capture. Where no stored capture exists any more, or a check fails, there is no lawful source: the pair is class RM-5 and gets its first and only case through the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c). **Frozen 7I ACC-05 words the capture of the row only.** Both forms are required wording of CNF-7K-09 (b) and (c). Until they exist ACC-05 would read the row a second time, which is why no stack is reopened after a lossy restore (AG-7K-08). (4) **Whoever captures first.** If ACC-05 reaches a `CLEAR` row whose clearance is unconsumed, its capture is the single capture of MRA-7K-08, and the restore's R4 publishes from that stored capture. RS-11 admits the reconciler only after the R4 holds its captures, so this is the exception. (5) **One case.** The insert is keyed by (group, `event_id`) under 7I ACC-20. A pair that has a case is not listed by 7I ACC-03 and is carried by that case. A gateway accounting insert that commits concurrently leaves both, as 7I ACC-20 states; the case then resolves through R2 as already committed. (6) **After the case.** A `PARKED` case is class RM-1: an R4 entry of the event that reaches the group takes the parked-duplicate path, with no handler (7G RTY-02, PAT-07), and R2 delivers the case's material. For CON-06 nothing is inserted without a handler commit, and an entry whose case is `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` is not acknowledged before T-14 (7G BIL-04). (7) **The reconciler is part of an open stack.** It is admitted at RS-11 with the consumers. A stack that consumes while the reconciler is not admitted is not reopened in the sense of MRA-7K-13. Before RS-11 nothing consumes, the rows are held by 7I ACC-06, and the age keeps running. (8) **A pair that has a case: an operational deadline, separate from the accounting deadline** (rewritten in the sixth remediation; FG-7K-R6-02). 7I ACC-17 does not apply to such a pair (7I ACC-03 lists only pairs without a case), and frozen 7G gives a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` no deadline that runs without an entry. 7K adds one and labels it as its own: the *operational replay deadline* of a captured row is its capture time (7G DSR-12 (c)) plus `T_obl`, on the database clock. It is fixed when the capture commits. No plan, approval, pause, stop or successor plan moves it, and it is never used for a pair without a case. Until it passes the pair is class RM-3 whatever the state of any operation, for as long as its source is eligible (MRA-7K-17). When it passes and the row has no `REPAIR` position record, the wait for a publication ends: each such case is parked with the stored capture as its material (MRA-7K-17 (4)), and the list of rows so disposed is recorded and escalated to the incident owner. A plan still carries its stop condition, no item result for `T_obl`, which ends a stalled operation and decides nothing about any pair. (9) **Cost, stated.** With `T_obl` at its candidate of 2 hours, a restore that takes longer leaves every pair that was unaccounted at the recovery point past its deadline when the stack reopens: one case for each, as after any outage of consumption longer than `T_obl` (BPR-7K-08; §17.4). The R4's republication then serves the pairs that already have a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` and the pairs still inside the deadline; the others are recovered by R2 (PRK-7K-11). That is the frozen behaviour, and 7K does not soften it. |

| # | For each restored (row, group) pair without an accounting row | Answer | Source |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Original publication time and positions | `published_at` of the restored row; every position record restored with it: generation, key, entry ID, `recorded_at` | 7I ACC-13; restored to the recovery point |
| 2 | Activation time of the restored incarnation | Held by the activation record. **Used for nothing in the deadline** | 7I JRN-12, JRN-13 step 7 |
| 3 | R4 source-capture time | The commit of the capture; clearance `CONSUMED`. **Used for nothing in the deadline** | MRA-7K-08; 7G DSR-12 (c) |
| 4 | R4 publication position | A position record with publisher kind `REPAIR` on the recovery generation, once the R4 has published. It is one more position of the pair for 7I ACC-16. It is never the earliest, so it never moves the age | 7I ACC-13 |
| 5 | Deadline of mandatory reconciliation | The earliest `recorded_at` among items 1 and 4, or `published_at`, plus `T_obl`; and at once when every covered position is `GONE` | 7I ACC-16, ACC-17, unchanged |
| 6 | Source of the computation | PostgreSQL only, one clock. No Redis value, no operation state, no restore time | 7I ACC-17 |
| 7 | The R4 has not published before the deadline | The response of MRA-7K-16 (3). With positions on superseded keys only, it comes at the reconciler's first pass | 7I ACC-16, ACC-19 |
| 8 | The R4 published and the gateway has not accounted | The pair waits while the R4 position is `UNDELIVERED` or `PENDING` and the frozen age is at most `T_obl`; then the response. Every position `GONE`: at once | 7I ACC-16, ACC-17 |
| 9 | The captured source is lost: erased, refused, or the item ended `SOURCE_MISSING` or `PROVENANCE_MISSING` | Re-classed at once by the operation, not at the deadline: RM-4 with a case, RM-5 without | MRA-7K-15 (4); 7I ERA-S6; 7G DSR-06, DSR-11 |
| 10 | A recovery case already exists | The pair is not listed and has no reconciler deadline. `OPEN_RETRY` and `HOLD` continue on the R4 entry (RM-3); at the operational replay deadline without a publication they are parked with the capture (MRA-7K-17 (4)); they move to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` by CNF-7K-09 (c) only when no eligible source exists (RM-4). `PARKED` and later states are RM-1 | 7I ACC-03; 7G RTY-02; MRA-7K-14 |
| 11 | May 7I ACC-05 capture the source again | **No.** The row is read once. A consumed clearance refuses, and so does a `BARRED` or a `REVOKED` one. ACC-05 uses the stored capture or nothing | MRA-7K-06, -08; CNF-7K-09 (b) |
| 12 | Permitted case transition | (none) → `PARKED` by ACC-05 with the stored capture as material; or (none) → `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` by CNF-7K-09 (c) where no lawful source exists. No other, and never a second case | 7I ACC-05, ACC-20; 7G PID-01 |
| 13 | May the pair be acknowledged | Neither the reconciler nor any recovery step acknowledges anything. An entry of the event on the recovery generation is acknowledged only in the frozen order: after the accounting commit; after the observation of a parked case; for Billing not before T-14 | INV-7K-08; 7G PAT-07, BIL-04; MRA-7K-15 (6) |
| 14 | Rule that lets the stack stay open | MRA-7K-13 with MRA-7K-16 (7): the capture refusal in force, the reconciler admitted with 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 unmodified, and every pair past its deadline holding its one case. A stack that cannot meet these is not reopened, and none can be met before CNF-7K-09 is consolidated | AG-7K-08 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| MRA-7K-17 [7K] | **Source and operation: one decision table** (sixth remediation; FG-7K-R6-02). The fifth remediation moved the pairs of an item that a stopped plan had left unpublished to class RM-4, and in the same rule let a later plan publish that item: a class defined by the absence of an admissible source, applied to a source that was still there. It also set no limit across successive plans. (1) **The class of a pair follows the eligibility of its source, never the state of an operation.** A source is *eligible* while the row's clearance is `CONSUMED` for the running incarnation, the stored capture exists with a matching digest and complete provenance, and no check of MRA-7K-08 refuses it. `DRAFT`, `PLANNED`, `APPROVED`, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, `STOPPED`, `CANCELLED` and `COMPLETED` (7G PLN-06) are states of a plan; none of them changes a class. (2) **Lineage.** Every R4 plan of a restore names the restore's incident reference (7G DSR-09) and, after the first, its predecessor. A successor plan is the unprocessed remainder of its predecessor and nothing else: a new governed operation with its own approval (7G CAN-04, PLN-05; 7I OPA-07). At most one publishes in the region at a time (7G THR-02). A row that has a `REPAIR` position record is in no successor plan. (3) **No plan restarts a clock.** The accounting deadline of a pair without a case is that of 7I ACC-17 (MRA-7K-16 (1)). The operational replay deadline of a pair with a case is fixed at the capture (MRA-7K-16 (8)). Both are read from records no plan can write. The cycle `STOPPED` → new plan → `STOPPED` therefore ends at a fixed instant, with a recorded disposition and a recorded escalation, however many plans were approved before it. (4) **Park with the capture.** At the operational replay deadline, for a row without a `REPAIR` position: each case of the row in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material moves, as that same case, to `PARKED` with a byte-exact copy of the stored capture as its material, under the gate and the checks of MRA-7K-16 (3); a `HOLD` case that stores crash-loop material is parked with its own material. R2 then serves it (class RM-1). Where the copy is refused because the source is no longer eligible, the case is class RM-4 instead. **Frozen 7G has this transition (T-07) for a worker, the isolation activity and the retirement executor only.** The trigger and the actor used here are required wording of CNF-7K-09 (c); until they exist the case stays in its state, blocked and visible, and no stack is reopened after a lossy restore (AG-7K-08). (5) **Nothing is recorded as reconciled or resolved because a replay failed.** A stop, a pause or a passed deadline writes no accounting row, no resolution and no owner disposition. Owner reconciliation is mandatory only where no eligible source exists (classes RM-4 and RM-5), and it ends only by 7G T-14. (6) The two tables below decide every situation. "ACC-05" means the frozen response of MRA-7K-16 (3) for a pair without a case. |

| # | Situation: item and plan state; case | Source eligible | Republication lawful | New plan permitted | Bound that applies | Permitted transition |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Item not yet started; plan `APPROVED` or `RUNNING` | Yes | Yes, by this plan | Not needed | No case: 7I ACC-16, ACC-17. With a case: the operational replay deadline | None by the operation. The pair stays RM-3 |
| 2 | Item running: publication attempt registered, not yet confirmed | Yes | In progress | No: one execution per item | As row 1 | None |
| 3 | Plan `PAUSED` (7G THR-04, THR-05) | Yes | When the plan resumes | No: the plan resumes | As row 1. A pause consumes no budget and moves no deadline | None |
| 4 | Plan `STOPPED` or `CANCELLED`; item unpublished; the capture still eligible | **Yes** | Yes | Yes: the remainder, in the lineage | As row 1 | **None, and not RM-4.** The pair stays RM-3 |
| 5 | Plan stopped; the capture was erased | No | No | Not for this row | — | At once. With a case: that case → `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` (RM-4). Without: the first and only case, `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` (RM-5). Both by CNF-7K-09 (c) |
| 6 | Item ended `SOURCE_MISSING` (7G DSR-06) | No | No | No | — | As row 5 |
| 7 | Item ended `PROVENANCE_MISSING` (7G DSR-11) | No: the capture exists but its provenance is incomplete, so it may not be published to a shared stream (7G DSR-13) and is not copied either | No | Not for publication | — | As row 5 |
| 8 | Item with an `UNKNOWN` publication outcome (7E WAO-05): an entry may exist; no position was recorded | Yes | Yes: the item is run again; at worst a duplicate entry (7I ACC-18 a) | Yes, if its plan has ended | As row 1. It counts as unpublished for both deadlines, because no `REPAIR` position exists | None. A stray entry that arrives after a case was parked takes the parked-duplicate path |
| 9 | A stored capture exists but its clearance is not `CONSUMED` for the running incarnation: revoked, mismatched, or of another incarnation | No | No | No | — | As row 5. The capture is never published and never copied |
| 10 | A second approved plan names the same event | As its row | Only if the row has no `REPAIR` position and its source is eligible. It then publishes from the same stored capture, never from the outbox row | Yes, once its predecessor is terminal | Unchanged. No deadline restarts | None |
| 11 | Existing case in `OPEN_RETRY` | Yes | Yes | As rows 1 – 4 | **The operational replay deadline** (not 7I ACC-17) | On the R4 entry: frozen T-09, T-02 or T-03. At the deadline without a `REPAIR` position: `OPEN_RETRY` → `PARKED` with the capture (4) |
| 12 | Existing case in `HOLD` | Yes | Yes | As rows 1 – 4 | The operational replay deadline | On the R4 entry: classified again; frozen T-07 at its own bounds. At the deadline: `HOLD` → `PARKED` with the capture, or with its own crash-loop material (4) |
| 13 | Existing case in `PARKED`, `REPLAYING` or `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | Not consulted for this group: the case carries its own material or its reconciliation (RM-1) | Lawful, and without effect for this group: the parked-duplicate path | Not for this pair | The case's own frozen bounds | None by a recovery step. R2 or T-14, as frozen |
| 14 | No case; the pair is unaccounted | Yes | Yes | As rows 1 – 4 | **7I ACC-16 and ACC-17, as frozen** | (none) → `PARKED` by ACC-05 with the capture (MRA-7K-16 (3)) |
| 15 | The first plan and its successors together exceed `T_obl` | Yes | Still lawful; without effect on a case that was parked | Permitted for rows still unpublished; it changes nothing in this row | Both deadlines have passed, and neither is restarted | No case: row 14 happened at the accounting deadline. With a case: (4) at the operational replay deadline |

| # | ACC-05 response still applies to a pair without a case | Owner reconciliation mandatory | Escalation | Stack stays serviceable | Evidence and audit record |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 – 3 | Yes, unchanged | No | None beyond the incident | Yes | Operation and item record; the registered publication attempt (7F HCG-26) |
| 4 | Yes, unchanged | No | Recorded: the stop, the condition that fired, the remainder | Yes | Operation record; audit row; the successor plan's predecessor reference |
| 5 | No capture can be used: class RM-5 by CNF-7K-09 (c) | **Yes** | To the owning domain (7G R6) | Yes, once the case exists | Erasure step and journal reference; item result; case history |
| 6 | As row 5 | **Yes** | As row 5 | As row 5 | Item result `SOURCE_MISSING`; case history |
| 7 | As row 5 | **Yes**; the owner's rebuild (7G R6) remains | As row 5 | As row 5 | Item result; the groups without an origin record (7G DSR-12 f) |
| 8 | Yes, unchanged | No | None, unless the plan stops: then row 4 | Yes | Publication attempt record; the absence of a position record |
| 9 | As row 5 | **Yes** | Integrity incident | Yes, once the case exists; the capture stays unused | Clearance record; alarm; audit row |
| 10 | Yes, unchanged | No | — | Yes | Both operation records; the lineage; the approval under 7I OPA-07 |
| 11, 12 | Not applicable: the pair is not listed (7I ACC-03) | No, while the source is eligible | **Mandatory at the operational replay deadline:** the recorded list of parked rows, to the incident owner | Yes | Case history entry by compare-and-swap (7G RSM-02); material digest equal to the capture's; audit row |
| 13 | Not applicable | Only if the case is `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | As the case's own state requires | Yes | Parked-duplicate observation (7G PAT-07) |
| 14 | Yes: this row is that response | No while a capture can be used; yes otherwise (RM-5) | The security signal of 7I ACC-05 | Yes | Case; material digest; signal |
| 15 | As row 14 | As rows 11, 12 | Mandatory, recorded once at each deadline. A further plan gets no new deadline | Yes | Operation lineage; the deadline record; the cases |

**The counterexample of the review, step by step** (regression case T-7K-46 (a), §26.1).

| Step | What happens |
|---|---|
| 1 – 2 | An event with protected content commits; Redis keeps the entry; one consumer group has a case for it in `OPEN_RETRY`, with no material |
| 3 | The subject's content is erased or sealed. By 7I ERA-S2 and ERA-S3 the entry and the case are untouched; the journal has the records |
| 4 | An earlier PostgreSQL backup is restored: the case is `OPEN_RETRY` again, the outbox row may be `PUBLISHED` again. The stack is quarantined; RS-5 fences every reader and publisher of the old keys |
| 5 | The journal is reconciled. The reconciler finds the erasure or seal record for the subject the case's attribution reference reaches. It writes `BARRED` for the case and for the row; or, while CNF-7K-09 is not consolidated, it writes nothing |
| 6 | The supersession inventories the old key with payload-free commands and finds the pending entry: class D-4 |
| 7 | The capture is attempted: **no step of the operation can make it.** No command that returns an entry's fields is issued on the key, and the recovery-operator credential is not used for one (SUP-7K-08) |
| Recovery material | None is created |
| New stream | The row's verdict is `BARRED`; both capture functions refuse it (MRA-7K-10); nothing of it enters the recovery generation |
| Replay | No material exists; R2 has no candidate (7I ERA-09) |
| Webhook | No entry reaches the fan-out consumer. Restored deliveries of the event were treated by 7I JRN-25 in quarantine |
| Read paths | The material view has nothing to decrypt. The old key is fenced from every runtime user and is then destroyed |
| Crash between check and write | There is no read of the entry to crash after. For the restored row: MRA-7K-09, one transaction |

#### 21.6.3 Crash and partial-failure matrix (regression case T-7K-47, §26.1)

| # | Fault | State found at restart | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The fence is applied on part of the cluster | Some nodes with the old ACL digest | Those nodes take no traffic; RS-7 does not start; the fence is completed or the restore stops. Nothing was read or written |
| 2 | Failure during the pending-entry inventory | Inventory partial | S2 and S4 are re-read. The inventory holds identifiers only; no entry was read |
| 3 | A step attempts to read an entry | — | Not possible by design: no step issues such a command (MRA-7K-07). The test asserts its absence from the Redis command log |
| 4 | Failure after the recovery generation was created | New keys empty, unwritten; old keys fenced | S7 is idempotent. No relay is enabled before S9 |
| 5 | Failure between group destruction and key deletion | Key at `GROUPS_DESTROYED` | The key is still fenced and now has no group. S8 resumes. No entry is consumed: no credential reaches it |
| 6 | Restart with both generations present | Any state above | Each key resumes from its recorded state. Relays are not enabled. The generations are never both writable |
| 7 | A Redis key is lost before the inventory | Key absent at S2 | Nothing depended on it. Pending IDs that the ledger knows are classified from PostgreSQL; an obligation with a `CLEAR` row is rebuilt from the row; one with no admissible source is left for its owner's reconciliation (MRA-7K-10; 7G DGL-04, DGL-05). No completion is recorded |
| 8 | A journaled erasure affects an event being recovered | Before activation: verdict `BARRED`. After activation, before the row's capture commits: found by the checks of MRA-7K-08 | No source is stored. If the erasure follows the capture commit, it erases the stored source by 7I ERA-12. The entry itself is never read in either order |

**A lossless event** (DRS-7K-04) needs none of RS-5, RS-6 and RS-8: the database holds every committed row, so the transport is not ahead.

**Transport lost as well** (kind F; or a region-wide failure of kind D): the same matrix (MRA-7K-14), with nothing to fence or destroy. Rows and cases are classed exactly as above, because no class reads a stream; an item that needs a source that does not exist has the frozen result `SOURCE_MISSING` and the owner's rebuild (7G DGL-04). No completion is recorded for them.

### 21.7 Consistency of the other stores

| ID | Store | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| DRS-7K-14 | Audit | Audit rows are restored with the mutations they describe, in the same database, to the same point: a mutation and its mandatory audit row are both present or both absent (OD-7D-02). Audit of the lost interval is lost with it; the restore itself, every reconciliation treatment and every gate clearance is audited in the new timeline (7I JRN-27). The nightly hash chain is recomputed from the restored state |
| DRS-7K-15 | Hot tier | HT state is never authoritative. After a lossy event: capacity reservations are reconciled to PostgreSQL by the frozen reconciler (6K §54.5); a denylist entry that outlives its revocation row only denies more; every key space that caches a PostgreSQL-derived outcome or decision, as listed in the namespace catalogue (5A §18), is invalidated at RS-6, so that no cached result of a lost transaction is served |
| DRS-7K-16 | Object storage and keys | Objects are not rolled back. After the journal pass the orphan reconciliation of 7I ERA-22 lists objects without a row and rows without an object. A key version destroyed after `P` stays destroyed (7I JRN-13 step 3); a version created after `P` is kept, decrypt-only if no restored record names it. A restored row whose key version is gone is unreadable content and is reported, not repaired |
| — | Held and sealed content | Holds are re-applied from the journal in sequence order before any erasure record that follows them (7I JRN-10). Seals are re-applied (7I SEAL-07). Until then the access guard denies every row past its deadline from the first statement (7I CAG-13). The subject index is restored to `P` together with the rows it describes |
| — | Erased data | 7I JRN-13, JRN-24 … JRN-26. 7K adds no treatment and removes none |
| — | Recovery cases | Restored to `P`. A case resolved in the lost interval is unresolved again and stays so until its obligation is completed or reconciled (7I JRN-25 rows 6, 7). An operation restored as `RUNNING` is recovered item by item (7G TGT-09) |
| — | Webhook attempts | Attempt history after `P` is lost. A delivery restored non-terminal and not named by an erasure record is sent again; the receiver deduplicates on `event_id` (7I JRN-30) |
| — | Observation accounting | Every hour from the restore point is permanently `UNMEASURED` with cause `RESTORE_GAP` (7J AOA-14). Windows that contain it give no verdict (OD-7J-11) |

### 21.8 The money gate (OD-7K-03)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-17 [OD-7K-03, condition 1] | **Closed by default on every incarnation change.** Automated charges, renewals, dunning, refund execution and invoice finalization are permitted only while a **financial clearance** exists for the running database incarnation. The clearance is bound to the incarnation in the same two places as the activation of 7I JRN-12: a record outside the database, and a row inside it that names the incarnation it was issued for. A recovered database carries the old incarnation's row, so the functions that start those operations refuse. A control stored only inside the database being restored would be restored with it; this one is not. Its physical form is a governed requirement (IO-7K-09). |
| DRS-7K-18 [OD-7K-03, conditions 1, 2] | **Two ways to issue a clearance, and no third.** (a) *Lossless event:* the recorded proof of DRS-7K-04. (b) *Lossy event:* a Billing reconciliation that covers the lost interval `(P, T_f]` with a margin on both sides, against the records of every external payment provider: every charge, capture, renewal, refund and reversal the provider holds for the interval is matched to a restored row by its provider reference or idempotency key, and every unmatched one is brought into the database through Billing's guarded functions. The reconciliation is recorded with its interval, its provider sources, its counts and its two actors, and is audited. There is no early release by decision alone. |
| DRS-7K-19 [OD-7K-03, condition 4] | **Absence is not evidence.** A payment, charge or refund that the restored database does not contain is never taken to mean that it did not happen. While the gate is closed no retry, renewal or dunning step is derived from a restored `PENDING`, unpaid or failed state. If a provider cannot supply its records for the interval, the gate stays closed for the operations that depend on that provider. That wait has no deadline: it is residual risk RR-7K-06. |
| DRS-7K-20 [OD-7K-03, condition 3] | **Unchanged by the gate.** Finalized invoices are immutable and late usage goes to the next invoice (7A BIL-04). Every amount is computed server-side (7A BIL-01). Idempotency keys are those of 6K. Refunds are settled only by the Billing reconciler from provider evidence (6M §19); the gate blocks the *execution* of new refunds, never the settlement of ones the provider already holds. |
| DRS-7K-21 [7K] | **Not gated**, because they move no money and finalize nothing: usage ingestion; payment-callback receipt and processing, which is itself provider evidence; quota enforcement; reads. Non-financial service reopens at RS-11 whether or not the gate is clear. |
| DRS-7K-22 [7K] | The gate is L1 in the precedence of §6.3. A billing run that finds it closed performs no partial work and records that it was refused. |

### 21.9 Recovery completion criteria

A restore is complete only when all hold, each proved from authoritative state and recorded:

1. The activation record exists for the running incarnation and the verification of 7I JRN-26 found no counter-instance.
2. For a lossy event: the supersession operation is `COMPLETED` (SUP-7K-10): no pre-event generation key exists; the recovery generation is onboarded for every group; every pending entry of every superseded key has its recorded class; every candidate has a verdict; every (event, group) pair without an accounting row is in exactly one class of MRA-7K-14, and the reopening precondition of its class holds (MRA-7K-13): the capture functions refuse every restored `PUBLISHED` row whose verdict is not `CLEAR`, every pair of class RM-3 has its captured source, every case of class RM-4 is that same case in `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, and every pair of class RM-5 has its one case. No identity has two cases. A supersession stopped under SUP-7K-09 does not meet this criterion.
3. No unaccounted (row, group) pair is older than `T_obl` without a case. The age is the frozen one of 7I ACC-17, from the pair's earliest recorded position, positions recorded before the restore included. **There is no exception for class RM-3 and no clock of the restore** (MRA-7K-16). Every R4 plan of the restore is terminal and every item it processed has a definite result (7G PLN-06); an item that ended `SOURCE_MISSING` or `PROVENANCE_MISSING` has moved its pairs to class RM-4 or RM-5. A plan that stopped with items unpublished is terminal and does not by itself complete the restore: every row it left either has a `REPAIR` position from a successor plan, or its operational replay deadline has passed and its cases are parked with the capture (MRA-7K-17).
4. Every restored operation is terminal or re-approved.
5. The reconciliation report of 7I JRN-27, and the list of cases left for owner reconciliation under MRA-7K-10 and classes RM-4 and RM-5 of MRA-7K-14, have been handed to the owning domains.
6. The financial clearance exists, or the gate is recorded as still closed with its reason and owner. A closed gate does not prevent closing the *restore*; it stays an open item of the incident.
7. The orphan reconciliation has run.
8. The lost interval and the achieved recovery point are recorded and communicated.

### 21.10 Drills

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| DRS-7K-23 [7K] | The monthly restore drill of 3F §11.4 is extended: it restores into an isolated stack, runs the journal reconciliation, reproduces the two counterexamples of 7I §14.8, runs the supersession on a synthetic transport that is ahead of the database, including the admission cases and the fault matrix (T-7K-46, T-7K-47), and measures each term of DRS-7K-02 (T-7K-35). A cross-region drill for class S is run before the path is relied on and at least yearly (T-7K-40). A drill that has not been run is reported as not run. |
| DRS-7K-24 [7K] | Journal checkpoints are written every `T_ckpt` ([CAND] 1 hour), which bounds the chain the reconciler must prove beyond the restored lower bound. The number of records to re-apply is still the erasures since `P`; a week-old recovery point re-applies a week of erasure batches. |

---

## 22. Security and Resilience Boundaries

### 22.1 Dependencies that fail closed

| ID | Dependency | Operations that stop | Obligation kept by | 7K value |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-7K-01 [FROZEN + 7K] | Key service | Parking; callback acknowledgement; replay; callback processing; material view (7I §12.2) | Pending entry; provider retry; `PARKED` case; owed receipt | A pre-claim check keeps the outage from spending attempts (7H XRT-09). A consumer that cannot park leaves the entry pending and opens its dependency gate; it does not acknowledge |
| SEC-7K-02 [7K] | Key service latency | Parking and callback acknowledgement call it once per item | — | Per-call timeout below the attempt budget; [MEAS] BM-7K-06. No data key is cached beyond the item it was issued for (7I KEY-06) |
| SEC-7K-03 [7K] | Secret manager | Webhook send (`SECRET_UNAVAILABLE`, frozen); credential refresh | Delivery schedule | Running workloads keep their in-process credentials (3F §10.1); new instances do not start |
| SEC-7K-04 [7K] | Authorization issuer | Lifecycle actions; erasure, replay and inspection approvals | The requested action is simply not performed | RTN-7K-12 |
| SEC-7K-05 [7K] | Egress path | Webhook and plugin requests | Delivery rows stay `PENDING` (pre-claim gate) | REG-7K-09 |
| SEC-7K-06 [7K] | Egress policy store | Each request reads the policy in force (7I EGR-05) | A request whose policy cannot be read is not sent | **No cache in V1** (`T_pol` = 0): the policy is read from the primary in the admission path. A cache may be introduced only with a stated maximum age, and a policy older than that age is never served; a tightening of the policy must take effect within that age (7I HE-7K-7I-05) |
| SEC-7K-07 [7K] | Journal store | Erasure, hold release, key destruction | Step at `INTENT_REQUESTED` | RTN-7K-07 |

### 22.2 Boundaries 7K keeps

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SEC-7K-08 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Voice path.** No rule of this document adds a network call, a lock, a probe, a telemetry export or a recovery step to the audio loop or the turn loop (INV-7K-13). The conversational latency figure remains a target: at most 750 ms under 6D (7A VOX-01), and under 800 ms at the median under the SRS (NFR-PERF-001). Neither is validated here; validation is P23's (7A §34.2). 7K's contribution is isolation: TOP-7K-13 … -15, DBS-7K-03, ASC-7K-07. |
| SEC-7K-09 [7K] | **Diagnostics expose no protected content.** Every runbook step and every probe of this document reads identifiers, states, counts, positions and sizes. None reads `env`, a payload, a body, material, an input or a secret, and none requires a credential that could (7I RED rules; 7E SCY-03). The supersession reads no entry at all: it issues no command whose reply carries an entry's fields (MRA-7K-07, SUP-7K-08). |

### 22.3 Origin verification for Billing ingestion (OD-7K-04; closes 7I HE-7K-7I-07)

HRD-01 was an optional recommendation of 7I. The owner adopted it for the Billing usage consumer (CON-06). Nothing below exists; it needs the governed function of IO-7K-08 and the controlled reconciliations of CNF-7K-05. The acknowledgement rules were corrected in the remediation (FG-7K-02): the first submission acknowledged an origin-unverifiable Billing entry as soon as its case committed, which frozen 7G BIL-04 forbids.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SEC-7K-10 [OD-7K-04, condition 1] | **The check.** Before CON-06 applies a billable effect for an event, it calls a guarded PostgreSQL function with the event's `event_id` and receives the authoritative `organization_id`, `event_type` and payload digest of that outbox row, and nothing else. The function is `SECURITY DEFINER`, executable by the Billing context principal only, reads one row by primary key, and returns no payload. |
| SEC-7K-11 [7K] | **Place in the pipeline.** Inside the owner transaction, after validation and the tenant gate (7F D-01 … D-08), after the shared retention barrier and the evidence-horizon gate (7F RET-7F-08, RET-7F-11), and before the guard statement. No frozen step is removed, reordered or weakened; the check is one added statement. |
| SEC-7K-12 [7K] | **Digest.** The digest is defined once, by the governed function, over the committed payload in the text form the relay embeds unchanged (7D MAT-08), so that the consumer can compute the same value from the bytes it received. A conformant relay never produces a mismatch; T-7K-31 proves that for payloads of every type CON-06 consumes, including the largest permitted. |

| Result | Condition | Effect | Classification and state |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ORIGIN_MATCH` | Row found; organization, type and digest equal the entry's | The guard and the effect proceed as frozen | — |
| `ORIGIN_MISMATCH` | Row found; any of the three differs | No effect. Security signal | The *entry* is an integrity fault: a `TRANSPORT_INTEGRITY` case identified by (group, key, entry ID), reason `ORIGIN_MISMATCH`, with the entry as encrypted material. The entry is acknowledged only after that case commits. **No case is opened on (group, `event_id`)**, so the genuine event's obligation is untouched |
| `ORIGIN_ABSENT`, duplicate | No row and no matching captured source (SEC-7K-16 a); **every** `usage_events` row the entry derives is already present under `uq_ue_idempotency`, inside the evidence horizon, and the group's completion record for the `event_id` exists | No effect | The frozen duplicate path (`ALREADY_COMMITTED`), acknowledged on the completion record (7I GWY-08 a). Nothing new is applied, so nothing needed verifying. Partial evidence is not this row |
| `ORIGIN_ABSENT`, not a duplicate | No row and no matching captured source; guard evidence absent or partial, or no completion record | No effect | `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`: a class `V` consumer case for (group, `event_id`) in `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, reason `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE`, with the exact material, in one transaction (7G T-08). **The entry is not acknowledged.** It stays pending until Billing's terminal decision (SEC-7K-20) |
| `ORIGIN_ABSENT`, decided by Billing | No row; the case for (group, `event_id`) is `RESOLVED` by Billing's terminal decision (7G T-14) | No effect; the handler is not executed | The acknowledgement is requested, citing that disposition (SEC-7K-22) |
| Unavailable | The function errors, the database is unreachable, or the call is refused for authorization | No effect | `TRANSIENT_DEPENDENCY`: the dependency gate opens; nothing is counted; the entry stays pending (7G FC-10). A refusal for authorization is also an alert: it is a configuration defect and is never worked around |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SEC-7K-13 [OD-7K-04, condition 2] | **Fail closed.** In every row but the first, no billable effect is applied. |
| SEC-7K-14 [7K] | **The genuine event is not blocked by a forgery.** After an `ORIGIN_MISMATCH`, the pair (billing group, `event_id`) is still unaccounted. It is completed by a genuine delivery of the same event if one exists, or by the accounting reconciler, which captures the authoritative row and opens the case that R2 then processes (7I ACC-05). The forged entry's case is keyed by its transport position, is resolved only as an integrity case (7G class `T`), and is the evidence on which that entry alone is acknowledged (7I GWY-08 b). Acknowledging it defers nothing owed: the authoritative row exists and contradicts the entry, so the entry is not a Billing obligation. The two treatments do not meet: a mismatch never creates or touches a case on (group, `event_id`), and an `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` case is never acknowledged before Billing decides. |
| SEC-7K-15 [OD-7K-04, condition 3] | **A cleaned-up origin row is not a reason to lose an event.** An outbox row is deleted 7 days after publication once every owed group is accounted (7I ACC-06). An entry that reaches Billing later, by late delivery or replay, finds no row. It is held, never rejected: a class `V` case cannot be permanently rejected by anyone (7G DAU-02), an unresolved case never ages out (7G PRT-02), and its material follows the frozen limits (7I RET-12, -13), after which the case remains for the owner's reconciliation. |
| SEC-7K-16 [OD-7K-04, condition 4] | **Evidence for safe release**, any one of: (a) a captured source in the recovery ledger for that `event_id`, taken byte-exactly from the outbox row while it existed (7G DSR-12), whose recorded organization, type and digest equal the entry's: the function consults it when the row is absent, and a match is `ORIGIN_MATCH`; (b) Billing's reconciliation, under its own authority, establishing from authoritative owner state what usage the event stands for and recording `OWNER_RECONCILED` (7G R6, T-14). A transport position record proves that an event with that identifier was published; it holds no organization, type or digest and is **not** sufficient by itself. An operator's statement is never evidence. |
| SEC-7K-17 [OD-7K-04, condition 5] | **Idempotency and replay protections are unchanged.** The guard is still `uq_ue_idempotency` on (`organization_id`, `source_system`, `source_event_id`, `occurred_at`), with `source_event_id` the bare `event_id` for a single-metric event and `<outbox_event_id>:<metric>` for each row of a multi-metric event (7F §28.7); the first submission described only the second form. The horizon gate still precedes the guard, and the 90-day horizon is unchanged; the completion record is still written in the effect's transaction; R2 still runs the whole pipeline, including this check; a Billing event beyond its horizon still takes the frozen reconciliation path (7F RET-7F-09; 7G §35). The check adds a precondition and removes none. |
| SEC-7K-18 [7K] | **Restore.** After a lossy database event an entry of the lost interval has no row and no guard evidence: `ORIGIN_ABSENT`, not a duplicate. The supersession already keeps such entries from being read (DRS-7K-10); the check is a second, independent barrier for the consumer where a duplicate financial effect matters most. |
| SEC-7K-19 [OD-7K-04, condition 6] | **Cost and gate.** One primary-key read on the outbox per Billing event, on the primary, inside the owner transaction. Its latency, its effect on the Billing group's throughput and its interaction with the relay's claim and mark statements and with cleanup on the same table are measured by BM-7K-09 before activation (AG-7K-10). No figure is stated. Until AG-7K-10 holds, CON-06 runs as frozen and 7I RR-03 stays covered by detective controls only. |
| SEC-7K-20 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Deferred acknowledgement.** 7G T-08 acknowledges a `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` entry after the case commits, "Billing: no (§35)"; 7G §22 and BIL-04 keep a Billing entry pending until Billing's terminal decision is recorded. `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` is a `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` reason of CON-06 and takes that treatment unchanged: BIL-01 … BIL-07 apply to it as they apply to `BEYOND_HORIZON`. No usage row is inserted and no statement is issued against `billing.usage_events` (BIL-01). The case with its exact material commits in one transaction (BIL-02). The entry stays pending. On every redelivery the recovery-case gate finds `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, the handler is not executed and no acknowledgement is requested (7G §50.1; RTY-02). If one were requested, the gateway's evidence function would return no acknowledgeable evidence, because the case is not in a state in which 7G permits acknowledgement (7I GWY-08 b), and the gateway would refuse. The pair has a case, so the accounting reconciler opens no second one (7I ACC-03). The pending entry pins its key's trim watermark (RTN-7K-08). |
| SEC-7K-21 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Who decides, and what nobody else can do.** The case leaves `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` only by 7G T-14: Billing's reconciliation path, under `recovery.reconciliation.record` for its own group, records `OWNER_RECONCILED` or `SUPERSEDED_BY_OWNER_RECONCILIATION` with an owning-domain reconciliation reference (7G DAU-04; 7I OPA-05), enforced in the ledger (7G MR-18). Event operations cannot resolve the case (7G DAU-02, DAU-06), cannot reject it (class `V`), cannot run the normal handler for it (7G BIL-07) and cannot settle the financial effect: if usage is owed, it is inserted by Billing's own guarded functions under Billing's principals, from authoritative owner state (SEC-7K-16 b), never from an operator's statement and never from the held bytes as such. The case is never resolved by elapsed time or by cleanup (7G BIL-07, PRT-02). |
| SEC-7K-22 [FROZEN + 7K] | **Acknowledgement after the decision, under the existing sequence.** Once T-14 has committed, the entry's next delivery, by the sweep at the latest `reclaim_min_idle` plus one sweep period later, finds the `RESOLVED` case. The worker executes no handler and requests the acknowledgement; the gateway finds the case as evidence (7I GWY-08 b; 7G T-14, "Billing: yes, now"), inserts the accounting row and only then issues `XACK` (INV-7K-08). **Retry.** A failed or lost `XACK`, or Redis unavailable: the entry is still pending and the same steps repeat; the accounting insert is a no-op on its key and `XACK` is idempotent (7G PAT-06). `XACK` returning 0, or the key already destroyed by a supersession: the recorded disposition stands and nothing is repeated (7G BIL-06, PAT-05). **Crash after the case commit:** the redelivery finds the case; no handler runs. **Crash after the decision, before `XACK`:** as the retry. 7G RTY-02 lets the pipeline continue for a case resolved without a terminal rejection, which for CON-06 would send an entry Billing has already decided back to the horizon gate and this check. Frozen BIL-04 says that 7G acknowledges such an entry citing the disposition, and 7K follows BIL-04; the wording of RTY-02 for this one case is part of CNF-7K-05. |
| SEC-7K-23 [7K] | **A forged entry and the genuine one, in every order.** |

| Authoritative row | Forged entry (same `event_id`, different organization, type or bytes) | Genuine entry | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| Present | `ORIGIN_MISMATCH`: integrity case on its position; acknowledged after that case commits | `ORIGIN_MATCH`: processed once | One effect. The order of the two does not matter: they share no case |
| Absent, the event was already processed inside the horizon | Derives the same rows: duplicate, nothing applied. Derives other rows: not a duplicate, held | Duplicate path | No second effect. A held forgery waits for Billing and blocks nothing: the genuine obligation is already complete |
| Absent, the event was never processed | The first of the two to commit opens the case with its bytes; the other is recorded on the same case as a differing variant (7G RPM-07, PAT-07) and is not acknowledged either | Same case | No effect from either. Billing decides from authoritative owner state, not from which variant arrived first, so the forgery can neither apply usage nor cause the genuine event to be discarded |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SEC-7K-24 [7K] | **Replay and a missing captured source.** In R2 there is no stream entry. An item whose check returns `ORIGIN_MATCH` through the captured source (SEC-7K-16 a) is processed. An item whose row and captured source are both absent and which is not a duplicate applies nothing; the case moves from `REPLAYING` to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` with reason `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` (7G T-13, hand-off to the owning domain) and waits for Billing as above. A legitimate event whose row was cleaned up under the 7-day retention therefore ends in one of three places and nowhere else: verified through a captured source; proven complete as a duplicate; or held for Billing. It is never silently acknowledged and never rejected (SEC-7K-15). |

---

## 23. Observability and Operational Thresholds

7K uses the 7J design as frozen. It defines no metric, renames none, and adds no label. Signals are cited by their 7J identifiers. Where this document says "alerted", the alert is a 7J rule whose threshold 7K is asked to supply (7J §23.4).

### 23.1 Capacity of the telemetry stack

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBS-7K-01 [7K] | **Cardinality** (HE-7K-7J-01). The figure 46 229 is arithmetic over 7J's catalogue with candidate instance counts; it is not a measured series count. 7K's instance counts differ from 7J's candidates only where §14 and §18 make them variables: the budget is recomputed as `Σ S × B × I` with the deployed `I` before activation and after every scaling-ceiling change. Two observations from the arithmetic, for the benchmark to confirm or refute: the observer class is at 5 691 per instance against the candidate per-instance limit of 6 000, a margin of about 5%, so any catalogue growth breaches it first; and the consumer class scales with workers per group, so raising `n_w` raises series linearly. The five candidate limits stay candidates until BM-7K-11. |
| OBS-7K-02 [7K] | **Alert evaluation** (HE-7K-7J-03). Rule-group evaluation time must stay below its interval at the peak series count and during an outage, when most alert expressions are true at once. Measured by BM-7K-11 in both conditions. |
| OBS-7K-03 [7K] | **Observer reads** (HE-7K-7J-04, -10). The observer uses its own database pool and one connection per ET shard (7J PRF-11). Database functions are index-supported with a statement timeout; Redis diagnostics never scan a stream or a PEL (7J PRF-08, PRF-10). Polling is no more frequent than the scrape interval. Index, hold, sealing and erasure figures are counts from maintained state (7J PRF-15). |
| OBS-7K-04 [7K] | **Traces, logs, exporters** (HE-7K-7J-05, -06, -11). Queue sizes, token-bucket rates and collector replicas are sized for the recovery scenario of §15.6, where fault-sampled spans exist for every failing item. Every queue is bounded and drops newest (7J ISO-04). Values are [MEAS] (BM-7K-11); none affects event processing. |
| OBS-7K-05 [7K] | **Regional telemetry failure** (HE-7K-7J-08). If a stack's telemetry is down, event processing continues unchanged (7J ISO-05); autoscaling freezes (ASC-7K-08); the external dead-man receiver reports the stack as blind (REG-7K-11). Operators then read authoritative state through the frozen tools. No decision of this document needs telemetry to be correct. |
| OBS-7K-06 [7K] | **Instrumentation overhead and the audio path** (HE-7K-7J-02). The candidate of 7J PRF-02 (at most 2% of per-event CPU at the 25% sampling ratio) and the zero-added-I/O rule for the audio path (7J PRF-01) are verified by BM-7K-13 and by 7J VT-7J-48. |
| OBS-7K-10 [7K] | **Storage** (HE-7K-7J-09): raw metrics 30 days at the measured series count and 15 s interval; SLO history 13 months at up to the candidate 2 000 series; traces 14 days at 25% plus the fault set; logs per 3F §18.3; alert history 13 months; the evidence prefix by its holds. Sized from BM-7K-11 inputs. All in region (7J RSD-7J-02). |

### 23.2 Thresholds

Every threshold below is provisional in 7J's sense (SLO-05): it is an operational alert level, not an objective and not a promise. Steady-state and catch-up values are separate (7J STM-04).

| Symbol | Meaning | Steady state [CAND] | During catch-up [CAND] | Derivation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oldest eligible outbox age | Publication is behind | 60 s | `T_recap,design` + catch-up time | Above the 30 s deferral plus one cycle |
| Gate open duration | Transport cannot confirm | `T_recap,alert` (10 min) | Same | RED-7K-10 |
| Replica disconnected | Approaching TF-23 | `V / 2` (80 s) | Same | RED-7K-16 |
| ET memory | Pacing zones | `θ_1`, `θ_2` | Same | BPR-7K-03 |
| Oldest pending age, per group | Group behind | `A_lag(g)` | `A_lag(g)` + catch-up time | BPR-7K-08 |
| Unaccounted pair age | Approaching a case | `T_obl / 2` | Same | BPR-7K-08 |
| Held `PUBLISHED` row age | Accounting interlock | `A_hold,alert` | Same | BPR-7K-08 |
| Trim-pin age | Residue and memory | `A_pin,warn`, `A_pin,max` | Same | RTN-7K-09 |
| Stale delivery claim | Dispatcher died | `T_claim_lease` + one sweep | Same | 7H |
| Unfinalized conversation age | Accounting not finalized | `T_fin_alert`; `T_fin_critical` | Same | VOX-7K-24 |
| Dispatch held for evidence | Provider ambiguity | 5 min | Same | §12.6 |
| Erasure step waiting | Journal or pass behind | 15 min; `T_dl,warn` before a deadline | Same (never relaxed) | RTN-7K-07 |
| Overdue-unprocessed content | Pass behind; guard denies | One pass period | Same | RTN-7K-21 |
| Quarantine | Database not activated | Immediate | — | DRS-7K-08 |
| `T_fold`, `T_final_lag`, `T_uncertain`, `T_unsettled` | Observation accounting | §23.4 | §23.4 | §23.4 |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBS-7K-07 [7K] | A threshold never triggers an automatic action on durable state. It alerts, scales a stateless role inside its ceiling, or changes a pacing zone. |
| OBS-7K-08 [7K] | Invariant alerts (7J class INVARIANT) have no catch-up variant and are in force from activation. |
| OBS-7K-09 [7K] | Every threshold is revisited after BM-7K-14 with the measured distributions; until then its status in the 7J rulebook stays provisional. |

### 23.3 Recovery observability

Each recovery of this document is observable through signals 7J already defines: gate state and outcome classes (relay); capability-gate failures by check; shard and replica state; reclaim, failure, parking, hold and isolation counts; replay operation and item states; unaccounted pairs and cases; journal step states and quarantine state; the Voice finalization signals; fold and window state. 7K requires that the two procedures it adds emit operation records in the recovery ledger's operation component and the audit trail: replica replacement (as a governed topology operation) and transport supersession (as an incident operation). Neither needs a new metric name; whether one is added is 7J's decision under its registry rules (IO-7K-12).

### 23.4 Observation accounting: validation of the 7J parameters (closes 7J HE-7K-7J-14)

7J leaves these to 7K: the lookback `L`, the fold cadence, `T_fold`, the settle delay, the grace `G`, the permitted watermark lag `T_final_lag`, the margin `m` against each source's guaranteed life `R_min`, `T_uncertain`, `T_unsettled`, and the 45-day candidate minimum for lifecycle evidence. 7K analyses each against the frozen rules. **Nothing here is measured.** The conclusions are conditions the measurement must test.

**Symbols.** `P_rf` routine fold period; `P_cf` completeness fold period; `D_cf` duration of one completeness fold; `B` the largest latency boundary of a stream's indicators; `Λ` the lifecycle-evidence lifetime.

| ID | Rule or finding |
|---|---|
| OAC-7K-01 [7K] | **The fold cannot delay the event plane, by construction.** It takes only its own stream lock, without waiting; it reads committed rows without row locks; it writes only accounting relations (7J AOA-04, AOA-09). The relay's claim uses `SKIP LOCKED` on rows the fold never locks. The fold has its own pool and a statement timeout. What is measured is its load on the primary's shared resources (BM-7K-12), not a blocking risk. |
| OAC-7K-02 [7K] | **`L`.** Correctness does not depend on it: the completeness fold has no lookback (7J AOA-05). `L` must exceed `P_rf` plus the longest interval between a source row's timestamp and its visibility, or the routine fold systematically misses rows and leaves them to the completeness fold. The candidate of one hour is kept. |
| OAC-7K-03 [7K] | **`R_min` per stream**, from the frozen rules: 7 days for the two outbox-based streams (rows live at least 7 days after publication; unlimited while unpublished); 30 days for the delivery-based streams. |
| OAC-7K-04 [7K] | **`m`.** A registration gap is declared when two consecutive committed completeness folds started more than `R_min − m` apart (7J AOA-11). For the rule to be sound, a row visible to neither fold must have been deletable in between; a row published just after the first fold's snapshot lives at least `R_min` after that. The second fold reads it only if it reaches it before deletion, so `m ≥ D_cf,max` plus the cleanup's timing slack. Candidate `m` = 24 hours. With `R_min` = 7 days a gap is then declared after six days without a completeness fold. |
| OAC-7K-05 [7K] | **`P_cf` sets the finalization delay.** An hour `H` can be finalized only by a completeness fold that started after `end(H) + B + settle delay` (7J AOA-20 F2). With the frozen minimum of one completeness fold per day, hours become final in a daily step, and an indicator's window is up to `P_cf + B + G + D_cf` behind real time: about 25 to 26 hours with the candidates. An hourly budget evaluation (7J EB-02) then sees the window move once a day. |
| OAC-7K-06 [CAND] | **Candidate cadence.** `P_rf` = 15 s (the scrape interval, the frozen maximum rate). `P_cf` = 1 hour **if** BM-7K-12 shows its load acceptable; otherwise daily, with the consequence of OAC-7K-05 accepted and stated on the dashboard. `G` = 1 hour and settle delay ≤ `G`, as 7J's candidates. |
| OAC-7K-07 [7K] | **`T_final_lag`** must be at least `P_cf + B_max + G + D_cf,max + margin`, or a healthy stream alerts as stale and its downstream finalizes around it (7J AOA-20 F3). Candidate: `P_cf` + 2 hours. |
| OAC-7K-08 [7K] | **`T_fold`** (oldest unregistered age) must exceed several routine periods and stay far below `R_min − m`. Candidate 15 minutes. |
| OAC-7K-09 [7K] | **Lifecycle band width.** The band of a status transition runs from its post-lock clock to the earlier of the fold's first sighting and the next transition's lock (7J AOA-27 e). Its width is about the lifecycle transaction's remaining duration plus one routine fold period: `w ≈ t_tx + P_rf`. |
| OAC-7K-10 [7K] | **Uncertain deliveries per transition.** A delivery is `uncertain` when its event-to-deadline window meets a band and holds no certainly non-`ACTIVE` instant. For an organization with first-delivery rate `λ_d` the expected number per transition is about `λ_d × w`. With `w` near 15 s, an organization producing one delivery per second yields about fifteen; the probability of at least one is `1 − e^(−λ_d × w)`. |
| OAC-7K-11 [7K] | **Finding: SLI-11 will often have no verdict.** One `uncertain` delivery on a timing band keeps the whole SLI-11 window without a verdict until its cohort hour leaves the window, up to 28 days (OD-7J-14). By OAC-7K-10, almost every suspension or reactivation of an organization that is actively receiving deliveries produces at least one. SLI-11 therefore has a verdict only in windows with no such transition in the preceding 28 days. This is frozen, intended, fail-closed behaviour (7J RR-7J-15), and 7K changes nothing. It is reported to the owner as a fact to weigh before approving any objective on SLI-11 (7J EB-01 requires an indicator established in three of the last four windows). **`T_uncertain`** is therefore an informational threshold; candidate: any non-zero count raises a ticket-severity notice, not a page. |
| OAC-7K-12 [7K] | **Finding: every database incarnation change blanks every accounted indicator for 28 days.** The first fold after activation appends a `RESTORE_GAP` for every stream (7J AOA-14), and one unmeasured hour keeps a window out of every verdict (OD-7J-11). The epoch is the incarnation, which changes on a lossless promotion too. The frequency of verdict-less periods is therefore the frequency of database promotions and restores, which is a property of the managed service and is [MEAS]. It is residual risk RR-7K-07 and is reported, not changed. |
| OAC-7K-13 [7K] | **Adequacy condition for the lifecycle-evidence lifetime `Λ`.** A delivery is settled at the first fold evaluation at or after its deadline plus the settle delay, and can be classified only if its window begins at or after the organization's `covered_from`, which is always older than `Λ` (7J AOA-27 c). The evidence is adequate for a delivery exactly when the time from its event's `occurred_at` to its settlement is below `Λ`. That time is `D_settle = A_fanout + D_reg + B + settle delay`, where `A_fanout` is the delay from the event to the creation of the delivery row and `D_reg` the delay to registration and evaluation (at most `P_cf` plus any observer outage). |
| OAC-7K-14 [7K] | **Assessment of 45 days.** (a) *Normal path:* `A_fanout` is seconds; `D_settle` is at most about a day. Adequate with a large margin. (b) *Design outages:* `A_fanout` up to the outage plus catch-up; an observer outage up to six days before a gap is declared (OAC-7K-04). Adequate. (c) *Recovery deliveries:* a fan-out performed by an R2 replay of a parked case, by an R3 backfill, or after a long consumer outage can create a first delivery more than 45 days after its event; replay material alone lives up to 90 days (OD-7I-09). For an organization whose older transitions have been removed, such a delivery's window begins before `covered_from`. **45 days is not adequate for these.** (d) *Restore:* the restored evidence is consistent with the restored rows; the hours are unmeasured anyway. |
| OAC-7K-15 [7K] | **Fail-closed response, already frozen, and sufficient.** In case (c) the delivery is settled `uncertain` with basis `EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE`, and the window is `NOT_ESTABLISHED`: no verdict, no budget movement (7J AOA-27 g; AOA-24). Nothing is assumed `ACTIVE`. 7K therefore does not accept 45 days as sufficient in general: it is sufficient for (a), (b) and (d), and for (c) it relies on the fail-closed branch. |
| OAC-7K-16 [7K] | **Validation required before the value is fixed** (T-7K-39; OD-7J-15 conditions). Measure the distribution of `D_settle` including recovery deliveries, the rate of case (c), and the resulting rate of `NOT_ESTABLISHED` windows. If that rate is unacceptable to the owner, the remedy is a larger `Λ` covering the replay horizon (at least 90 days plus `P_cf` and margin), which is the owner's to approve through 7L because it lengthens the retention of lifecycle provenance. 7K recommends neither value as final. |
| OAC-7K-17 [7K] | **A finite lifecycle.** Evidence is created in the lifecycle transaction; confirmed by the Organization-side pass; kept at least `Λ`; and removed by that pass in a prefix, moving `covered_from` forward (7J AOA-27 c). Its maximum age in normal operation is `Λ` plus the pass period plus the time the organization's row was locked when the pass tried. The latest transition of an organization is kept until its confirmation is older than `Λ` (7J RR-7J-16). 7K sets the pass period ([CAND] daily) and nothing else; the final finite maximum, with erasure, backup and restore treatment, is 7L's to reconcile and AG-7J-14 stays closed until then (7J HE-7L-7J-07). **`T_unsettled`** is set well below `Λ`: candidate 30 days. |
| OAC-7K-18 [7K] | **Volumes to measure** (BM-7K-12): obligation records per stream against their lifetime (7J AOA-23); hour-ledger and cohort rows; routine and completeness fold duration; the window read functions (at most 672 rows per scope); index cost on the outbox, the delivery history, the completion records and the ledger; post-final corrections for past-dated origins, of which EV-079 is the main source because its `occurred_at` is the service-end time (7B OCC-01) and recovery finalization can be hours late; the lifecycle listing and the share-lock cost of the confirmation pass against delivery admissions. |

## 24. SLO Candidates and Benchmark Methodology

### 24.1 Status of objectives

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| SLO-7K-01 [OD-7J-01] | Every numeric event-pipeline objective of 7J §11.2 remains `PROPOSED / REQUIRES BENCHMARK`. 7K approves none, attains none and promotes none. |
| SLO-7K-02 [7K] | 7K's contribution is the benchmark that produces the evidence (BM-7K-14) and the analytical observations below, which are hypotheses for that benchmark and not results. |
| SLO-7K-03 [7K] | **Observation on SLO-P1** (99% of rows published within 2 s). `published_at` is set after the durable confirmation. Under `appendfsync everysec` the confirmation waits for a background fsync on two nodes (7E §50.2: "up to about a second"), and an idle loop adds up to `d_idle_max`. Whether 99% within 2 s is reachable is open; the benchmark must report the distribution of `W` before the candidate is considered. |
| SLO-7K-04 [7K] | **Observation on the consumer objectives.** An entry is readable by a group as soon as `XADD` returns, before its confirmation. Occurrence-to-completion (SLI-17) is therefore not gated by the confirmation wait. It is gated by the gateway round trips and the handler path (§17.3), and for any entry held by a dead worker by `reclaim_min_idle` (CSM-7K-02). |
| SLO-7K-05 [7K] | **Observation on recovery latency.** After a single ET node failure, publication pauses for `T_recap` (RED-7K-10). Every publication objective is missed for that interval. An objective approved without regard to `T_recap` and to the failure rate of ET nodes would be unattainable by design. |
| SLO-7K-06 [7K] | **Observation on measurability.** OAC-7K-11 and OAC-7K-12: an objective on an accounted indicator has no verdict for 28 days after any database promotion, and SLI-11 has none after most suspensions of active organizations. |
| SLO-7K-07 [FROZEN] | Invariants are not objectives and have no budget (7J SLO-06). |
| SLO-7K-08 [7K] | Benchmark results are delivered to the owner with their conditions. Approval is the owner's (7J HE-7L-7J-11). |

### 24.2 Benchmarks

None has been executed. Each states what it measures, under which load, and which parameters it fixes. "Load" refers to the shapes of §15.2 at a declared scale; the scale is an input the owner supplies or the first production measurements supply, and every result is reported with it.

| ID | Measures | Method | Fixes |
|---|---|---|---|
| BM-7K-01 | Relay publication: `XADD` throughput; distribution of `W`; batches per shard; loop throughput for `n_loop` = 1 … `n`; with and without instrumentation | Synthetic outbox rows of the measured size mix; three-shard ET cluster as §9; the frozen batch and lease | `t_wao`, `T_publish`, idle delays, `n_loop,max`; evidence for or against any change of batch or lease (7D POL-07); 7J HE-7K-7J-12 |
| BM-7K-02 | Outbox behaviour under backlog: claim latency at `N_b` pending rows; duplicate rate against `σ`; vacuum behaviour; import and campaign bursts | Backlogs of increasing size; injected process pauses | `σ`; `ρ_cu`; job concurrency bounds |
| BM-7K-03 | ET memory and placement: `o_e`, `o_p`, `o_g`, `s̄_f`; single-key ceiling; rewrite copy-on-write; per-shard CPU by family | Per-family streams at measured sizes; rewrite under peak append | Slot placement; `f_mem`; `θ_1`, `θ_2`; `N_signal`; `ρ_key` |
| BM-7K-04 | Replication under peak append: replica lag distribution; `NOREPLICAS` rate | Peak append on each shard | `LAG_BOUND` |
| BM-7K-05 | Consumption: handler time per group; gateway capacity `c_gw`; round-trip cost; recovery-case read; completion and accounting inserts; token cost; lag under load | Each group at its peak mix through the gateway | `COUNT`, `BLOCK`, `K_in`, `n_w`, `A_lag(g)`, `T_token`, pressure thresholds, reclaim tuning |
| BM-7K-06 | Per-entry database overhead: tenant-context sequence; guarded calls; lock hold of CON-03 and CON-09; key-service call time; transition proofs | Micro-benchmarks and group runs | Budgets of §17.3; key-service timeout |
| BM-7K-07 | Autoscaling behaviour: reaction time, oscillation, scale-in churn | Step and ramp loads | Scaling windows and ceilings |
| BM-7K-08 | Delivery: dispatcher throughput; per-attempt transactions; admission lock wait; list-route cost with the guard | Endpoints of mixed latency; mutation concurrent with admission | Dispatcher values; `T_handoff`; lock timeout |
| BM-7K-09 | Origin check: added latency per Billing event; throughput of the Billing group with and without it; contention with claim, mark and cleanup on the outbox | Billing group at peak with a concurrently draining relay and a running cleanup | AG-7K-10 |
| BM-7K-10 | Storage growth: ledger, material, provenance, deliveries, history, accounting, completion, journal | Sustained run with the frozen retentions scaled in time | §17 sizing |
| BM-7K-11 | Telemetry: series at scale; scrape cost; rule evaluation in steady state and outage; trace and log ingestion | The catalogue at deployed instance counts | The five limits; queue sizes; storage |
| BM-7K-12 | Observation accounting and observer reads: fold durations; primary load; window reads; lifecycle listing | Streams at scale; routine and completeness folds | `P_cf`, `T_final_lag`, `m`, `T_fold` |
| BM-7K-13 | Voice isolation: turn-loop latency with the event plane idle, at peak, in catch-up and during replay | Synthetic calls with the full event plane under each condition | Evidence for INV-7K-13; input to P23 |
| BM-7K-14 | Each candidate objective under normal load, peak load, dependency outage, consumer recovery, durability shortfall and catch-up | The scenarios of §15.6 end to end | The evidence 7J OD-7J-01 condition 5 requires |
