## 25. Operational Runbooks and Escalation

### 25.1 Runbooks

Runbooks are specified by their decision content. None exists as a file.

| ID | Situation | Entry signal | Decisions and order | Never |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RB-7K-01 | Publication paused (gate open) | Gate-open duration | Identify the failing capability check; follow RB-7K-02 or -03; confirm the outbox reserve | Mark rows; restart relays to "clear" the gate |
| RB-7K-02 | Shard without capability, node expected back | `NO_REPLICA` and others of §9.3 | Wait up to `T_rejoin_wait`. A node that returns after a process start is flagged: admit it by steps 6 – 8 of RED-7K-18 through the states of RED-7K-34. A node that returned in the same process and whose baseline does not hold: E-1 first, then the same admission (RED-7K-38). A `WAITAOF` shortfall after admission is diagnosed here and reopens no admission (RED-7K-35) | Manual promotion; lowering `min-replicas-to-write`; publishing a test entry to "prove" the replica; waiting for a confirmation before declaring the admission complete; recording a node as not admitted without setting its flag; treating a recorded state as if it withheld capability |
| RB-7K-03 | Replica replacement | Wait exceeded; volume lost; replica AOF fault; TF-23 | RED-7K-18 steps 1 – 8, with approval, in the order of RED-7K-34: record `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` (step 6); clear the flag in the running configuration, confirm, attest the running nodes and store the baseline (step 7); record `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE` when the frozen check holds (step 8); note the first confirmed position afterwards (RED-7K-35). On failure RED-7K-31 | Joining an unattested node; reusing an unwiped node; making the node failover-eligible before `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED`; writing `no` to a start configuration; declaring capability on elapsed time; holding the operation open, or publication closed, for a first confirmation; a canary write |
| RB-7K-04 | ET memory pressure | AMBER or RED | Find the pinning group or operation; restore it; RTN-7K-09 | Deleting entries, keys or groups; raising `maxmemory` beyond `f_mem` |
| RB-7K-05 | Consumer group behind or down | Lag over `A_lag` | Restore workers, gateway or dependency; watch `T_obl` | Acknowledging to clear a backlog |
| RB-7K-06 | Transport state lost, database intact | Shard `LOST`; integrity signals | Rebuild the shard; compute the loss set; PRK-7K-11; approvals | Replaying before the shard is capable and onboarded |
| RB-7K-07 | Missing stream entry | Integrity case | PRK-7K-13 | Clearing pending IDs before cases are durable |
| RB-7K-08 | Parked and reconciliation-pending cases ageing | Case age | Owner reconciliation; R2 after a fix; class authority of 7G §24.4 | Rejecting a class `V` case |
| RB-7K-09 | Voice finalization stalled or corrupt | VOX-7K-24 signals | Find the cause; Voice-owner repair; clear the marker; re-invoke | Supplying totals or a time by hand |
| RB-7K-10 | Database restore or promotion | Incarnation change; quarantine | §21.5 RS-1 … RS-12 | Activating without the journal; serving a replica because it is present; reading any entry of a superseded key; capturing a restored row that is not `CLEAR`; opening a second case for an identity that has one; moving a case by a recovery step other than the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c); moving a pair to owner reconciliation because an R4 plan stopped while its capture is eligible; approving a successor plan as anything but the unprocessed remainder; treating a new plan as a new deadline (MRA-7K-17); admitting outbox cleanup before the R4 holds its captured sources; deleting a key through break-glass |
| RB-7K-11 | Billing gate closed | Clearance absent | §21.8 reconciliation; clearance | Releasing by decision alone; inferring non-payment from absence |
| RB-7K-12 | Enterprise provisioning; conformance | Request; drift | REG-7K-10 | Converting an existing organization; provisioning on a stale record |
| RB-7K-13 | Topology generation migration | GEN-7K-01 met | GM-1 … GM-12 | Changing `P_f` in place; live resharding; retiring with a pending entry; emptying a PEL by any command other than a genuine acknowledgement |
| RB-7K-14 | Handler-contract cutover window | Governed change | 7F CUT-1 … CUT-9 with CSM-7K-11 … -15 | Starting during an outage or catch-up |
| RB-7K-15 | Regional outage | External dead-man | REG-7K-13, -14; class E: wait or restore in region; class S: DRS-7K-05 | Any automatic cross-region action; activating on a journal copy whose head cannot be confirmed |

### 25.2 Escalation

| Level | Trigger | Who |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A provisional threshold of §23.2 | On-call for the owning service |
| 2 | An invariant alert; a quarantine; a missed privacy deadline; a held financial operation; a residency drift | On-call, the service owner and the security or privacy owner, immediately (7J severities) |
| 3 | A class S promotion decision; a class E regional loss; a provider that cannot supply reconciliation records | The platform owner |

An escalation is accountability. It never authorizes an action that a frozen rule forbids, and it never ends a wait that only evidence or a recorded decision can end (FCL-7K-08).

### 25.3 Incident completion

An incident involving the event plane is closed only when: the overload exit of BPR-7K-17 holds; every gate is closed; no shard is below `CAPABLE`; every operation opened for it is terminal; every case it opened is resolved or has a named owning-domain reconciliation; where a restore occurred, §21.9 holds; and the lost or delayed work is stated in numbers read from authoritative state.

---

Common preconditions unless a row says otherwise: a three-shard event-transport cluster as §9; the components under test implemented with their governed migrations; a synthetic load of the §15.2 shapes at a declared scale; two organizations at least. ### 26.1 Regression specifications for the freeze-gate findings

**Execution status: SPECIFIED — NOT EXECUTED.** None of the cases below has been run. The common preconditions and the common security condition of §26 apply to each. "Fails if" is the failure result: any one observation listed there fails the case. "Idempotency" states what a repeated run must leave unchanged.

**T-7K-11 (a) … (am): durability admission of a restored replica (FG-7K-05, FG-7K-R3-01, FG-7K-R4-01, FG-7K-R5-02, FG-7K-R6-01, FG-7K-R7-01, FG-7K-R7-02).** *Frozen requirement:* 7E ACC-01, ACC-02, ACC-07, WAO-01 … WAO-05, WAO-07, FMD-03, FMD-06, FMD-09, MF-01, MF-04, MF-08, MF-22, TF-01 … TF-05, TF-09 … TF-11, TF-15, TF-16, TF-21, TF-22, CAP-04, CAP-07, HP-05, HP-06, RC-01, RC-02, TOPO-11, TRM-01 … TRM-07, TRM-09, BST-01 … BST-04, RET-03; 7D PRB-02, PRB-05, AMB-01 … AMB-05, DSP-03, TPT-10, CLN-05; 7G BKL-02, BKL-05, BKL-09, BKL-10; 7I ACC-03, ACC-11, ACC-13, ACC-15, ACC-16, ACC-18, ACC-19; OD-7E-02. **Oracle (fifth remediation).** Every statement below that a required entry is present, missing or lost is decided by the retained-set oracle of RED-7K-39. A position record whose entry was trimmed under 7E TRM-02 is expected to be absent and never fails a case. Cases (x) … (aa) were added in the fifth remediation, and cases (ab) … (ae) in the sixth; with (aa) they are the counterexamples A … H of FG-7K-R6-01 (§32.11). **Oracle (seventh remediation).** Every case fixes `C_q` and `U_q` at its quiesce point, records TE-7 for what it publishes and TE-1 … TE-5 for every removal, and demands after the fault the IDs of `C_q` and no others. No case judges a removal by the watermark of `q`, and none demands an entry because it was present. Cases (af) … (am) were added in the seventh remediation; with (x), (y), (aa), (ab), (d) and the cases of T-7K-10 and T-7K-16 below they are the regression set of FG-7K-R7-01 and FG-7K-R7-02 (§32.12). *Preconditions:* a shard with confirmed entries, each a `PUBLISHED` row with a position record (7I ACC-13), and entries without confirmation only where a case introduces them; synthetic payloads; the deployed Redis build, configuration revision and volume class. *Privacy and security:* the replacement node is attested and carries the ACL digest before it joins; the oracle reads entry IDs, never test bodies into a log. *Gate:* AG-7K-02. Cases (b), (c), (g) and (o) are the validation of V-1, V-2 and V-3; until they pass on the deployed pair of builds, RED-7K-31 refuses admission and AG-7K-02 is closed. Cases (i) … (m) were added in the third remediation; each asserts the state the operation record shows (RED-7K-34) and that no publication, canary or `WAITAOF` reply was needed to reach it. Cases (n) … (w) were added in the fourth remediation. **In every case the oracle for "not capable" is the frozen probe and the `nofailover` flag in `CLUSTER NODES`, never the operation record**; a case fails if the record says *not admitted* while Redis shows the replica unflagged and nothing sets the flag.

| Case | Fault injection and exact sequence | Authoritative state and evidence | Expected result | Fails if | Crash / restart; idempotency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Replace a replica (RED-7K-18). Attempt publication at every step before step 8 | Outbox rows; position records; `CLUSTER NODES` flags; operation record | Zero `CONFIRMED` outcomes and zero rows marked `PUBLISHED` before `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`; the node shows `nofailover` until step 7, and step 7 starts only from `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` | Any confirmation before `REPLICA_ADMITTED_AND_CAPABLE`; the node is failover-eligible before `HISTORICAL_DURABILITY_VERIFIED` | Repeating the procedure on an admitted shard changes nothing |
| (b) V-1 | Before the replacement, let every group acknowledge a prefix of each key and let the trim worker run, so that the primary's dataset has already been trimmed. After the replica reports its post-synchronization rewrite complete, kill the replica process; separately, cut power to its host. The ID of `U_q` is present or absent; that is recorded and fails nothing | An ID of `C_q` is missing; the case passes with `C_q` empty; the ID of `U_q` is demanded | A second kill gives the same result |

Kill the primary's process, and separately its host, and hold it down beyond the node timeout (7E TF-01, TF-03) | `C_q`, `U_q`; O-4 on the promoted node; `CLUSTER NODES`; outbox | The replica is promoted and holds every ID of `C_q`: its fsync preceded the confirmation. Every ID of `C_q` is on the node started alone (O-4). The entries trimmed before `q` are not demanded, whether or not they are present again. O-5 is not applied: no rewrite completed. **T-7K-22 (a) … (e): ordinary generation retirement (FG-7K-03).** *Frozen requirement:* 7E LCY-07 steps 3 – 5; 7F TKO-07, HCG-17; 7G TPG-05, BIL-04; 7I ACC-15, OPA-07, RDS-10. *Preconditions:* two generations live after GM-7; every group consuming. *Privacy and security:* the boundary reading extracts an ID only; each destructive command is issued by the identity that holds it, and the case fails if another identity is used. *Gate:* AG-7K-13.

| Case | Fault injection and exact sequence | Authoritative state and evidence | Expected result | Fails if | Crash / restart; idempotency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Kill a worker that holds an entry on an old key; request GM-10 at once | `XPENDING`; accounting rows | R-5 fails; after the sweep reclaims and the entry is processed and acknowledged, R-5 holds | Retirement proceeds with a pending entry | GM-10 re-read before GM-11 gives the same result |
| (b) | Drop the reply of an `XACK` on an old key | `XPENDING`; accounting row | If the entry is still pending, R-5 fails until the redelivery acknowledges; one accounting row | The entry is counted as acknowledged from the worker's view | Duplicate acknowledgement is harmless |
| (c) | A Billing entry on an old key is held for reconciliation | Case state; `XPENDING` | R-5 fails for as long as Billing has not decided; after T-14 and the acknowledgement, it holds | Retirement proceeds on "a case with material exists" | — |
| (d) | A case exists in `OPEN_RETRY` for a pending entry | Ledger; `XPENDING` | R-5 and R-6 both fail | Either is treated as satisfied by the case | — |
| (e) | Pause a relay before GM-8; resume it after GM-9 | Stream last ID; the relay's outcome | The `XADD` is refused; the row is republished to the new generation; `B(K)` unchanged | An entry appears above `B(K)` | — |
| restart | Kill the operation between `XGROUP DESTROY` and key deletion | Per-key recorded state | Resumes at `GROUPS_DESTROYED`; nothing consumed in between | A key is deleted without recorded R-1 … R-10 | A second run finds `DELETED` |

**T-7K-31 (a) … (j): Billing origin verification and deferred acknowledgement (FG-7K-02).** *Frozen requirement:* 7G BIL-01 … BIL-07, T-08, T-13, T-14, PAT-05 … PAT-07, DAU-02, DAU-04; 7F RET-7F-08, RET-7F-09, §28.7; 7I GWY-08, GWY-10, ACC-06. *Preconditions:* CON-06 with the check enabled; events of each of its five types; the 90-day horizon unchanged. *Privacy and security:* the origin function returns no payload; an authorization refusal is an alert; no operator identity can resolve the case or insert usage. *Gate:* AG-7K-10.

| Case | Fault injection and exact sequence | Authoritative state and evidence | Expected result | Fails if | Crash / restart; idempotency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) Origin match | Deliver a genuine entry | Outbox row; `usage_events`; completion; accounting | One row set; acknowledged after accounting | — | Redelivery inserts nothing |
| (b) Origin mismatch | Publish an entry with a real `event_id` and altered bytes, row present | Integrity case on (group, key, entry ID); no case on (group, `event_id`) | Nothing applied; that entry acknowledged after its case commits; the pair still open | A case exists on (group, `event_id`); usage inserted | Redelivery of the forgery finds its case |
| (c) Absent, complete duplicate proof | Delete the row after accounting; redeliver the entry | `usage_events` rows; completion record | `ALREADY_COMMITTED`; acknowledged; no new row | A second row set | — |
| (d) Absent, no duplicate proof | Deliver an entry with no row, no captured source, no usage rows | Case `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE`, material; `XPENDING` | No usage; **entry still pending**; no accounting row | `XACK` issued; accounting row written; usage inserted | Ten redeliveries: no handler run, no acknowledgement, one case |
| (e) Row legitimately cleaned up | Process an event; let 7 days pass and cleanup run; deliver a late duplicate; separately deliver a late entry of an event held more than 7 days by a key-level hold | As (c) for the first; as (d) for the second | First: duplicate. Second: held for Billing, not rejected, not applied | Either is silently dropped, rejected or applied unverified | — |
| (f) Missing captured source | R2 of a case whose row and captured source are both gone | Item result; case state | Nothing applied; `REPLAYING → RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` | The item is recorded as processed | Re-running the item gives the same state |
| (g) Redis retry after reconciliation | Record Billing's T-14; make `XACK` fail, then make Redis unavailable, then restore it | Case `RESOLVED`; accounting row; `XPENDING` | Acknowledged after recovery; one accounting row | The handler runs; a second accounting row | `XACK` returning 0 leaves the disposition unchanged |
| (h) Crash after case commit | Kill the worker after the case of (d) commits | Case; `XPENDING` | Entry pending; redelivery finds the case; no handler | Acknowledgement; second case | — |
| (i) Crash after the decision, before `XACK` | Kill the gateway after the accounting insert | Accounting row; `XPENDING` | Next delivery acknowledges; insert is a no-op | Usage inserted by the worker | — |
| (j) Concurrent forged and genuine | Deliver both at once, with the row present; repeat with the row absent and unprocessed | Cases; `usage_events` | Row present: one effect, one integrity case. Row absent: one case with two variants, nothing applied, neither acknowledged | The forgery blocks, discards or duplicates the genuine effect | Both orders give the same final state |

**T-7K-40 (c) … (g): cross-region journal copy (FG-7K-04; OD-7K-05).** *Frozen requirement:* 7I JRN-04, JRN-05, JRN-06, JRN-13, JRN-16, JRN-18, JRN-20, JRN-29, RSY-13, KEY-05; OD-7K-02 conditions 1 – 8; OD-7K-05 (option B). *Preconditions:* CNF-7K-10 consolidated and implemented; a class S stack with the record of REG-7K-17; a recovery region ready (REG-7K-24). **These cases cannot be run today** (REG-7K-16). *Privacy and security:* no class E stack is used; the origin's keys are verify-and-decrypt only in the recovery region; no byte of a class E stack leaves its region in any case. *Gate:* AG-7K-08 for class S cross-region recovery.

| Case | Fault injection and exact sequence | Authoritative state and evidence | Expected result | Fails if | Crash / restart; idempotency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (c) | Stop journal replication; commit an erasure at the origin; let PostgreSQL replicate the step confirmation; promote | `S_db`, `H_j`; activation record | C-3 fails; quarantine persists; nothing served | An activation record is issued | A second reconciliation fails the same way |
| (d) | Stop both replications after the copy's last record; commit an erasure at the origin; promote with the origin store readable, then repeat with it unreachable | `H_o`, `H_j`; activation record | Readable: C-4 fails. Unreachable: `H_o` cannot be established. Quarantine persists in both | Activation in either | — |
| (e) | Replace one copied object with a different record of the same sequence number | Chain hash; object versions | C-2 fails; quarantine persists | The record is applied | — |
| (f) | Promote with the origin object store permanently unreachable. Present in turn: a provider replication-lag figure of zero; the copy's own head; a copied `CHECKPOINT`; a healthy monitoring status; a recorded administrator assertion that the copy is complete. Then attempt each override available to an operator, an application service and the incident process | `H_o` (absent); activation record; incident record; audit | Completeness is unknown. The stack stays quarantined; ordinary reads and writes are denied; no consumption, replay or financial run starts; no protected content is read; no journal head is created in the recovery region; the incident record holds the evidence and the unresolved outcome | An activation record is issued on any of the five substitutes; any guarded function serves; a sequence is started under `R_r`; the outcome is recorded as recovered | Repeating the attempt gives the same result; the quarantine has no expiry |
| (g) | With the copy authorized, make the recovery region and the replication channel unavailable. In the origin run a subject erasure, a time-based batch, a seal, a hold placement and a key destruction past their escalation thresholds | Step states; origin journal; deadline records | Every operation completes in the origin under the frozen write-ahead protocol and waits for nothing in the recovery region; no deadline is extended or recomputed because of replication lag | An origin operation waits at `INTENT_REQUESTED` for the recovery region; a deadline is derived from the lag | — |
| after | Promote with a complete copy; append in the recovery region | New chain's first record; origin copy | The first record names the origin head and hash; no origin sequence number reused; the copy is not writable | A new record under an origin sequence number | A later reconciliation accepts only that lineage |

**T-7K-46 (a) … (aw): admission of recovered material (FG-7K-01, FG-7K-R2-01, FG-7K-R2-02, FG-7K-R3-02, FG-7K-R5-01, FG-7K-R6-02; case (ae) rewritten and cases (as) … (aw) added in the sixth remediation; case (ab) from the fourth remediation; case (aa) rewritten and cases (ac) … (ar) added in the fifth).** For (aa) and (ac) … (ar): `T_obl` is set to a short test value; "frozen age" is database `now()` minus the earliest `recorded_at` of the pair's positions (7I ACC-17); every expected result names the permitted transition and the behaviour of the deadline, and the oracle reads PostgreSQL. *Frozen requirement:* 7I INV-09, INV-18, JRN-06, JRN-13 … JRN-27, ERA-03, ERA-08, ERA-12, ERA-13, ERA-S1 … ERA-S3, HLD-03, HLD-10, HLD-11, HLD-15, HLD-18, SEAL-01 … SEAL-10, SEAL-14, CAG-01 … CAG-18, RET-01; 7E ROLE-06; 7G RPM-01, DAU-02, PRT-02. *Determinism:* every concurrency case uses a named pause point (before the gate is requested; while the capture holds the gate; after its commit) at which the competing transaction is started and observed to block or to complete; no case depends on timing. *Preconditions:* CNF-7K-09 consolidated and implemented, except case (f); a consumer case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material for an event of a PII-bearing type; its entry on a pre-event key. *Privacy and security:* the oracle searches every store, the recovery generation, every delivery body and the tooling's logs for a marker placed in the event's payload. *Gate:* AG-7K-08.

| Case | Fault injection and exact sequence | Authoritative state and evidence | Expected result | Fails if | Crash / restart; idempotency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) The review's sequence | Commit the event; erase the subject; restore an earlier backup; reconcile the journal; run the supersession. Repeat with a seal in place of the erasure | Clearance `BARRED`; non-capture record; **the Redis command log of every identity** | No command whose reply carries an entry's fields on any superseded key; the marker is in no store, no stream, no replay item, no delivery, no view | The marker is found anywhere; `XRANGE`, `XINFO STREAM`, `XREADGROUP`, `XAUTOCLAIM` or `XCLAIM` without `JUSTID` appears in the log | A second supersession run changes nothing |
| (b) Each bar | One run per row 1 – 7 of MRA-7K-04, for a case and for a restored `PUBLISHED` row, and the hold alone | Clearance verdict and reason | `BARRED` with the row's reason in each; the hold alone is `CLEAR`; a hold with any other row is `HELD_RESTRICTED` | A `CLEAR` verdict where a bar applies | — |
| (c) Contradictory journal | Remove a journal record; alter one | Activation record | Quarantine persists; no clearance is written; the supersession does not start | Any clearance exists | — |
| (d) Concurrency of the row capture | With a `CLEAR` row, at each pause point start in turn: a subject erasure with its intent only `INTENT_REQUESTED`; one at `INTENT_DURABLE`; its erase transaction; a sealing transaction; an organization erasure; a hold placement; a hold release; a clearance revocation | Clearance state; stored source; step and seal records | Started before the gate: capture refused, clearance `REVOKED` (a hold alone: capture proceeds). Started while the gate is held: it blocks, then acts on the stored source (erasure erases it; a hold reaches it). After the commit: the same | A stored source of an erased, sealed or organization-erased subject exists when both transactions have ended; the capture holds the gate across any call outside PostgreSQL | Every order ends in the same state |
| (e) Crash at every stage of the row capture | Kill the process before the transaction; after the gate; after the row read; after the encrypted write; after the commit | Stored source; clearance; process dumps and logs | No source and an unchanged clearance in the first four; one source and `CONSUMED` in the last | Plaintext in a file, a log or a dump; two stored sources | A restart completes with one source |
| (f) No clearance mechanism | Run the restore on a build without the CNF-7K-09 elements | Operation record; RS-11 state | Nothing is captured or republished; the stack is not reopened; the restore does not complete | Any capture or republication; any reopening | — |
| (g) Read-time expiry and delay | Delay every Redis reply by more than every timeout; skew the Redis clock forwards and backwards; make the eligibility source fail (index unverified; a function raises; a lock timeout) during a row capture | Redis command log; stored source | No effect on eligibility: the capture makes no Redis call and uses the PostgreSQL clock only; a failing source gives no capture | A decision changes with the Redis delay or clock; a capture succeeds while a check could not be evaluated | — |
| (h) Outcome per reason | For each row 1 – 10 of the table of MRA-7K-10: build the state, run the reconciliation and the restore, and kill and restart the reconciler once in the middle | The row's "evidence" column; ordinary stores; vault; cases; activation record | Exactly the row: where content stays, whether it is sealed, no tombstone added by 7K, an unresolved `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` case per open obligation with the right reason family, and the quarantine verdict of the row | A case made terminal; a held or uncertain row deleted or tombstoned; activation in rows 9 or 10; content of a `BARRED` row in Redis, a replay, a delivery, a usage row or a read path | The restarted reconciler produces the same verdicts and no second case |
| (i) Attribution-uncertain row inside its retention | A restored `PUBLISHED` row whose attribution cannot be resolved, three days old, with an unaccounted group; let `T_obl` pass after reopening | Clearance `BARRED`, `ATTRIBUTION_UNPROVEN`; outbox row; case | The row is untouched and uncaptured; the accounting reconciler's capture is refused; the group has its case; the row ages out by its frozen lifecycle | The row is captured at `T_obl`; it is deleted or altered by a recovery step; no case exists | Repeated reconciler passes open no second case |
| (j) Clearance count | Remove one clearance record before activation | Activation record | Quarantine persists | Activation | — |
| (k) Billing | A `BARRED` restored row of a type CON-06 consumes | `usage_events`; cases | No usage row; no entry for it in the recovery generation; the Billing group's obligation is a case for Billing | Any usage inserted from it | — |

**Cases (l) … (z): the matrix of MRA-7K-14 (FG-7K-R3-02).** *Frozen requirement, in addition:* 7G PID-01, PID-03, RTY-02, RTY-05, RTY-10, RSM-01, RSM-02, RSM-04, T-01 … T-14, TGT-12, DSR-04, DSR-06, DSR-07, DSR-11, DSR-12, BIL-04, BIL-06; 7I ACC-03, ACC-05, ACC-06, ACC-11. **Every case asserts, in addition to its own result:** no handler of any group runs from a source that MRA-7K-15 (5) does not admit, read from the handler invocation records and the Redis command log of every identity; no (event, group) pair that was owed before the run is afterwards without an accounting row, a case or a recorded class; no `XACK` is issued without the evidence of INV-7K-08 or 7G PAT-01; no identity has two cases.

| (r) `PARKED` with material | With the row present and `CLEAR`; separately with the row absent | Case; material digest | Class RM-1. Untouched by recovery; R2 processes it from its own material. Transition (none) → `PARKED` with the stored capture | The pair is skipped because its key was superseded; a position on a superseded key is left out of the age or of the classification | — |
| (ae) Publisher down indefinitely | As (ad), for a pair with no case and for a pair with a case in `OPEN_RETRY`; hold the repair publisher down past the capture time plus `T_obl` | Operation records; item results; capture time; cases | No case: as (ad). With a case: the plan's stop condition fires and the operation is `STOPPED`; **the pair stays RM-3 and no entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) is applied for the stop**. At capture time plus `T_obl`, with no `REPAIR` position: transition `OPEN_RETRY` → `PARKED` with a copy of the stored capture; the row is listed in the recorded escalation | The pair is moved to RM-4 or to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` although its capture is eligible; the pair waits past the operational replay deadline; the reconciler reads a plan's state as a reason to wait or to act | A later plan's entry takes the parked-duplicate path; never two cases |
| (af) R4 completes just before the deadline | Pair with no case. The group processes the R4 entry and the gateway commits the accounting row one statement before the reconciler's pass at the deadline; then the opposite order | Accounting row; cases; `XPENDING` | Accounted first: no case; `XACK` after the accounting commit. Reconciler first: a `PARKED` case, and the later accounting insert also commits; both exist (7I ACC-20); R2 resolves the case as already committed | Two cases; an effect applied twice; an acknowledgement before the accounting commit | — |
| (ag) R4 crashes during publication | Kill the repair publisher after its `XADD` is confirmed and before its position record; restart | Position records; item result; stream | The item is run again: a second entry with the same `event_id`, whose position is recorded. The unrecorded entry can only add a delivery (7I ACC-18 a). The deadline is unchanged | The unrecorded entry is used to extend or to restart the deadline; the item is marked published without a position record | Idempotent on (`event_id`, key, entry ID) |
| (ah) Duplicate R4 delivery | Deliver two R4 entries of one event to a group with no case, and to a group whose case is `PARKED` | Guard rows; accounting; case observations | First group: one effect; the second entry is `ALREADY_COMMITTED`; one accounting row. Second group: the parked-duplicate path twice, no handler | Two effects; a second accounting row or a second case | — |
| (ai) Existing `OPEN_RETRY` case | Pair with a case in `OPEN_RETRY`, frozen age above `T_obl` | ACC-03 listing; case history | The pair is not listed, gets no ACC-05 response and no second case. On the R4 entry the frozen gate continues the pipeline: T-09, or T-03 at the fifth failure with the new entry's material | A second case; a transition applied on the age of 7I ACC-17, which does not apply to a pair that has a case; any transition before the operational replay deadline, or after a `REPAIR` position was recorded | — |
| (aj) Existing `HOLD` case | The same with a compatibility hold, and with a crash-loop hold that stores material | Case; material | As (ai). The compatibility hold is classified again on the new entry and parks at `compat_hold_max_age` with that entry's material; the crash-loop hold keeps its stored material | As (ai); stored crash-loop material is replaced or dropped | — |
| (ak) No case, source consumed | Pair with no case at its deadline; clearance `CONSUMED`; stored capture present | Clearance; stored capture; the case's material digest; the statement log | ACC-05 reads no outbox row; transition (none) → `PARKED` with a copy of the stored capture | A second read of the row; a case without material while the capture exists | A second pass finds the case |
| (al) Captured source erased after recovery | Erase the stored capture by a subject erasure after reopening: before the deadline; and between the reconciler's listing and its insert | Erasure step; operation record; case | Before: re-classed at once to RM-5; transition (none) → `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` by CNF-7K-09 (c), no material. During: the insert takes the gate, finds no capture, stores nothing and applies (c) | Material stored after the erasure committed; a `PARKED` case without material | — |
| (am) Outbox row aged out | Let cleanup delete a captured row once every owed group has an accounting row or a case (7I ACC-06); separately attempt cleanup while one pair has neither | Outbox; ACC-03 listing | First: deleted; nothing is listed; the stored capture and the cases carry what is owed. Second: refused; the pair stays listed and its deadline runs | A row with an unaccounted pair that has no case is deleted; the deadline stops because the row is old | — |
| (an) PostgreSQL and Redis outages | Redis unreachable across the deadline; separately PostgreSQL unavailable across it | Cases after recovery | Redis down: 7I ACC-16 is `UNKNOWN`, the deadline is computed from PostgreSQL alone and the response comes. PostgreSQL down: nothing runs and nothing is decided; on its return the age includes the outage and the response comes at the first pass | The deadline is extended by either outage; a completion is inferred | — |
| (ao) Reconciler restart | Kill the reconciler between classification and insert; and after the insert, before its commit returns | Cases; material | The pair is listed again; the insert is retried or found; one case | Two cases; material stored twice for one case | Idempotent on (group, `event_id`) |
| (ap) Concurrent completion and insert | Pause the gateway's accounting insert and the reconciler's insert at their commits; release them in both orders | Accounting row; case | Both may exist (7I ACC-20); one effect; the case resolves through R2 as already committed | A lost accounting row; two cases; the case resolved by the reconciler | — |
| (aq) Billing deferred acknowledgement | CON-06 pair with no case at its deadline, with a stored capture; and the same with the capture erased | `usage_events`; case; `XPENDING` | With the capture: `PARKED`; R2 under dual control (7I OPA-07); no usage row without a handler commit. Without it: `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`; an R4 entry that reaches CON-06 is **not** acknowledged before T-14 (7G BIL-04) | A usage row written by the reconciler or by the R4; an `XACK` before Billing's decision | — |
| (ar) Source missing or unverifiable | An R4 item ends `SOURCE_MISSING`; an item ends `PROVENANCE_MISSING`; a stored capture whose digest does not match its record | Item results; cases | Each is re-classed at once: RM-4 with a case, RM-5 without; transition by CNF-7K-09 (c) only; nothing is published and nothing is stored | A source rebuilt from a log, an audit row or a stream; a completion recorded | — |
| (as) Stopped plan, eligible source | Pair with a case in `HOLD`. Stop the first plan with the item unpublished, before the operational replay deadline; approve the remainder as a successor plan; let it publish | Operation lineage; `REPAIR` position; case history | After the stop: class RM-3 unchanged, no transition, no owner record. The successor publishes from the same stored capture; on delivery the case is classified again as frozen | Any case transition caused by the stop; a publication from the outbox row; a successor that contains a row already holding a `REPAIR` position | Re-submitting the stopped plan starts nothing (7G PLN-06) |
| (at) Successive plans past the deadline | Stop and re-approve plans repeatedly so that no `REPAIR` position exists at capture time plus `T_obl`; then let a further plan publish | Capture time; operation lineage; case; escalation record | The deadline is the one fixed at the capture, whatever the number and timing of the plans. At it: the case is parked with the capture, once, and the row appears in the recorded escalation. The later publication takes the parked-duplicate path | The deadline moves with a plan's approval, start or stop; no disposition and no escalation at the deadline; an owner disposition, an accounting row or a resolution is written because a replay failed | The park is one compare-and-swap; a second pass finds `PARKED` |
| (au) Unknown publication outcome | Drop the reply of the confirmation so that the item's outcome is `UNKNOWN`; an entry exists and no position was recorded | Publication attempt record; position records; stream | The item is not marked published; it is run again and records its position; one effect per group. If the deadline passes first: row 11 or 14 of MRA-7K-17, and the stray entry takes the parked-duplicate path | The unrecorded entry is treated as a publication for either deadline; the item is marked published without a position record | — |
| (av) Ineligible source | Four fixtures for a pair with a case and a pair without: the capture erased; the clearance not `CONSUMED` for the running incarnation; a digest mismatch; item result `PROVENANCE_MISSING` | Clearance; capture; item results; cases | Each at once: RM-4 with a case, RM-5 without, by CNF-7K-09 (c); owner reconciliation mandatory; the capture is neither published nor copied | A case parked with a capture that is not eligible; a plan approved for the row | — |
| (aw) Second plan for a published row | Approve a second plan whose selection contains a row that already has a `REPAIR` position | Plan candidate set; position records | The row is not a candidate of the successor; nothing is published for it again by plan; no deadline changes | A second publication by plan; a deadline recomputed from the second plan | — |

**T-7K-47 (1) … (8): the supersession under faults (FG-7K-06).** *Frozen requirement:* as T-7K-47 in the table above. *Preconditions:* CNF-7K-08 consolidated and implemented for rows that reach key deletion; a transport ahead of a restored database, with pending entries of every class D-1 … D-6. *Injection and sequence:* each numbered fault of §21.6.3 at its point; then restart the operation. *Authoritative state and evidence:* the S3 inventory before and after; the per-key recorded state; the ACL digest of every node; the audit rows. *Expected result:* the "Result" column of §21.6.3 for each row; in every row the two S3 inventories are equal, and the completion record exists once. *Fails if:* an entry of a superseded key is delivered to any consumer; a command whose reply carries an entry's fields is issued on a superseded key by any identity; a key is deleted before each of its pending entries has a class; a command is issued by an identity that does not hold it, or by break-glass; a case is resolved, an accounting row written or an acknowledgement issued by the supersession; a completion is recorded for an obligation whose source is missing. *Crash / restart; idempotency:* a third run after completion changes nothing and returns the stored completion record. *Privacy and security:* as T-7K-46. *Gate:* AG-7K-08.

---

## 27. Owner Decisions

### 27.1 Register

Five questions met every criterion for an owner decision: frozen contracts do not settle them, they have materially different options, and they concern residency, disaster-loss tolerance, financial correctness or a security control. Four were put to the platform owner on 2026-10-12 as options with a recommendation, **before** the sections they govern were written, and were approved in writing the same day with conditions. The fifth, OD-7K-05, was raised in the second remediation and approved on 2026-10-13 as option B. Every condition is mapped to a rule below.

| ID | Subject | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| OD-7K-01 | Recovery posture of an `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` stack when its contracted region is lost | In-region only | APPROVED |
| OD-7K-02 | Cross-region business recovery for stacks that host no `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization | Copy kept where authorized; promotion only by decision | APPROVED |
| OD-7K-03 | Automated financial operations after a database restore or a promotion that may have lost commits | Gated until reconciled | APPROVED |
| OD-7K-04 | 7I HRD-01, origin verification for the Billing consumer | Adopted for Billing | APPROVED |
| OD-7K-05 | Durability of the erasure journal of a class S stack across its two regions | Option B: the journal stays authoritative in its origin region; an asynchronous recovery copy; no promotion without proof of completeness against the origin journal | APPROVED (2026-10-13) |

Open owner decisions: **0**. OD-7K-01 … OD-7K-05 are approved. No earlier decision was reopened; OD-7K-05 narrows what OD-7K-02 can be claimed to deliver and leaves its conditions in force (§27.2). No new owner decision is raised in the fourth remediation: each of its choices is fixed by a frozen source, fixed by OD-7K-05, or left to 7L with required wording (§32.9). **Approval of a policy is not approval to activate it:** class S cross-region recovery stays closed until AG-7K-08 holds.

### 27.2 Decision records

**OD-7K-01 — `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` regional loss.**

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Question | If the whole contracted region of an `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` stack is lost, what recovery posture applies? |
| Source evidence | 7A RES-02, RES-03; 7I RSY-01 … RSY-14, CRF-06 (one creatable pair, one region); 7E RSD-03, RSD-04; 3F §11.1, §11.3, §12 (a second region holding a database replica, object replication and the weekly snapshot) |
| Why frozen contracts do not settle it | They forbid every copy outside the contracted region. They do not say what loss is accepted when that region is gone, and 3F's regional targets assume a second region |
| Option A (chosen) | In-region only. Multi-zone inside the region; backups, journal and keys in a separate in-region failure domain. Region loss means unavailability; destruction means unrecoverable loss. No regional objective is claimed |
| Option B | A second in-country site counted as part of the contracted region. Needs contract wording, legal review and a governed change of the 7I region model |
| Option C | A cross-border copy by tenant consent. Changes frozen residency policy |
| Implications | Security and residency: A changes nothing; B and C widen where data lives. Reliability: A has no defence against regional destruction. Cost: A lowest. Operations: A simplest |
| Recommendation | A |
| Consequence of deferral | Class E topology and every regional recovery statement would be undefined |
| Blocks | The design freeze of §20 and §21 for class E |
| Owner conditions → rules | (1) every copy, backup, Redis persistence file, object, key, telemetry store and the journal in region → REG-7K-03; (2) no second-country or cross-region site → REG-7K-04, REG-7K-06, REG-7K-14; (3) unrecoverable loss documented → DRS-7K-01, RR-7K-03; (4) no regional objective claimed → §21.1, DRS-7K-02; (5) any change needs a separate residency and contractual decision → DRS-7K-01, ADR-7K-11 |

**OD-7K-02 — Cross-region recovery for other stacks.**

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Question | For a stack that hosts no `INDIA_ENTERPRISE` organization, is the cross-region recovery of 3F §12 an approved V1 path? |
| Source evidence | 3F §10.1, §11, §12 (replica, replication, manual switch, targets); 7A RES-03 (no cross-region event flow by default; a governed decision is required); 7E RSD-04, CNF-7E-13; 7I JRN-12, JRN-13, JRN-16 (quarantine and journal reconciliation on promotion) |
| Why frozen contracts do not settle it | 3F provides the copy; 7A requires a governed decision for any cross-region flow; 7I makes a promoted database unusable without the journal. None says whether promotion is permitted, by whom, or with which safeguards |
| Option A (chosen) | Keep the copy as a data-safety copy; promote only on a recorded owner decision per incident, through quarantine, journal reconciliation, a new event-transport generation and disaster replay |
| Option B | The same sequence, pre-approved for on-call under the runbook |
| Option C | No cross-region copy for any stack in V1 |
| Implications | Reliability: A and B give a recovery path; C none. Risk: B can promote when the primary was recoverable, losing commits. Cost: C lowest. Residency: none for these stacks if their contracts allow |
| Recommendation | A |
| Consequence of deferral | Class S recovery, journal placement and the 3F targets would be undefined |
| Blocks | The design freeze of §20 and §21 for class S |
| Owner conditions → rules | (1) copies only where residency policy **and** contractual rules authorize → REG-7K-05; (2) no automatic promotion → REG-7K-14, DRS-7K-05; (3) explicit incident authorization → DRS-7K-05 (2); (4) fencing of the old primary → DRS-7K-05 (3), RS-2; (5) verification of lag and recovery point → DRS-7K-05 (4), DRS-7K-06; (6) frozen 7I quarantine and journal reconciliation before activation → DRS-7K-05 (5), RS-7, DRS-7K-09; (7) no global cross-region Redis event bus → REG-7K-06, DRS-7K-05 (6); (8) replicated data never automatically safe to serve → DRS-7K-05, REG-7K-15 |
| Difference from the option text | The option offered the copy for every non-enterprise stack. Condition 1 is stricter: a stack has a copy only with a recorded policy and contract authorization. The condition governs |
| Narrowed by OD-7K-05 (2026-10-13) | Every condition above stands. OD-7K-05 narrows what the approved path can be claimed to deliver: the cross-region data-safety copy may assist recovery from a partial regional failure, when the authoritative origin journal remains readable and the copy is proved complete against it. It gives no guaranteed recovery after permanent loss of the origin journal (REG-7K-21; DRS-7K-01a). This is a controlled clarification of the approved capability, recorded here; no frozen source is edited |

**OD-7K-03 — Financial operations after a lossy database event.**

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Question | Should automated money-moving runs wait for a Billing reconciliation with the provider after a restore or lossy promotion? |
| Source evidence | 3F §10.1 (restore with a non-zero recovery point); 6K, 6M (reconcilers; refund settlement by the reconciler; `102_5H2` receipts); 7A BIL-01 … BIL-07; 7I JRN-12 (incarnation) |
| Why frozen contracts do not settle it | They define how Billing reconciles. They define no behaviour for the interval in which the database lacks payments, refunds and charges that exist at the provider |
| Option A (chosen) | Gate charges, renewals, dunning, refund execution and invoice finalization until Billing records the reconciliation |
| Option B | Gate outbound money movement only |
| Option C | No gate; alert only |
| Implications | Financial correctness: A prevents duplicate external effects and finalization on incomplete data; B allows the latter; C allows both. Cost of A: delayed collection during recovery |
| Recommendation | A |
| Consequence of deferral | A restore could be followed by duplicate charges |
| Blocks | The design freeze of §21.8 |
| Owner conditions → rules | (1) the listed operations blocked after a restore or a promotion that may have lost commits → DRS-7K-17, DRS-7K-04; (2) an authoritative, auditable reconciliation covering the lost interval and the provider records before reopening → DRS-7K-18; (3) invoice immutability, server-authoritative calculation, idempotency and reconciler-only refund settlement preserved → DRS-7K-20; (4) missing restored records never proof of non-occurrence → DRS-7K-19, FCL-7K-03 |
| Difference from the option text | The option offered an early release by a recorded decision of two owners. The approval contains no such path. **None exists in this document** (DRS-7K-18) |
| Raised by | 7K itself, during the restore analysis; no incoming handoff asked for it |

**OD-7K-04 — Origin verification for Billing.**

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Question | Adopt 7I HRD-01 for the Billing usage consumer? |
| Source evidence | 7I HRD-01 (recommended, optional), RR-03, HE-7K-7I-07 (exit: "decision on HRD-01 recorded"); 7I ACC-06 (rows deleted after 7 days once accounted); 7F RET-7F-08, RET-7F-09; 7G DAU-02, PRT-02 |
| Why frozen contracts do not settle it | 7I leaves the choice open and hands it to 7K by name |
| Option A (chosen) | Adopt for CON-06 in V1 |
| Option B | Design now, enable after measurement |
| Option C | Do not adopt |
| Implications | Security: A and B detect a forged or altered entry and an entry with no authoritative origin. Performance: one primary-key read per Billing event, unmeasured. Reliability: late entries need a durable held path |
| Recommendation | A |
| Consequence of deferral | 7I's handoff exit condition stays open |
| Blocks | Production activation of the check only (AG-7K-10); not the design freeze |
| Owner conditions → rules | (1) verify `event_id` with authoritative organization, type and digest through a guarded least-privilege function before a billable effect → SEC-7K-10, -11, -12; (2) fail closed on mismatch, missing evidence, authorization failure or database unavailability → SEC-7K-13 and the result table; (3) never permanently reject or silently lose a legitimate delayed or replayed event because the row was cleaned up → SEC-7K-15; (4) durable held path and release evidence defined → SEC-7K-15, SEC-7K-16; (5) 7F idempotency and 7G replay protections not weakened → SEC-7K-17; (6) bounded performance and concurrency benchmark before activation; no invented result → SEC-7K-19, BM-7K-09, AG-7K-10 |

**OD-7K-05 — Durability of the class S erasure journal across regions. STATUS: APPROVED — OPTION B (2026-10-13). Owner question: CLOSED.**

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Question | For a class S stack with an authorized cross-region copy (OD-7K-02), when does an erasure-journal record count as durable: when it is durable in the origin region, or only when it is durable in the recovery region as well? |
| Source evidence | 7I JRN-06, JRN-07, JRN-09 (write-ahead protocol and its crash points); JRN-13 step 1, JRN-16 (completeness and fail-closed quarantine); JRN-20, JRN-29 (journal outage and deadlines); OD-7K-02 conditions 6 and 8; REG-7K-20, REG-7K-21 |
| Why frozen contracts do not settle it | 7I defines one journal in one region. OD-7K-02 authorizes a copy and requires the frozen reconciliation before activation, but says nothing about how a copy that lags can be proved complete once the origin is gone |
| What is **not** proof of a complete copy, under any option | Ordinary asynchronous replication lag, however small. The copy's own head. A `CHECKPOINT` that was itself copied. Each describes what arrived, and none can show that nothing later was written at the origin |
| Decision | **Option B.** For an eligible class S stack the erasure journal remains authoritative in its origin region, and its durability requirement is satisfied under the frozen 7I origin-region write-ahead protocol. A separately authorized recovery-region copy may replicate asynchronously after the necessary 7L contracts are reconciled. Cross-region recovery is permitted only if the recovery process can prove the replicated journal complete against the authoritative origin journal. If the origin journal is permanently lost, or its complete head cannot be established, the recovery-region database stays quarantined and cannot be promoted into service. No operator, application service or incident-response process may override this |
| Owner conditions → rules | (1) the origin journal is authoritative; durability under frozen 7I JRN-06, -07, -09, -20, -29 → REG-7K-26; (2) the copy is asynchronous, separately authorized, and exists only after the controlled reconciliation → REG-7K-05, -16, -17; CNF-7K-10; AG-7K-08; (3) recovery only on proof of completeness, C-1 … C-5 with `H_j = H_o` → REG-7K-20; DRS-7K-05 (5); (4) nothing substitutes for the reading of `H_o` → REG-7K-21; (5) completeness unknown: the eight outcomes → REG-7K-21, U-1 … U-8; (6) no override → REG-7K-21; DRS-7K-09; (7) complete origin-region loss with its journal is not a supported recoverable disaster, and no objective or guarantee is claimed → DRS-7K-01a; §21.1; §21.2; (8) nothing of the list in REG-7K-26 is introduced; an outage of the recovery region delays no origin operation; lag relaxes no deadline → REG-7K-26; T-7K-40 (g); (9) class E is unchanged and explained separately → REG-7K-26; DRS-7K-01, -01a; (10) the limitation is accepted, stays in force unless the owner changes the policy, and weakens no control → REG-7K-21; RR-7K-09 |
| Difference from the option text | The approval is stricter and more explicit than option B as it was offered below. It names the eight outcomes of unknown completeness, among them that no alternative journal head is created and that incident evidence is kept; it forbids an override by an operator, an application service and the incident process by name; it calls the scenario "not a supported recoverable disaster"; and it lists the mechanisms that must not be introduced. The conditions govern |
| Relation to OD-7K-02 | OD-7K-02 stands with all eight conditions. OD-7K-05 narrows its recoverability claim, as recorded in the OD-7K-02 record above |
| Residual risk | RR-7K-09: **explicitly ACCEPTED** by the owner as a V1 design limitation |
| Not approved by this decision | Production implementation or activation. The cross-region copy is still gated by the reconciliation of CNF-7K-10 (a) … (f), by IO-7K-22 and by its validation. A promotion still needs every proof of DRS-7K-05 and REG-7K-20. Recovery kind F is not production-activatable until its authorized path is proven (AG-7K-08). Once it is activated, the limitation of option B remains |
| Author's recommendation | The author had recommended option A. The owner chose option B. The recommendation had no force and has none |

| | **A. Dual-region durable acknowledgement before completion** | **B. Asynchronous copy; loss of the origin journal prevents promotion (CHOSEN)** | **C. Independent durability witness** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Exact policy and guarantee | A journal record on which a database change depends (erasure, time-based batch, seal and key-destruction intents; a hold placement) counts as durable only once it is durable in both regions. After the loss of the origin, journal store included, the copy holds every record that any committed erasure, hold, seal or key destruction depended on | The copy is replicated asynchronously. A promotion completes only if the origin journal's head can be read from the origin store (C-4). If that store is permanently lost, the stack is not promoted from the copy: it stays quarantined. No recovery-time or recovery-point objective is claimed for that case | A third, separately failing store records, before a record counts as durable, enough to prove the origin's head without the origin. Guarantee: none until the design is specified and validated. Knowing the head detects a missing tail; it does not supply the missing records, so the design must also show that the records themselves survive |
| Relation to OD-7K-02 | Delivers the recovery path OD-7K-02 approved, for complete origin loss as well. Adds a condition to it | Keeps every condition of OD-7K-02. Narrows what its path delivers: recovery from a partial regional failure only. OD-7K-02 would carry that statement | Would deliver the path if validated. Until then identical to B |
| Security and erasure | No erasure, seal or key destruction commits unless its intent is in both regions. The journal writer needs a confirmation from the recovery region: one more cross-region channel beside those of TOP-7K-05 | The erasure protocol is unchanged. No erased data can be served from a copy, because an unprovable copy never activates | A new trusted component with its own credentials, residency and tamper analysis. It would hold sequence numbers and hashes of the origin's journal outside the origin region |
| Replication consistency | Synchronous for those records; PostgreSQL replication stays asynchronous, and C-1, C-2, C-3 and C-5 still apply. Records written after the fact (`COMPLETED` and the like) can still be missing from the copy and are then handled as the pending intents of 7I JRN-09; that this is safe against a database copy that is also behind must be shown in the amendment | Asynchronous. Lag has no effect on correctness, because completeness is never inferred from the copy | To be specified. At least the head, and by the row above also the records, must be durable outside the origin before a dependent change commits |
| Effect of an outage of the recovery region or of the link | Erasure, sealing, hold release and key destruction in the origin wait at `INTENT_REQUESTED`, as in a journal outage (7I JRN-20). Content past its deadline stays denied by the guard meanwhile (7I CAG-12); hold placement would wait as well | None on the origin | Depends on whether the witness is in the write-ahead path; if it is, as A for the witness's own outages |
| Cost and operations | Cross-region latency on every journal append; the recovery region becomes a dependency of privacy work in the origin; monitoring and a runbook for that dependency | Lowest. One more replicated prefix | Highest: a new component to design, place, secure, operate and validate, against INV-7K-01, which admits no new infrastructure dependency without justification and approval |
| Erasure deadlines | Can be missed during an outage of the recovery region. The deadlines remain obligations and a missed one is a privacy incident (7I JRN-29) | No effect | Possible, as A, if the witness is in the write-ahead path |
| Supports complete loss of the origin region | Yes, subject to the validation named under replication consistency | **No** | Unproven |
| Amendments to frozen 7I | CNF-7K-10 (a) – (f); and 7I JRN-06 (what `INTENT_DURABLE` means), JRN-07, JRN-09, JRN-19, JRN-20, JRN-29, SEAL-07 for a class S stack with a copy; REG-7K-20 C-4 replaced by a proof from the copy | CNF-7K-10 (a) – (f) only | CNF-7K-10 (a) – (f); 7I JRN-01 ("no other database product or managed service is introduced"), JRN-05, JRN-13, JRN-18, JRN-19; a residency analysis of the witness |
| Benchmarks and drills | Journal append latency and erasure throughput with dual acknowledgement; a recovery-region outage drill that measures deadline lag; an origin-loss drill with the database copy ahead of and behind the journal copy; T-7K-40 (c) – (e) | T-7K-40 (c) – (e); a drill showing that the stack stays quarantined when the origin store is unreadable, and that a partial failure with the store readable completes | All of A's, and the failure modes of the witness itself, before any guarantee is stated |

The option table is kept as the record of what was put to the owner. Options A and C are not in force, and no rule of this document assumes either.

### 27.3 Matters settled without an owner decision

Each follows from a frozen source, and none is a new policy: backup retention is kept as 3F states it (REG-7K-07); endpoint auto-suspension stays not adopted (BPR-7K-14); fairness shares are equal because no entitlement exists (7H §20.1; FAR-7K-03); the event-transport cluster has no off-node backup because such a copy is never a recovery source (7E ROLE-05; RED-7K-03); every numeric objective stays a candidate (OD-7J-01).

---

## 28. Architecture Decision Records

| ID | Context | Decision | Rejected | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-7K-01 | Many recovery actors can reach one object | Layered precedence with one arbiter per object (§6.3) | A recovery coordinator service; distributed locks | No new component; outcomes single-valued by compare-and-set |
| ADR-7K-02 | One shard without capability pauses all publication (7E HPR-02) | Accept; minimize the pause; size the outbox for it (RED-7K-09 … -11) | Second replica with a changed confirmation rule; shard-aware claiming | Residual RR-7K-01; both rejected options remain available as governed changes |
| ADR-7K-03 | Replica loss and return | Start every node not failover-eligible and clear the flag only by the admission; replace by a governed operation; treat a same-process return as unverified once its baseline no longer holds, and flag it (RED-7K-17 … -21, -36 … -38) | Automatic replica migration (forbidden by 7E TOPO-10); manual promotion; a start configuration that makes a returning node eligible at once; relying on a recorded state to withhold capability | After any process start, capability returns only through the durability admission of RED-7K-19, which rests on Redis behaviour V-1 … V-3 that is validated, not assumed, and never waits for a later confirmation (RED-7K-34, -35). A replica that returns in the same process can be capable before any record; that interval rests on frozen 7E and V-1 … V-3 and is ended by E-1, not prevented (RED-7K-38) |
| ADR-7K-04 | Catch-up can move a backlog into Redis | Pace the relay by transport memory zones (BPR-7K-03 … -06) | Let the transport reach `OOM`; throttle producers | Backlog stays in PostgreSQL; producers never blocked |
| ADR-7K-05 | The event-transport cluster's backups | None off-node (RED-7K-03) | Daily snapshot copy as for the hot tier | Less residue; CNF-7K-02 |
| ADR-7K-06 | Slot placement of the twelve keys | Separate the three high-volume families; fix before provisioning (RED-7K-05 … -07) | Default even split | Candidate until BM-7K-03; remedy is a new generation |
| ADR-7K-07 | Generation migration spans 7E, 7F and 7I | One combined procedure with an ACL fence for old producers and a symmetric abort (§9.8) | Time-based retirement; relying on a deployment gate alone | No frozen text edited; CNF-7K-03. Retirement needs lag 0 and an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09); key deletion has no frozen authority (CNF-7K-08) |
| ADR-7K-08 | Conversation recovery | Call first, then conversation; the finalizer acts only on a terminal call; corrupt state gets a durable marker (§12) | Finalizing on elapsed time or on hot-tier state; an operator-supplied time | W2 not activatable before the marker exists |
| ADR-7K-09 | Scheduling | Ticks are not intent; state scans; a scheduler role with replicas (§14.3) | A singleton scheduler; a beat schedule as the only trigger | Missed ticks lose nothing |
| ADR-7K-10 | Fairness on shared streams | Bound occupancy, do not reorder (§13.6) | Per-tenant streams (rejected by OD-7E-03); skipping entries | Starvation freedom stated with its unavoidable term |
| ADR-7K-11 | Regional topology | Stack classes E and S (OD-7K-01, OD-7K-02; the class S recovery claim narrowed by OD-7K-05) | One topology for every profile | 3F narrowed for class E; CNF-7K-01. Two separate limitations: DRS-7K-01 and DRS-7K-01a |
| ADR-7K-12 | A lossy database event leaves the transport ahead | Supersede pre-event generations; rebuild obligations from the restored database (§21.6) | Draining the old streams; a per-entry origin check for every consumer | CNF-7K-04, CNF-7K-08. A separate operation with its own proof (§21.6.1) |
| ADR-7K-13 | Financial operations after a restore | A clearance bound to the database incarnation (OD-7K-03; §21.8) | A flag inside the restored database; alert only | Default closed after every incarnation change |
| ADR-7K-14 | Origin verification | Adopted for CON-06 with a held path for cleaned-up rows (OD-7K-04; §22.3) | Rejecting entries with no row; applying them unchecked | CNF-7K-05 |
| ADR-7K-15 | Capacity figures | A symbolic model with a parameter registry (§15) | Hardware numbers without measurement | Values arrive by benchmark without redesign |
| ADR-7K-16 | The obligation deadline | `T_obl` above the design outage; never suspended (BPR-7K-08) | A switch that silences the deadline during an outage | A long consumer outage opens cases, by design |
| ADR-7K-17 | After a restore, bytes in a superseded stream or a restored row may belong to content that was erased, sealed or undecidable | No stream entry is ever read. A restored row is captured only on a clearance from the restore reconciler, in one transaction under the exclusive hold gate (§21.6.2) | Capturing once the journal is reconciled; a check before and after a Redis read; a bounded read under the gate; giving the tooling a journal credential | Default deny; a rare obligation without a row goes to its owner (MRA-7K-12); one matrix classes every (event, group) pair, an existing case continues through R4 republication and is never duplicated (MRA-7K-14); CNF-7K-09 |
| ADR-7K-18 | OD-7K-02 authorizes a cross-region copy; frozen 7I binds the journal to one region; an asynchronous copy cannot prove itself complete | Keep the journal authoritative in its origin region under the frozen write-ahead protocol; specify an asynchronous copy, its completeness conditions and the new regional sequence; activate none of it before 7L; no promotion without the origin head (OD-7K-05, option B; §20.5) | Replicating the prefix as an ordinary object prefix; trusting the copy's own head; a dual-region synchronous acknowledgement (option A); a durability witness (option C); any override | CNF-7K-10 (a) … (f); RR-7K-09, accepted: no recovery after permanent loss of the origin journal |
| ADR-7K-19 | An origin-unverifiable Billing entry | Held unacknowledged until Billing's terminal decision, as frozen 7G treats a beyond-horizon Billing entry (SEC-7K-20 … -22) | Acknowledging once the case commits | A longer trim pin; CNF-7K-05 |

## 31. Contradiction and Residual-Risk Register

### 31.1 Conflicts and controlled reconciliations

No frozen document is edited. Each item is a reconciliation for its owner, consolidated by 7L.

| ID | Sources | Conflict | 7K position | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CNF-7K-01 | 3F §11.1, §11.3, §12 versus 7A RES-02, 7I RSY-07, OD-7K-01 | 3F replicates the database, objects and the weekly snapshot to a second region for the platform as a whole | Not provisioned for class E; provisioned for class S only with a recorded authorization (REG-7K-04, -05) | Resolved by owner decision |
| CNF-7K-02 | 3F §11.2 versus 7E ROLE-05, 7I RR-05 | 3F copies Redis snapshots to object storage daily | Not applied to the event-transport cluster (RED-7K-03) | Minor |
| CNF-7K-03 | 7E LCY-07 versus 7F TK-1 … TK-9, 7I ACC-15, GWY-03 | The generation migration is specified in three documents with different steps | One combined order (§9.8); no step of any source is removed | Minor (already recorded as 7F CNF-7F-14) |
| CNF-7K-04 | 7E LCY-07 step 4; 7G TPG-05, GR-6, T-07 versus §21.6 | Retirement of a key requires a drain and a disposition per pending entry; after a lossy database event the old keys are destroyed without a drain | Ordinary retirement now meets both rules in full (GEN-7K-09). The supersession is a separate disaster operation for a fault outside the 7E model: every pending entry gets a recorded class (SUP-7K-05), authoritative obligations are rebuilt from PostgreSQL (DRS-7K-13), and nothing is consumed. 7G should record "superseded by restore" as a named outcome of that operation | Reconciliation owed; not a contradiction inside 7K. Its activation depends on CNF-7K-08 |
| CNF-7K-05 | 7F §28.7 (CON-06 card); 7G §10, §22, §35, T-08, T-13, PID-07, PAT-07, RTY-02; 7I HRD-01 ([REC]), GWY-08 versus OD-7K-04 | The origin check adds a pre-guard step and two case reasons. `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` takes the Billing deferred acknowledgement of 7G BIL-04, which 7G words for `BEYOND_HORIZON` only. `ORIGIN_MISMATCH` uses a position-keyed integrity case for an entry that exists, where 7G PID-07 words that identity for a missing entry | Adopted by owner decision. **Required wording.** 7G §22 gains the row "`RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` — `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE`, Billing (CON-06): `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`; material yes; `XACK` no, until Billing's terminal decision (§35)". 7G BIL-01 … BIL-07 read "beyond-horizon or origin-unverifiable". 7G RTY-02 gains: "for CON-06, an entry whose case was resolved by T-14 is acknowledged citing that disposition and its handler is not executed". 7G PID-07 and §39 admit the reason `ORIGIN_MISMATCH` for an entry that is present. 7F §28.7 lists the pre-guard step. 7I GWY-08 (b) names the two reasons | Resolved by owner decision; errata owed before AG-7K-10 |

| CNF-7K-08 | 7E LCY-07 step 5, SCY-05; 7G §38, §42, T-07, AUT-06; 7I RDS-10, RDS-11, RDS-13, RDS-15, matrix 6, OPA-07 versus §9.8, §21.6.1 | 7E makes key deletion an operator-only step of generation retirement. 7I denies `DEL` and `UNLINK` on stream keys to every runtime user, gives the recovery operator `XGROUP DESTROY` and explicitly not `DEL`, and forbids break-glass as a recovery path. The frozen relay key pattern covers every generation | 7K fails closed: no key is deleted and no generation change is started (GEN-7K-11, SUP-7K-09). **Required wording.** (a) 7I matrix 6 gains a *stream-lifecycle* user: allowed `UNLINK` on `stream:global:d:*`; denied everything else, every read of an entry and every SIGNAL key included; credential "retrievable by the tooling workload only while an approved generation-retirement or supersession operation is `RUNNING` and has recorded, for the key named, the proofs its procedure requires". 7I RDS-10 then reads "…`DEL` and `UNLINK` on stream keys, except `UNLINK` by the stream-lifecycle user". (b) 7I states that the containment of RDS-13 and a governed ACL revision of RDS-15, applied as a step of an approved operation, are not the break-glass access of RDS-11. (c) The relay and repair-publisher key patterns are per generation. (d) 7G §42 and the 7I capability matrix gain the operation kinds *generation retirement* and *transport supersession* under `recovery.group.operate`, with the hard dual control of OPA-07. (e) 7I ACC-15 states how a registration is closed when its key is superseded: every position recorded on the key stays covered until it has an accounting row or a case | **Blocks activation** (AG-7K-08, AG-7K-13). | CNF-7K-09 | 7I JRN-13, JRN-15, JRN-25, JRN-26, ACC-05, CAG-05, HLD-10, RET-01, ERA-13, ERA-S1 … ERA-S3; 7G RPM-01, RSM-04, T-08, T-13, DSR-12 versus §21.6.2 | After a restore, frozen 7I records nowhere whether pre-event content may be stored again. Its two readers of a `PUBLISHED` row capture unconditionally. It has no entry into `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` for an obligation whose content may not be read. Its verification before activation knows nothing of either | 7K fails closed: no entry is ever read; nothing is captured without a clearance; no clearance can exist today; the stack is not reopened (MRA-7K-06, -07, -10, -13). 7K asks for **no** tombstone and **no** deletion: a `PUBLISHED` row stays "not altered" (7I RET-01). **Required wording, covering every reason of MRA-7K-04.** (a) *Clearance.* 7I JRN-13 step 5 gains: "the reconciler writes, through a restore-only function, a recovery privacy clearance for every restored `PUBLISHED` outbox row and for every recovery case restored in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material: `CLEAR` only if no erasure, time-based erasure, seal or organization-erasure record, applied or pending, before or after the recovery point, reaches its attribution reference or organization, its attribution is resolved in a verified subject index, and no deadline that applies to it has passed or is unknown; otherwise `BARRED` with the reason, and `HELD_RESTRICTED` where a hold applies together with another reason; the verdict is computed once per event, and the record of a row and the record of every such case of the same event carry the same verdict". (b) *Refusal.* 7I ACC-05 and CAG-05 (7), and 7G DSR-12, gain: "a `PUBLISHED` row that has a clearance record is captured only if that record is `CLEAR`, unconsumed and for the running incarnation, in one transaction that takes the organization's hold gate in exclusive mode and re-checks the current timeline; a row whose record is not `CLEAR` is never captured and never republished; a row whose record is consumed, with its capture stored, is never read again; ACC-03, ACC-16 and ACC-17 apply to its pairs unchanged, with the age of ACC-17 taken from the earliest recorded position of the row or from `published_at`, positions recorded before the restore included, and with no state of a replay operation and no time of activation, capture or republication entering the classification or the deadline; when ACC-16 or ACC-17 sends such a pair to ACC-05, ACC-05 does not read the row: in one transaction, under the organization's hold gate in exclusive mode and the current-timeline checks of the capture applied to the stored capture, it inserts the case of ACC-05 (class `V`, `PARKED`, `OBLIGATION_UNACCOUNTED`) with a byte-exact copy of the stored capture, its digest and its provenance as the case's material; where no stored capture exists or a check fails it stores nothing and applies the entry of (c)". **No waiting clause is requested, and 7L must not adopt one.** The fourth remediation asked for an exemption while a replay was not terminal, which could last without limit against ACC-17, and for an age restarted at activation; both are withdrawn (MRA-7K-16). The copy is made by a ledger function that returns no content; 7I lists it in the inventory of CAG-05 with the material guard. 7I HLD-10 lists this capture among the exclusive holders of the gate. (c) *The obligation.* 7G T-08, RPM-01 and RSM-04, and 7I JRN-25, gain a restore-scoped entry, one compare-and-swap under the case's row lock. In quarantine it is applied by the restore reconciler through its restore-only functions, which keep refusing once the database is activated (7I JRN-27 is not changed). After activation it is applied through a **separate** guarded ledger function that is not restore-only and makes the same compare-and-swap: by the restore's supersession or disaster-replay operation while that operation is `RUNNING`, under the dual control that operation already carries (7I OPA-07), for a pair that loses its source; and by the accounting reconciler of 7I ACC-05 for a pair that ACC-16 or ACC-17 sends to it when no stored capture can lawfully be used (b). Neither can resolve a case (7G DAU-02). **A second form, for a source that is still eligible (MRA-7K-17 (4)).** 7G T-07 gains a restore-scoped trigger and actor: "for a case restored in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` whose event's row has an eligible stored capture and no `REPAIR` position record when `T_obl` has passed since the capture time recorded under DSR-12 (c): that same case moves to `PARKED`, class `V`, with a byte-exact copy of the stored capture, its digest and its provenance as material, in one transaction under the hold gate and the checks of (b), applied by the pass of the accounting reconciler, which for this purpose lists the pairs the restore recorded as class RM-3 with a case; a case that stores crash-loop material is parked with its own material; no state of a replay plan enters the condition and no plan moves the time". The reason code is 7L's to name. The first form of (c) applies only where no eligible source exists. "For a case restored in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD` without material whose event has no admissible source (its verdict is not `CLEAR`, its row is absent or tombstoned, or its capture or replay item failed closed; a replay plan that stopped is none of these): **that same case** moves to `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`; for each owed group of a row whose verdict is not `CLEAR`, or whose source was lost or may not be read a second time, that has neither an accounting row nor a case: the first and only case of the identity is inserted in `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`; in both: class `V`, no material; reason `PRIVACY_MATERIAL_ERASED` with the journal reference where an erasure record reaches it (reasons 1 – 3), and otherwise a distinct reason meaning that the source may not be read, or does not exist, after a restore (reasons 4 – 7, and a missing source), with the verdict recorded; never `RESOLVED`; never a second case for an identity (7G PID-01)". A case restored in `HOLD` with stored crash-loop material and no admissible source takes the frozen parking transition with that material, as 7I JRN-25 row 7 already does where an erasure reaches it. The name of the second reason is 7L's to fix. (d) *Verification.* 7I JRN-26 gains: "the number of row clearances equals the number of restored `PUBLISHED` rows; every candidate has a verdict and the verdicts of one event are equal; every obligation of (c) that is known in quarantine has its case, as the same case where one existed; no identity has two cases; any difference keeps the environment quarantined". Reasons 8, 9 and 10 need no wording: 7I JRN-24, JRN-25 and JRN-16 already decide them | **Blocks activation** (AG-7K-08). Not a contradiction inside 7K |
| CNF-7K-10 | 7I JRN-01, JRN-04, JRN-05, JRN-13, JRN-18, JRN-19, RSY-13, RSY-14, KEY-05 versus OD-7K-02, OD-7K-05, §20.5 | The table of §20.5. Frozen 7I describes one journal, one region, one sequence and one reader; OD-7K-02 authorizes a recovery copy for class S, and OD-7K-05 fixes it as an asynchronous copy of a journal that stays authoritative in its origin region | 7K fails closed: no journal prefix or key is copied and kind F is not activatable (REG-7K-16). **Required wording, which is the whole reconciliation under option B.** (a) 7I RSY-13 and JRN-19: "for a class S stack with a recorded cross-region authorization, one read-only, asynchronously replicated copy of the journal prefix may exist in the designated recovery region; the origin journal remains authoritative; a class E stack never has a copy". No 7E rule is weakened, the replica count and `WAITAOF 1 1` are unchanged, and the admission never requires a confirmation, so it does not conflict with 7E CAP-08 or with the probe's rule that it writes nothing. **Not enforceable, and not claimed:** for a replica that returns in the same process (paths 3a, 4b, 6a) no frozen mechanism withholds capability.
