### 31.2 Residual risks

Stated, not closed. None is rated Minor by the author; the rating is the reviewer's.

| ID | Risk | Why it remains | Mitigation in this document | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RR-7K-01 | The loss of one event-transport node or one zone pauses all durable publication in the region, and group reads and acknowledgements on the affected shards, until capability returns. During that time, and until the returning node is admitted, the affected shard has one durable copy. If the admission keeps failing, the pause has no upper bound. **Every process restart of a replica now needs an admission before capability,** which lengthens the pause after a plain restart. **A replica that returns in the same process can be failover-eligible and its shard capable before anything was verified;** that interval is not prevented, has no enforced bound, and is safe only on frozen 7E together with V-1 … V-3 | The frozen gate is per relay loop; the confirmation rule and `min-replicas-to-write` need the one replica (OD-7E-02); durability of a restored replica must be established, not assumed; the frozen capability check has no input for a verified replica (CNF-7K-11 (b)) | A start configuration that makes every started node ineligible; the admission of RED-7K-19, which a healthy shard can always complete because it needs no publication (RED-7K-34); detection and E-1 for a same-process return (RED-7K-38); governed replacement; outbox reserve; alerting (RED-7K-09 … -39) | For acceptance (7L). Reducing the pause needs an amendment of OD-7E-02 or of the claim function; removing the unenforced interval needs CNF-7K-11 (b) |
| RR-7K-02 | A consumer group that stays offline holds the region's transport in a paced zone; events of every family are then late | Groups are never deleted automatically and entries are never discarded (7E RET-02) | Forecast, escalation, the frozen governed exits (RTN-7K-09) | For acceptance (7L) |
| RR-7K-03 | Permanent destruction of a class E stack's contracted region is unrecoverable | OD-7K-01 | None possible inside the residency rule | **Accepted by the owner in OD-7K-01** |
| RR-7K-04 | A conversation whose durable accounting state is corrupt is never billed until an owner repairs it; no deadline forces that | Synthesizing usage or a time is forbidden (OD-7B-02) | Durable marker; alerts; escalation before Billing's 90-day horizon (VOX-7K-16 … -24) | For acceptance (7L); bounded only by a recorded owner action |
| RR-7K-05 | An erased subject's event stays in transport files for the trim-pin age plus the rewrite interval, and in database backups for up to one year | Entries are not deleted individually; backups are immutable (7I RR-05) | Pin escalation; rewrite cadence; no off-node transport backup; restore quarantine | Inherited from 7I; for legal review (7I LR-05) |
| RR-7K-06 | After a lossy database event the financial gate can stay closed indefinitely if a provider cannot supply its records | OD-7K-03 allows no release without reconciliation | Escalation to the platform owner (§25.2) | **Follows from the owner's conditions in OD-7K-03**; listed so that it stays visible |
| RR-7K-07 | Every database promotion or restore leaves every accounted indicator without a verdict for 28 days; SLI-11 also after most suspensions of active organizations | Frozen 7J behaviour (OD-7J-11, OD-7J-14) | None; reported (OAC-7K-11, -12) | For the owner before any objective is approved |
| RR-7K-08 | Work in the lost interval of a database restore is lost, including events that consumers or receivers had already acted on externally | Inherent in restoring to an earlier point | Recovery point recorded and communicated; supersession; the financial gate; receiver deduplication | For acceptance (7L) |
| RR-7K-09 | A class S stack whose origin region is lost together with its authoritative journal cannot complete cross-region recovery: the recovery-region database stays quarantined and is not promoted. No recovery-point objective, no recovery-time objective and no regional-disaster guarantee exists for that case. The copy helps only in a partial regional failure in which the origin journal stays readable | The journal is authoritative in its origin region under the frozen write-ahead protocol, and a copy replicated asynchronously cannot prove that it has the origin's last records (OD-7K-05) | Completeness conditions C-1 … C-5; the outcomes U-1 … U-8; no override (REG-7K-20, -21, -26) | **Accepted by the owner in OD-7K-05 (option B) as a V1 design limitation.** Binding; not an open question. It stays in force after activation unless the owner approves a later change of policy |
| RR-7K-10 | After a lossy restore, a case whose content may not be stored again has no deadline that forces its resolution, and after the supersession it has neither a pending entry nor material | Storing the content again is forbidden; inventing a completion is forbidden; software cannot compel the owning domain's decision | Non-capture record; the case never ages out; the list handed to the owning domain and the privacy owner (MRA-7K-10) | For acceptance (7L), together with CNF-7K-09. An alert or a named owner is not a bound (FCL-7K-08) |

## 32. Freeze-Gate Validation and Final Status

### 32.1 Criteria

| # | Criterion | Result | Evidence |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every applicable incoming 7K handoff is handled | Met at design level: 92 of 92 rows have rules. 76 keep an open measurement gate | §3 |
| 2 | No unresolved contradiction with 7A–7J | The independent review found six (§32.6). Each is corrected inside 7K with a fail-closed rule. Four of the corrections need a frozen contract to say something it does not say yet; those are CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10, each with required wording and a closed activation gate. Eleven reconciliations recorded in all. The second review found two more and the third review two more (§32.7, §32.8); each is corrected inside 7K, and the third round adds no reconciliation and no gate. The fourth review found one more (§32.9), the fifth two more (§32.10) and the sixth two more (§32.11); each is corrected inside 7K, and none of these rounds adds a reconciliation or a gate | §31.1; §32.5 … §32.11 |
| 3 | No degradation of durable-publication semantics | No acceptance condition changed; pacing only delays | INV-7K-05; BPR-7K-05 |

| 8 | No duplicate business or financial effect from recovery | Guards; the finalization guard; the financial gate; the origin check | §12; §21.8; §22.3 |

| 10 | No unapproved residency transfer | Class E has no cross-region element; class S only on recorded authorization | §20 |
| 11 | No erased-data resurrection declared safe | 7I procedure unchanged. | 18 | No objective promoted without owner authorization | None | SLO-7K-01 |

OD-7K-05 was approved as option B on 2026-10-13; its residual risk is recorded as accepted, not as closed or removed. None is open, and none is raised in the fourth remediation | §27 |

### 32.3 Findings of the author's own review

| ID | Severity | Issue | Impact | Correction | Regression coverage |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-7K-01 | P1 | A draft of BPR-7K-08 suspended the obligation deadline of 7I ACC-17 during a declared outage | A switch that silences the deadline could hide an obligation lost through a transport that reports false state | The deadline is never suspended; `T_obl` is set above the design outage instead | T-7K-42 |
| F-7K-02 | P1 | The financial gate was first conceived as a flag in the database | The flag would be restored with the database it protects and could read "open" | Clearance bound to the incarnation, held outside and inside (DRS-7K-17) | T-7K-35 |
| F-7K-03 | P1 | An origin mismatch was first mapped to a case on (group, `event_id`) | A forged entry would park the genuine event's obligation and block it (7G RTY-02) | Integrity case keyed by the entry; the genuine pair stays open (SEC-7K-14) | T-7K-31 |
| F-7K-04 | P1 | Supersession would have destroyed the only copy of an entry behind a case in `OPEN_RETRY` or `HOLD`, which holds no material | A valid obligation would end as `SOURCE_MISSING` | Capture from the superseded key by recorded observation before destruction (DRS-7K-11 d) | T-7K-35 |
| F-7K-05 | P1 | The recovery finalizer was specified without a durable record of a corrupt conversation | Re-evaluation on every pass with no record; "explicit reconciliation" would be only an alert | Durable marker required; W2 not activatable without it (VOX-7K-16) | T-7K-32 |
| F-7K-06 | P1 | A replaced replica was declared capable on link state alone | Its AOF could be incomplete; a simultaneous restart could then lose confirmed entries | Restored-replica validation (RED-7K-19) | T-7K-11 |
| F-7K-07 | Minor | Probe backoff reset on every gate close | Duplicate entries under a flapping transport | Backoff survives a close (OBX-7K-04) | T-7K-10 |
| F-7K-08 | Minor | The option text of OD-7K-02 and OD-7K-03 was wider than the approval | Would have overstated what is approved | Conditions applied as written; differences disclosed (§27.2) | — |

P0: 0. P1: 6 found, 6 corrected in this document. Minor: 2 found, 2 corrected. **These are the author's own counts for the first submission, and the independent review did not accept two of the corrections:** F-7K-04 (capture before destruction) was itself the defect FG-7K-01, and F-7K-06 (restored-replica validation) was the defect FG-7K-05. The independent result for that submission was P0 = 1, P1 = 5 (§32.6).

**Left for the independent review to rate, not rated by the author:** CNF-7K-04, CNF-7K-08 … CNF-7K-11; residual risks RR-7K-01 … RR-7K-10; whether the fail-closed branches of §32.6 are sufficient while their reconciliations are open; the dependency of VOX-7K-19 and VOX-7K-20 on a value the 6D amendment has yet to name; the unread sections listed in §32.2.

### 32.4 What this document does not establish

It does not establish that any value in it is right, that any procedure works on the real providers, that any objective is attainable, or that any component exists. It establishes a design that is consistent with the frozen sources as read, a method to obtain the values, and the gates that keep unverified behaviour out of production.

### 32.5 Cross-phase regression review after the remediation

Each line names what was compared and what was found. A finding is listed only where the frozen text contradicts 7K; none was added to lengthen the list.

| # | Subject | Result |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Redis acceptance, 7E FMD-09, failover assumptions | **One error found and corrected:** §9.3 said consumption continues on a shard without a replica; group commands are write commands and are refused there (RED-7K-29). The admission of RED-7K-19 adds a precondition to 7E RC-02 and changes neither the replica count nor the acknowledgement command (CNF-7K-11). Third remediation: the admission no longer contains a post-capability confirmation, so it is consistent with 7E CAP-08 (capability before any claim loop) and with the probe that never writes (RED-7K-34, -35). Fourth remediation: the precondition of TR-1 is the `nofailover` flag read from Redis, which every started node carries (RED-7K-36); for a replica that returns in the same process no enforcement is claimed (RED-7K-38). 7E TOPO-11 and CAP-07 are met for the running nodes at TR-2; their wording for a start value is CNF-7K-11 (a). Fifth remediation: I-DUR and PO-1 concern the required retained set bounded by the watermark of 7E TRM-02, not every entry ever confirmed; 7E TRM-01 … TRM-10 are unchanged, and the admission issues no command on a stream key (RED-7K-39) |
| 2 | 7F consumer cards and evidence horizons | **One wording error corrected:** the Billing dedup key (SEC-7K-17). CON-06 consumes five types on four families, so an entry held for Billing can pin the `voice`, `knowledge`, `campaign` or `workflow` key (RTN-7K-08). The horizons of all eleven consumers are unchanged |
| 3 | 7G Billing deferred acknowledgement, terminal dispositions, case identity | FG-7K-02. No class `V` case is resolvable by event operations in any 7K path. CNF-7K-05 extended |
| 4 | 7I privacy and key-access gates on recovered material | FG-7K-01. The capture needs generate-only on `REPLAY_MATERIAL`, the right 7I KEY-06 gives a parking workload; 7K asks for no decrypt right, no journal reader and no unmasked view |
| 5 | 7I erasure journal and restoration | FG-7K-01, FG-7K-04; CNF-7K-09, CNF-7K-10. OD-7K-05 leaves 7I JRN-06, -07, -09, -20 and -29 as written (REG-7K-26). Fourth remediation, two corrections: the wait of a class RM-3 pair is bounded by the deadline of 7I ACC-17 (MRA-7K-15 (3)); the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) after activation is a separate function, so that 7I JRN-27 is not contradicted. Fifth remediation: the first of those two corrections was itself the defect FG-7K-R5-01; 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17 now apply to a restored pair exactly as frozen, with no restart of the age and no condition on a replay's state (MRA-7K-16) |
| 6 | Regional profiles and immutable region binding | REG-7K-02 and REG-7K-24 now say that kind F re-homes a stack and writes no organization's binding. Class E unchanged |
| 7 | 7H callback and external-delivery replay | No 7K rule on these changed. 7K's statements rest on 7I JRN-25 rows 1 – 5 and 9 and on JRN-30, which were re-read; 7H's own text was not re-read in this round |
| 8 | 7J observation accounting after an incarnation change | No change (§21.7; OAC-7K-12) |
| 9 | Voice recovery and EV-079 finalization | No change. An EV-079 entry that is origin-unverifiable is held for Billing and applies nothing (SEC-7K-20); recovery still writes no usage (INV-7K-12) |
| 10 | Financial clearance after a lossy restore | Consistent with the Billing changes: usage ingestion is not gated (DRS-7K-21); a held Billing entry on a superseded key is class D-2 and its missing entry is covered by 7G BIL-06 |
| 11 | Authority of every Redis destructive command | SUP-7K-08 lists each. Key deletion has no frozen authority (CNF-7K-08). The topology commands of RED-7K-18 belong to the topology identity of 7E FMD-09 (b), not to a recovery operator |
| 12 | Pre-production verification and claimed guarantees | No test, benchmark, drill or provider validation was executed. No guarantee of this document depends on V-1 … V-3 being true without their validation; where one would, the gate is closed (AG-7K-02) |

| FG-7K-R2-02 (P1) Exhaustive `BARRED` handling | MRA-7K-10 and §21.9 required that no restored row without `CLEAR` stay serviceable, while CNF-7K-09 worded only the completed-subject-erasure case, and with a tombstone that 7I RET-01 does not allow | The outcome table of MRA-7K-10; MRA-7K-03, -04 (rows 5, 7), -11, -13; §21.9 criterion 2; CNF-7K-09 (a) – (d) | 7I RET-01, CAG-02, CAG-08, ACC-05, ERA-17, HLD-03, HLD-11, HLD-18, SEAL-02, SEAL-07, SEAL-14, JRN-16, JRN-24 … JRN-26; 7G T-08, T-13, RPM-01, DAU-02, PRT-02 | T-7K-46 (b), (h), (i), (j), (k) | Ten reasons, each with its frozen treatment, evidence, where content stays, vault, tombstone, obligation, quarantine verdict and amendment. 7K deletes and alters nothing; the barrier is the refusal of the two capture functions; no case becomes terminal; reasons 9 and 10 keep quarantine closed. **Not activatable** before CNF-7K-09 |
| Open owner decision | REG-7K-21 identified the choice and the first remediation handed it to 7L | OD-7K-05 (§27.1, §27.2); REG-7K-21; CNF-7K-10; RR-7K-09; AG-7K-08; §3 rows 16, 27, 35 | 7I JRN-06, JRN-09, JRN-13, JRN-16, JRN-20, JRN-29; OD-7K-02 | T-7K-40 (c) … (e) | **Open.** Three options recorded with their consequences; none is in force; class S cross-region recovery stays closed |

**Before and after.**

| Counterexample | After the first remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A subject's erasure commits between the clearance pre-check and the `XRANGE` | The entry was read; the later transaction refused to store it | No `XRANGE` exists. The entry is never read |
| Redis answers the read late, after the deadline or the erasure | The same: read, then refused | No Redis call is part of any eligibility decision |
| A restored `PUBLISHED` row is attribution-uncertain and still inside its retention, with an unaccounted group | `BARRED`, but nothing said what became of the row; the frozen reconciler would capture it at `T_obl` | Untouched and unreachable: both capture functions refuse it; the group's obligation is a case for its owner; the stack reopens only with that refusal in force (MRA-7K-10 row 6, MRA-7K-13) |
| A restored row under a legal hold whose subject also has an erasure | Covered only by a tombstone rule written for erased subjects | `HELD_RESTRICTED`: no deletion, no tombstone, not captured; preserved as 7I already preserves it (row 5) |
| The origin region and its journal store are lost | Called an open question for 7L | Owner decision OD-7K-05, open; the stack stays quarantined meanwhile |

**Regression of the first six corrections.** FG-7K-01: strengthened; nothing is re-created from a stream, and a restored row only with `CLEAR`. FG-7K-02: unchanged; `ORIGIN_UNVERIFIABLE` stays pending until T-14 (SEC-7K-20). FG-7K-03: unchanged; lag 0 **and** empty PEL (GEN-7K-09). FG-7K-04: unchanged; class E absolute, class S copy gated, now also on OD-7K-05. FG-7K-05: unchanged; no timer or offset is proof (RED-7K-30). FG-7K-06: per-key recorded operation kept; one step and one class withdrawn; break-glass still never used (SUP-7K-08, -09). The supersession's fence proof no longer reads a stream's last entry: it uses group positions, which carry no payload (SUP-7K-03).

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open: OD-7K-05. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10, -11. *(Record of that round; "Now" in the table above means that round. OD-7K-05 was approved as option B on 2026-10-13: §32.9.)*

**Regression of the eight earlier corrections.** FG-7K-01: unchanged; no stream entry is read in any class (MRA-7K-07; MRA-7K-15 (5)). FG-7K-02: unchanged; no Billing acknowledgement before T-14, in class RM-1 and on an R4 redelivery (SEC-7K-20; MRA-7K-15 (6)). FG-7K-03: unchanged; lag 0 **and** an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09). FG-7K-04: unchanged; no journal copy; OD-7K-05 open. FG-7K-05: strengthened; a timer, an offset and now also a later `WAITAOF` reply are not proof of historical durability (RED-7K-30), and eligibility follows that proof. FG-7K-06: unchanged; key deletion has no frozen authority and break-glass is never used (SUP-7K-08, -09). FG-7K-R2-01: unchanged; the only content read is of a restored row inside the transaction that decides its eligibility (MRA-7K-08). FG-7K-R2-02: unchanged; every `BARRED` reason keeps its row of MRA-7K-10; no `PUBLISHED` row is tombstoned or altered (7I RET-01).

**Changes of this round that go beyond the two findings, stated so that the reviewer can rate them.** (1) Restored `PUBLISHED` rows are recovered by R4 only. The second remediation also named the accounting reconciler's capture and R2; with a clearance that is consumed once, a second group of the same row could not have been served that way. (2) §21.9 criterion 3 admits a pair of class RM-3 that is older than `T_obl` while its R4 entry is being consumed. (3) The entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) is asked to be usable after activation by the restore's operation, for a pair that loses its source to an erasure that commits after reopening. (4) RED-7K-34 states that for a replica without the `nofailover` flag the frozen check cannot enforce the order of the admission, and that safety there rests on frozen 7E, V-2 and V-3; the earlier text asserted the order for both paths without naming what enforced it. (5) A `HOLD` case that stores crash-loop material and has no admissible source is given a named route; the earlier text did not mention it.

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open: OD-7K-05. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10. Deferred with wording and no gate: CNF-7K-11. *(Record of that round. | Situation | After the third remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A replacement replica | Flagged at its start; TR-1, TR-2 | The same. The value `no` is no longer written to a start configuration |

| A replica AOF fault that is repaired | "The durability admission … if the node is a replica", with nothing to keep the node out | E-1 at the first reading of the fault; replacement on an emptied volume. If the status clears before E-1, path 6a, stated with what it rests on |

With a clearance that is consumed once, R4 is the one reader that serves every group of the row |
| 2 | §21.9 criterion 3 admits a class RM-3 pair older than `T_obl` while its R4 entry is being consumed | **CORRECTION REQUIRED; corrected** *(that correction was itself the defect FG-7K-R5-01: §32.10)* | 7I ACC-17: a pair older than `T_obl` "goes to ACC-05 whatever ACC-16 reports", and "this rule, not ACC-16, is what guarantees detection against a gateway or a Redis that reports false state". The exemption had no bound, and the wording asked of 7I ACC-05 opened no case at all for a consumed row. Now the age restarts at the R4's position record (7I ACC-13) and the deadline applies from there; a pair past it gets its one case through CNF-7K-09 (c) (MRA-7K-15 (3); §21.9 criterion 3; class RM-5; T-7K-46 (aa)) |
| 3 | The entry of CNF-7K-09 (c) is usable after activation by the restore's operation | **CORRECTION REQUIRED; corrected (wording)** | 7I JRN-27: the restore-only functions are "granted to the restore reconciler alone" and "refuse to run while the database is activated". The earlier wording used "the same guarded ledger function" in quarantine and after activation, which cannot both be true. Now a separate function that is not restore-only, callable by the restore's operation while `RUNNING` under 7I OPA-07, and by the reconciler of 7I ACC-05 at the bound of change 2; it resolves nothing (7G DAU-02); JRN-27 is not changed (T-7K-46 (ab)) |
| 4 | RED-7K-34 states that the frozen check cannot enforce the admission order for an unflagged replica | **CORRECTION REQUIRED; corrected** | This is FG-7K-R4-01. 7E CAP-04 (8), HP-05 and MF-22 act on the `nofailover` flag; 7E RC-02 and `shard_capable` have no input for a verified replica; 7E TOPO-11 |
| 5 | A `HOLD` case that stores crash-loop material and has no admissible source | **ACCEPTED** | 7G CLP-03: the raw bytes are stored and the case is `HOLD`. 7G CLP-06: such a case is parked "using the material stored by CLP-03". 7I JRN-25 row 7: "From `OPEN_RETRY` / `HOLD` with held material: the frozen parking transition first". 7I ERA-13: no new transition is invented. The route of MRA-7K-14 is that frozen parking, with the stored material |

**Regression of the ten earlier corrections.** FG-7K-01: unchanged; no stream entry is read in any class (MRA-7K-07; MRA-7K-15 (5)), and the new RM-5 entry reads no row a second time. FG-7K-02: unchanged; no Billing acknowledgement before T-14 (SEC-7K-20; MRA-7K-15 (6)). FG-7K-03: unchanged; ordinary retirement needs lag 0 **and** an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09). FG-7K-04: unchanged; class E has no copy of anything; for class S no journal prefix or key is copied before CNF-7K-10, and no promotion completes without `H_o` (REG-7K-16, -21). FG-7K-05: strengthened; a timer, an offset, a later `WAITAOF` reply and now a recorded state are neither proof nor a control (RED-7K-30); `WAITAOF 1 1`, one replica and `noeviction` are unchanged. FG-7K-06: unchanged; key deletion has no frozen authority and break-glass is never used (SUP-7K-08, -09). FG-7K-R2-01: unchanged; the only content read is of a restored row inside the transaction that decides its eligibility (MRA-7K-08). FG-7K-R2-02: unchanged; no `PUBLISHED` row is tombstoned or altered, and no transition leaves `BARRED` or `HOLD` except through the entry of CNF-7K-09 (c). FG-7K-R3-01: unchanged; no transition needs a publication or a confirmation, and capability is required by none before TR-2 (the progress paragraph of §9.5; T-7K-11 (i)). FG-7K-R3-02: unchanged; one identity, one case (MRA-7K-15 (1)); the two corrections above add no second case and no financial effect from an unverified Billing event.

**Choices of this round that go beyond the finding and the decision, stated so that the reviewer can rate them.** (1) Every node starts not failover-eligible, so a plain restart of a replica now pauses publication in the region until its admission completes. The third round already required an admission on every rejoin (§9.3, RED-7K-17 as it then read) without enforcing it; the cost is now real and is in RR-7K-01. (2) A replica whose AOF faulted is re-admitted only as a replacement on an emptied volume. (3) The validation record of V-1 … V-3 is bound to the builds of both nodes and to the volume class, which constrains a rolling upgrade: a mixed pair needs its own record. (4) A drift check at `T_drift` and step E-1 are added to the topology identity's existing duties; they are detection and reaction, not prevention, and are described as such. (5) A class RM-3 pair past `T_obl` goes to its owning domain without material, although a lawful captured source exists; the alternative, a `PARKED` case with that source as material, is named in CNF-7K-09 (b) for 7I's owner and is not assumed. (6) 7E FMD-09 (b) does not say whether a change of eligibility needs an approval per instance; 7K assumes neither and lists it in CNF-7K-11 (c). (7) New identifiers: RED-7K-36 … -38, REG-7K-26, DRS-7K-01a, P-7K-21b, HE-7L-7K-14, T-7K-11 (n) … (w), T-7K-40 (f), (g), T-7K-46 (aa), (ab).

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open owner decisions: none. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10. Deferred with wording and a validation gate in place of a consolidation: CNF-7K-11.

### 32.10 Fifth freeze-gate remediation record

Fifth independent review: **NOT APPROVED — P1 = 2.** It confirmed OD-7K-05 as integrated, FG-7K-R4-01 as corrected at the level of the specification, and the earlier Billing, privacy, case-identity, retirement and supersession corrections as intact. "State" is the author's statement, not a rating.

| Finding | Root cause | Corrected rules | Source contracts | Regression | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FG-7K-R5-01 (P1) Deadline of a restored pair | The fourth remediation corrected an unbounded exemption with a rule of its own: it restarted the age of a class RM-3 pair at the R4's position after activation, where 7I ACC-17 takes the earliest recorded position, and it let the pair wait "while the restore's disaster replay … is not terminal and has not yet published", which has no limit. It also sent a pair past the bound to its owner without material while a lawful capture was stored | MRA-7K-13 (6), -14 (admissible source; class RM-5; notes), -15 (3), (4); **new** MRA-7K-16 with its 14-row table; §21.9 criterion 3; CNF-7K-09 (b), (c); IO-7K-21; AG-7K-08; T-7K-46 (aa), (ac) … (ar) | 7I ACC-03, ACC-05, ACC-06, ACC-13, ACC-16 … ACC-20, RET-01; 7G DSR-04, DSR-08, DSR-11, DSR-12, RPM-01, RSM-04, RTY-02, PAT-07, PID-01, PID-03, PLN-06, BIL-04 | T-7K-46 (aa), (ac) … (ar) | Corrected in 7K. One clock, the frozen one, from the earliest original position; no clock of the restore; no state of an operation is a condition. The response is the frozen case of 7I ACC-05, with the capture already stored under 7G DSR-12 as its material, because the row is read once. **Not activatable:** frozen ACC-05 words only a capture of the row; the wording is CNF-7K-09 (b), which now forbids a waiting clause |
| FG-7K-R5-02 (P1) Historical durability and authorized trimming | I-DUR and PO-1 required "every entry confirmed on that shard" on both nodes, and the tests required every position of every `PUBLISHED` row. 7E TRM-02 lets the trim worker remove an entry every group has acknowledged, and a rewrite then drops it from the files. The invariant could not hold on a healthy shard, and a correct node would have failed the tests | RED-7K-19 (I-DUR), PO-1 (a), (b), table B; **new** RED-7K-39 with the classes ES-1 … ES-9 and the oracle O-1 … O-6; RED-7K-20, -24, -30, -33, -34, -37, -38 (wording: required entry); T-7K-10, T-7K-11 (b), (x) … (aa), T-7K-16 | 7E ACC-01, WAO-01 … WAO-08, TRM-01 … TRM-10, FMD-06, FMD-09, TF-10, TF-11, TF-16, TF-20, TF-22, BST-04; 7I ACC-02, ACC-11, ACC-15 … ACC-17; INV-7K-08 | T-7K-11 (a) … (aa) | Corrected in 7K. The invariant covers the required retained set: confirmed entries at or above the watermark of 7E TRM-02 that no lawful trim removed. `WAITAOF 1 1`, one replica and `noeviction` are unchanged, and no command is added. An authorized removal is shown by PostgreSQL accounting, never by age. **Unproven and gated:** V-1 … V-3; no durability is reported as demonstrated |

**The deadline, before and after.**

| Situation | After the fourth remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A pair published 3 hours before the stack reopens; `T_obl` 2 hours; the R4 published 5 minutes ago | Age 5 minutes, from the R4 position after activation. No case for another 115 minutes | Age 3 hours, from the original position. The case of 7I ACC-05 at the reconciler's first pass |
| The capture is stored; the repair publisher is down for a week | "Not terminal and has not yet published": no case, for a week | The pair's positions are on superseded keys: `GONE`; the case at once. A pair that has a case: the plan's stop condition after `T_obl`, then class RM-4 |
| The R4 published; the group never consumes | A case `T_obl` after the R4 position, without material | A case at the original deadline, `PARKED`, with the stored capture as material; R2 |
| The stored capture was erased | Class RM-5 | The same |

**The invariant, before and after.**

| Situation | After the fourth remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| An event acknowledged by every group and trimmed a day ago; a replica is replaced | PO-1 (b): "every confirmed entry is therefore inside the snapshot". False; T-7K-11 (b) would fail a correct node | Class ES-4: outside `Q(K)`. The snapshot does not contain it and need not. O-3 passes on its accounting rows |
| A pending entry is missing from the replica after a restart | Fails | Fails (ES-2; O-4) |
| An entry is deleted with `XDEL` while one group has not acknowledged it | Fails, but indistinguishable from the previous row | Fails O-3 as a removal without the trim proof (ES-5); 7I ACC-16 classes it `GONE`; ACC-05 |
| A position record exists and its entry does not | Counted as a loss | Decided by the accounting rows of its pairs. The record is history (ES-9) |

**The five design choices the review asked to be checked again.**

| # | Choice | Result | Basis |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Start-time `nofailover` against the attestation of 7E CAP-07 and TOPO-11 | Kept. No counterexample to durability or to the promotable set. One literal mismatch is disclosed and not rated here: between a primary's restart and step 7, its running value is `yes` while its last attestation says `no`; the frozen check does not read a primary's flag, and the flag has no effect on a primary (RED-7K-36 (4), (6); CNF-7K-11 (a)) | 7E CAP-04 (8), CAP-07, TOPO-11, MF-22 |
| 2 | The boundaries of V-1, V-2 and V-3 | Kept, and restated on the corrected scope: each concerns the dataset a node's files reproduce, so each is tested on a dataset that has already been trimmed (T-7K-11 (b), (x), (z)) | 7E TRM-02; RED-7K-39 |
| 3 | The unenforced interval of a same-process return | Kept as stated in RED-7K-38. Its argument now speaks of required entries; a trim the replica had not yet received leaves its earlier file with more, never less | 7E ACC-01, TF-10, TF-11 |
| 4 | The invariant after lawful trimming | **Corrected:** FG-7K-R5-02 | 7E TRM-01 … TRM-10 |
| 5 | The R4's captured source against the earliest original deadline | **Corrected:** FG-7K-R5-01 | 7I ACC-17 |

**Regression of the eleven earlier corrections.** FG-7K-01: unchanged; no stream entry is read in any class, and the retained-set oracle is a test instrument on synthetic entries, not a recovery step. FG-7K-02: unchanged; no Billing acknowledgement before T-14, also for a case opened at the deadline (MRA-7K-16 (6); T-7K-46 (aq)). FG-7K-03: unchanged; ordinary retirement needs lag 0 **and** an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09; class ES-7). FG-7K-04: unchanged; untouched by this round. FG-7K-05: strengthened in scope; no timer, offset, later `WAITAOF` reply or recorded state is proof, and now neither is the age of an entry or of a position record (RED-7K-30, RED-7K-39 (4)). FG-7K-06: unchanged; no key-deletion authority, no break-glass. FG-7K-R2-01: unchanged; the outbox row is read once, in the transaction that decides its eligibility, and the case opened at the deadline copies the stored capture inside the ledger without reading the row. FG-7K-R2-02: unchanged; a `BARRED` row is never captured and its pair goes to class RM-5; no transition leaves `BARRED`, `HOLD` or `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` except those already named. FG-7K-R3-01: unchanged; no transition of the admission needs a publication. FG-7K-R3-02: unchanged; one identity, one case; the insert at the deadline is the frozen one of 7I ACC-20, and a pair that has a case is never listed. FG-7K-R4-01: unchanged; the flag, not a record, withholds capability. OD-7K-05: unchanged; no promotion without the origin head, no objective claimed.

**Choices of this round that go beyond the two findings, stated so that the reviewer can rate them.** (1) The case opened at the deadline takes its material from the stored capture. The fourth round named this as an option for 7I's owner and chose the case without material; with the frozen clock restored, that choice would have sent every pair of a restore longer than `T_obl` to its owner without material, so the frozen response of 7I ACC-05 is now kept and only the origin of the bytes differs. It is still wording 7I has to carry (CNF-7K-09 (b)). (2) A stop condition on the restore's R4 plans, `T_obl` without an item result, bounds the wait of a pair that already has a case. It uses the frozen state `STOPPED` and adds no transition; it is a new use of `T_obl`. (3) `Q(K)` is defined on the current watermark; 7E records no applied watermark, so a past trim is shown lawful by PostgreSQL accounting only. (4) New identifiers: MRA-7K-16, RED-7K-39, the classes ES-1 … ES-9, the oracle steps O-1 … O-6, T-7K-11 (x) … (aa), T-7K-46 (ac) … (ar).

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open owner decisions: none. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10. Deferred with wording and a validation gate in place of a consolidation: CNF-7K-11.

### 32.11 Sixth freeze-gate remediation record

Sixth independent review: **NOT APPROVED — P1 = 2.** It confirmed FG-7K-R5-01 and FG-7K-R5-02 as corrected at design level; both new findings are in wording the fifth remediation wrote. "State" is the author's statement, not a rating.

| Finding | Root cause | Corrected rules | Source contracts | Regression | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FG-7K-R6-01 (P1) Missing registered group | RED-7K-39 defined the required set by `W(K)`, which 7E TRM-02 computes over the groups *present* on the key. With a registered group missing, an entry every present group had acknowledged was "below the watermark" and fell out of the set, although 7E TRM-03 forbids trimming then and 7E BST-03 recreates the group at `0` to read every retained entry again | RED-7K-19 (I-DUR); PO-1 (a), (b); RED-7K-39 (a) … (e), (1), (3), (4), (6), (7); classes ES-1 … ES-13 (ES-10 … ES-13 new); oracle O-1 … O-4; T-7K-11 (x), (z), (ab) … (ae) | 7E TRM-01 … TRM-10, BST-01 … BST-04, GRP-07, GRP-08, RET-03, ACC-01, FMD-06, FMD-09; 7I ACC-02, ACC-11, ACC-15, ACC-16, ACC-17, ACC-19 | T-7K-11 (aa) … (ae) | Corrected in 7K. `Q(K)` is every confirmed entry that no lawful trim removed; a trim is lawful only when every registered group is present and trimming is not paused; the evidence of an authorized removal covers every group of the registered owed-group set, present or not. No persisted watermark is invented, no payload is read, and the admission still reads no stream key. **No new validation dependency:** AG-7K-02 already requires every case of T-7K-11 |
| FG-7K-R6-02 (P1) Stopped R4 plan | MRA-7K-15 (4) moved the pairs of an item that a stopped plan left unpublished to RM-4, the class without an admissible source, while MRA-7K-16 (8) let a later plan publish the same stored capture. Nothing limited successive plans | MRA-7K-14 (class RM-3), -15 (2), (4), -16 (8) and row 10; **new** MRA-7K-17 with its two tables; DRS-7K-11 (b); §21.9 criterion 3; CNF-7K-09 (c); IO-7K-21; RB-7K-10; T-7K-46 (ae), (as) … (aw) | 7G DSR-01 … DSR-13, PLN-01 … PLN-07, TGT-12, THR-02, THR-04, THR-05, CAN-04, RSM-01 … RSM-04, T-07, T-14, RTY-02, PAT-07, DAU-02; 7I ACC-03, ACC-05, ACC-17, ACC-18, OPA-07; 7E WAO-05 | T-7K-46 (ae), (as) … (aw) | Corrected in 7K. A pair's class follows the eligibility of its source and never a plan's state. A successor plan is the remainder only and restarts no clock. A pair with a case has an operational replay deadline fixed at the capture; at it the case is parked with the capture, with a recorded escalation. **Not activatable:** frozen 7G T-07 has no such trigger or actor; the wording is CNF-7K-09 (c), second form |

**FG-7K-R6-01: the eight counterexamples.** "Must survive" and "may be absent" are stated for a correct node; the evidence column names what decides.

| # | Case | After the fifth remediation | Now: must survive | Now: may be absent | Authoritative evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Two registered groups, one missing; the present group acknowledged everything | Every entry was below `W(K)`: outside the set | **Every retained entry** (ES-10) | Only what a lawful trim removed before the group went missing | PostgreSQL: the missing group's open registration (7I ACC-15). Redis, as observation: the group absent; the trim worker's anomaly (7E TRM-03) |
| B | The missing group returns, recreated at `0` | Not addressed | Every retained entry until the recreated group acknowledges it (ES-11) | Entries trimmed lawfully earlier | Registration unchanged; accounting rows of earlier deliveries, which make redeliveries duplicates |
| C | A group is removed by authorized retirement | Not distinguished from a missing group | Entries not yet lawfully trimmed; the retired group no longer pins (ES-13) | Entries trimmed under the watermark computed without it | Registration closed at its boundary; an accounting row or the retirement's disposition for each pair at or below it |
| D | A future group registered after earlier lawful trims | Not addressed | Entries from its explicit start position on | Everything below its start, as far as that group is concerned (ES-12) | The registration record with its start (7I ACC-15); 7E BST-04 |
| E | The primary fails while a registered group is missing | Entries acknowledged by the present group were not required on the replica | Every retained entry, on the promoted node (I-DUR; ES-10) | As A | As A; T-7K-11 (ad) |
| F | A replica is replaced and its historical durability proved while a group is missing | PO-1 was stated over the watermark-based set | Every entry in the primary's dataset; PO-1 (b) copies it whole and reads no group | As A | The admission's operation record; no command on a stream key |
| G | An illegal trim crosses a missing group's owed range | Passed as "below the watermark" | — (the entries are gone) | Nothing lawfully: O-3 fails | The missing group's pairs without accounting rows: 7I ACC-16 `GONE`, then ACC-05. The trim itself: issued while no watermark was defined |
| H | A legal trim before a later governed group movement | Covered (ES-6) | Present entries from the group's new position on | The entries trimmed before the movement | Accounting rows of the first delivery; the operation record; T-7K-11 (aa) |

**FG-7K-R6-02: before and after.**

| Situation | After the fifth remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A plan stops with an item unpublished; the capture is intact; the pair has a case in `OPEN_RETRY` | RM-4: the same case → `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` without material, "or" a further plan publishes the same capture | RM-3, unchanged. A successor plan may publish. At capture time plus `T_obl` without a publication: the case is parked with the capture; R2 |
| Plans are stopped and re-approved for a week | Each plan had its own stall condition; nothing was cumulative | One deadline, fixed at the capture. The disposition and the escalation happen once, at that instant |
| The capture was erased | RM-4 / RM-5 | The same |
| A pair without a case | 7I ACC-16, ACC-17 | The same |

**Regression of the thirteen earlier corrections.** FG-7K-01: unchanged; no stream entry is read by any recovery step, and the park copies a stored capture inside the ledger. FG-7K-02: unchanged; a CON-06 case parked with the capture is served by R2 under dual control, with no usage row without a handler commit, and a `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` Billing entry is not acknowledged before T-14. FG-7K-03: unchanged; ordinary retirement needs lag 0 **and** an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09; ES-7, ES-13). FG-7K-04 and OD-7K-05: untouched by this round; class E in region only, no journal copy before CNF-7K-10, no promotion without the origin head, no objective claimed. FG-7K-05: unchanged; no timer, offset, later reply, record or age is proof. FG-7K-06: unchanged; no key-deletion authority and no break-glass. FG-7K-R2-01: unchanged; the outbox row is read once; a second plan publishes from the stored capture (MRA-7K-17, row 10). FG-7K-R2-02: unchanged; a `BARRED` or otherwise ineligible source is never published and never copied (rows 5 – 7, 9). FG-7K-R3-01: unchanged; no transition of the admission needs a publication. FG-7K-R3-02: unchanged; one identity, one case; every transition of MRA-7K-17 is of the same case. FG-7K-R4-01: unchanged; the flag withholds capability. FG-7K-R5-01: unchanged; the age of 7I ACC-17 is not restarted and no state of an operation is a condition, for a pair without a case; the operational replay deadline is a different clock, labelled as 7K's, used only for a pair that 7I ACC-03 does not list. FG-7K-R5-02: kept; a lawfully trimmed entry is still never demanded.

**Choices of this round that go beyond the two findings, stated so that the reviewer can rate them.** (1) `Q(K)` now contains an entry every registered group acknowledged until a lawful trim removes it (ES-3); the fifth round had left it outside. This is 7E FMD-06 as written, and the oracle now demands every ID present at the quiesce point. (2) The operational replay deadline starts at the capture time, not at the pair's original position: a pair with a case has no frozen deadline, and starting at the original position would park every such case at reopening after any restore longer than `T_obl`. It is a 7K rule and is labelled as one. (3) At that deadline the case is parked with the capture instead of waiting or going to its owner; this is the mechanism MRA-7K-16 (3) already uses for a pair without a case. (4) The stop condition of a plan is kept but no longer decides anything about a pair. (5) New identifiers: MRA-7K-17, classes ES-10 … ES-13, T-7K-11 (ab) … (ae), T-7K-46 (as) … (aw).

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open owner decisions: none. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10. Deferred with wording and a validation gate in place of a consolidation: CNF-7K-11.

### 32.12 Seventh freeze-gate remediation record

Seventh independent review: **NOT APPROVED — P1 = 2.** It accepted FG-7K-R6-01 and FG-7K-R6-02 at specification level; both new findings are in the validation oracle that the fifth and sixth remediations wrote. "State" is the author's statement, not a rating.

| Finding | Root cause | Corrected rules | Source contracts | Regression | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FG-7K-R7-01 (P1) Historical trim judged by the current watermark | O-3 failed a position that was absent "at or above a defined `W(K)`", and ES-5 and RED-7K-39 (4) described an unlawful removal the same way. `W(K)` is recomputed from the present positions of the groups (7E TRM-02): after a group is recreated at `0` (7E BST-03) it lies below what lawful trims already removed, so a lawful removal was reported as unauthorized. Case (aa) also asked a governed `XGROUP SETID` to go below an earlier trim point, which 7G BKL-09 refuses | RED-7K-39 (b), (c), (e), **new** (f) … (i), (3), (4), (6), (8); ES-4, ES-5, ES-6, **new** ES-14; **new** TE-1 … TE-7; O-2, O-3; T-7K-11 (x), (y), (aa), (ab), **new** (ag), (ak), (al) | 7E TRM-01 … TRM-10, BST-03, BST-04, RET-01 … RET-05; 7G BKL-01 … BKL-11, GR-1 … GR-9; 7I ACC-11, ACC-13, ACC-15 … ACC-20 | T-7K-11 (aa), (ag), (ak), (al) | Corrected in 7K. A removal is judged by the state at its execution and by the PostgreSQL records of its pairs, never by a later watermark. Where the state at execution was not recorded, which is the ordinary position in production, neither a lawful trim nor a loss is asserted and the frozen accounting response decides. **No applied-watermark record is invented;** the execution-time evidence is synthetic and exists in a test only |
| FG-7K-R7-02 (P1) Unconfirmed entries demanded by the oracle | O-4 failed when any ID present at `q` was missing, and O-5 demanded equality with the primary's dataset. 7E guarantees the survival of an entry whose own `WAITAOF 1 1` returned `[≥ 1, ≥ 1]` (ACC-01, FMD-06), not of every entry that is visible: an `UNKNOWN` entry may be present and may vanish in a permitted fault | RED-7K-39 (1), (3), **new** (9) with CE-1 and CE-2; **new** classes PF-1 … PF-9; `P_q`, `C_q`, `U_q`; O-1, O-2, O-4, O-5, O-6; rows T-7K-10, -11, -16 of §26; T-7K-11 (b), (g), (x), (z), (ab), (ad), **new** (af), (ah), (ai), (aj), (am); **new** T-7K-10 (a), (b); **new** T-7K-16 (a); AG-7K-02 (an inconclusive case has not passed) | 7E MF-01 … MF-09, ACC-01 … ACC-12, FMD-01 … FMD-09, WAO-01 … WAO-08, TF-01 … TF-23; 7D TPT-02, TPT-10, PRB-01 … PRB-05, AMB-01 … AMB-05, DSP-03, LOOP-04; 7I ACC-13 … ACC-20 | T-7K-11 (af), (ah), (ai), (aj), (am); T-7K-10 (a), (b); T-7K-16 (a) | Corrected in 7K. The oracle demands the confirmed required IDs `C_q` and no others. Equality is an oracle for V-1 only, under three stated preconditions. I-DUR and RED-7K-19 are unchanged: `Q(K)` was already defined over confirmed entries, and the defect was in the oracle. **No confirmation method is added:** CE-1 and CE-2 read outcomes the frozen mechanism already produced |

**FG-7K-R7-01: the counterexample of the review.**

| Step | Sixth remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Both groups acknowledge through `1000`; `XTRIM K MINID 1001` removes `800` | Lawful (ES-4) | Lawful (e); the test records TE-1 … TE-5 for it |
| A governed operation pauses trimming; a group's cursor is behind the trim | ES-6 | ES-6. A governed repair to `500` is refused (7G BKL-09); a recreation at `0` (7E BST-03) and a test identity produce the state |
| The watermark is `501` or lower; the position record of `800` remains; Redis has no `800` | **O-3 failed:** "absent at or above a defined `W(K)`" | O-3 passes for `800`: both pairs are accounted and the trim was lawful at its execution. The watermark of `q` is not consulted |
| Must `800` reappear | Not demanded by O-4, yet reported by O-3 as a removal without authorization | No. It has left `Q(K)` for good (g); it is in no `C_q` |
| Does the group operation restore a delivery guarantee | Excluded in ES-6, contradicted by O-3 | No (RED-7K-39 (6)). A backfill is an approved R3 plan with the sources of 7G BKL-05, or `SOURCE_MISSING` |
| The same absence in production, with no record of the state at the trim | Treated as not authorized | ES-14: neither a loss nor a lawful trim is asserted. An unaccounted pair goes to 7I ACC-16 and ACC-17, then ACC-05; with every pair accounted nothing is owed and nothing is opened |

**FG-7K-R7-02: the counterexample of the review.**

| Step | Sixth remediation | Now |
|---|---|---|
| `XADD` returns `R`; `WAITAOF 1 1` returns `[1, 0]`, or the reply is ambiguous | `UNKNOWN` (7E WAO-05); the oracle did not record it | `UNKNOWN`; TE-7 records the replies; `R` has neither CE-1 nor CE-2 |
| `R` is visible at the observation point | Counted among the IDs "present at `q`" | In `P_q` and in `U_q`, not in `C_q` (PF-4, PF-5) |
| No later acknowledgement covers `R`; a permitted crash; `R` is absent after the restart | **O-4 failed** | O-4 does not demand `R`. No loss and no breach of I-DUR is reported |
| The outbox row | Unchanged frozen behaviour | Unchanged: `PENDING` or `CLAIMED`, never `PUBLISHED` on that attempt, never `FAILED`; published again with the same `event_id` (7D DSP-03, AMB-01). O-6 fails if a position or a `PUBLISHED` mark exists for `R` |
| Another copy survived | Not addressed by the oracle | A duplicate with one effect (7D AMB-05; 7F): T-7K-11 (ai). Survival confirms nothing |

**The regression cases the review required.** Each is specified with a pass and fail oracle; none has been executed.

| # | Required case | Test | Frozen guarantee behind the oracle |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Confirmed entry survives a primary crash | T-7K-10 (a) | 7E ACC-01, TF-02, TF-10 |
| 2 | Confirmed entry survives an eligible failover | T-7K-10 (b) | 7E TF-01, TF-03, FMD-06 |
| 3 | Confirmed pending entry survives a replica replacement | T-7K-11 (af) | 7E FMD-06, TF-10; PO-1 (b), which rests on V-1 |
| 4 | Lawfully trimmed confirmed entry remains absent | T-7K-11 (ag) | 7E TRM-02, TRM-05 |
| 5 | `UNKNOWN` entry disappears | T-7K-11 (ah) | 7E ACC-02, WAO-05, MF-08; 7D PRB-05, DSP-03 |
| 6 | `UNKNOWN` entry survives and later creates a duplicate | T-7K-11 (ai) | 7E FMD-03; 7D PRB-02, AMB-01, AMB-05 |
| 7 | AOF rewrite after an authorized trim | T-7K-11 (x) | 7E TRM-02; V-1, which the case validates |
| 8 | AOF rewrite fails | T-7K-16 (a); T-7K-11 (l) for the rewrite of an admission | 7E HP-06, ACC-01 |
| 9 | Replica restarts during synchronization | T-7K-11 (d), (aj) | 7E TF-10, TF-11, CAP-04 |
| 10 | Missing registered group | T-7K-11 (ab), (ac), (ad) | 7E TRM-03, BST-03 |
| 11 | Governed group cursor moves backward after a lawful trim | T-7K-11 (aa) | 7E TRM-06, TRM-07; 7G BKL-09 |
| 12 | Current watermark below a historical lawful-trim boundary | T-7K-11 (ak) | 7E TRM-02, TRM-05, BST-03; 7G BKL-09 |
| 13 | Unlawfully deleted unaccounted entry | T-7K-11 (y) | 7E TF-20; 7I ACC-16, ACC-05 |
| 14 | `PUBLISHED` row whose position record outlives lawful transport retention | T-7K-11 (al) | 7I ACC-11, ACC-03; 7D TPT-10 |
| 15 | Redis entry whose attempted publication never became `PUBLISHED` | T-7K-11 (am) | 7E TF-21; 7I ACC-18 (a), (d); 7D PRB-02 |

**Regression of the fifteen earlier corrections.** FG-7K-01: unchanged; no stream entry is read by any recovery step, the oracle and the evidence TE-1 … TE-7 are test instruments on synthetic entries that read identifiers only, and no entry of a superseded stream is read (PF-9; MRA-7K-07). FG-7K-02: untouched; no Billing acknowledgement before T-14 (PF-7 refers to ES-2 for a Billing case). FG-7K-03: untouched; ordinary retirement needs lag 0 **and** an empty PEL (GEN-7K-09; ES-7, ES-13; PF-9). FG-7K-04 and OD-7K-05: untouched by this round. FG-7K-05: unchanged; no offset, `INFO` field, reply on another connection, record or age is evidence of durability or of confirmation (RED-7K-39 (9)). FG-7K-06: unchanged; no identity gains a command, and the removing commands of the cases are issued by identities that exist in the test cluster only. FG-7K-R2-01, FG-7K-R2-02: untouched. FG-7K-R3-01: unchanged; no transition of the admission needs a publication, and CE-2 is read by the oracle, not by the admission. FG-7K-R3-02: untouched; one identity, one case. FG-7K-R4-01: unchanged; the flag withholds capability (T-7K-11 (aj) relies on it). FG-7K-R5-01: untouched; the clock of 7I ACC-17 is applied as frozen (PF-1; RED-7K-39 (6)). FG-7K-R5-02: kept and tightened; a lawfully trimmed entry is never demanded, now also where the current watermark lies below it. FG-7K-R6-01: kept; the evidence of RED-7K-39 (4)(i) and O-3 (i) still covers every group of the registered owed-group set, present or missing, a trim issued while a registered group is missing still fails O-3 (T-7K-11 (ab)), and every confirmed retained entry stays required while a group is missing (ES-10; O-4). FG-7K-R6-02: untouched; MRA-7K-14 … -17, CNF-7K-09 (c) and AG-7K-08 are unchanged, and PF-8 cites MRA-7K-17 (2) and (3) without altering them.

**The three ancillary choices the review asked about.** (1) *Confirmed entries stay in the required set until a lawful trim removes them:* kept (RED-7K-39 (1); ES-3; PF-3). It is 7E FMD-06 as written; after this round it applies to confirmed entries only, which is what the definition already said. (2) *The capture-time operational deadline for an existing case:* kept, unchanged (MRA-7K-16 (8); MRA-7K-17 (3)). (3) *The controlled 7L park-with-capture amendment:* kept, unchanged; it is CNF-7K-09 (c), it is not an implemented or frozen capability, and AG-7K-08 stays closed.

**Choices of this round that go beyond the two findings, stated so that the reviewer can rate them.** (1) O-4 also fails when an ID of `C_q` is shown as acknowledged by a group that had not acknowledged it at `q`; this is the "deliverable at least once to every group" half of 7E FMD-06, which the earlier oracle read but did not test. (2) O-5 now compares a node with its own reading before the kill instead of with the primary's reading at `q`, and is named an oracle for V-1 only. (3) Case (aa) no longer asks a governed `XGROUP SETID` to go below an earlier trim point: 7G BKL-09 refuses a position below the stream's first entry, so the cursor behind a trim is produced by the recreation at `0` of 7E BST-03 and, in (ak), by a test identity. The review's counterexample is kept in full in (ak), with the governed repair to `500` shown as refused. (4) A third verdict, *inconclusive*, exists beside pass and fail, and AG-7K-02 says that it is not a pass. (5) A confirmed entry whose relay stopped before the mark (7E TF-21) is demanded only where the test recorded CE-2; without it the oracle does not claim that it was confirmed. An `UNKNOWN` entry that a later reply on the same connection would have covered stays outside `C_q`. (6) New identifiers: RED-7K-39 (f) … (i) and (9); CE-1, CE-2; ES-14; PF-1 … PF-9; TE-1 … TE-7; `P_q`, `C_q`, `U_q`; T-7K-11 (af) … (am); T-7K-10 (a), (b); T-7K-16 (a).

**Remaining contradiction known to the author:** none inside 7K. Open owner decisions: none. Deferred with wording and closed gates: CNF-7K-05, -08, -09, -10. Deferred with wording and a validation gate in place of a consolidation: CNF-7K-11.

### 32.13 Final status

**PHASE 7K — READY FOR INDEPENDENT FREEZE-GATE RE-REVIEW**

OD-7K-01 … OD-7K-05 are approved; no owner decision is open, and none was raised in this round. The two P1 findings of the seventh review are corrected in this revision and are for the independent reviewer to rate. Nothing in this revision was executed against Redis, PostgreSQL or a provider: V-1, V-2 and V-3, every test, every benchmark and every drill remain specified and not run, the test evidence TE-1 … TE-7 has not been recorded for any case, the 76 measurement obligations are open, and no durability is reported as demonstrated. Approval and freezing are decisions of the independent reviewer and the platform owner.
